# WAHALA! decisions log

Gameplay and tuning decisions taken during implementation, with the reason. The Game Design Bible stays authoritative; entries here are either the founder's approved D1-D16 defaults or implementation choices where the Bible left a gap. Update this file whenever playtesting changes a number or a rule.

## Approved defaults (D1-D16, plus clarifications)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | Players pass through each other; generators, concrete and soft blocks block. Prevents corridor deadlocks and body-block griefing. | `systems/stepping.ts` |
| D2 | Remote Starter affects generators placed **after** pickup. Unused remote generators auto-detonate after 20 s (failsafe). One button detonates all of the owner's remote generators. | `systems/generators.ts`, `config.generator.remoteFailsafeTicks` |
| D3 | Vehicle damage = number of **unique flame tiles of one chain reaction that lie on the vehicle's route**, summed across separate chains in one journey (never per-bomb counting, no double counting inside a chain). Keke 4, taxi 6, Danfo 9. Every damaging encounter slows the vehicle (x1.8 for 1.5 s). | `systems/vehicles.ts` `applyBlastExposure`, `config.vehicles` |
| D4 | A Kill-Switched generator frees its owner's capacity slot. | `queries.activeGeneratorCount` |
| D5 | Disarmed generators can still be kicked / punched / carried and still explode from flame, chains and vehicles. Cleanup: a disabled dud disappears after 25 s without exploding. | `generators.updateGeneratorTimers` |
| D6 | Vehicle-entry selection is real-time: the world keeps running, the selector stands still, **8 s** window; cancelling or timing out keeps the voucher. | `systems/vehicles.ts`, `app/vehicleEntry.ts` |
| D7 | Keke stops at its 2nd player hit, taxi at its 1st, Danfo never (all stop at concrete). Taxi breaks at most 2 soft blocks, Keke 1, Danfo any number. | `config.vehicles.specs` |
| D8 | **Any** border tile with a legal route is selectable (28 entries on Danfo Bus Park, corners count once per side). 1.2 s warning (lane + gate lamps + horn) before the vehicle moves. Keke: choose entry, then the turn tile + side. | `systems/entries.ts` |
| D9 | Okada absorbs **all** lethal hits of the tick it breaks in, then ignores those same events (until their flames expire / for 0.75 s for vehicles). A separate later event kills the rider. | `systems/lethality.ts` |
| D10 | Flames destroy floor pickups, except the one uncovered by that same blast. | `systems/pickups.ts` |
| D11 | Contextual controls: kick is automatic on walking into a generator; one context button (carry/drop/punch/throw), skill, detonate (only with Remote), disarm (only with charges), vehicle (only with a voucher). | `ui/TouchControls.tsx` |
| D12 | Push and Shove both move a rival one tile (shove has a shorter cooldown 3 s vs 4 s); Daze freezes for 1 s then 2 s of immunity. | `systems/skills.ts` |
| D13 | "Skip to results" fast-forwards the real simulation (bots only) to its true outcome. | `MatchController.skipToResults` |
| D14 | Arena 15 x 11 tiles, pillars on odd/odd tiles, 4-fold mirrored soft blocks. | `arenas/danfoBusPark.ts` |
| D15 | Generic "Yellow Cab" branding for the taxi. | `data/vehicles.ts` |
| D16 | Wahala Crush, crowd throws and floor traps ship **off by default**. | `app/settings.ts` |

## Implementation choices (not in the Bible)

- **Movement model:** a step commits its destination tile immediately; the renderer interpolates. One step = 10 ticks (6 tiles/s). Roller Skates 9/8/7, Okada -2 ticks, slow curse +4.
- **Flame behaviour:** a neighbouring generator absorbs an arm (and chains); soft blocks are removed only after the whole chain resolves so simultaneous blasts never disagree about what blocks what. Flame lifetime 0.6 s.
- **Chain events:** one chain reaction = one `BlastEvent` / event id; separate triggers in one tick that do not chain are separate events.
- **Spawn safety:** each spawn clears an L of three tiles plus the two corner-turn tiles (`(2,1)` and `(1,2)` relative to the corner) so a player can always escape their own first blast. Added after the e2e suite showed a corner could be sealed by random soft blocks.
- **Carry rule:** +1 s only on the first pickup of a **timed** generator; picking up remote/disabled generators gives nothing. Carried generators tick, can explode in hand, and are thrown if the carrier owns Gloves.
- **Jump Sneakers** hop exactly one generator (2 s cooldown). **Hot Fuel** is a one-generator boost: its arms burn through the first soft block of each arm.
- **Curses:** slow, reversed controls, weak fire (range 1) for 8 s. Pickup weights are in `config.pickups.weights`.
- **Death loot:** up to 2 held upgrades dropped on the nearest empty tiles (BFS), never into fire.
- **Wahala Crush:** see "Gameplay corrections" below (supersedes the original 0:40 start / 0.8 s cadence). An Okada rider under a landing block is shoved to the nearest free tile (Okada consumed). Crush kills inside one tick can draw.
- **Crowd throws:** first at 0:25, then every 9-16 s, 2 s telegraph at a fixed tile (dodgeable). Bottle dazes; rare firecracker is lethal on its tile and the four around it (and sets generators off). Okada absorbs it.
- **Floor traps** (Lekki Roadworks only): pothole = brief stumble; oil = slide 2 tiles. Never lethal.
- **Pause policy:** auto-pause when the tab is hidden; Esc/P toggles; pausing is blocked after the match ends.
- **Pidgin captions** are original lines written for this project; a native-speaker review is recommended before shipping.

## Presentation overhaul (branch `claude/presentation-overhaul`) - no gameplay rules changed

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P1 | Assets are **generated from code** (numpy/scipy/Pillow -> glTF), not downloaded: the sandbox network blocks every CC0/asset host (Poly Haven, Quaternius, Kenney, Sketchfab, Mixamo, Freesound ...) and Blender's wheel is impractical. Provenance is therefore clean (all original), quality is bounded by what procedural modelling can do; see the overhaul report for honest limits. | `tools/assets/`, `docs/ASSET_PIPELINE.md` |
| P2 | Characters are rigged glTF skinned meshes (22 bones, 16 clips) driven by an `AnimationMixer` (`RiggedCharacter`); clip choice is a pure function of existing sim state (move/carry/mounted/dazed/dead) plus one-shots from `SimEvent`s. The procedural primitive models remain only as a load-failure fallback. | `render/assets/rigged.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts` |
| P3 | All models load through one `AssetLibrary` (Vite glob of `src/assets/models/**.glb`, base-path safe); the title screen shows load progress and the menu's live demo starts only when the cast is ready. | `render/assets/library.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| P4 | HUD / title portraits are rendered from the 3D models (throw-away WebGL context) instead of drawn faces. | `render/portraits.ts`, `assets/portraits.ts` |
| P5 | A bots-only "attract" match runs behind the title menu (not on the `low` quality profile, never with `?nobots` / `?noattract`); it is a normal sim instance with the same code path and never touches the player's match. | `app/AttractController.ts` |
| P6 | Music is removed. The audio engine now produces SFX, engine/vehicle loops, a layered crowd ambience and event-driven crowd reactions (all synthesised; **no recorded Nigerian crowd or music is bundled and none is claimed**). Recorded files can be dropped into `public/audio/` to override any slot. | `src/audio/` |

## Visual pass v3 (branch `claude/wahala-visual-v3`) - presentation only, no gameplay rules or tuning changed

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| V1 | Camera pitch 58 -> 51 degrees, FOV 36 -> 44 (56 portrait); the look-at point is re-centred for each fitted distance so the perspective-shrunk far edge and the 1.5 m far wall top fill the HUD-free band. Picking still ray-casts this camera onto the floor; a unit test round-trips every tile on desktop, landscape phone and portrait. | `render/cameraRig.ts`, `tests/render/camera.test.ts` |
| V2 | Permanent vs destructible is separated by value, height and hue: pillars 1.14 m pale precast concrete with a blue/yellow foot band; walls 1.05 m with pilasters (stepping down to 0.4 on the camera side so no tile is hidden); crates warm light pine at 0.74 m, barricades and tyres; soft-block variants are weighted (crates > barricades/tyres > sacks > market goods) instead of uniformly mixed. Collision and tile positions are untouched. | `tools/assets/env_pillars.py`, `env_walls.py`, `env_soft.py`, `render/assets/props.ts` |
| V3 | Taller pillars can hide a player in the row behind them, so occluded parts of every player are drawn as a slot-coloured silhouette (stencil-masked so limbs never tint their own body). Generator countdown numbers already draw on top. | `render/assets/xray.ts` |
| V4 | Characters get stylised game proportions (thicker limbs, broader chest, 1.2x head about the chin, chunkier trainers) and are drawn 1.62 tiles tall (was 1.5); faces have fuller jaws, broad noses, fuller lips and dark lid lines; a warm Fresnel rim separates them from the floor. | `tools/assets/chargen.py`, `build_characters.py`, `render/assets/rim.ts`, `rigged.ts` |
| V5 | The Okada is drawn 1.55x (wheels and front end stick out beyond the rider), in Bajaj red rather than danfo yellow, with the rider seated on the seat, a faint golden Fresnel shield bubble, an OKADA badge, speed dust, a mount pop and a dismount hop. These read `Player.mounted` only; the one-hit rule is unchanged. | `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `okadaShield.ts`, `tools/assets/veh_okada.py` |
| V6 | Colour grade is folded into tone mapping (`CustomToneMapping`: ACES + warm balance + saturation + gentle S-curve) instead of a post-processing pass, so it costs nothing on phones. No SSAO pass: depth cues come from stronger sun shadows, baked AO in the atlases and the grade. | `render/grading.ts`, `render/lighting.ts` |
| V7 | Explosions light the scene through a fixed pool of point lights (0/1/3 by quality; the count never changes during a match, so no shader recompiles); flames have an alpha-blended orange body plus an additive hot core so they keep their mass on the pale floor. Vehicles trail additive speed streaks. | `render/views/FlameViews.ts`, `VehicleViews.ts`, `render/quality.ts` |
| V8 | The crowd is a street, not a stadium: watchers (calm poses flipping to reactions with match excitement), facing conversation pairs and street walkers (two-frame stride), 20 outfits incl. ankara, wrappers, kaftan + fila and basin carriers. Each pose is an InstancedMesh refilled per frame with only the people in that pose. 70 / 170 / 320 people by quality; ~2.3k triangles each after vertex-cluster decimation. Visual only (crowd sound is not part of this pass). | `tools/assets/crowd_lib.py`, `build_crowd.py`, `render/assets/crowd.ts` |
| V9 | Model folder grew from 14.6 MB to ~16.0 MB (crowd pose pairs, taller wall/pillar atlases at reduced JPEG quality), slightly over the 15 MB target in the pipeline doc; accepted for now, the danfo/taxi/keke atlases are the next place to trim. | `src/assets/models` |

## Gameplay corrections (branch `claude/wahala-controls-crush`) - from human playtesting

**Wahala Crush schedule.** The collapse began with ~80-90 s left, far too early. It is now paced from the real tile count (`crushSchedule` in `sim/state.ts`, config `crush.*`):

| Moment (time left) | What happens |
| --- | --- |
| 2:00 - 0:45 | Arena fully open, nothing telegraphed. |
| 0:45 | First tile telegraphed (`crush.startRemainingTicks`, default 45 s, configurable for playtests). It lands 1.2 s later (`crush.warnTicks`), so the first block closes at 0:43.8. |
| 0:45 - 0:02 | Outer ring inwards (same clockwise spiral), tiles telegraphed at an even cadence so that every tile except the last has landed at 0:02 (`crush.finalTileLeadTicks`). 15 x 11 arena: ~0.28 s per tile; each tile warns 1.2 s ahead so about four upcoming tiles are always visible. |
| 0:02 | Only the final safe tile is left. It is telegraphed at 0:01.2. |
| 0:00 | Final tile lands. Anyone still alive dies in that tick, so nobody left = draw (simultaneous); one survivor earlier = normal win. |

An Okada rider on the very last tile is not saved (no free tile exists to shove them to). Classic Timer is unchanged (no collapse, draw by timeout at 0:00). A match shorter than the start threshold starts collapsing immediately. `CrushState.warnAt` holds the per-tile schedule; bots read it for their danger map.

**Movement input.** Measured root cause: the sim reads `Intent.move` as a level and starts a new step the tick the previous one ends (10 ticks), while `InputManager` kept the direction alive for 8 more ticks after release. A normal 100 ms tap (6 ticks) therefore produced two tiles (old behaviour measured: 1-tick and 3-tick taps = 1 tile, 6- and 9-tick taps = 2 tiles). Fix in `input/moveGate.ts` for all devices: a press is one request delivered on the first tick the player can step (`readyToStep`) and discarded after that whether or not it moved; at most one press is buffered, for at most 10 ticks; a direction held 12 ticks repeats at the player's own step cadence; release stops immediately and nothing is carried over. The simulation, step durations, speed upgrades and the Okada bonus are untouched. Stick input got a small hysteresis so a wobbling stick near the diagonal cannot register extra presses.

## Lagos audio (branch `claude/wahala-lagos-audio`) - no gameplay rules changed

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | Music is **pre-rendered audio files** (Ogg Vorbis + MP3 fallback) generated offline from original note data, not a runtime oscillator sequence. Supersedes P6 ("music is removed"). Loops are gapless by construction (wrap-around mixing) and the player loops `[0, manifest samples)`. | `tools/audiogen/`, `src/audio/music.ts`, `public/audio/music/` |
| A2 | Four independent persistent volumes (master 80, music 45, Lagos ambience and voices 65, effects 85) in their own store `wahala.audio.v1`; mute stays in `GameSettings.muted` (shared with the HUD button). The engine follows the store itself; the legacy `setVolumes()` is ignored while a store is active so stale callers cannot override saved choices. The old `musicVolume`/`sfxVolume` fields in `GameSettings` are now unused. | `src/audio/prefs.ts`, `src/ui/AudioSettings.tsx` |
| A3 | The crowd is a **street crowd, not a stadium**: `roar` and `cheerBig` reactions are demoted to quiet cheers; the babble beds sit under the speech and duck while a line is spoken. | `reactions.ts` (street mode), `ambience.ts`, `buses.ts` |
| A4 | **Spoken Pidgin is synthetic placeholder speech**, flagged as such in the data (`voices.json` `synthetic: true`), the settings panel and the docs. Reason: no Nigerian voice, no Pidgin/Yoruba/Igbo TTS and no recording host was reachable and paid services were excluded. Kokoro-82M (Apache-2.0, offline) reads the Pidgin spelling through its English front end (A/B: 0.88 vs 0.72 for hand-written IPA, Whisper round-trip proxy). Real recordings replace any line through `public/audio/voices/recorded.json` (licence statement required). | `tools/audiogen/build_voices.py`, `src/audio/pidgin.ts`, `docs/AUDIO.md` |
| A5 | Crowd speech pacing: category cooldowns of 8-30 s, at least 4.5 s between unforced reaction lines, one reaction voice at a time, shuffle-bag line choice with a 40 s recent-line memory, speaker rotation, 0.6 s trigger de-duplication, ambient scenes held back 2.5 s after any reaction. A first real-browser run produced a reaction every ~3 s, which was too chatty. | `src/audio/pidgin.ts` |
| A6 | Levels were set from measurements of a real-Chromium recording of the engine output (loudness per layer, speech-to-text round trip of the lines inside the mix): first version had spoken lines only 0-2 dB above the babble bed (inaudible), so the bed was lowered, lines raised, presence-EQ'd and the beds/music duck for speech. Still **not judged by ear**. | `tools/audio/browserSmoke.mjs` |

| U3 | Because the rejected voices are gone, the two tweaks made for them are reverted to the pre-audio-branch behaviour the founder heard on the approved branches: the synthesised crowd bed levels (`levelsFor`) and the crowd's big cheers/roars (engine no longer turns on street mode; the option stays in `reactions.ts`). Supersedes A3. | `src/audio/ambience.ts`, `src/audio/engine.ts` |

It remains available on its branch. | - |

## Cinematic camera overhaul (branch `claude/wahala-camera-overhaul`) - presentation only, no gameplay rules changed

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | **Founder directive (2026-10-22) supersedes the camera wording of Bible sections 1 and 12** ("fixed elevated camera showing the entire standard grid"). The default gameplay camera is now a low, close three-quarter **action camera** (41 deg above the floor, 38 deg FOV, about 11 world units from the focus, camera ~7 m up instead of ~15 m) that follows the local player with critically-damped motion (0.3 s position, 0.55 s zoom). It never rotates (north is always up, controls never change meaning), never cuts during play and never zooms in on its own. | `src/render/cameraRig.ts` |
| C2 | Fairness guard for the closer view (Bible 1.5, "no inexplicable offscreen deaths"): the camera widens just enough to keep in frame every live generator whose cross can reach the player (same row/column within range + 2, or within 2 tiles), every vehicle whose remaining route passes within 2 tiles, and the player's own vehicle while it drives. The same rules are unit-tested. | `src/render/cameraDirector.ts`, `tests/render/camera.test.ts` |
| C3 | The **whole-arena overview** remains one key/tap away (`O`, or the map button next to pause/mute; the choice persists in settings as `cameraView`). The camera also pulls out to the overview automatically while choosing a vehicle entrance (every border arrow must be visible and clickable), for the first half of the countdown (establishing shot, then a glide down to the player), and on a draw. A win ends on a close-up of the winner; when the local player is out the camera frames the survivors. | `GameRenderer.render`, `cameraDirector.directShot` |
| C4 | Phones: the action camera may show more street beyond the side/south walls so a player in a corner never stands under the on-screen D-pad or action buttons (unit-tested for every open tile). | `MARGIN_COMPACT` in `cameraRig.ts` |
| C5 | Obstacles are drawn squatter (pillars ~0.92 m, soft blocks ~0.6 m) and characters at 1.86 tiles (Gemini WIP had 2.02): under a low camera a tall pillar hides the lane behind it, and the characters must stay the top of the visual hierarchy. Footprints, positions and collision are unchanged. | `ArenaView.ts`, `rigged.ts` |
| C6 | Each arena has its own art direction data (light, fog, exposure, floor/obstacle tints) and set dressing: Danfo Bus Park = golden late-afternoon sun, bay lines, crates and tyres; Balogun Market = high midday sun, terracotta floor, Ankara-wrapped stall pillars, sacks/water/drinks, bunting and canopies; Lekki Roadworks = cool blue-hour light, dark patched asphalt with lane lines, chevron-banded pillars, barricades/tyres, half-built frame, tower crane, excavator, floodlights and a lit skyline. The breakable-prop **family** per arena is visual only (which model draws a soft block); tile layout and density are untouched. | `src/render/arenaLook.ts`, `assets/arenaSets.ts`, `assets/props.ts` |
| C7 | Gemini's WIP commit `3c4c4b4` changed two gameplay numbers in `src/sim/config.ts` (`pickups.hiddenChance` 0.4 -> 0.58, `arena.softDensity` 0.62 -> 0.38). Both are restored: they change pickup economy and match pacing, which this presentation task must not touch. | `src/sim/config.ts` |

## Gritty gameplay v1 (branch `claude/wahala-gritty-gameplay-v1`) - founder corrections of 2026-10-23

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| G1 | **Founder correction supersedes C1-C4 for standard matches:** every standard 2-4 player match keeps the WHOLE playable arena on one shared screen for the entire match (countdown, play, spectating, results). One fixed cinematic three-quarter camera at 46 degrees, fitted inside the HUD-free band (and clear of the phone touch controls). It never follows a player or zooms towards survivors; the `O` key and map button are hidden. | `render/cameraModes.ts`, `render/cameraRig.ts`, `tests/render/camera.test.ts`, `e2e/game.spec.ts` |
| G2 | The close follow camera (C1-C4) is kept as the reusable **stadium** camera mode for the future 32-player stadium arena. No arena uses it yet (`STADIUM_ARENAS` is empty); `?cam=stadium` forces it for testing, with its overview toggle. No 32-player gameplay was added. | `render/cameraModes.ts` |
| G3 | **Mandatory endgame floor traps** in every standard match, every arena, both modes: a symmetric wave of potholes and oil slicks is telegraphed on the floor for 1.5 s and goes live at exactly 0:30 left (the start of the final-30-seconds escalation), a second wave at 0:15. No setting can turn them off. Reason: founder requirement that the arena gets more dangerous at the end. Times and warning are `config.hazards.endgameWaveRemainingTicks` / `endgameWarnTicks`; layouts are arena data (`endgameTraps`). | `sim/systems/hazards.ts`, `sim/arenas/*.ts`, `tests/endgame-traps.test.ts` |
| G4 | Endgame trap fairness: only a step that STARTS onto a live trap triggers it (a trap appearing under a standing player does nothing until they move); waves never land on pillars; in Wahala Crush never on the final safe tile or its four neighbours; a crushed tile buries its trap. Traps stay non-lethal (stumble / slide). The Crush schedule (0:45 warning, 0:43.8 first landing, final tile at 0:00) is unchanged and re-tested with the traps on. | `sim/state.ts` `createEndgameTraps`, `sim/systems/hazards.ts` |
| G5 | The old "Floor traps" option becomes **"Early traps"** (traps for the whole match, on top of the endgame waves) and is available on every arena; it defaults to on only for the Lekki trap arena. Match duration, crate density, pickup odds and every other balance number are unchanged. | `sim/arenas/*.ts`, `ui/TitleScreen.tsx` |
| G6 | Music: the founder's ten approved Pixabay tracks form the playlist (rotation per match, no immediate repeat, arena playlists and signature tracks supported); none could be downloaded from this environment (the proxy refuses pixabay.com), so the game stays silent until the files are dropped in. Endgame escalation from 0:30: pitch-preserved tempo 1.00 -> 1.09 by 0:00 plus a brightness lift with gain compensation. Details in `docs/AUDIO.md`. | `src/audio/playlist.ts`, `src/audio/escalation.ts`, `src/audio/music.ts` |
| G7 | Visual language: a world-space weathering patch on every lit material (`grit.ts`: dusty desaturation after texture/vertex/instance colour, grime and soot mottling, street dirt from the ground up, dust on upward faces, roughness), per class (scenery, floor, vehicle, crowd, player, skin). Colour comes from goods and paint, faded, never toy-bright. Presentation only. | `render/grit.ts` |
| G8 | Grade and light: a harder cinematic grade (firm S-curve, crushed blacks, mild bleach bypass, teal/amber split tone), a heavier vignette, and per-arena moods (smoggy bus-park afternoon with sodium street lamps; hazy market noon with bare bulbs under the stalls; Lekki night shift under floodlights and work lamps). Lamps are point lights without shadows and are skipped on the low profile (which gets a stronger fill instead). | `render/grading.ts`, `render/arenaLook.ts`, `render/lighting.ts` |
| G9 | Each arena's surroundings are its own module with its own architecture (bus terminal / dense market / construction site), not a recoloured shared set. Parked and background vehicles always use the detailed GLB models at the gameplay scale (1.32); the `env_wall_d` wall variant with a flat painted cartoon Danfo mural is no longer used. | `render/arenas/` |
| G10 | Background life is real animated characters (the cast rigs, re-dressed in muted work clothes so nobody resembles a player) acting out fights, arguments, conductors, sellers, workers, onlookers and walkers on randomised timings, capped at 16 / 10 / 4 actors (high / medium / low). The static spectator crowd is thinned where the street has its own business. Atmosphere (burning tyres, wrecks, black smoke, exhaust, dust) is three particle draw calls. None of it stands on the board. | `render/life/StreetLife.ts`, `render/fx/Ambient.ts` |
| G11 | The cast is drawn with adult head proportions in play (head bone x0.82 after the animation pose); the GLBs, clips and menu portraits are unchanged. | `render/assets/rigged.ts` |

## Nine arenas / City Rumble (branch `claude/wahala-nine-arenas-city-rumble`) - founder instruction of 2026-10-23

The founder's nine-arena / City Rumble / tutorial / mobile-launch instruction supersedes older planning documents
where they conflict (including the unlaunched cinematic-depth/tutorial prompt). Numbers below are initial values from
bot batches (`scripts/arena-balance.ts`), not from human playtests.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| N1 | **Arena rules are configuration.** An `ArenaDef` may carry `config` overrides (merged over `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, under any test override) and a `forcedMode`. Power Yard and City Rumble rules live there; no `if (arenaId === ...)` in rules code. | `sim/arenas/types.ts`, `sim/state.ts` |
| N2 | **Stage hazards replace the "Early traps" option** (supersedes G5 and the floor-trap part of D16). Each arena's floor traps (`floorTraps`) and `stageHazards` are always on; the menus have no switch. `MatchSettings.stageHazards` exists only so tutorials and test scenarios can run without them. The MANDATORY endgame waves (G3/G4) are separate and unchanged in every arena. | `sim/systems/stage.ts`, `app/settings.ts` |
| N3 | Stage hazard engine: kinds sweep (vehicle along a road route: lethal, sets off generators, stops at concrete/crates), strike (lethal tile set for a time), push (one-tile slide, optional douse into duds), drop (new crates), teleport (manhole pairs). Fairness: lethal telegraph >= 1.5 s (`stage.minLethalWarnTicks`), at most 1 lethal event warning or running (`maxLethalEvents`), none in the last 5 s or once Wahala Crush starts closing, targeted debris only on a player with a free neighbour, drops >= 2 tiles from every player and never on a player/generator/pickup, manholes only to a clear and non-burning end, 2.5 s per-player cooldown, arrival never triggers (no loops), 0.3 s climb-out pause. Okada absorbs lethal stage hits like any other. Everything uses the match RNG (replays identical). | `sim/systems/stage.ts`, `config.stage`, `tests/stage-hazards.test.ts` |
| N4 | Road tiles `=` in a layout are open floor that never receives a crate (traffic lanes, manholes, power plates). The RNG draw order of crate generation is unchanged. | `sim/state.ts` `buildTiles` |
| N5 | **Generators first:** pickup weights petrol 30, socket 30 (of 97 beneficial points = 62 %), skates 7, voucher 6, okada 4, boots 3, grip 3, killswitch 3, bombpass 3, gloves 2, remote 2, sneakers 2, diesel 1, hotfuel 1, curse 5; `hiddenChance` 0.4 -> 0.45. Caps unchanged (6 generators, range 8). Bot batch: matches still end decisively on every arena. | `config.pickups`, `tests/generator-power.test.ts` |
| N6 | **Generator Pass (Bomb Pass)**: walk over grounded generators (own and rivals') for the rest of the match; never through concrete or crates; no fire protection; fuse/owner/mode untouched; walking into a generator kicks it first if you have Boots and it can slide, otherwise you walk over it (then Jump Sneakers). Dropped as death loot. Bots plan through generator tiles when they hold it. | `systems/movement.ts`, `systems/stepping.ts`, `ai/world.ts` |
| N7 | **Surulere Power Yard** (only there, via config): `generator.blastMode: 'fullLine'` - every blast covers its whole row and column to the arena edge, passing through pillars (no flame drawn on the pillar tile itself), crates (every crate in the line breaks) and other generators (which chain in the same event). Everyone starts with 2 sockets (max 4); petrol/diesel/hot fuel never spawn there (range is meaningless). No other stage hazards: the blasts are the hazard. Warning on the arena card and at the countdown. Bot matches ~20 s. | `sim/arenas/surulerePowerYard.ts`, `systems/explosions.ts`, `ai/danger.ts` |
| N8 | Six new 15 x 11 symmetric layouts (Oshodi carriageways + flyover piers; Ajegunle compound-wall stubs + manholes; Lagoon bollards; Yaba plazas + kiosks; Third Mainland pillar-free carriageway). Oshodi first had four crate-free roads and bot matches lasted ~20 s; reduced to two carriageways (~25 s, the fastest arena after the Power Yard by design). Bot batch (medium/hard x4, 20 matches): bus park 43 s, market 57 s, Lekki 52 s, Oshodi 25 s, Ajegunle 37 s, lagoon 51 s, Power Yard 20 s, Yaba 30 s, Third Mainland 27 s; winners in 90-100 %; stage-hazard deaths 0-10 % (bots read the telegraphs). | `sim/arenas/*.ts`, `scripts/arena-balance.ts` |
| N9 | **City Rumble** board: 41 x 31, three-tile avenues, building blocks, 32 farthest-point spawns (any prefix of 8/16/24 is spread out), Wahala Crush always on: the ring closes from 3:00 left, last ring tile lands at 0:30, an 11 x 9 core never closes; five-minute match; endgame waves inside the core; bus traffic on the avenues until the closing starts. Standard arenas keep max 4 players. | `sim/arenas/cityRumble.ts`, `crush.keepCore` |
| N10 | AI: the danger map includes stage sweeps/strikes and full-line blasts; Generator Pass changes walkability; manholes count as tiles to avoid; Easy bots now avoid floor traps too (with traps on most arenas their oil slides into their own fire doubled Easy self-eliminations; solo test 8/12 -> 5/12). | `ai/danger.ts`, `ai/world.ts`, `ai/profiles.ts` |
| N11 | 32 slot colours (the four classic ones, then golden-angle hues in two lightness bands) and 32 bot names. | `assets/palette.ts`, `app/names.ts` |
| N12 | **One signature song per standard arena** (founder table, `docs/AUDIO.md`); City Rumble rotates five of them. | `public/audio/music/manifest.json` |
| N13 | Music bus +2.5 dB (`MUSIC_LIFT_DB`); warning duck on music 0.8 -> 0.7 so alarms stay on top. **Superseded by M8.** | `audio/buses.ts` |
| N14 | GitHub Pages: the preview is published from this branch to a `gh-pages` branch by `deploy.yml`; Pages must be pointed at it once by an admin (the workflow token cannot create a Pages site). See `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`. | `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` |
| N15 | CI: Chromium's `--single-process` flag is used only with the container's preinstalled Chromium (`WAHALA_CHROMIUM`); on GitHub runners it closed every new browser context, which is why the e2e job had been red. | `playwright.config.ts` |

## Nine arenas / City Rumble (branch `claude/wahala-nine-arenas-city-rumble`) - visual workstream E

Presentation only: no simulation, AI, collision, tile layout, density or timing changed.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| E1 | **Whole-arena camera re-composed** (still fixed, still frames every tile, never follows): pitch 46 -> **42 degrees**, its own longer lens (vertical FOV 38 -> **31 degrees**, 45 in portrait) so the camera sits further back (desktop distance to the look-at point **23.8**, camera at y 15.9 / z 16.7; landscape phone 27.8; portrait 48.3). The lower angle shows the height and side faces of obstacles and walls; the longer lens keeps near-row tiles at most 40% wider than far-row tiles, so the taller near obstacles are not blown up. The follow (stadium) camera keeps 41 degrees / 38 FOV; FOV is now part of the smoothed pose. Unit-tested on desktop, landscape phone and portrait (35-45 degrees, whole arena in the HUD-free band, picking round-trips, 1.7 m pillar tops inside the band). | `render/cameraRig.ts`, `tests/render/camera.test.ts` |
| E2 | **Supersedes C5 (squat obstacles).** Permanent blockers are drawn about character height: 1.62-1.66 m bus-park precast pillars (stepped plinth, chamfered shaft, cast joint, overhanging cap, broken corner with rebar on one variant), and per-arena families of 1.42-1.78 m (the roadworks column's rebar cage reaches 1.95; characters are 1.86). Breakables are 0.75-1.08 m (were ~0.6) with heavier posts, steel corner brackets and straps on crates, five-tyre stacks, fuller sack heaps, three-high drink crates, taller barricades with a water-filled block. Footprints stay inside the tile (0.84-0.92). | `tools/assets/env_pillars.py`, `env_pillars_city.py`, `env_soft.py`, `env_soft_city.py` |
| E3 | **Occlusion is handled by dissolving, not by shortening:** an obstacle on the tile directly south of (in front of) a live player, a generator or a pickup dithers away up to 72% of its pixels above a solid 0.3-0.7 m foot (ordered screen-door dither in the shader, per-instance value eased over ~0.12 s), so depth, shadows and instancing stay intact and the blocker still reads. Players keep their x-ray silhouettes on top. | `render/assets/occlusionFade.ts`, `views/InstanceSet.ts`, `views/ArenaView.ts` |
| E4 | **Obstacle style registry** keyed by arena id for all ten arenas (pillar models, breakable family + weights, drawn scale, extra tints, pillar debris); an arena whose own models are missing falls back to the bus-park set, then to the procedural stand-ins; unknown ids get the default. The per-arena breakable families of C6 moved here (same families for the three existing arenas, plus cable drums at the roadworks). Pillar dressing that used to be procedural (market sheet collars, roadworks rebar) is now modelled into the market pier and roadworks column. | `render/assets/obstacleStyles.ts`, `assets/props.ts`, `tests/render/obstacleStyles.test.ts` |
| E5 | New GLBs: pillars `market` (lime-washed sandcrete pier, rusted sheet collar, stall bracket), `column` (unfinished cast column, rebar cage, half-stripped plywood formwork, tape), `pier` (Oshodi flyover pier with capital and drain pipe), `wallstub` (broken sandcrete compound-wall stub), `bollard` (lashed timber piles + cast-iron bollard), `cabinet` (transformer cabinet with fins and HV cables), `kiosk` (shuttered kiosk with painted neon trim), `jersey` (two stacked jersey barriers); breakables `cabledrum`, `drums` (oil drums incl. a red-white checkpoint drum), `fishcrates`. Signs are fictional (no real utility or company names). | `src/assets/models/` |
| E6 | **Perimeter:** panels 1.05 -> **1.5 m** high and 0.30 -> **0.40 m** thick on a stepped footing with a projecting coping (north drawn 1.62 m, east/west 1.5 m stepping to 1.05 / 0.69 m over the last two rows), piers 1.24 -> **1.85 m** and 0.42 -> 0.6 m wide; the south (camera-side) run is a new low, 0.5 m deep **0.42 m barrier** (`env_wall_low`, was a squashed 0.57 m wall) so the nearest row stays visible. The unused cartoon-mural wall `env_wall_d` is deleted from the bundle and the generator. Side-platform spectators moved 0.09 m out with the thicker wall. | `tools/assets/env_walls.py`, `render/assets/backdrop.ts`, `assets/crowd.ts` |
| E7 | **Fire is fully instanced:** every burning tile's floor glow, lane beam, body flame, tongue and white-hot core, plus the centre glow and cross, are seven draw calls in total whatever is burning (a 15-tile row-and-column blast or a board on fire); capacity grows on demand. Arm tiles ignite outwards in sequence (a jet running down the lane), tips are smaller, flames are taller so they read over the new obstacles. Lights stay the fixed pool of V7 (only blast centres). | `render/views/FlameViews.ts`, `render/fx/Billboards.ts` |
| E8 | **Explosions and destruction:** a bigger fireball and rising flame column, long-arcing sparks, tumbling 3D casing fragments; every arm tile throws a lick of fire and an ember along its lane and leaves a soot streak; occasional ground cracks at blast centres (more likely for long ranges). Scorch, soot and cracks are three instanced decal pools (96 / 384 / 48, oldest recycled) that stay most of a match. A smashed breakable throws real lit, shadow-casting fragments of its material (planks, rubber, burst sacks, cardboard, plastic, sheet metal; one draw call per shape, physics with bounce and settle), a flame lick, a dust cloud and smoke. | `render/fx/Effects.ts`, `fx/Debris.ts`, `fx/Decals.ts`, `textures.ts` |
| E9 | **Broadcast-style match clock:** a dark slab hanging from the top edge with big condensed digits (about 7.4vh, min 2.05rem), a gold round tag and a time bar; red and pulsing in the final 30 s. Its height stays inside the HUD-free top inset (10% desktop, 13% phone); pause/mute moved slightly outwards. `data-testid="timer"` and the `m:ss` text are unchanged. | `ui/Hud.tsx`, `ui/styles.css` |
| E10 | Model folder 16.9 MB -> **18.0 MiB** (11 new models; rebuilt pillars/walls use lower-quality normal and roughness/metal JPEGs, new `Mat.normal_q` / `orm_q` knobs, so the rebuilt ones are smaller than before). Within the 19 MB ceiling set for this workstream. | `tools/assets/env_lib.py` |

### Nine arenas / City Rumble - integration decisions (arena art, hazard presentation, City Rumble)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| N16 | **City Rumble is 3:00, not 5:00** (supersedes the 5:00 of N9): the city closes from 2:00 left and only the core is left from 0:30. Bot batches at 8/16/24/32 competitors ended on their own after ~55-77 s on average (half the field gone by ~20 s), so a five-minute clock never reached the closing city. Medium/hard: 0 draws and 0 timeouts in 40+ matches; easy-only fields 1-2 draws in 10 (same-tick easy-bot blunders, never forced by the arena). | `sim/arenas/cityRumble.ts`, `scripts/simulate.ts` |
| N17 | City Rumble bots reason about a local window on boards above 400 tiles (danger rebuilt only for changes within 11 tiles, rival checks on the 5 nearest within 12, path search capped at 18 steps with cheap whole-board guidance fields) and have deterministic work budgets (easy 8 / medium 14 / hard 26 units per re-plan, at most 4 routine re-plans per tick; urgent re-plans never delayed). Standard arenas: identical decisions to before (fingerprint of 36 matches). 32 medium bots: 0.43 ms/tick average, 3.3 ms p99 in the container. | `ai/scale.ts`, `ai/zone.ts`, `scripts/ai-fingerprint.ts` |
| N18 | City Rumble presentation: follow camera keeps rivals within 4 tiles and reachable danger in view and never fits the whole city; spectating follows your eliminator (else the nearest survivor), cycle with Q/E, A/D, arrows or buttons; the minimap never shows pickups; phones get a compact survivor count + closing clock + 2-line kill feed, desktop adds nearby/leaders and a full roster; skip-to-results fast-forwards in 40 ms slices. Off-screen players skip animation. | `cameraDirector.ts`, `app/spectate.ts`, `ui/RumbleHud.tsx`, `ui/Minimap.tsx` |
| N19 | Stage hazard presentation: lethal hazards paint red floor tiles that fill from the entry end during the warning (glow alone washed out on bright floors); sweeps drive the real GLB vehicles (traffic: keke when `event.id % 3 === 1`, else a bus; checkpoint: taxi); HUD alerts name roads TOP/BOTTOM/LEFT/RIGHT/MIDDLE plus an arrow (rows are not labelled on the board), at most two alerts at once; the Power Yard rule banner shows during the countdown. | `render/views/StageHazardViews.ts`, `app/stageAlerts.ts`, `ui/StageAlerts.tsx` |
| N20 | Arena art: the six new surroundings are built from code at runtime (no new GLBs); none of them uses the static spectator stands (street life instead), Lagoon/Oshodi/Power Yard/Third Mainland have no static crowd at all; point lamps: Oshodi 0, Power Yard 0, Ajegunle 0, Third Mainland 2, Lagoon 2, Yaba 4 (none cast shadows; low quality skips them). Third Mainland removes the shared street plane so its lagoon can sit 9 m below the deck. Tests check that nothing stands on the board and the camera side stays under 1 m. | `render/arenas/*`, `tests/render/arena-sets-*.test.ts` |
| N21 | The HUD clock's ring uses the real match length (2:00 standard, 3:00 City Rumble). | `app/hud.ts` `totalSeconds` |

## Mobile joystick and music-first audio (branch `claude/wahala-mobile-joystick-audio`) - founder instruction of 2026-10-24

No simulation rule, cadence or balance number changed: the joystick feeds the existing touch direction into the
same MoveGate as the keyboard, D-pad and gamepad.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | **Floating thumbstick replaces the touch D-pad.** The stick appears where the thumb lands anywhere in the lower-left zone and reads the drag relative to that point; the knob follows the thumb (clamped to its radius) but the stick only ever reports one of the four grid directions or none. Radius 14% of the short side (44-72 px), dead zone 24% of it, release zone 16% (no edge flicker), hysteresis: another axis must be 1.35x stronger to take over (crossing the origin reverses at once), the origin follows the thumb beyond 1.35 radii so a reversal is always a short flick. One finger owns it; other fingers stay free for the buttons. A short tap that never leaves the dead zone still picks a vehicle entrance under the zone. | `input/joystick.ts`, `ui/TouchControls.tsx` |
| M2 | Every direction change is a press into `InputManager.setTouchDir`, so the MoveGate still turns it into exactly one legal tile, holding repeats at the player's own step cadence (speed upgrades included), collisions are the sim's, and the vehicle-entry selector still gets its `onNav` step. | `input/InputManager.ts` |
| M3 | Activation zone: the whole lower-left of the screen below the portraits, up to the left edge of the action-button column (`.stick-zone`, `touch-action: none`, no text selection or callout), so it can never take a touch meant for a button and never scrolls the page. Orientation change / resize drops the touch. The stick is see-through so tiles under it stay readable. | `ui/styles.css` |
| M4 | **Phone layout (landscape, height <= 480 px):** the action buttons move into one column at the right edge (28% of the screen height wide: GENERATOR at the bottom, the contextual buttons stacked above), and the camera's near-corner limit is derived from that same strip (`PHONE_CONTROL_STRIP = 0.28`, `compactNearX`), so the board grows into the space the old fan of buttons and fixed D-pad reserved: 844x390 board 472x211 -> 590x250 px, 568x320 318 -> 368 px wide, 932x430 520 -> 652 px wide; the far edge sits closer to the 13% top band. Still the fixed whole-arena camera at the approved 42-degree three-quarter angle; desktop and tablets unchanged. | `render/cameraRig.ts`, `ui/styles.css`, `tests/render/camera.test.ts` |
| M5 | The match clock on short phones is capped (digits 1.45-2.2 rem) so it stays inside the 13% top band. | `ui/styles.css` |
| M6 | How to Play shows the floating thumbstick (zone, ring and knob, "thumb anywhere here") in the touch legend and describes it in the move and vehicle-entry captions. | `ui/HowToPlay.tsx`, `ui/howToPlayControls.ts` |
| M7 | City Rumble keeps its follow camera and minimap; the new button column applies there too. | - |
| M8 | **Music-first recommended mix:** master 85, music 90, ambience and voices 45, effects 50 (was 80 / 45 / 65 / 85). The music bus goes back to unity (`MUSIC_BASE` 1.73 -> 1.0, supersedes N13's +2.5 dB lift) so the louder default is not applied twice: measured with ffmpeg, the music lands at about -16.8 LUFS with ~0.9 dB of limiting instead of 1.6-2.5 dB of pumping. Warnings follow `0.8 * sqrt(effects)`; the music duck deepens for warnings (0.7 -> 0.5) and big blasts (0.6 -> 0.45). The duck's "already deep enough" check now compares all three depths (it compared only the ambience, so a blast during a spoken-line duck left the music at the shallower depth). | `audio/buses.ts`, `audio/prefs.ts` |
| M9 | Saved mixes: new storage key `wahala.audio.v2`. A mix saved under `v1` that equals the old defaults becomes the recommended mix; any other saved mix is respected, its music slider converted (x 1.73, 5% steps) so it sounds as before. "Use recommended mix" (title screen and pause menu) restores the new defaults in one press for anyone, including a browser that saved an older mix. | `audio/prefs.ts`, `ui/AudioSettings.tsx` |


## Nigerian voice integration and clean gameplay HUD (branch `claude/wahala-voice-integration`) - founder instruction of 2026-10-24

No simulation rule, timing or balance number changed. Audio and HUD presentation only.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| V1 | **The founder's Gemini TTS clips ship** (first batch: 19 of the 47 planned voice lines, all validated: 24 kHz/16-bit mono PCM, 0.57-4.59 s, unclipped). The rejected `conductor_cms_01` (both versions) is never imported, listed or kept. The founder manifest is kept verbatim as the plan; the game reads a generated catalogue (`voices.json`) and a generated list of approved clips (`recorded.json`), replacing the old Kokoro-era catalogue. | `tools/audio/voices/`, `public/audio/voices/` |
| V2 | Clips are trimmed to 40 ms of silence, loudness-matched to -16 LUFS (peaks limited to -1.5 dBFS, boost capped at +8 dB) and encoded MP3 mono 24 kHz 64 kbps (0.33 MB for 19 clips vs 1.8 MB WAV): MP3 is the one format every target decodes with `decodeAudioData`, including older iOS Safari. Deterministic importer; masters committed, not deployed. | `tools/audio/voices/import_voices.py` |
| V3 | Voice triggers use the founder manifest's event names; all 17 gameplay triggers and 3 background schedulers are wired now, so a later clip needs no code change. A request may list fallbacks (a self-elimination tries the comedic line, then the groan, then the plain elimination line). Mapping thresholds: generator warning when the fuse is within 75 ticks and the human is in its cross; a blast speaks 30 % of the time (55 % when a chain or within 3 tiles of the human); bot eliminations 85 %; Keke warnings only for a bot's Keke. | `audio/pidginReactions.ts` |
| V4 | Director: one foreground line at a time; a forced, more urgent line (lower manifest priority) interrupts with a 60 ms fade; trigger rests for the played line's manifest cooldown; 4 s between unforced starts; background talk only in its zone, at most one at a time, each line resting >= 40 s. Forced: the human's danger warning, own elimination, Okada save, victory, final 30 s. | `audio/pidgin.ts` |
| V5 | **Voice bus**: foreground lines bypass the warning-band notch and follow the "Ambience & voices" slider on `1.3 x sqrt(v)` (0.87 at 45 %); music ducks to 0.5 under a line and 0.4 under an urgent one, crowd beds to 0.4 / 0.3, released smoothly when it ends. Background conversations stay on the quieter notched ambience path (about 8 dB below), never duck the music. Defaults, sliders and the music-first mix (M8) unchanged. | `audio/buses.ts` |
| V6 | Clips load per scene after the first gesture: none on the title screen; a match fetches only the lines its arena can play (two at a time, most urgent first) without delaying the start; failures are remembered and never re-requested. Zones are data: `bus_park` = Danfo Bus Park, Oshodi, City Rumble; `market` = Balogun Market, Oshodi, Yaba After Dark, City Rumble. | `audio/pidgin.ts`, `voices.json` |
| V7 | **Crowd speech bubbles removed from gameplay** (the caption generator and its line bank are deleted). They sat at the bottom centre (over the bottom row and the lower-left spawn on phones) and the toast column sat over the far rows. The crowd now speaks through the clips; a moment without a clip is silent. | `app/MatchController.ts`, `ui/Hud.tsx` |
| V8 | Essential text kept and moved into the **HUD rail** beside the board (under the left portraits): stage-hazard alerts, status notices (vehicle incoming, floor traps, Okada saved you, manhole, generator soaked, City Rumble counts), the Power Yard rule (countdown) and subtitles. Its width comes from the board's on-screen corners (`renderer.boardScreenCorners()`, re-read every 250 ms) so it stops 10 px short of the board edge at its lowest point; no scale animations inside it. City Rumble (follow camera) keeps the column top-centre. Clock, portraits, inventory chips, countdown, spectate bar, entry panel unchanged. | `ui/HudRail.tsx`, `ui/hudRail.ts`, `render/cameraRig.ts` |
| V9 | **Subtitles setting: Off (default) / On / Large**, title screen and pause menu, saved with the game settings. Shows the text of the line playing now, for its length (min 1.6 s), one at a time, never covering a gameplay line with background talk. | `app/subtitles.ts`, `ui/AudioSettings.tsx` |
| V10 | The GitHub Pages preview is now published (by the unchanged CI-gated `deploy.yml`) from `claude/wahala-voice-integration`. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` |

## W - V2 gameplay revolution (branch `claude/wahala-gameplay-rumble-v2`) - founder requirements of 2026-10-25

### WE - Training Academy (stream E)

No simulation rule, timing or balance number changed. The Academy runs the real simulation and only watches it.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WE1 | **How to Play is replaced by the Training Academy.** Every lesson: (1) objective shown first and kept on screen; (2) a brief scripted demonstration in the real engine (same `Intent`s a player sends); (3) the human's attempt with the real controls, normal HUD and TouchControls; (4) each task is ticked only by real state/events (e.g. `generatorPlaced`, `contactUsed`, `specialUsed`, `protectionBroken`, `rewardGranted`, `playerRespawned`, a blast the player survived); (5) gentle tips for common mistakes; (6) unlimited retries; (7) forward only when completed or explicitly skipped. The practice starts by itself 2 s after the demo (or at once with "Skip demo"). | `app/TutorialController.ts`, `tutorial/practice.ts` |
| WE2 | **20 lessons in 5 courses**, Beginner first and short: Beginner (move & turn with the floating stick, A places, escaping, plus-shaped reach, shelter behind pillars/crates); Power-ups (Extra Socket chains, Petrol, Square Blast, Okada & Armour); Generator tricks (kick, punch, disarm, Generator Pass, Remote Detonator = hold B); Button B (melee hit, one-press signature vehicle); Match rules (mandatory Wahala Crush + 0:30 floor traps, Survival rewards, Deathmatch respawns, Team Rumble friend vs enemy). **Carry is not taught** (out of normal play). The old watch-only chapters (stage hazards, Power Yard rule, City Rumble minimap) are dropped because every lesson must now have a real practice; the arena rule and hazards keep their in-match rail notices. | `tutorial/index.ts`, `tutorial/lessons/*` |
| WE3 | **Failure and retry:** being caught (any cause) or a task failure (e.g. a remote generator set off before reaching the safe marker, the 20 s failsafe) shows a plain explanation for ~2 s, then the attempt restarts from the latest **checkpoint** (a structured clone of the real state at the start of the checkpoint task), never from a faked state. Deathmatch / Team lessons use the real respawn rules instead (`onCaught: 'respawn'`). | `tutorial/practice.ts` |
| WE4 | **Mistake tips** (shown in the column, each at most every 2.5 s): standing on a red tile of your own generator with < 1 s left; walking away from a marker (path distance grows twice: "Wrong way! The marker is down and to the right"); B tapped with nobody adjacent / while recharging; B next to the dummy in the vehicle lesson ("B is a hit"); a quick tap instead of a hold for the Remote; a generator that cannot reach the target (checked with `previewBlast`); dodging when asked to let the Okada / Armour take the hit; standing on a Crush warning tile. | `tutorial/practice.ts`, lessons |
| WE5 | **Scenario liberties** are plain state writes a match could reach: emptied crates, placed crates/pickups, start tiles, a starting contact ability (Boots in the Punch lesson, to show replacement), the Crush lesson clock starting at 0:47 (said in its caption). Dummies are passive; the one scripted rival (Disarm) acts only through real `Intent`s. Nothing is faked: placements, blasts, eliminations, vehicles and rewards are the simulation's own. | `tutorial/setup.ts`, `tutorial/lessons/*` |
| WE6 | **Floor markers** ("go here") are presentation only: the scenario director exposes `markers(state)`, `MatchController` passes them as `FrameInfo.markers`, and `OverlayViews` draws a green tile + breathing ring with its existing overlay batches. | `app/MatchController.ts`, `render/types.ts`, `render/views/OverlayViews.ts` |
| WE7 | **Layout:** the lesson column sits left of the game; canvas, HUD and touch controls move right of it, so no lesson text (objective, instruction, tips, praise, demo caption) is ever over a tile, the bottom row or the lower-left spawn. The old floating demo subtitle and practice notice over the board are gone. Phones (<= 520 px tall) get a 170-220 px column; facts and the controls legend fold into "Tips & controls". The store's `board` corners are now published in page coordinates (canvas offset added), which also places the HUD rail correctly beside the shifted canvas. | `ui/HowToPlay.tsx/.css`, `app/session.ts` |
| WE8 | **Device wording:** instructions use tokens `{A}`, `{B}`, `{holdB}`, `{move}` filled from the legend, which reads the real binding tables by action name (`place`, `special`, `detonate`); touch wording names only "the A button" / "the B button" and the stick's whole lower-left activation area (highlighted on the real layout during movement tasks). | `tutorial/hints.ts`, `ui/howToPlayControls.ts` |
| WE9 | **Narration:** only approved clips, through the existing voice engine (`previewVoice`), only with `settings.narration` on: the Escape lesson cues "run" (`ON_GENERATOR_CRITICAL_PROXIMITY`) and "narrow escape" (`ON_PLAYER_NARROW_ESCAPE`). Everything else is written; the normal in-match reactions and optional subtitles still play from real events. | `app/session.ts`, `tutorial/types.ts` |
| WE10 | **Progress on this device only**: `localStorage` key `wahala.academy.v1` = `{completed, skipped}`. The Academy opens at the first lesson neither completed nor skipped; any lesson can be watched or practised straight from the lesson list; "Forget my progress" clears it. Settings are never written. | `tutorial/progress.ts` |



### Workstream C: elimination economy, credit, Square Blast, protection

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WC1 | **City Rumble power comes from eliminations, paid at once.** In all three Rumble rulesets crates hide nothing and the eliminated drop nothing; the living credited eliminator receives the rung's items in the elimination tick (no floor pickup a camper could steal). Ladder by credited eliminations this life (`stats.streak`): 1 socket + petrol; 2 the character's contact tool + skates; 3 armour + socket; 4 Square 3 x 3 + petrol; 5 remote + voucher + socket; 6 Okada + armour + petrol; 7 Square 5 x 5 + socket + skates; 8 armour + voucher + petrol + socket; 9 Square 7 x 7 + Okada + contact; then rungs 6-9 cycle. Rising access: raw generator power first, a tool, protection, Square Blast, control, then several items per elimination. Deterministic, no RNG. | `sim/config.ts rewards`, `sim/systems/rewards.ts` |
| WC2 | **Aggression pays, hiders stay weak.** Rumble rulesets raise the standard caps (`RUMBLE_ECONOMY`): capacity 6 -> 10, reach 8 -> 10, Armour 1 -> 3 plates, Square tier 2 -> 3 (7 x 7). These remaining limits are technical (bounded explosion / danger-map work with 32 fighters, the plus still stops at concrete); speed keeps its ceiling (grid step timing). No equalising: survival keeps every reward to the end. Measured with `scripts/rumble-economy.ts` (power score = upgrades above the start, Square / Armour / Okada counting 2 each), survival on City Rumble, 32 bots: standard 10/10 matches reach a result (9 wins, 1 simultaneous draw) in 34-107 s, at the 4-left showdown the top eliminator scores 14.5 on average vs 1.4 for the finalist with the fewest eliminations; expert 6/6 wins in 51-91 s, 23.0 vs 1.0 (top fighters reach the 10-socket / 7 x 7 / 3-plate caps). | `sim/config.ts`, `sim/modes.ts` |
| WC3 | **No wasted rung, contact tools never replaced.** A rung item that would do nothing becomes the first useful `rewards.fallback` item. `contact` = the character's signature tool (Street Baller kick, Lekki Boy punch, Campus Queen disarm) for a fighter with none; a Disarm holder gets charges. Generator Pass stays a Classic crate find: contact abilities are exclusive and Pass would replace a stronger tool. Carry and curses are never rewards. The HUD previews the next rung (`HudMe.nextRewards`) in the compact `ProgressionBadge` (kills, next reward, Okada / Armour pips, square tier), pinned under the clock in City Rumble. | `sim/systems/rewards.ts`, `ui/ProgressionBadge.tsx` |
| WC4 | **Elimination credit**: an opponent's flame -> its owner; the victim's own / a teammate's flame -> who set the chain off (kicker / puncher / vehicle owner that rammed the first generator, `Generator.touchedBy`, else the first generator's owner); vehicle -> owner; spectator throws -> never; otherwise the newest opponent melee attacker within 3 s (was 4 s: long enough for a shove into fire / traffic / the closing city, short enough that an old scuffle never scores an accident), else nobody. Same tick, several causes: the first credited cause in hit order scores, other credited opponents and melee attackers assist. A killer who falls in the same tick scores but is not paid and its streak does not grow. Teammates never score off each other. One death = one credit. | `sim/systems/attribution.ts`, `lethality.ts`, `contact.ts`, `vehicles.ts` (one line each) |
| WC5 | **Protection layers**: one layer (Okada first, then Armour) absorbs every lethal event of a tick, those events stay harmless for their grace window; separate hits take separate layers. Wahala Crush now goes through the same rule: a protected player loses one layer and is shoved to the nearest free tile (preferring one not burning or about to close); with no free tile left the landing is `terminal` and kills through every layer (crush no longer consumes the Okada itself). Visuals: steel-blue Armour shell + one plate pip per layer beside the name, shards + flash when a plate breaks. | `sim/systems/lethality.ts`, `crush.ts`, `render/views/okadaShield.ts`, `PlayerViews.ts` (hook) |
| WC6 | **Bots chase rewards**: with rewards on, the hunt pull and the value of catching a rival rise with the value of the next rung (and with the bot's own protection layers), capped at 2.5x. Rivals are discounted by their layers (a rival with L layers counts 1 / (L + 1) of a kill: an Okada rider stays at half, as before); the Remote logic treats protected rivals as "maybe" only. Anti-camping only through these incentives and the existing endgame pressure (closing city, endgame traps); no invisible damage for standing still. Classic bot behaviour is unchanged (factor 1, same discounts). | `ai/goals.ts`, `ai/analysis.ts`, `ai/remote.ts` |
| WC7 | **Square Blast**: union of the plus arms and the (2r+1) x (2r+1) square, each tile once; also on Power Yard full lines. Monotone-wave propagation (kept from Phase 0, now documented and tested): never through concrete or the edge, crates are hit but shield what is straight behind them, generators in the square chain but stop the wave; Hot Fuel pierces only the plus arms. The square also covers its centre row / column beyond a short plus (a range-1 tier-2 blast is a full 5 x 5). AI danger maps, placement valuation and rival what-ifs use the simulation's exported `squareFootprint`. Presentation: an electric blue-white footprint frame from placement, blue-white square flame tiles and a square detonation ring. | `sim/systems/explosions.ts`, `ai/danger.ts`, `ai/analysis.ts`, `render/views/{GeneratorViews,FlameViews,squareBlastLook}.ts`, `render/fx/Effects.ts`, `docs/GAMEPLAY_V2.md` |
| WC8 | **Armour and Square Blast stay out of Classic crates for now** (weights 0). The AI handles both, but any weight change reshuffles every seeded Classic match and the AI ordering baselines that workstream B is re-tuning for the seven levels; revisit once those are settled. | `sim/config.ts pickups.weights` |



### Stream D: City Rumble modes, teams, respawning, scoring, main menu (`v2/d-modes`)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WD1 | **City Rumble is the main entry.** The menu opens on City Rumble (`settings.play = 'rumble'`), with Classic Battle one tap away on the same screen as the quick 2-4 player game. The Play button reads "Play Rumble" for Rumble. One human against AI bots, offline: the menu never mentions online play or matchmaking. | `ui/TitleScreen.tsx`, `ui/MatchSetup.tsx` |
| WD2 | **Rules are rebuilt from the menu for every start** (`matchRules`): friendly fire exists only in Team Rumble, the 5 / 10 minute clock only in Deathmatch and Team Rumble, Classic always asks for Wahala Crush. A Classic match after a Team Rumble has no teams, no respawns and the crush; rematch keeps the mode. | `app/names.ts` |
| WD3 | **Teams start apart.** Two teams split the competitors' spawn tiles down the middle of the map (west = Green, east = White; ties by row, then index), so 4 v 4 to 16 v 16 start on opposite halves and the human (player 0, south-west spawn) is Green. More than two teams (tests only) take angular sectors. Sizes differ by at most one. An explicit `PlayerSetup.team` wins. | `sim/modes.ts assignTeams` |
| WD4 | **Respawn spot.** Never a blocked or occupied tile, a burning tile, a tile inside any live generator's predicted blast (chains included, `previewBlast`), a telegraphed or running lethal stage hazard, the rest of a vehicle's route or a crowd-throw landing. Among the rest: distance to the nearest living opponent (capped at 20), -30 when an opponent is closer than `respawn.safeDistance` (6), +8 (`homeSideBonus`) on the own team's side, -12 for a spawn used in the last 8 s (`spawnCooldownTicks`: two fighters killed together never reappear on the same tile, nobody gets farmed on one spawn), -8 within 6 tiles of where the fighter fell (`deathAvoidRadius`), -3 per dangerous neighbour. If every arena spawn is unusable, any safe open floor tile with a free neighbour: a respawn is never lost. | `sim/systems/respawn.ts`, `config.respawn` |
| WD5 | **Spawn protection ends early when the protected fighter places a generator** (`respawn.protectionEndsOnAttack`): the 2.5 s are for getting away, not for attacking while untouchable. | `sim/systems/respawn.ts` |
| WD6 | **Ranking reviewed and kept.** Deathmatch: credited eliminations, then fewer deaths, then more assists, then the earlier last elimination; still level at the top = draw (`tie`). The live leaderboard shares a place between identical stats. Team Rumble: team eliminations, then fewer team deaths, else a draw; assists never decide a team match. A friendly-fire or accidental death is nobody's elimination but counts as a death. | `sim/systems/match.ts`, `app/hud.ts` |
| WD7 | **One source of truth for scores**: kill counts, the kill feed, the spectator target and the results summary all read the simulation's credit (`eliminatedBy`, `stats`), never a count of their own. An uncredited teammate kill shows as "friendly fire" in the feed. | `app/spectate.ts`, `app/hud.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts` |
| WD8 | **Being down is temporary in the respawn modes**: no "skip to results"; a "Back in N" banner names who got you and the camera watches them; the human's input is collected again the tick they are back; a "Spawn protection" tag shows while it lasts. The city-closing clock is hidden (respawn modes never close). The kill feed keeps a fall for its 7 s even after the fighter is back. Team Rumble: the score bar keeps Green left and White right; the "Nearby" list shows opponents only. | `ui/RumbleHud.tsx`, `app/hud.ts` |
| WD9 | **Bots respect teams.** `World.rivals` / `rivalAt` hold opponents only (melee, hunting, generator targets, vehicle routes, remote detonation all read them); teammates are `friends` / `friendAt`. With friendly fire on: a teammate in the planned blast or in a blast it would chain counts as -8 opponents; a placement is refused when a teammate within reach (range + 2) could no longer get clear (6-tick reaction); no remote detonation that would catch a teammate; no vehicle route through a teammate. Measured on 16-bot Team Rumbles with friendly fire (seeds 9-12, Standard, `previewBlast` at the moment of placing): with the direct-blast penalty alone 26 % of placements (307 / 1165) still reached a teammate, mostly through chains; counting chained blasts brought it to 9 % (94 / 1085). `tests/modes-bots.test.ts` keeps it under 15 %. The remaining friendly-fire deaths are mostly teammates walking into old blasts, the same failure as bots' own-generator deaths (streams B / C). | `ai/world.ts`, `ai/analysis.ts`, `ai/remote.ts`, `ai/vehicleAI.ts` |
| WD10 | The `Observation` carries the match settings (teams, friendly fire and the clock are public rules), so bots can follow them. | `sim/observe.ts` |
| WD11 | **Team markings**: every fighter keeps their own character and wears a jersey band round the chest and an armband on each upper arm in the team colour (Green `#1fd46a`, White `#f4f1e6`), attached to the rig bones so they follow every animation; the ground ring, occlusion silhouette, name tag, minimap dot and HUD use the team colour too (white dots get a dark rim on the map). | `render/views/teamMarkings.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `ui/Minimap.tsx`, `assets/palette.ts` |
| WD12 | **Spawn protection is visible**: a pulsing light-blue ring at the feet and an 8 Hz body blink while it lasts; a respawn plays a small blue shockwave and resets the death pose. | `render/views/teamMarkings.ts`, `render/assets/rigged.ts revive` |



### Stream A: controls, combat, input resolution, one-press vehicles, camera shake

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WA1 | **Exactly two action buttons on every device.** Touch: a large left movement area plus A = GENERATOR and B = SPECIAL, always in the same place and look (phones: both in the 28vh right strip, B above A; tablets/desktop-touch: B left of A). New abilities never add, move or hide a button; only B's label changes (skill name next to a rival, vehicle name + voucher icon with a voucher, a "HOLD: BOOM!" line and a filling ring while a remote generator is live). Keyboard: A = Space / J, B = K / E / F / L / Left Shift. Gamepad: A = A, B = B or X. The old carry / punch / disarm / detonate / vehicle keys and buttons are gone (V and C, Q and R, X no longer do anything in play). | `input/keyboard.ts`, `input/gamepad.ts`, `ui/TouchControls.tsx`, `ui/styles.css` |
| WA2 | **Corner-turn buffer** (stick, keys and pad alike): a perpendicular turn pressed while walking, still held, and blocked right now keeps the player walking the way they were going for at most ONE tile (`TURN_BUFFER_TILES`) and is taken the moment it is legal. Only when the player was walking (a move handed over within 18 ticks), never for a reversal, a released flick or a standing player; every hand-over is still exactly one step, so no skipped tile and no turn the player did not ask for. Legality comes from the sim (`moveWouldAct`: a step, or a contact / jump on the generator in the way; inverted-controls curse honoured). Stick dead zone, release zone and hysteresis unchanged (M1). | `input/moveGate.ts`, `sim/systems/contact.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts` |
| WA3 | **Contact abilities have no button** (verified, refined, tested): moving into a grounded generator uses the one equipped ability. Kick slides it tile by tile until anything blocks it; Punch flies it 3 tiles over obstacles (first free tile from there, airborne so the renderer arcs it, never detonates mid-air); Disarm switches it off with a charge (wasted on nothing: a disarmed generator costs no charge; LOCKED: it still explodes from flame / vehicles); Pass walks through with no fire protection. Last acquired replaces the previous one (a replaced Disarm loses its charges). The HUD shows one compact chip (KICK / PUNCH / DISARM xN / PASS), not a button. Kick swings the leg, Disarm crouches, Punch throws (animations from `contactUsed` / `generatorThrown`). Bots use the same contact moves (their old Kill Switch / carry button paths are gone). | `sim/systems/contact.ts`, `ui/Hud.tsx`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `ai/emergency.ts` |
| WA4 | **B tap vs hold.** With no live remote generator, B fires the special on the PRESS (no delay at all). With a live remote generator, B waits: released before 400 ms (24 ticks) it fires the special on release; held for 400 ms it detonates (once, at the threshold, while still held) and the release does nothing. A long press never also fires the tap. Several sources on B count as one press. The ring on the touch button fills over the same 400 ms. Remote rules (ownership, failsafe, Kill Switch, chains) untouched. | `input/specialHold.ts`, `input/InputManager.ts` |
| WA5 | **One-press vehicles, the same for bots.** The border selection / confirmation is gone from normal play: B with a voucher (no rival adjacent) launches at once on the route `chooseAutoVehicle` picks. Scoring, on the stretch the vehicle really drives (taxi / Keke soft-block crash and player-hit limits applied): an opponent on the road scores TRAPPED when reaction (0.35 s, `special.autoVehicleReactionTicks`) plus walking off the road takes longer than the warning plus driving time (walls, crates, generators, flames, daze, a step in progress all count), else PRESSURE scaled by the margin; opponents beside the road add a little; Okada / armour count 0.4; the owner's tile is never driven over, teammates are avoided; with nobody threatened the lane closest to an opponent wins. Ties by border order: deterministic. The existing 1.2 s warning (route drawn on the board) is the fair warning. Bots call the same pure function on their Observation and only decide WHEN to press B (their danger + rival model, as before); B is a melee press while an opponent is adjacent, so they wait for that to clear. `app/vehicleEntry.ts` stays only for scripted tutorial demos (`ScenarioUi`); the human input path never opens it. | `sim/systems/autoVehicle.ts`, `sim/config.ts`, `ai/vehicleAI.ts`, `ai/tactics.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts` |
| WA6 | **Camera shake bounded** (root cause: +0.2 per landed Crush tile and +0.35 per trap wave into a 53 Hz jitter, saturated for the whole City Rumble closing). Now trauma: at most 0.3 added per frame, capped at 0.6, decays 3/s (a jolt is gone in 0.2 s); the offset is trauma^2 x 0.05 tile at a few Hz; Crush landings add none, a trap wave 0.15. Setting `shake` (pause menu chip): auto = OFF on touch screens, subtle with a mouse; off; subtle. prefers-reduced-motion forces off. Shake is a translation applied last; picking (`screenToGrid`) and the HUD's board corners use the un-shaken camera, so touch targets never move. | `render/shake.ts`, `render/fx/Effects.ts`, `render/cameraRig.ts`, `render/GameRenderer.ts`, `app/session.ts`, `ui/ShakeToggle.tsx` |
| WA7 | B priority kept as Phase 0 defined it: adjacent opponent (faced tile first, never a teammate) -> melee, even on cooldown (then nothing: never a surprise vehicle); else voucher -> vehicle; else nothing. There is no other "ordinary active special" in the game yet, so step 3 of the founder order is empty. Melee hits are recorded for elimination credit (`recordHit`). Bots melee through the same B press. | `sim/systems/actions.ts`, `sim/systems/skills.ts`, `ai/tactics.ts` |
| WA8 | Carry is out of normal play everywhere: no button, no key, no legend row, bots never carry. The legacy `action` intent and its code stay for old tests only. | `input/*`, `ai/emergency.ts`, `ui/howToPlayControls.ts` |

### Workstream B: bot levels, Classic rules, Mobile Test Arena, sideline spectators

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WB1 | **Seven real bot profiles** replace the interim 7-onto-3 mapping. `tier` (easy/medium/hard) is kept only to select existing code paths (remote policy, loiter rule, verify budget, City Rumble limits); every level has its own numbers for reaction, re-plan cadence, hazard lookahead, chain reading, safety margin, placement verification, hunt / rival appetite, mistakes, trap analysis and specials. Rookie and Beginner are Easy-family, Casual/Standard/Skilled Medium-family, Expert/Nightmare Hard-family. Same Observation, same rules at every level. | `ai/profiles.ts` |
| WB2 | **Rookie is for a first match**, not a renamed Easy: 30-45-tick re-plans, 36-tick reactions, 40-tick hazard horizon, barely hunts (`huntWeight` 0.02, `rivalValue` 1), never traps, never launches a vehicle, never melees, passive on the sideline, hesitates (30 % of re-plans, up to 1.5 s, only while standing safely), and **mercy**: it never places a generator that would leave a nearby player without a way out, judged with full hazard awareness assuming that player reacts 0.5 s late (Remote Starter: the earliest it would detonate). Beginner = the pre-V2 Easy plus mercy (0.3 s) and vehicles 40 % of the time. Measured (tests/ai/rookie.test.ts, 16 seeds, a passive novice dummy that only flees blasts 0.5 s late and keeps off the closing ring): vs Rookie the novice survives 85.3 s on average, wins/draws 10/16 and is never eliminated by the bot; vs Standard 62.0 s, 0/16, eliminated by the bot 14/16. Rookie placements leaving the adjacent novice no escape: 0 (Standard: 8). | `ai/profiles.ts`, `ai/mercy.ts`, `ai/brain.ts` |
| WB3 | **Specials per level**: `vehicleUse` / `meleeUse` probabilities gate the tactics layer per opportunity (a declined launch is not reconsidered for 5 s, a melee for 0.75 s; both the legacy `vehicle`/`skill` intents and V2 `special` are gated, melee when an opponent is adjacent, else vehicle), plus the existing `useGeneratorTools`, `smartRemote`, `trap`, `vehicle` style. New `teamwork` (0..1: Rookie 0.1 ... Nightmare 1) for the team logic (workstream D) and `sideline` (throws from the sideline when out). | `ai/profiles.ts`, `ai/brain.ts` |
| WB4 | **Ladder under real match rules** (Danfo Bus Park, stage hazards, mandatory crush; fixed seeds, seats alternate; draws count half). Every-other-level: Casual vs Rookie 0.69 (60), Standard vs Beginner 0.89 (80), Skilled vs Casual 0.82 (60), Expert vs Standard 0.65 (100), Nightmare vs Skilled 0.66 (60); anchors: Nightmare vs Standard 0.72 (60), Standard vs Rookie 0.98 (60), Expert vs Beginner 0.94 (80). Adjacent pairs (100 duels, tuning runs): Beginner/Rookie 0.54, Casual/Beginner 0.58, Standard/Casual 0.76, Skilled/Standard 0.63, Expert/Skilled 0.64, **Nightmare/Expert ~0.50** - in bot-vs-bot play the top two are statistically level (no tuning of cadence, rival analysis, margins or appetite separated them, 0.48-0.52); Nightmare differs by re-planning every 2 ticks with 1-tick reactions and no deliberate mistakes, which shows against humans rather than against Expert. | `tests/ai/ordering-*.test.ts` |
| WB5 | **Per-opponent difficulty** without setup screens: `ui/DifficultyPicker.tsx` - Classic: "All bots" select plus one row per bot slot (tap the level to cycle, or pick from a small list) with a one-line description; City Rumble: one preset plus the "Mixed roster" toggle (spread by `names.ts botDifficulty`). Default Rookie; legacy easy/medium/hard saves migrate to beginner/standard/expert. | `ui/DifficultyPicker.tsx`, `ui/difficultyPicker.ts` |
| WB6 | **AI fix: sealing-proof escapes are judged as committed routes.** The check re-searched from the placement tile with hindsight, so a route up a one-tile corridor towards a rival passed whenever some sideways route existed, and the bot then ran into the dead end the rival closed. It now searches from where the bot is when the seal appears, with its own generator still in the way. Regression test; only the Hard family uses it. (Small edit in workstream C's `ai/analysis.ts`.) | `ai/analysis.ts`, `tests/ai/sealing.test.ts` |
| WB7 | **Standard** = the pre-V2 Medium with slower reactions (28 ticks) and re-plans (20-28) and a keener hunt (0.25): it presses but reacts late; a no-chain-reading variant was rejected because it blew itself up in ~45 % of duels. Expert/Nightmare are patient (hunt 0.15, rival value 7-8, margin 1, minimum placement gain 2), which beat the pre-V2 Hard tuning by ~0.1 share. | `ai/profiles.ts` |
| WB8 | **Wahala Crush is mandatory in every standard four-fighter match**; tests: the app always starts every standard and experimental arena with it, `stageHazards` off never removes the crush or the endgame waves, no standard arena config touches `crush`/`match`/`forcedMode`, and final-tick cases (two on the final tile -> draw at exactly 0:00; a blast on the final tick -> one simultaneous draw; the other player crushed earlier -> win at that tick and the winner is never crushed; three players -> the last two draw; a spectator changes nothing; never a timeout with players standing). `settings.mode 'classic'` stays for tutorials/tests. AI duel tests and `npm run simulate` now default to crush. | `tests/crush-mandatory.test.ts`, `tests/ai/helpers.ts`, `scripts/simulate.ts` |
| WB9 | Bot sideline policy: Rookie/Beginner passive; others walk the south row to the column of the living player nearest to them, always off by 1-2 tiles (never pre-aimed), and wait 6 s on top of the rule's cooldown. Same Intent, same rules. | `ai/sideline.ts` |
| WB10 | **Mobile Test Arena** (experimental, `ArenaDef.experimental`, `EXPERIMENTAL_ARENA_IDS`, kept out of `STANDARD_ARENA_IDS`): Danfo Bus Park reduced to 13 x 9 (primary) and 15 x 9, concrete on odd/odd tiles, 4-fold symmetry, corner spawns with the standard clearance, one Danfo bay sweep in the middle row, an oil stain, two mandatory endgame waves; presentation reuses Bus Park's look, set and floor. Crush closes it to one tile at 0:02 and the last at 0:00 (tested). **Measured on-screen tiles** (Playwright, phone landscape, fixed arena camera; near edge / far edge / average row height): 640x360 - 15x11 27.7 / 21.4 / 19.6 px, 15x9 27.7 / 22.5 / 20.9 px, 13x9 31.7 / 25.1 / 23.6 px; 844x390 - 15x11 39.4 / 28.4 / 26.8 px, 15x9 39.4 / 30.2 / 28.7 px, 13x9 45.0 / 33.4 / 32.2 px. 13 x 9 gives ~14 % wider and ~20 % taller tiles; 15 x 9 is width-bound (same width, ~7 % taller rows). The nine official layouts are unchanged. | `sim/arenas/mobileTest.ts`, `render/arenas/mobileTest.ts`, `e2e/mobile-arena.spec.ts` |
| WB11 | **Sideline spectators** (classic ruleset only; off in every City Rumble mode): the eliminated walk a lane outside the board (west column, south row, east column; corners connect, cannot throw) one tile per 8 ticks; A throws straight in, landing 3 tiles in or the nearest valid tile on that line (open floor, no generator, pickup or player at throw time, not a crush-warned tile, never the final safe tile or its neighbours once closing has started); 1.2 s telegraphed flight, then an ordinary timed generator of range 2 (fuse starts on landing); one live throw per spectator, 8 s cooldown (also after elimination), none in the last 6 s. Never credited, never rejoin, cannot win; results count living players only. | `sim/systems/spectators.ts`, `sim/config.ts`, `sim/modes.ts` |
| WB12 | Sideline UI: `ui/SpectatorControls.tsx` (touch: the same floating stick plus one THROW button, dimmed while recharging; desktop/gamepad: a one-line hint; a SIDELINE bar with the recharge meter and Skip to results - under the clock on phones); overlay markers for spectators on the lane, the local aim tile and each throw's landing. `MatchController` keeps sending the eliminated local player's input (not in tutorials) and lets eliminated bots decide while they are spectators.
