Browser-checked at 844x390. | `ui/SpectatorControls.tsx`, `render/views/OverlayViews.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts`, `app/hud.ts` |

### WF - City Rumble stages (stream F)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WF1 | **Five new City Rumble stages**, listed first in `RUMBLE_ARENA_IDS` (the original `cityRumble` district stays last; `STANDARD_ARENA_IDS` unchanged): `rumbleOshodi` (Oshodi Rush Hour), `rumbleLagosIsland` (Lagos Island Markets), `rumbleOjuelegba` (Ojuelegba Underbridge), `rumbleApapa` (Apapa Docks), `rumbleLekkiNight` (Lekki Nightworks). All are 41 x 31 except Apapa (45 x 31: a container yard is long, the extra columns give its straight aisles their length). | `sim/arenas/rumble*.ts`, `sim/arenas/index.ts` |
| WF2 | Every V2 stage is authored as its top-left quadrant and **mirrored four ways** (`rumbleKit.mirrorQuadrant`): every side of the city faces the same streets, which is what makes 32-player spawns and side-vs-side teams fair. Hazard zones and traps are mirrored the same way. | `sim/arenas/rumbleKit.ts` |
| WF3 | **Spawns in mirrored pairs**, farthest-point order from the south-west: slot 2k is on the west half and slot 2k+1 its exact 180-degree mirror, so any even prefix is spread and balanced, and the default `id % 2` team split is west vs east tile for tile. A pair whose halves would be close (near the centre) is scored by that distance too. Measured minimum Manhattan gaps (8 / 16 / 32 spawns): 14-17 / 9-12 / 5-7 (the original district: 16 / 8 / 4). | `rumbleKit.pairedSpawns`, `tests/rumble-stages.test.ts` |
| WF4 | Stages with one-tile lanes (Lagos Island, Ojuelegba) and two-tile aisles (Apapa) use an **aisle spawn clearance** (three tiles along, then the turn into the cross alley) instead of the corner L, which does not fit between stall rows; the turn tile is outside the cross of a generator dropped on the spawn. | `rumbleKit.AISLE_CLEARANCE` |
| WF5 | Shared Survival rules for the V2 stages: 3:00 clock, the city closes ring by ring from 2:00 left around a core that never closes (11 x 9, Oshodi and Apapa 13 x 9 to keep the flyover band / loading apron whole), the last ring tile by 0:30, warn 1.5 s, the mandatory 0:30 / 0:15 trap waves inside the core. Respawn rulesets (Deathmatch, Team) never close the city (unchanged `modes.ts`). | `rumbleKit.rumbleStageConfig` |
| WF6 | **Up to two lethal stage events at once** on the V2 stages (`stage.maxLethalEvents: 2`, an arena config override; standard arenas and `cityRumble` keep 1). The boards are about eight times a standard arena: with one at a time most of the city went long stretches with no hazard. Every lethal event is still telegraphed >= `minLethalWarnTicks`, still quiet once the city starts closing and in the last 5 s. | arena `config.stage` |
| WF7 | **Three new hazard styles** (presentation and credit only; the mechanics are the existing `strike` / `sweep`): `truck` (Apapa: an articulated container truck races a port road; a new procedural model driven like the other sweep vehicles, alert TRUCK, deep horn, never voiced as a Danfo), `cargo` (Apapa: a container lowered by a quay crane onto a marked 2 x 2 aisle zone, 2.2 s warning, one container model over the zone, heavy shake) and `goods` (Lagos Island: bales and cartons tumbling off a shopfront onto two marked aisle tiles, 1.8 s warning, the arena's own soft-block models fall). HUD alert texts and synthesised warning / impact sounds added. | `sim/types.ts` (appended), `render/views/StageHazardViews.ts`, `render/views/hazardTruck.ts`, `assets/stagePresentation.ts`, `app/stageAlerts.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts`, `ui/StageAlerts.tsx` |
| WF8 | Stage hazard choice per stage: Oshodi = traffic on 10 long routes (carriageways, cross roads, BRT, both directions, 4 ticks/tile) + Danfos in and out of 4 corner bays; Lagos Island = `goods` strikes + `merch` drops (4 crates, route obstructions) + slow traffic on the access roads; Ojuelegba = traffic on the two three-lane roads + taxis (`checkpoint` style) down the queue lanes under the bridge + `merch` drops (vendors' obstacles); Apapa = `cargo` strikes (28 zones) + `truck` sweeps on the haul and quay roads; Lekki = targeted crane debris + `debris` strikes on 16 machine zones. Every sweep route starts at the board edge. | `sim/arenas/rumble*.ts` |
| WF9 | Every Rumble stage uses the stadium follow camera: `STADIUM_ARENAS` is now derived from `RUMBLE_ARENA_IDS` instead of naming `cityRumble`. | `render/cameraModes.ts` |
| WF10 | **Obstacle families by region** (`ObstacleStyle.pillarZones`, presentation only): flyover piers under the Ojuelegba bridge and the Oshodi flyover band, building corners elsewhere; poster-covered precast posts in the Lagos Island plazas and shopfront piers on its access roads; scaffold bays in the Lekki maze corners; precast transit-shed panels in the Apapa corners. Zoned styles pick models with a mixing hash (the legacy per-tile hash is always even, so a two-model family drew only its first model); zone-less styles keep the legacy hash, so the approved standard arenas do not reshuffle. Three new generated pillar models: `prop_pillar_container_a/_b` (two stacked short boxes, fictional markings) and `prop_pillar_scaffold` (tube-and-coupler bay, plywood hoarding, debris netting). | `render/assets/obstacleStyles.ts`, `render/views/ArenaView.ts`, `tools/assets/env_pillars_v2.py` |
| WF11 | Each stage has its own look (light, fog, lamps), painted floor and surroundings module (`render/arenas/rumble*Art.ts`), built from the existing kits (Oshodi flyover / signs / umbrellas, market stalls, roadworks machines and structures, Third Mainland trucks and booth, StreetLife, Ambient). Lekki Nightworks keeps the approved Lekki Roadworks night values (sky, fog colour, sun, hemi, exposure, grit). Nothing stands on the board and the camera side stays under 1 m (tested on every triangle). No fights or area-boy scenes in the V2 stages; shop, ship and sign names are fictional. | `render/arenas/`, `tests/render/rumble-stage-sets.test.ts` |
| WF12 | **Music**: one founder track per stage as playlist, start and signature: Oshodi `pixabay_601035`, Lagos Island `pixabay_554543`, Ojuelegba `pixabay_515507`, Apapa `pixabay_455231`, Lekki Nightworks `pixabay_623364`. | `public/audio/music/manifest.json`, `tests/audio/assets.test.ts` |
| WF13 | `RumbleStagePicker` (cards: preview or mood gradient, lighting, board size, tagline, hazards; swipe strip, one card per screen width on phones) writes `settings.rumbleArenaId`; stream D places it in the menu. | `ui/RumbleStagePicker.tsx` |

### W-lead - integration decisions

- **WL1 - Rumble spawns never sit in a stage hazard.** Integration review of the five stages found Lagos Island
  spawns 22 / 23 and Apapa 18 / 19 inside strike zones (telegraphed, so not instantly lethal, but a fighter could
  start or respawn into a falling container). `pairedSpawns` now takes the stage's strike-zone tiles to avoid (the
  spawn tile and its way out); test "no spawn stands on a stage hazard" per stage.
- **WL2 - Rookie checker precision.** After integration the Rookie test flagged one placement (seed 19) that the bot's
  own mercy rule had correctly judged escapable: the test treated a flame line that died 7 ticks later as a wall. Only
  fire still burning when the novice starts to move blocks now.
- **WL3 - Preview publishing moves to the V2 branch** once the full CI check passes on it (`ci.yml` job
  `deploy-preview`); the voice-integration branch is left untouched.


## UI - Stitch menu and navigation (branch `claude/wahala-stitch-uiux-v1`) - founder brief of 2026-10-25

Presentation only: no simulation, AI, arena, camera or collision rule changed. Report: `docs/STITCH_UI.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| UI1 | The one-page title screen is replaced by the approved Google Stitch direction as a **multi-screen menu**: Home -> Game mode -> Match setup -> Fighter -> Ready -> play, with Arena as a detour from Setup / Ready, plus Settings and the Training Academy menu. Navigation is a small pure state machine (`stack` of screens) in the UI store, not a router: Back pops, Continue walks the flow, and an **Edit from Ready** opens that one screen on top of Ready so Continue / Back return straight to Ready. | `app/menu.ts`, `ui/menu/MainMenu.tsx` |
| UI2 | Every menu control writes the real `GameSettings` (and the separate persistent audio preferences) through the existing `commands.updateSettings` / `audioPrefs`; there is no parallel setup state. `sanitizeSettings` (also run by `loadSettings` and before every start) repairs any value the menu could not have produced (removed arena, unknown fighter, odd sizes), so a stale save can never block a match. | `app/settings.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| UI3 | **Quick play** on Home starts the saved setup in one press (returning players); **Review** opens Ready. Results: Rematch (same setup), **Change setup -> Ready** (every choice editable there), Main menu -> Home. Pause: Resume, Settings, Restart, Main menu. | `ui/menu/HomeScreen.tsx`, `ui/Overlays.tsx` |
| UI4 | **Keyboard and gamepad drive the menus** like a console: the D-pad / arrows move focus to the nearest control in that direction, A / Enter / Space press it, B / Esc go back. Menu input is offered to the showing menu before the match (`app/menuNav.ts`); in the pause menu's Settings, Esc / P / Start close Settings and the match **stays paused** (never resumes behind a menu). The gamepad is now also polled on the title screen. | `ui/menu/focusNav.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| UI5 | Settings keep the engine's two stores apart: **Audio** (persistent prefs: four volumes, voices, recommended mix; plus the game settings mute and subtitles), **Graphics** (quality, Rumble camera follow / whole stage), **Controls** (touch controls auto / on / off; the real keyboard / gamepad / touch legend from `howToPlayControls`), **Accessibility** (subtitles off / on / large, camera shake, Academy narration; reduced motion is honoured automatically). Changes apply and save instantly. **Restore defaults** (two presses) resets the audio prefs and only the device preferences (`PREFERENCE_KEYS`), never the match setup. No toggle without an implementation. | `ui/menu/SettingsScreen.tsx` |
| UI6 | **Menu art**: the Stitch key art (Home and menu backdrop) and the three full-body fighter portraits, served locally as WebP in two widths (`srcset`), built by `tools/ui/build_menu_art.py` from masters in `tools/ui/masters/`. The script crops the mock fighting-game HUD baked into the Campus Queen image and blends out real-world brand marks (telecom, bank and beer signs in the key art; sportswear and league logos on the Street Baller kit). The portraits are labelled concept art; fighter text comes only from the character / vehicle registries and the skill config (no ratings, ranks, locked slots or powers). | `src/assets/ui/`, `ui/menu/art.ts`, `ui/menu/fighters.ts` |
| UI7 | The menu covers the screen with its own backdrop, so the **live attract demo no longer runs behind it** (it only cost battery behind an opaque menu); it stays available with `?attract`. Menus avoid full-screen `backdrop-filter` and blur filters (costly on phones and on software WebGL); the key art pushes in once when Home opens (no endless animation) and not at all with reduced motion. | `app/session.ts`, `ui/menu/menu.css` |
| UI8 | Fonts: Bebas Neue (display) and Space Grotesk (text) bundled with `@fontsource` (no CDN at runtime). | `package.json`, `ui/menu/menu.css` |
| UI9 | Phones: menus work in portrait and landscape (separate compositions for <= 700 px wide and <= 520 px tall: horizontal mode strip, compact two-column setup, roster grid, sticky full-width main action in portrait); every tap target >= 44 px; safe-area insets on every edge; `overflow: clip` on the menu root so focus scrolling can never shift it sideways. Combat still asks for landscape, but the **pause menu now shows above the rotate notice**, which also gained a Pause button, so a portrait player can always reach Settings or quit. | `ui/menu/menu.css`, `ui/App.tsx` |
| UI10 | The Academy's in-match lesson column keeps its layout (beside the board, never over it) and takes the menu's colours and type; the Academy menu lists the real lessons (Beginner = the beginner course, Advanced = the other four) with this device's progress, Continue / replay any lesson, Reset progress (two presses). Leaving a lesson returns to the Academy menu. | `ui/menu/AcademyScreen.tsx`, `ui/HowToPlay.css` |
| UI11 | Preview publishing moves to `claude/wahala-stitch-uiux-v1` (the founder's new test branch): `ci.yml` deploys only from that branch, only after the full check passes on the pushed commit. | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` |


## RE - City Rumble Evolution V1 (branch `claude/wahala-rumble-evolution-v1`) - founder brief of 2026-10-26

Contracts and ownership: `docs/RUMBLE_EVOLUTION.md`. Classic Battle keeps the cross, one starting generator and
every existing rule.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RE-B1 | **City Rumble blasts are radial; Classic keeps the cross.** All three Rumble rulesets set `generator.blastMode = 'radial'` (`RUMBLE_ECONOMY`). The footprint is a grid disc `dx^2 + dy^2 <= R^2 + R` (R 1 = 3 x 3, R 2 = 21 tiles, R 3 = 37, R 4 = 69, R 5 = 97), so a fighter diagonally next to a generator is never safe. R = the generator's range (Petrol Top-Up), capped by `generator.maxRange`, which Rumble sets to 5 (the old plus reach 10 would make a 300-tile disc). | `sim/systems/blastShape.ts`, `config.ts` |
| RE-B2 | **Cover rule = wave AND line of sight.** A disc tile burns when the outward blast wave can walk there (the V2 Square Blast wave: never into concrete; a crate or another generator is hit but passes nothing on) and the straight centre-to-centre line crosses only open floor without generators (exact corner crossings touch neither side). The wave alone leaked around a single pillar far behind it; the line alone could squeeze between two diagonal walls. Together, permanent walls and crates cast a readable shadow, corners never leak, and a diagonal neighbour is still hit in the open. | `blastShape.ts radialFootprint` |
| RE-B3 | **Destruction timing unchanged.** Every blast of a tick is resolved against the tile map at the start of the tick (two simultaneous blasts both see a crate, it breaks once, after both); a crate destroyed earlier is open floor for every later blast. Chains, fuse, remote detonation, kick / punch / disarm and vehicle exposure are untouched. Hot Fuel lets the wave and the line pass ONE crate (it still breaks). | `explosions.ts`, `tests/radial-blast.test.ts` |
| RE-B4 | **Square Blast successor in radial mode:** the Square Blast tier squares the disc off - the footprint becomes the full square of radius `min(R + tier - 1, radialMaxSquareRadius)` (4 by default, 6 in City Rumble since RE-C4) under the same cover rule (tier 1 at R 2: 21 -> 25 tiles; tier 2: 49). The union with the disc of the V2 rule would have added nothing at tier 1. The reward ladder and the AI keep treating it as a blast-size upgrade. | `blastShape.ts radialSquareRadius` |
| RE-B5 | **One geometry for everyone.** The simulation, the on-screen danger footprint (`previewBlast`) and the bots share `radialFootprint`; radial flame tiles off the row / column are marked `radial` and drawn like arm tiles (never as a second blast centre with its own light). | `explosions.ts`, `render/views/FlameViews.ts` |
| RE-C1 | **Two generators from the start in City Rumble** (`baseCapacity: 2` in `RUMBLE_ECONOMY`; respawns reset to it). Classic stays at one. | `config.ts` |
| RE-C3 | **Marked supply crates, never farmable.** An arena may place `$` crates: always a crate, each hiding one basic upgrade (`config.supply.kinds`, default Extra Socket / Petrol Top-Up, picked with the match RNG). They are the only crate rewards in Rumble (crates still drop nothing), are destroyed once and never refill. The marking is public (`Observation.supplies`), the content is not. `x` marks an authored barrier (always a crate: a shortcut to blast open), `_` floor that never gets a random crate. | `state.ts placeSupplies`, `observe.ts` |
| RE-L1 | **Attack lanes.** An arena may restrict summoned attack vehicles to named board-edge road mouths (`config.vehicles.lanes`); every vehicle consumer reads `allBorderEntries`, so the UI, the one-press special and the bots follow automatically. Default null = any border tile, as before. | `sim/systems/entries.ts` |
| RE-A1 | **Assisted movement lives only in the input layer; the grid rules are untouched.** The founder found corners, obstacles and generators hard to steer with the thumbstick. The assist only chooses WHICH of the four directions `MoveGate` hands over on a tick; every hand-over is still one orthogonal tile, resolved by the unchanged simulation (collisions, kicks, curses). Setting `GameSettings.moveAssist`: `auto` (default) / `on` / `off` (Settings > Controls). Resolved per device in use: `off` = the pre-assist gate exactly (WA2's one-tile corner buffer stays; differential test against a verbatim copy); `auto` = full assistance while the last-used device is touch, `basic` (WA2 + the danger check on the carried tile) with keyboard / gamepad; `on` = full assistance on every device. (The Phase 0 contract table called the setting `assistedMovement`; the field is `moveAssist`.) | `input/moveAssist.ts`, `input/moveGate.ts`, `input/InputManager.ts`, `app/settings.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| RE-A2 | **Early-turn planning (full assistance).** A perpendicular turn pressed while walking and still held, that is blocked here, is carried along the corridor for at most `TURN_REACH_TILES = 2` tiles - but only when it really opens within that reach (`planTurn`: every tile on the way open and safe, the turn tile open and safe); otherwise it is delivered at once and the player faces the wall (no wandering). Still one tile per hand-over, never a skipped tile, no corner cutting: moves are orthogonal single steps onto bare floor. A turn whose flick is released is one plain press (release stops at once: nothing is carried after the thumb lifts). A turn that waited is dropped, not delivered, if its tile became dangerous meanwhile. WA2's one-tile buffer is unchanged for `off` / `basic`. | `moveAssist.ts` (`planTurn`), `moveGate.ts` (`carryFor`) |
| RE-A3 | **Sidestep against a wall.** A direction that stays held for the repeat delay (`MOVE_REPEAT_DELAY_TICKS`, so a tap is still exactly one tile) and is blocked, with the opening one tile along the corridor (side tile and the tile beyond it both plain safe steps), slides to it and then goes on (`planSidestep`). Exactly one side must qualify; with two, the thumb's secondary axis, then the way the player last walked, decides, else nothing happens. Never when the thing in front is a crate (a player pressed against one is about to bomb it), never around a generator that is armed (its footprint is unsafe), never when a kick / punch / jump would act. | `moveAssist.ts` (`planSidestep`), `moveGate.ts` (`assistHold`) |
| RE-A4 | **Diagonal thumb = primary axis now, secondary axis as the next turn.** `VirtualStick.wish` is the weaker axis once it reaches 0.7 of the main one (about 35 degrees off the axis), kept until it drops below 0.5 (about 27 degrees), always perpendicular to `dir`, cleared with the direction: a wobble inside the band never flickers or zig-zags. The gate takes the wish only on a hold repeat (a flick is exactly one tile in the main direction), at the first tile where it is a plain safe step, then lets the primary direction move once before taking it again (an open field gives a clean right-down-right-down staircase; a corridor with a side street turns into it and follows it). Keyboard and gamepad have no diagonal, so no wish. | `joystick.ts` (`wishFor`), `moveGate.ts` |
| RE-A5 | **Direction-switch settling (touch, full assistance).** A thumb that goes straight from one direction to another waits `SWITCH_SETTLE_TICKS = 2` (33 ms) before the new press may be handed over, so the stray direction of a swipe through the stick centre is overwritten instead of costing a tile. A first touch from rest has no delay; keyboard and gamepad never settle. Dead zone, release zone and the 1.35 switch ratio (M1) are unchanged. | `InputManager.setTouchDir`, `moveGate.press` |
| RE-A6 | **Safety rule: assistance never auto-travels into danger.** Any move the assist substitutes or continues on the player's behalf (a carry, a sidestep, a wish turn, a delayed turn) must be a plain step onto open floor (`MoveBoard.open`: bare floor, no grounded generator - never a kick or a jump) and `MoveBoard.safe`: no flame, no generator tile, no blast footprint of an armed generator or its chain (`previewBlast`, so the City Rumble radial blast too), no tile of a warned or live lethal stage event or a falling-goods drop, no Wahala Crush warning, no live floor trap or endgame trap warning, no manhole, no route of an attack vehicle. Built from the `GameState` the match already has, read-only, lazily (only when a decision needs it); past 12 armed generators near the player the footprint check uses a cautious superset instead of following chain reactions tile by tile (a pathological dense chain cost 18 ms). The direction the player explicitly holds is never second-guessed by this rule: assistance adds help, it does not take control away. | `moveAssist.ts` (`hazardTiles`, `buildMoveBoard`) |
| RE-A7 | **Existing rules stay intact and measured.** A press is exactly one tile; only a deliberate hold repeats at the player's step cadence; release stops at once; keyboard and gamepad hand moves over on the same ticks as before with the setting at off, auto or on in open ground (test), and under Auto they gain only the danger check on WA2's carried tile (they do not become sticky: no two-tile carry, no sidestep, no wish). The inverted-controls curse is folded into the board (`vec`), so planning matches what the simulation will do. Tutorials and sideline spectators pass no board and so move exactly as before. | `tests/input-assist*.test.ts` |
| RE-A8 | **The toggle is a motor-accessibility control, so its chips keep the 44 px tap target** even in the compact landscape-phone menu layout (`.mn-opts-tall`); the text says only what the assist does and that it never walks the player into a generator, flames or a warned hazard. "Restore defaults" resets it with the other device preferences (`PREFERENCE_KEYS`). | `ui/menu/SettingsScreen.tsx`, `menu.css`, `app/settings.ts` |
| RE-L2 | **Rumble blast experiment switch.** The research reconciliation (late research pack, memo 01) asks that the radial blast be prototyped behind an explicit Rumble-only toggle and compared with the cross. `MatchSettings.rumbleBlast` / `GameSettings.rumbleBlast` = `'radial'` (default, the founder's Rumble blast) or `'cross'` (Rumble rules with the Classic plus and its old reach cap 10; the two-generator start and the rewards stay). Shown as "Blast shape" on the City Rumble Setup screen and in the Ready rules; Classic ignores it. | `sim/modes.ts`, `app/settings.ts`, `ui/menu/SetupScreen.tsx` |
| RE-L3 | **Research hierarchy.** The late research pack is design input, not rules: engine source > founder decisions > tested observations > reconciled hypotheses > report prescriptions. Rejected from it: generator HP / falloff / cooldowns / simultaneous-blast caps, hazard damage caps, two-level combat, navmesh or diagonal movement, falling containers or debris at Ojuelegba, NPC shoves or crowd-surge damage, UE5 systems. Digest: `docs/RUMBLE_RESEARCH_DIGEST.md`. | docs |
| RE-L4 | **Radial metric answers to the research checklist:** Euclidean tile-centre disc (`dx^2 + dy^2 <= R^2 + R`, i.e. within R + 1/2), not a Chebyshev square; line of sight is a thin centre-to-centre line where an exact corner crossing touches neither side, combined with the outward wave so a blast never squeezes between two blocked corners; destructible cover absorbs the pulse that destroys it; one playable layer (no vertical blast layers). Starting radius 2 and cap 5 stay provisional until the measured comparison (sections AI / C). | `blastShape.ts` |
| RE-C2 | **One measurement harness for Rumble balance.** `scripts/rumble-metrics.ts` (+ `scripts/rumbleMetrics/`) runs seeded bot matches per stage x ruleset x bot count x named config variant (`legacy` = plus blast, 1 starting generator, reach cap 10; `evolution` = the defaults; `nosupply`, `sq6` ...) and reports eliminations (total / per minute / time of the first / in the first 30 and 60 s), engagement (living opponent within 6 tiles, standing in an opponent's live `previewBlast` footprint; whole match and first 60 s), generator placements per fighter-minute and the share aimed at an opponent, camping (>= 5 s inside a 3 x 3 area, mean distance to the nearest opponent), self-eliminations, kill concentration (top eliminator vs median fighter: kills and peak power), result, length and ms per tick, and (late research brief) first-encounter time (a fighter inside a live opponent footprint or a credited hit), runs of 15 s or more with no opponent within 6 tiles, eliminations by cause (own / rival / team blast, vehicle, stage hazard, crush, melee), live generators over time, supply crates opened and pickups taken / wasted / burned, and per-spawn-slot survival, kills and wins (`--slots`). `--ladder` prints the bot-independent economy curve (what a fighter owns after n credited eliminations, with the blast area it burns on open ground). Deterministic per seed (only ms/tick is wall clock); the collector only reads the state. Bot-only numbers depend on the bots: measured before the bots understood the radial blast they mostly show bots killing themselves (evolution: first elimination at ~3 s, 5-18 self-eliminations per match), so economy variants cannot be told apart by them yet - the lead re-runs the tables after the AI merge. | `scripts/rumble-metrics.ts`, `tests/rumble-economy-evolution.test.ts` |
| RE-C4 | **Ladder under radial caps; Square Blast squares up to radius 6.** (1) A ladder reward that cannot do anything now is replaced by the fallback list; in radial mode that includes a Square Blast tier that would not widen the footprint (`rewards.ts rewardUseful`). (2) `RUMBLE_ECONOMY.generator.radialMaxSquareRadius` 4 -> 6 (RE-B4's square cap): with 4, once reach is 4-5 tier 1 made the 9 x 9 / 11 x 11 square and tiers 2-3 changed nothing (kills 7 and 9 paid a dead reward), so the elimination ladder flat-lined at 121 tiles from kill 6. With 6 the tier-2 reward (kill 7) takes a radius-5 blast from 121 to 169 tiles (13 x 13); tier 3 is never paid (it would not widen it). Measured with `--ladder` (Street Baller; same for all three fighters): burnable tiles per generator on open ground after kills 0 / 1 / 4 / 6 / 7 = 21 / 37 / 81 / 121 / 169, generators 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 6, so coverage (generators x tiles) goes 42 / 111 / 324 / 605 / 1014 and 1690 at the plateau; power score strictly rises through kill 9 (0 -> 29) and never falls (tested). Cost: no measurable change (ms/tick 0.5-0.8 for 32 bots, both variants; a ten-generator radius-6 square chain resolves in < 3 ms). The ladder runs dry after kill 9 (everything capped / owned); beyond that only spent protection (Armour plates, the Okada, voucher, Disarm charges) is paid again - checked by a test. Flame reach stays capped at 5 (Phase 0). Capacity cap stays 10; not raised (nothing in the data asks for it). | `sim/config.ts RUMBLE_ECONOMY`, `sim/systems/rewards.ts`, `tests/economy.test.ts` (tier expectation) |
| RE-C5 | **Eight marked supply crates on each of Oshodi, Lagos Island, Apapa and Lekki Nightworks.** Two `$` per quadrant (mirrored, so 8; none on Ojuelegba, owned by RE-D), in the contested ground between the spawns and the core, at least 5 tiles (Manhattan) from every spawn (not even the mirror images of the spawns come closer), never on a road, trap or strike zone; spawns stay exactly where they were (checked tile for tile). Each holds socket or petrol (match RNG), once, never refilled; 8 supplies = 0.5 per fighter at 16 bots, 0.25 at 32 - a reason to leave the spawn, not a way to skip fighting (one supply is worth about half of the first kill rung). Crates a closing ring reaches are lost with the rest of the ring (Survival). `mirrorQuadrant` accepts `_ x $` (mirrored like everything else). The supply is drawn on one plain wooden crate model (`ArenaView`) with yellow straps, a stencilled `$` plate on the camera side and the lid, and a pulsing painted floor frame (`SupplyMarkViews`); marks are flat on the crate (nothing hidden), dissolve with it for a fighter behind, and vanish with the crate. Checked in Chromium (software GL) on Oshodi desktop and phone size and on Lekki Nightworks. | `sim/arenas/rumble{Oshodi,LagosIsland,Apapa,LekkiNight}.ts`, `rumbleKit.ts`, `render/views/SupplyMarkViews.ts`, `ArenaView.ts` |
| RE-C6 | **Open findings for the lead (not changed on purpose).** (a) Deathmatch snowballs hard with the current bots: one fighter takes ~140 of ~450 eliminations per 5 minutes and sits at the 29-power plateau while the median fighter has 4 (legacy: 175 of 280). The founder accepts strong leaders, so nothing was flattened; re-measure with radial-aware bots before judging. (b) Survival is decided in 15-25 s with the radial-blind bots (legacy 45-70 s) because they kill themselves; the opening metrics will mean something only after the AI merge. (c) Spawn fairness flag: on Lekki Nightworks with 32 bots the corner slots 2 (2,0) and 3 (38,30) survive 0.58 / 0.76 of the match and take 5 of 12 wins with few kills (12 seeds, radial-blind bots, no null run) - re-measure with 50 seeds after the AI merge before changing that layout. (d) Each supply is a fixed 2-way pick (socket / petrol); `supply.kinds` could add `skates` without code. | `docs/RUMBLE_EVOLUTION.md` section C |
| RE-AI1 | **Bots read the radial blast from the simulation's own geometry.** When `generator.blastMode === 'radial'` every footprint a bot reasons about (danger-map nodes, placement value, chained rivals, remote detonation, mercy / friendly-fire / trap checks) is `radialFootprint` with the explosion rule's wave blocker (another triggerable generator is hit and chains, passes nothing on), through `ai/blast.ts`. The danger map keeps its chain relax and its refine pass (a crate an earlier blast breaks is open floor for a later one). A node's observation footprint is reused within one decision unless a what-if generator (placement, kick, sealing) or a crate cleared by an earlier blast lies inside its box. The plus / Power Yard code paths are untouched: Classic bot decisions are byte-for-byte identical (every intent of 36 standard-arena matches + one large-board Classic match hashes the same before / after). | `ai/blast.ts`, `ai/danger.ts`, `ai/analysis.ts` |
| RE-AI2 | **Radial tactics.** A rival on a diagonal counts as "right next to me" when choosing where to stand (a radial blast reaches it as easily as an orthogonal one); the cheap "can this blast concern that player" filters use the radial reach (diagonals, Square Blast corners) instead of the range; with one of their own generators already down, Standard / Skilled bots also check pure cut-off placements (the second generator closes a rival's way out), which Expert / Nightmare already did. Cover needs no special rule: refuges are the tiles the danger map leaves safe, so wall and crate shadows count as cover and the diagonals do not. | `ai/goals.ts` |
| RE-AI3 | **A plan the fresh danger map makes lethal is re-planned at once (radial only).** The dominant own-blast death in 32-bot radial Rumble was a bot's generator being chained early by a rival's later placement while the bot's re-plan waited for the large board's shared per-tick allowance. After every danger-map rebuild the followed plan (each wait / step window and its refuge) is re-checked; if it became lethal the re-plan is urgent and never deferred. | `ai/brain.ts` |
| RE-AI4 | **Marked supply crates are early targets.** A placement that breaks a marked supply crate (`obs.supplies`, the public marking - the bots never read `state.hidden`) is worth `SUPPLY_VALUE = 5` on top of the crate; the revealed upgrade is then an ordinary pickup goal. Arenas without `$` crates are unaffected. | `ai/goals.ts` |
| RE-AI5 | **Affordable radial footprints.** `radialFootprint` (lead-owned) was optimised internally - offsets compiled once per shape into typed arrays, a shared wave buffer instead of a per-call allocation - with identical output (`tests/ai/radial.test.ts` compares it with the Phase 0 implementation on 300 random boards, order included; `tests/radial-blast.test.ts` unchanged and green). With the footprint cache of RE-AI1 and closure-free blockers the radial-aware bots went from 2.68 to 1.46 ms per tick on the same 32-bot match (identical outcome); radial matches still cost about 1.1-1.8 x the cross per tick at 24-32 bots (p99 about 9-10 ms against 7-8 ms), measured in `docs/RUMBLE_EVOLUTION.md` section AI. | `sim/systems/blastShape.ts`, `ai/danger.ts`, `ai/analysis.ts` |
| RE-AI6 | **Measured, not assumed.** `npm run simulate` gained `--game survival|deathmatch|team`, `--minutes`, `--friendly-fire` and `--blast plus|radial` (the same Rumble ruleset with the cross, for comparison) and reports an engagement proxy: the share of alive samples with an opponent within 6 tiles and the mean distance to the nearest opponent, overall and with 6 or fewer fighters left (where camping would show). | `scripts/simulate.ts`, `ai/headless.ts` |
| RE-AI7 | **No invented rules, measured experiments only.** The bots model exactly the simulation's rules (no generator HP, cooldowns, chain caps or damage falloff). Authored barriers (`x`) are ordinary crates to the bots (blasting one opens the shortcut; `tests/ai/radial.test.ts`); bus pull-out / traffic sweeps are ordinary `sweep` stage events in the danger map (a `depart` flag, if added, changes nothing for them). A "chain-link exposure" rule (check the escape against the earliest tick one more rival generator could chain mine to a ticking neighbour) was tried and dropped: over 36 32-bot matches it moved own-blast deaths by -11 / +10, i.e. noise. | `ai/danger.ts`, `ai/goals.ts` |
| RE-E1 | **Living environments are data, built generically.** An arena declares `env` (a `StageEnvironment`, or a factory of the board size) on its `ArenaArt`; `GameRenderer` builds it (`LivingEnvironment`, a `View`) and creates nothing when it is absent. Ambient traffic, crowd zones, reaction rules, practical lights, audio names, occlusion hints and a per-quality budget are all fields of that one object, in tile coordinates relative to the board, so a new Lagos stage adds a data file and one registry line. `?noenv` switches it off for measuring. Noncombatant NPCs are presentation only: they are never simulation entities, so they cannot be hit, block anything or be credited a kill or a perk (`tests/living-env.test.ts`: no NPC keys in the state, every elimination credit is a competitor id, the env modules import no stepping API from the sim, and a match with the environment attached ends byte-identical to one without). | `render/env/types.ts`, `render/env/LivingEnvironment.ts`, `render/arenas/newArenas.ts`, `render/GameRenderer.ts` |
| RE-E2 | **Ambient traffic is kept visibly and structurally apart from attack vehicles and stage-hazard vehicles.** Own muted clones of the model materials (never the ones `VehicleViews` / `StageHazardViews` use), lamps dimmed, no headlight beams, sprites, lights or telegraph colours, a darker grade per kind (buses duller than Danfos). Never on the board: every route is validated against the board rectangle plus a 1.2 tile margin; an elevated deck must lie north of the north edge; the strip directly south of the board (2.5 tiles) takes only vehicles under 1 m (okadas), people never. Vehicles shrink in and out at the ends of a path, steer through corners, and crawl at 40 % speed with reduced motion. Positions are a pure function of the plan and the render clock. | `render/env/plan.ts` (`validateEnvironment`), `render/env/Traffic.ts` |
| RE-E3 | **Danfo departure hazard.** A sweep may be flagged `depart: true`: a parked Danfo pulls out of a bay (route starts on a road tile INSIDE the board) and leaves at the board edge (route ends on it); `rumble-stages.test.ts` accepts a flagged route only if it ends at the edge, never loosens other routes. The picture: parked and visible on its bay for the whole telegraph with blinkers (faster as the moment nears), two horn rings, rolling back a hand's breadth with reversing lamps, then accelerating out. **The simulation keeps the uniform `stepTicks`**: the bots' danger windows (`ai/danger.ts`) assume it, so a sim-side ramp would put them behind the real vehicle; the pull-out is eased in the picture only, never ahead of the lethal tile and at most 0.8 tile behind it once at speed. Fairness: lethal telegraph >= `minLethalWarnTicks` as for every sweep, max lethal events, quiet period and first-event delay unchanged; a departure is DELAYED (looked at again a second later, deterministically) while its bay holds a player, a generator or a crate (nothing appears on top of anyone) and while anyone standing on the lane could not walk off it before the vehicle reaches their tile: `laneEscapeSteps` counts the steps from every lane tile to an open tile off the lane through the live board (crates, grounded generators, flames and other lethal events are not exits), and the fighter needs their own step time per tile plus `stage.escapeInputTicks` (12 ticks) within `warn + tileIndex * stepTicks`. The same check runs on the arena data for every flagged route in `rumble-stages.test.ts` with the slowest fighter (10 ticks a step); events never combine with another lethal event (`maxLethalEvents` 1) or with the closing city (`quiet`). | `sim/systems/stage.ts`, `sim/arenas/types.ts`, `render/views/departure.ts`, `render/views/StageHazardViews.ts` |
| RE-E4 | **Budgets per quality tier.** low: 4 vehicles of one model, 14 people (instanced, no animated rigs; the research's provisional low tier is 12-20 people and 2-4 background vehicles), 4 light glows; medium: 10 vehicles / 3 models, 36 people of which 5 animated rigs, 8 glows; high: 18 / 4 models, 70 people / 10 rigs, 14 glows. A stage may lower, never raise, these (`budgetFor`). Planned draw-call targets 24 / 60 / 90 (checked by the plan and tests for every board size): each vehicle model is merged by material (about 8 draws) and every vehicle is one instance; rigs about 5 draws each; figures one draw per pose; the lights one draw call; exhaust and dust one particle system (not on low). Rigs and ambient vehicles cast shadows only on high (a shadow caster is drawn twice). With the follow camera, what is out of view is neither moved nor drawn. | `render/env/types.ts` (`ENV_BUDGET`), `render/env/plan.ts` |
| RE-E5 | **Crowds react to SimEvents only, inside protected zones.** Reaction rules (`blast`, `elimination`, `stage`) pick duck / flinch / scatter / cheer / look by distance to the zone rectangle, a minimum blast radius and a per-person chance (a larger blast convinces a few more); a zone's own rules replace the environment's per kind. Scatter moves a few steps away and walks home; positions are clamped to the zone at every frame; reduced motion turns scatter into a flinch in place. People are distinct from fighters by construction: muted darkened clothing, scale 0.80-0.90 against the cast's 1.0, no team colours / outfits / markers / name tags, bystander materials tagged and never shadow-casting except rigs on high. A few real rigs (`StreetLife`: seller pairs, touts, labourers, onlookers) are mixed with instanced `crowd.glb` figures (a procedural silhouette when the file is missing). Randomness is a seeded generator advanced by the events; no `Math.random`. | `render/env/plan.ts` (`stimuliFrom`, `ruleFor`), `render/life/CrowdZones.ts`, `render/life/StreetLife.ts` |
| RE-E6 | **Lighting and audio identity are hooks, not engines.** Practical lights are glow quads (camera-facing, plus an optional pool of light on the street) in a single instanced additive mesh with a pure flicker function; no scene lights are added (no shader recompiles, no per-pixel cost). Audio identity is a list of existing synthesised sound names (ambience loops and crowd one-shots); `tests/living-env.test.ts` checks every name against the recipe registry. Nothing is generated and no recording is introduced; the audio engine reading `LivingEnvironment.audioHooks()` is a later step (not done here). | `render/env/Lights.ts`, `render/env/types.ts` |
| RE-E7 | **Default Ojuelegba environment, written from the board bounds.** Danfos and buses (with the odd truck, flyover only) cross on the deck north of the board (two lanes, 6.6 m up), Danfos and buses dominate the Western Approach and turn along the outer lane with a few taxis and a lone keke, Danfos, a bus and taxis pass on an east ring road; bus-stop commuters and food sellers wait under the bridge edge, traders work Shopfront Lane's outer (north-east) edge, touts call passengers at the Eastern Danfo Park and mechanics work the rear service loop. Lights: bare LED tubes (#E8F4FF) at the stop, phone-shop LEDs at the stalls, sodium (#FF9F3A) at the loop and the park, soft bounce pools under the deck so the shade stays readable. Rows and columns marked `ALIGN` in `ojuelegbaEnv.ts` are guesses to be re-aligned with the rebuilt stage; the existing static art still places four parked vehicles on the old deck, which the moving traffic now duplicates (remove them from the art when the stage is rebuilt). | `render/env/ojuelegbaEnv.ts` |
| RE-E8 | **Research reconciliation (late brief, 26 Oct).** Adopted: no okadas in Ojuelegba's ambient traffic (commercial okadas have been banned in Surulere / Lagos Mainland since 2022; Danfos and buses dominate, taxis few, kekes sparse, trucks on the flyover only; a test pins this); flyover traffic is visual and non-colliding (instanced, the equivalent of the research's baked overhead loop) and the stage's scheduled sweeps stay the only gameplay traffic; civilians live in protected frontage strips off the board (bus-stop commuters and food sellers, touts at the Danfo pocket, traders beyond Shopfront Lane, mechanics at the rear loop), react with short authored animations, never collide, loot or target, and never stand where they could hide a generator, a blast footprint or a hazard warning (zones are validated off the board); fictional touts (no union names, no extortion props). Adapted: the contract also records cutaway regions (`occlusion.cutaway`) and per-arena safety notes (`safety.safePockets`, `safety.exclusions`) as data for the art and tests (the simulation does not read them). Deferred: upper-window spectators (needs building facades), a moving machinery set, deck audio passes, conductor calls (only with an approved founder clip, never TTS). Rejected and not built: crowd surges or NPC shoves as damage, NPCs as obstacles / loot / targets, agbero chip damage, falling containers or debris at Ojuelegba, 150-NPC VAT crowds / Mass systems, generated audio. | `render/env/ojuelegbaEnv.ts`, `render/env/types.ts` |
| RE-E9 | **Audio separation stays as built.** Hazard and generator warnings sit on their own bus that is never ducked, and `stageWarning` / blast events already duck the crowd, engine and music paths (`eventRouter.ts`, `buses.ts`); the environment adds no audio of its own and only names existing recipes, so ambient beds cannot mask a warning. | `audio/eventRouter.ts` (unchanged) |
| RE-D1 | **Ojuelegba Underbridge becomes the five-district prototype, after the approved research layout (J-A..J-E).** One 45 x 33, NOT mirrored board (`rumbleOjuelegba`; id, name, music, menu card and its place in `RUMBLE_ARENA_IDS` kept). Camera looks north, so the bands read far: Rear Service Loop (E) / centre: Western Approach (A), Underbridge Pier Court (B), Eastern Danfo pocket (C) / near: Shopfront Lane (D) and C's crossing refuge. Districts and links are data (`districts`, `districtLinks`): always-open A-B, B-C, A-D, D-C, A-E, E-C; B-E only through the marked empty workshop-yard partition (`x`). Three A-to-C paths (pier court, shopfront flank, rear loop); no district needs a blast to be reached; every district has two or more exits. Size: within the lead's 41-49 x 31-35 range rather than the research's ~36 x 28 start, because the stage carries 32 competitors with radial blasts (about 31 walkable tiles each) and must give every district its own ground at follow-camera scale; neither number is canonical. A first draft (west-east bands, extra breaches, mirrored-stage habits) was replaced the same day when the research pack arrived. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOjuelegba.ts`, `tests/rumble-ojuelegba.test.ts` |
| RE-D2 | **67 % walkable, not the research's 45-60 %.** 33 % of the board is permanent cover (buildings on the far perimeter, workshops, piers, packed buses, shop blocks, low lock-ups on the near edge); 14 % is road. The other Rumble stages are 69-84 % open; going to 50 % with 32 fighters left no room for farthest-point spawns with a radial way out (min gaps fell under 5 at 32). Spatial rhythm follows the research: a 4-lane primary street (rows 15-18), 2-3 wide local routes (service lanes, shop lane, pavements), a few one-tile doorway recesses (3 articulation tiles on the whole board), irregular pockets round the clear bay. | layout |
| RE-D3 | **Non-mirrored spawns: balanced farthest-point picks.** `balancedSpawns` (rumbleKit, additive) picks farthest-point over the whole board, never letting one half get two picks ahead, interleaves west / east (slot 2k west, 2k+1 east: every even prefix is half and half, Team Rumble's split by x is balanced by count at every competitor count), then widens the tightest pairs by moving a late slot within its half. `RADIAL_CLEARANCE`: three steps straight on (dx 3 is outside the base R 2 disc) plus the tile beside the spawn; spawns avoid traps and every tile within 2 steps of a supply crate. Result: min gaps 13 / 8 / 6 (8 / 16 / 32); every district gets spawns; mean walk to the core 24.9 vs 22.2 steps and to the nearest supply 7.9 vs 7.7 (west vs east, 32 spawns). Only the 4-fold mirror and mirror-pair checks of `rumble-stages.test.ts` are skipped for this stage (`runIf(mirrorSoftBlocks)`); a halves-hold-comparable-ground check replaces them. | `rumbleKit.ts balancedSpawns`, `tests/rumble-stages.test.ts` |
| RE-D4 | **Vehicles use the through road.** `config.vehicles.lanes` = the four lanes of the road's west and east mouths and the two lanes of the departure strip's depot gate (south): summoned Danfo / taxi / Keke attacks run A -> under the bridge -> C (or back) between the piers, never through the walking gaps; every kind has 10 legal entries and the one-press special finds a lane route (tested per character). | `rumbleOjuelegba.ts LANES` |
| RE-D5 | **Hazards: traffic on the through road, the signalled bus pull-out, dumped goods.** Buses / kekes sweep the four road lanes (right-hand traffic, 2 s warning). The signature hazard is a `danfo` DEPARTURE (`depart: true`, RE-E3): a loaded bus waits in its bay at the foot of the departure strip (row 31), pulls out up the strip onto the road and leaves at the east edge (2.5 s warning; the sim holds the announcement while anyone on the lane could not walk off it). Pavements beside the strip and the bays are safe. No falling containers, debris or bridge collapse (research: real fatal truck accidents at Ojuelegba). | `rumbleOjuelegba.ts stageHazards` |
| RE-D6 | **Breakable cover is marked.** `x` tiles: the B-E workshop-yard partition, the shop lane's cross-passage partition, three parcel stacks in the bus pocket (alternate crossing angles). Each is drawn as a road barricade or tyre stack (`obstacleStyles.softFor`, a new optional per-tile hook honoured by `softVariantFor`) inside a painted yellow-and-black floor frame that stays after the break (an empty marked footprint). District clutter has its own family per district (tyres / barricades on the approach, cartons / kegs under the bridge, tyres / crates in the pocket, sacks / cartons in the lane, drums / cable drums in the service loop). | `render/assets/obstacleStyles.ts`, `props.ts`, `rumbleOjuelegbaArt.ts` floor |
| RE-D7 | **Eight supply crates**, four per half, in every district (E 3, A 1, B 1, C 1, D 2), each with three or more open sides and never within 2 steps of a spawn. | layout |
| RE-D8 | **Board cover: permanent tiles drawn as the real objects.** New generic hook `ArenaArt.cover` (`render/arenas/boardCover.ts`, `CoverBuilder`): the arena view draws no per-tile pillar on the original concrete a cover claims (crush blocks still drop in as pillars), widens the occlusion fade by the object's reach, and fades whole sections (four columns) of a group. Ojuelegba's render-only cover map (`ojuelegbaCoverMap.ts`, one letter per tile, tested against the layout) becomes merged meshes (`ojuelegbaCover.ts`): pier stubs with black-and-white base bands, 18 parked Danfos from the GLB (route cards in the windscreens), shop buildings with fictional fascias, workshops with roller shutters, perimeter buildings with windows, the yard wall, kerb islands and the railed refuge, kiosks, stalls, low lock-ups on the near edge, tyre racks, the bus stop, the generator house, a skip. All signage is fictional (`ojuelegbaSigns.ts`); the bus stop board carries no place name. | `render/views/ArenaView.ts` (hook), `render/arenas/newArenas.ts` (`cover`), `ojuelegba*.ts` |
| RE-D9 | **The flyover is drawn over the board only as its outer edges, cut away near the camera.** The deck runs north-south over tile columns 15-29 and on north of the board (set; the living environment's deck traffic runs there). Over the board: the two north-south fascia girders with soffit strips and the upper pier shafts with their hammerheads, ending over the south bent; no central deck and no east-west crossheads (seen from the camera a crosshead lies across the frame and hid the pavement behind it in the first frames). A screen-space cutaway (ordered dither, `ojuelegbaDeck.ts`) removes everything in the lower ~60 % of the frame (between the camera and the fight) and an ellipse round the screen centre (the followed fighter); the beams show only towards the top of the frame. Occluded fighters keep their x-ray silhouettes. | `render/arenas/ojuelegbaDeck.ts` |
| RE-D10 | **Research reconciliation (Ojuelegba).** Adopted: the J-A..J-E graph and bands, a few large piers in unequal bays, packed nose-to-tail Danfos, the departure strip, a marked empty partition for the cross-passage and the B-E shortcut, the bypass loop with two exits, vehicles on the through road only, the signalled pull-out, tonal variation on piers, reflected light under the deck (LED tubes, a 0.34 shade not a cave), no okada clusters (one incidental bike), no place-name billboard, fictional signage. Adapted: walkable share (RE-D2), board size (RE-D1). Deferred: upper-window spectators (the living environment's zones are E's; noted), night lighting. Rejected (never built): two-level / deck combat, falling containers, HP / damage falloff / cooldowns, agbero shoves, navmesh / diagonal movement, UE5 systems. | this section |
| RE-AI8 | **Crowded chains, after the economy merge.** On the merged branch (8 supply crates per stage, `radialMaxSquareRadius` 6) own-blast deaths under radial crept back above the cross (6 seeds x 3 stages: 119 vs 116 at 32 bots, 62 vs 54 at 16). Two radial-only changes: (1) every placement check assumes the worst linked chain - ticking generators whose boxes are at most one generator apart (Chebyshev distance <= box a + box b) all go off with the earliest of their group (`DangerQuery.linkedChains`), so a bot no longer drops a generator into a cluster it could not leave before that cluster's first blast; (2) in City Rumble every level models chain reactions and checks its escape before placing (`radialProfile`: `chains` on, `placeVerify` 1) - with two generators each and discs instead of lanes, chain blindness and unchecked placements turned the low levels' own second generator into a self-trap. Every other handicap (cadence, reactions, lookahead, mistakes, hesitation, hunt, specials) is kept, so Rookie stays the weakest. Result on the same seeds: 78 vs 116 (32 bots) and 48 vs 54 (16 bots); 8 / 16 / 24 / 32-bot matrix 14 / 24 / 40 / 54 vs 13 / 24 / 49 / 63. This replaces RE-AI7's "parity" conclusion. Classic: identical fingerprint and intent hashes. | `ai/danger.ts`, `ai/analysis.ts`, `ai/profiles.ts`, `ai/brain.ts` |

## OJ - Ojuelegba identity pass: audio completion, A-hold detonation, trap readability (branch `claude/wahala-ojuelegba-identity-pass-v1`) - founder brief of 2026-10-28

No map, spawn, camera, traffic or combat-balance change. The visual pass (`592b409`) is untouched.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OJ1 | **The last 17 founder voice clips are imported** (`crowd_*` x8, `market_*` x7, `park_fare_exchange_01`, `park_route_exchange_02`), so all 47 planned voice lines now have an approved clip. The attached `Sounds.zip` held 47 WAVs (+ 47 macOS `__MACOSX` resource-fork stubs, ignored); 30 were byte-identical to the masters already shipped (the brief said 32: the repo, `recorded.json` and `docs/AUDIO.md` all count 30). They went through the sanctioned importer only (`import_voices.py --add`): the 30 existing MP3s re-encoded bit-identically, `voices.json` is unchanged, `conductor_cms_01` stays rejected, and no music file or manifest was touched. | `tools/audio/voices/masters/`, `public/audio/voices/recorded.json`, `recorded/*.mp3`, `tests/audio/assets.test.ts` |
| OJ2 | **Button A: tap = place a generator, hold = set off your remote generators.** Same mechanism and threshold as B's hold (`HoldButton`, 24 ticks = 0.4 s, WA4): with no live remote generator a press places at once (no delay, as before); with one, the press waits, a release before the threshold places, a hold detonates once and the release then places nothing. So a quick tap can never detonate and a hold never also drops a generator. The sim is unchanged: `Intent.detonate` still triggers only the player's OWN `remote`-mode generators (`detonateRemote`), so ownership, blast rules, chains and costs are as before; timed fuses are never set off by hand. **B keeps both jobs** (tap = special, hold = detonate), so existing habits, the tutorial and the legend stay valid; A-hold is the new primary. Keyboard (Space / J), gamepad (A) and touch all go through the same `InputManager`. | `input/specialHold.ts`, `input/InputManager.ts`, `ui/TouchControls.tsx` |
| OJ3 | **Arming cue on every device.** Touch: the same filling ring and `HOLD: BOOM!` label on A as on B. Keyboard / gamepad: the Remote chip in the HUD inventory row reads `HOLD A` and fills from `InputManager.detonateProgress()` each frame (larger of A and B). Nothing is drawn over the board. | `ui/Hud.tsx`, `ui/styles.css` |
| OJ4 | **Floor traps are readable; their rules are not touched.** Oil slides 2 tiles, a pothole stumbles for 0.6 s, no damage, same tile footprint. Oil is now a glossy amber spill with a rainbow sheen, specular streaks and a yellow/black caution rim (it was a near-black disc); the pothole gets a pale rubble halo and orange/white caution dashes. On contact (`hazardTriggered`) a ring and droplets / rubble chips burst on the tile; the fighter plays `Stumble` and falls (oil: backwards onto the back; pothole: a forward trip) for exactly as long as the sim keeps them sliding / dazed, then eases back to standing with a small hop. The pose is derived from `Player.slide` / `dazedUntil` only (pure `render/views/slip.ts`). | `render/textures.ts`, `render/views/TrapViews.ts`, `render/fx/Effects.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `render/views/slip.ts` |
| OJ5 | **A clear slip sound**: new synthesised `oil_slip` (rubber skid glide, whoosh, body slap, two scuffs) for an oil hit; `pothole_thud` stays for potholes and `oil_squeak` stays for the endgame wave going live. | `audio/recipes/blasts.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts` |
| OJ6 | **Traffic is untouched**: ambient traffic, road wires / traps, per-arena density, the self-hit and damage rules and the summoned Danfo / taxi / Keke attacks are verified by their existing tests (listed in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`), not changed. | - |

## UX2 / PF - Mobile-first flow and adaptive graphics (branch `claude/uiux-adaptive-performance-v1`, from `15466fc`)

Presentation only: no simulation, AI, input, arena geometry or rule changed. Reports: `docs/STITCH_UI.md` (flow),
`docs/PERFORMANCE.md` (graphics). Supersedes UI1's "Arena as a detour" and the numbered step strip.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| UX1 | **No numbered steps.** The "Step 1-4" strip and the "Continue to setup" button are removed. Flow: Home -> Select game mode -> Arena and settings -> Fighter -> Review -> Start Match. Classic Battle on Home goes straight to its arena and settings (its mode is implied; "Mode: ... change" is on the screen). | `app/menu.ts`, `ui/menu/*` |
| UX2 | **Tapping a mode picks it and moves on.** From Ready's Edit it returns to Ready, unless the game itself changed (City Rumble <-> Classic): then the arena and settings open first, because that game's arena and rules are different. | `ModeScreen.tsx` |
| UX3 | **"Select Arena" is part of the setup screen**, not a screen behind a "change" link: picture cards of the game's arenas / stages, the chosen one marked "Selected" and described (lighting, board size, special rule, hazards). The old Arena screen and its Confirm step are gone (the `arena` menu screen no longer exists; saved menu states containing it are repaired by `validMenu`). The default arena is still the saved or default one, but it is shown as a choice. | `ArenaPicker.tsx`, `SetupScreen.tsx` |
| UX4 | **Bot difficulty is one slider over the seven existing levels** (Rookie ... Nightmare, "Very easy" to "Extremely hard"), with the level named and described above the thumb. Not six: the bot ladder has seven levels and Rookie is the default, so none was dropped. "Same for all bots" vs "Mixed roster" (City Rumble) / "Set each bot" (Classic) is a two-way choice with a plain sentence under it ("Mixed bot difficulty - each opponent uses a different difficulty level, from X to Y, centred on Z"). The range shown is computed from the same spread table the match uses (`MIXED_SPREAD`, tested against `buildPlayers`). | `DifficultyControl.tsx`, `difficultyPicker.ts`, `app/names.ts` |
| UX5 | **The selected fighter fits one screen**: its card (portrait, name, one-line description from the character data, melee, cooldown and vehicle from the sim data and config) comes first, abilities beside or below it, the roster is a compact strip / list. Checked at 390x844, 360x740, 375x667, 844x390, 667x375, 915x412, 1280x720, 1440x900 without any scrolling. No invented stats. | `FighterScreen.tsx`, `flow.css` |
| UX6 | **Review** keeps one row per choice (mode, arena, fighter, opponents, bot level) each with its own Edit; the bot-level row says whether the level is for all bots or a mixed roster; the final button is "Start Match". An Edit of the arena or the settings returns to Review through a "Back to review" button. | `ReadyScreen.tsx` |
| PF1 | **Graphics: one ladder of 8 rungs** (Very Low, Very Low+, Low, Low+, Medium-Low, Medium, Medium-High, High) instead of three presets; six are selectable by hand, **Auto is the default**. Saved `low / medium / high` still load; unknown values fall back to Auto. | `render/quality.ts`, `app/settings.ts` |
| PF2 | **Auto = a first guess from hints + measured frame times.** Hints (WebGL level, GPU string, memory, cores, screen, DPR) only choose the start rung and a ceiling; every missing hint falls back to a safe start (3 on mobile, 5 on desktop); software renderers start at Very Low and may not climb past Low. | `render/perf/capabilities.ts` |
| PF3 | **The controller reacts to sustained behaviour only**: 4 s window, median / p90 / spike count, 45 fps median to go down (24 fps: two rungs, sooner), 55 fps p90 for 15 s to go up, 8 s / 30 s cooldown and dwell, window emptied and 3 s pause after every change, an explosion burst defers a normal drop. Isolated slow frames never count. | `render/perf/adaptive.ts` |
| PF4 | **No oscillation**: a just-raised rung that fails is blocked (90 s, doubling to 15 min); two drops that do not speed frames up (a 30 Hz cap, a CPU-bound device) stop the descent, restore the quality and pause Auto (2 min, doubling). | `adaptive.ts` |
| PF5 | **Live vs built knobs.** A rung change applies only cheap live knobs (pixel ratio, shadow pass and map size, particle density, crowd / street-life share, atmosphere emission, animation stride); content fixed at arena build (pool sizes, parked buses, flame lights, antialiasing, set content) follows the rung at match start, and the rung is remembered per device so the next match builds right. No material recompile unless the shadow pass flips. | `GameRenderer.applyQuality`, `Crowd.setShare`, `StreetLife.setShare`, `AmbientFx.setScale` |
| PF6 | **A manual level is final**: Auto's controller is not consulted, no notice appears. Switching Auto on continues from the rung the player was looking at. | `GameRenderer.setQuality` |
| PF7 | **The notice** goes through the existing HUD rail toast (top-left under the portraits, never over the board): "Graphics reduced · Very Low", "Graphics increased · Medium" (direction and the level now in use); at most one per 25 s and three per match. Settings > Graphics always shows the level in use. | `app/qualityNotice.ts`, `SettingsScreen.tsx` |
| PF8 | **Gameplay is untouched by quality**: sim, AI, input, bomb timing, collisions, blasts and match rules never see the quality system (tested: the same seed plays out identically at High, Very Low and Auto with mid-match changes). Bot think rate, collision work and debris simulation were measured (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`) but NOT reduced: doing so would change gameplay or determinism, so it needs a founder decision. | `e2e/quality.spec.ts` |
| PF9 | **Audio was inspected and left alone**: voices are capped at 24 with priorities, background conversations are one line every 7-15 s, three looping beds. No gameplay cue or voice line is shed. | `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` |
| PF10 | **Measuring on devices**: `?fps` shows level, render scale and sim / ai / render ms per frame; `__WAHALA__.quality()` and `feedFrames()` exist for tests and tuning; `scripts/quality-bench.mjs` compares the rungs. | `app/sectionTimers.ts`, `scripts/quality-bench.mjs` |

## LA - Lagos arenas, build 1: Oshodi Transport Metropolis (branch `claude/wahala-lagos-arenas-v1`, from `1e099f6`) - founder brief of 2026-10-30

Source: _WAHALA Canonical Lagos City Rumble Map Build Bible v2_. Ojuelegba is untouched. Report: `docs/OSHODI_METROPOLIS.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LA1 | **The researched Oshodi is the canonical Oshodi stage and REPLACES the old generic "Oshodi Rush Hour"** (founder decision, 2026-10-30; first built as a separate `rumbleOshodiMetro`). It took the id `rumbleOshodi`, as Ojuelegba did when it was rebuilt, so the saved default, the Rumble setup default, the founder's Oshodi track and the voice zones carry over; the old sim, art and screenshots are deleted and only one Oshodi card shows. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts`, `index.ts`, `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |
| LA2 | **Six districts on a 45 x 33 non-mirrored board** (the bible's O1-O6), routes direct / upper / lower plus four cross-links, none needing a blast. The engine is single-level, so "grade-separated" means the Skywalk Quarter and Cappa band never share a tile with a road or track lane (tested). | `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |
| LA3 | **The railway is a new sweep style, `train`, on two track lanes (x 36 / 37, y 10-22), one at a time, 3.5 s warning, 15 tiles/s, every 16-24 s.** It enters and leaves through solid rail-bridge blocks three tiles deep, so it never crosses the upper and lower routes; every track tile is one step from a track-free tile. A sweep (not a strike) so the lethal footprint is the car it shows: one short railcar, not a train longer than its hazard. | `sim/types.ts`, `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts`, `render/views/hazardTrain.ts` |
| LA4 | **One lethal stage event at a time on this stage** (`maxLethalEvents: 1`, the Rumble default is 2): the railcar never overlaps traffic or a bus departure, so the safe-route guarantee is literal. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts` |
| LA5 | **No south road.** A Cappa slip road from the south edge split the lower route and made the grade-separated route impossible; the attack lanes are the main road's eight mouths only (>= 8 entries per vehicle, tested). | `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |
| LA6 | **Spawns are an explicit, tested list.** The stock farthest-point picker put 17 of 32 fighters into the wide Skywalk band and one on the rails; a seeded offline max-min search gives gaps of 12 / 8 / 4 for 8 / 16 / 32 starts and a start in every district. | `SPAWNS` |
| LA7 | **Warning for the railcar**: lane burn-down, alternating red crossing lamps at both lane ends and the crossing, `rail_bell` + `train_horn` panned to the track, a HUD alert that names the track. Three synthesised recipes, no recording; never voiced as a Danfo or Keke. | `StageHazardViews.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts`, `audio/recipes/vehicles.ts` |
| LA8 | **Night is a review flag, `?tod=night`**, on the same geometry (`ArenaArt.nightLook`, `render/timeOfDay.ts`). The night key light is as strong as the approved Lekki night (tested); only this stage ships a night look. A player-facing switch is not built. | `render/arenaLook.ts` |
| LA9 | **No music, no voice, no recording added.** Through the replacement the stage keeps the old Oshodi's founder-assigned track (`pixabay_601035`) and its `bus_park` / `market` voice zones. | `tests/audio/assets.test.ts` |
| LA10 | **Boss policy**: only `ArenaDef.futureBoss` metadata (reserved, read by nothing). No inspiration is chosen; none may be before documented research and approval. | `sim/arenas/types.ts` |
| LA11 | **Core connectivity fix.** The Survival core had one tile (17,12) walled off by a bay shelter, the terminal wing and a bus; the shelter tile (18,12) is opened (sim layout and cover map) and the whole core is asserted to be one region. No spawn clearance, supply, route or district link changed. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts`, `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |
| LA12 | **Generic stage tests treat Oshodi as the second multi-district, non-mirrored stage** (with Ojuelegba): railcar routes may start out of a solid portal block instead of an edge, eight attack-lane mouths per vehicle are enough here (LA5), Team Rumble's open-floor tolerance is 12% (measured 11.6%) and the 32-start spread floor is 4. Their fairness is pinned in `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts`. | `tests/rumble-stages.test.ts` |
| LA13 | **Oshodi identity pass (visual).** The stage's backdrop is rebuilt from the 109 inspected references: two OPEN black-steel interchange terminals (deep roof truss frames on tall columns, skylit roof panels with gaps, a lit glass frontage at the back of the hall, painted bays with parked buses, a blue bay canopy; the first ring-drum attempt read as gasometers and was replaced), a Terminal 3 glass frontage under a sloping truss roof, a long covered truss skywalk on two cable-stayed lattice pylons with a big truss portal, a freight siding of grain and container wagons east of the board, a sea of trader umbrellas on the west edge and wide approach roads with lane dashes. All of it stands OFF the board (`oshodiMetroTerminal.ts`); the sim grid, spawns, districts and links are unchanged. The expressway deck and skyline moved 4-10 m north to make room. | `render/arenas/oshodiMetroTerminal.ts`, `oshodiMetroLandmarks.ts`, `tests/render/oshodi-metro-*.test.ts` |
| LA14 | **Opt-in ambient-vehicle boost.** `StageEnvironment.boost` raises a tier's vehicle / people caps for one stage; the shared caps (`ENV_BUDGET`) and every other stage are unchanged. Oshodi uses it (medium 64 vehicles, high 96) so its roads read as bus-choked: four slow, bumper-to-bumper LOOPS (outer and inner, west and east: out one lane, U-turn at the far end, back the other) of danfos and buses beyond both mouths, at the top budget priority; the elevated deck traffic takes the leftover. Instanced vehicles add triangles, not draw calls (model count unchanged). Ambient traffic still never enters the board. | `render/env/types.ts`, `plan.ts`, `oshodiMetroEnv.ts`, test "opt-in budget boost" |
| LA15 | **Main-road sweep cadence** (Oshodi data only): first wave 8 s (was 10), then every 5-7.5 s (was 6-9), warning still 2 s, so crossing the road is risky but possible. The railcar and bus-bay departures are unchanged; one lethal event at a time still holds. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts` |
| LA16 | **Terminals stay in view; the weaponised road stays the sim's.** The tall north perimeter blocks (x 2-33, rows 0-1) that hid the terminals are replaced by a low 2.3 m trading arcade (cover kind `arcade`, letter F; same `#` tiles, sim unchanged), so Terminal 2 and the bay terminal read from the zoomed-out camera without raising any roof. Ambient loops stay scenery: they have no sim counterpart and never enter the board (a render-side collision would put game rules in `render` and drift from the deterministic sim). The road's danger is the existing telegraphed traffic wave on rows 15-18 (warned 2 s, lethal to whoever stays on the lane, never to a mover), now covered by tests; the lane warning is the lightweight hazard corridor. | `render/arenas/oshodiMetroCover*.ts`, `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |
| LA17 | **The board doubles to 63 x 47 (2961 tiles, 1.99x the 1485 of the first build), grown EAST and SOUTH so the original six districts keep their coordinates** (the interchange, the railcar lanes at x 36-37, the road at rows 15-18, every cover letter). Four playable expansion districts: Papa Ajao Market (x 45-62, y 0-18: stalls, containers, kiosks), the Freight Yard (x 45-62, y 19-32: container rows, workshops, skips), the Interstate Bus Park (x 0-31, y 33-46: four rows of parked buses, a ticket hall, shelters, shops) and the Agege Motor Strip (x 32-62, y 33-46: stalls and containers). The old east and south walls are opened in broad gaps (nine links), the main road runs on to the new east edge, and an automatic fill pass leaves no open rectangle larger than 28 tiles or 8 x 5 outside the old core. 68% walkable (65% before); roads are 9% of the board. 32 fresh starts (gaps 22 / 14 / 8 for 8 / 16 / 32 starts, every district at least one), 14 supply crates (7 per half), 8 new traps. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts`, `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |
| LA18 | **The Survival core stays at the interchange, placed explicitly.** `keepCore` gains optional `x0` / `y0` (additive; every other stage still centres it): the core is x 21-31, y 12-20 (the plaza's east edge and the junction, on the main road). For such a core the crush closes in rings AROUND THE CORE, farthest first (a board-centred spiral left a band of up to 64 tiles cut off from the core at the end; the worst now is 3). The half-balance of walking distance to the core is relaxed to 14 tiles: the core sits in the west half of the grown board, and Team / Deathmatch rulesets never close the city. | `sim/config.ts`, `sim/state.ts`, `ui/minimapModel.ts` |
| LA19 | **Two lethal stage events at once** (the shared V2 big-board default) instead of one, with the road wave every 5-8 s (first at 8 s): the longer road (63 tiles) kept the single slot busy and starved the railcar to one or two a match. Every event is still telegraphed and refuge-checked; none in the quiet seconds. | `sim/arenas/rumbleOshodi.ts` |
| LA20 | **The road wave is shown from the first tick of its warning.** On Oshodi the wave's bus or danfo is drawn at the far end of the approach road when the lane starts burning, covers one tile per sim step (the sim's own speed) and enters the board's road mouth on the tick the sweep starts, on the same row and in the same direction; orange pulse rings ring it so it is told apart from the scenery. Ambient loops stay presentation (no simulation, never on the board). Every other stage keeps its short approach (`sweepApproachTiles`). | `render/views/StageHazardViews.ts`, `tests/render/sweep-approach.test.ts` |
| LA21 | **Fair-share ambient traffic** (`StageEnvironment.fairShare`, opt-in): routes of one priority share a small vehicle budget round-robin, so the low tier still draws vehicles on both the west and the east road. Other stages keep first-route-first. | `render/env/plan.ts`, `tests/render/oshodi-metro-env.test.ts` |
| LA22 | **The road wave's vehicle stands alone.** While a traffic wave is warned, ambient ground vehicles fade out (smoothstep, gone inside 3.5 tiles, full size beyond 8) around the wave vehicle's approach position (`render/env/waveClearance.ts`, same arithmetic as the hazard view; opt-in `StageEnvironment.yieldToWave`, Oshodi only). Presentation only: no sim state, no vehicle added, per-frame cost is one distance test per ground vehicle per wave. The orange rings stay. | `tests/render/wave-clearance.test.ts` |
| LA23 | **Review of the west/east core-distance gap: accepted, core not moved.** Walking distance to the core edge for the 32 starts: median 21, max 49 tiles (slot 31), mean 15.2 west / 26.4 east. At 6 tiles/s (10 ticks a tile) the farthest start reaches the core in about 8 s, the human's slot 0 in 6 s, while the first tile only lands 60 s into the match (2:00 left) and the ring closes farthest-first, so no start is disadvantaged by the gap. The half split only exists in Team Rumble, which never closes the city, and in Survival every fighter is on their own. Supply distance stays balanced (<= 4 tiles between halves). Terminal 3 now stands wholly inside the board's east edge (x 17-31 world, tiles 48.5-62.5; it was 3.5 m over), narrower by 4 m. | `tests/rumble-oshodi.test.ts` |

## SD / FF / MC / PA - Core rules and mobile UX (branch `claude/wahala-core-rules-mobile-ux-v1`, from `24e6387`) - founder brief of 2026-10-31

| #   | Decision | Where |
| --- | -------- | ----- |
| SD1 | **Rumble draws are replaced by kill comparison, then sudden death** (rule restated by the founder 2026-10-31). Survival / Deathmatch / Team Rumble only; Classic keeps its draw rules exactly. Sudden death starts when the match timer expires or a blast takes down everyone still active. The credited kills of EVERY player count, the ones who have just fallen included (a team adds its members). One clear highest total wins at once (`decidedBy` `timeoutKills` / `simultaneousKills`, a dead player can win it); the players tied on the highest total are the ONLY contenders. Never an id or execution order. Deathmatch's old scoreboard tie-breaks (fewer deaths, assists, earlier last kill) still order the board (`rankPlayers`, `timedResult`) but no longer decide the match. | `sim/systems/match.ts`, `suddenDeath.ts` `resolveByKills` |
| SD2 | **Sudden death is a round inside the same match, not a new phase** (`match.suddenDeath`). Stage `transition` (4 s: the SUDDEN DEATH card, input held, contenders protected, world keeps running) then `countdown`. Everyone who is not a contender stands down (no death counted, nobody credited); fallen contenders are brought back with the normal respawn routine; contenders are seated spread apart on open floor in the compacted area (the never-closing core every Rumble stage has; the board's open bounding box otherwise). | `sim/systems/suddenDeath.ts` |
| SD3 | **The finishing blast is full-size and hits every contender wherever they stand** (not only those in the core), through spawn protection. It is an ordinary lethal hit (`cause.kind 'suddenDeath'`) resolved by `applyLethalHits`, so protection is the existing Okada then Armour, one layer per blast, and nothing else was invented. There is no "extra life" item in this codebase (Deathmatch respawning is the only extra life and it is over at time up), so none is consumed. | `sim/systems/lethality.ts` |
| SD4 | **Blasts at 60 s, 30 s, then every 15 s, until one contender is left** (`config.suddenDeath.blastDelayTicks` = [60 s, 30 s], `repeatBlastTicks` = 15 s; no fixed count: each blast takes one layer from every survivor, so the round always narrows). Last side standing wins (`decidedBy: 'suddenDeath'`; a fight can end the round before any blast). There is NO separate mutual-elimination result: contenders who fall together in a fight re-enter the same resolution (kills of every player decide; a still-shared lead starts a fresh round for the tied players); a BLAST that takes the last contenders down together with the lead still shared leaves no winner to produce, so it is the only draw, `suddenDeathExhausted` (a unique leader among all players still wins). `suddenDeathMutual` was removed. | `sim/config.ts`, `sim/systems/suddenDeath.ts` |
| SD6 | **The kill ranking stays on screen in sudden death**: the title card lists each contender's kills, the blast clock in the HUD rail carries the contenders' live kill totals (`SuddenDeathHud.ranking` is everyone's kills, contenders flagged), and the Rumble HUD's own leaders / standings are untouched. | `app/hud.ts`, `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx` |
| SD5 | **Presentation reuses what exists**: the title card is a full-screen card (4 s, the only transient text that covers the board and only between the whole-match end and the countdown), the blast clock lives in the HUD rail, the blast flashes the screen; sound reuses `crush_warning`, `crush_slam` and `explosion_rumble`. No music, voice or recording was added. The 3D renderer draws no special blast effect. | `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx`, `audio/eventRouter.ts` |
| FF1 | **The game has three selectable characters, not 32; City Rumble seats up to 32 competitors, so the same model repeats.** Every competitor slot therefore gets a deterministic identity: one of 8 hue rotations of the character's cloth colours (greys and whites stay) times one of 4 headwear styles (none, cap, beanie, bucket hat) in a contrasting colour = 32 distinct visible kits, any characters. Slot 0 is the unmodified look (menu art, the player's own fighter). Applied to the glTF characters (cloth vertex colours recoloured per mesh and hue, cached; headwear primitives on the head bone), the procedural fallback and the HUD portraits. No stats, abilities, hitboxes or assets changed; no new pipeline. The fighter-selection screen still offers the three base fighters. | `assets/fighterIdentity.ts`, `render/assets/outfit.ts`, `tests/fighter-identity.test.ts`, `scripts/identity-shots.mjs` |
| MC1 | **Touch layout**: A in the lower-right corner, B diagonally above-left of it (never stacked), both clear of the screen edge (>= 12 px) and the safe areas; a large bounded movement pad that says "press anywhere on this pad" and lights while touched (the real touch zone and all joystick behaviour are unchanged); buttons carry their A / B letter and visible idle / pressed / holding / remote / disabled states (visual only: B stays pressable when dimmed). Landscape phones: the two control strips grew from 0.28 to 0.32 of the screen height (`PHONE_CONTROL_STRIP`), so the board is about 5-7 % narrower than before to make room for the diagonal. | `ui/TouchControls.tsx`, `ui/styles.css`, `render/cameraRig.ts`, `e2e/touch-layout.spec.ts` |
