| PA1 | **Pause silences the whole match.** Music pauses (as before); the ambience fades to silence but keeps its loops (resume cannot double them); generator / vehicle loops, spoken lines and every one-shot including those scheduled for later are stopped; gameplay events are ignored until resume; menu sounds still play. Found and fixed on the way: a countdown paused on its last tick replayed the GO beep every frame. | `audio/engine.ts`, `audio/ambience.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts`, `tests/audio/pause.test.ts` |
| FP1 | **FlowFrame Performance** is the product name of the adaptive graphics system (not claimed as a registered trademark); name, description and mark live in `render/perf/brand.ts`; shown on the loading screen. The controller, thresholds and notices are unchanged. | `docs/FLOWFRAME_PERFORMANCE.md` |

## MD / MV / MC2 - Game modes, corner movement and the A/B cluster (branch `claude/wahala-modes-controls-movement-v1`, from `207ef8b`) - founder brief of 2026-10-31

| #   | Decision | Where |
| --- | -------- | ----- |
| MD1 | **Mode order and defaults.** City Rumble offers three modes, in this order: **Free-for-All** (the default and the first card; sim ruleset `deathmatch`), **Team Deathmatch** (`team`) and **Last Player Standing** (`survival`, the no-respawn mode). The ids and the sim rulesets are unchanged; only the player-facing names, order and the default (`rumbleMode: 'deathmatch'`) changed. Classic Battle stays a separate game after the three. A saved or unknown mode falls back to Free-for-All. | `app/setupSummary.ts`, `app/settings.ts`, `ui/menu/ModeScreen.tsx` |
| MD2 | **Wording says the rule outright**: Free-for-All "Respawn enabled · highest kills wins", Team Deathmatch "Respawn enabled · team with most kills wins", Last Player Standing "No respawn · last survivor wins" (shown on each mode card, data-testid `mode-rule-<id>`, and repeated in the Ready summary). Nothing calls the no-respawn mode the default or the flagship. | `RUMBLE_MODE_INFO`, `ui/menu/ReadyScreen.tsx` |
| MD3 | **Match length is 3 or 5 minutes** (was 5 or 10) for Free-for-All and Team Deathmatch (default 5). A saved 10 falls back to 5. When time expires the most credited kills win, and a tie among the leaders goes to the approved sudden-death process (SD1-SD6, unchanged). | `sim/modes.ts` `TIMED_MINUTES`, `app/settings.ts` |
| MD4 | **Rules of the three modes (no sim change)**: Free-for-All respawns after 3 s with spawn protection, the clock decides, highest credited kills win. Team Deathmatch respawns, two teams, the team with the highest total kills wins when the timer expires. Last Player Standing is no respawn, last survivor wins; time up, or the last fighters falling together, use the kill ranking and sudden death. | `sim/modes.ts`, `sim/systems/match.ts` |
| MD5 | **Team colours**: Team 1 green; Team 2 white / light grey (`0xe8ecef`) with a dark outline (`0x101418`), never purple. The 3D kit (jersey and armbands) and the team ring have a dark outline shell; names get a text outline and team dots a dark ring wherever they appear (HUD, roster, scoreboard, sudden-death card, results). Team members no longer take the per-slot jersey hue (FF1): that hue rotation had been recolouring the White team (it read as purple); teammates keep the character's own look and differ by headwear shape only. | `assets/palette.ts`, `render/views/teamMarkings.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts` |
| MD6 | **Planned, not implemented:** Capture the Flag and multi-team (3-4 team) battles. Only documented (`docs/MODES_AND_CONTROLS.md`). The arena-size assessment for Team Deathmatch and Last Player Standing is a report with measurements; no arena or map geometry was changed. | `docs/MODES_AND_CONTROLS.md`, `scripts/mode-arena-assessment.ts` |
| MV1 | **Corner-turn assistance** (the grid, determinism and "a short tap is exactly one tile" are unchanged). Every assisted level (touch and "On"; keyboard / gamepad under Auto) now: plans a turn up to 2 tiles ahead and carries the player along the corridor to the first opening; remembers a turn tapped while walking for 24 ticks after the finger came off; buffers a press 14 ticks (the slowest step is 13) instead of 10; and has a bump guard: a direction the sim already refused (wall, crate) is offered again only after 24 ticks, at once if the obstacle is gone, and a fresh press is never swallowed. Touch / "On" also follow the bend of a one-tile-wide corridor (never round a crate, never into a room corner, never back where they came from, never into a danger). The keyboard keeps its precision: no sidestep and no bend following. "Off" is exactly the old behaviour. | `input/moveAssist.ts`, `input/moveGate.ts`, `tests/corner-movement.test.ts`, `e2e/corner-movement.spec.ts` |
| MC2 | **A/B cluster like a physical controller**: A is the lower-left button of the cluster, B diagonally above and to the right of it (any later button would continue the arc up and right; there are two today). Round, domed, lit face buttons with a printed letter and a label, no panel behind them; pressed / holding / remote / disabled states keep the same look; the cluster stays 12 px clear of the right edge and the safe areas, buttons >= 44 px. | `ui/styles.css`, `e2e/touch-layout.spec.ts` |
| MC3 | **Larger action buttons that name their action** (founder correction 2026-11-01; the supplied controller image is used for POSITION only, never for size). Landscape phones: first action 23vh, second 20.5vh (was 19 / 17), a gap of about 3vh (>= 8 px) between them, 12 px clear of the edge and the safe areas; the control strips grew to 36vh (`PHONE_CONTROL_STRIP` 0.36, was 0.32) so the board's near corners stay clear of them (the board is about 4 % narrower than under MC2). Portrait / tablet: the cluster is 60vh / 56vw (max 320 px). The buttons show the action's own name and icon, never a controller letter: GENERATOR with the generator icon (HOLD: BOOM! when a remote is waiting), and the second action's own label (the fighter's ability PUSH / DAZE / SHOVE, or the vehicle name with its voucher icon). The position is the verified arrangement: the first action lower-left, the second diagonally above and to the right; no panel behind them; pressed / holding / remote / disabled and the spectator THROW button are unchanged. Keyboard and gamepad bindings are untouched. | `ui/TouchControls.tsx`, `ui/styles.css`, `render/cameraRig.ts`, `e2e/touch-layout.spec.ts` |
| MC4 | **Button labels are sized from the button, not the screen** (founder visual review 2026-11-01: GENERATOR was about 95 px wide inside a 90 px button at 844x390 and also wider than the button at 568x320, so the round edge clipped it). Each action button is a size container (`container-type: size`) and its label is `12cqw` with tight tracking (0.03em) and no wrapping, so the word is a fixed share of the circle at every size the button is drawn at (the old vh-based font sizes stay only as a fallback for a browser without container units). Sizes, positions, the icon and the names (GENERATOR, PUSH / DAZE / SHOVE, DANFO / YELLOW CAB / KEKE, THROW, HOLD: BOOM!) are unchanged. `e2e/touch-layout.spec.ts` now checks that every corner of every text run and icon lies inside the round face (circle minus the 3 px border and a 2 px margin) at all five landscape sizes and in every state (ability, each vehicle, remote line), and that the label is at least 7 px. | `ui/styles.css`, `e2e/touch-layout.spec.ts` |

## MV - Touchscreen diagonal corner movement (branch `claude/mobile-diagonal-corner-input-v1`, from `f072484`) - founder brief of 2026-11-01

| #   | Decision | Where |
| --- | -------- | ----- |
| MV3 | **A diagonal thumb on the touchscreen stick is two cardinal grid moves, never an analogue or a diagonal step.** When the stick is what is held, points diagonally (the existing `wish`, MV1) and assisted movement is Auto-on-touch or On, the gate plans the WHOLE route from the player's tile before moving: vertical first (up-left Up -> Left, up-right Up -> Right, down-left Down -> Left, down-right Down -> Right, whichever axis the thumb leans on most), the reverse order only if that route is blocked. A route is valid only when every step is a plain, safe step (`canStep`: floor, no crate / wall / grounded generator, no flame, blast footprint, warned hazard or vehicle route), the intermediate tile included; if neither order is valid nothing diagonal happens and the previous cardinal behaviour carries on unchanged (main-axis step, early-turn carry, sidestep, bend following, bump guard). A started route is atomic: the second step is owed to the next ready tick and survives the thumb lifting or swinging away; it is dropped (the player stands on the intermediate tile) only if the player is no longer on it or its next tile has since turned solid or dangerous. A held diagonal repeats whole routes at the player's own step cadence (after the usual 12-tick repeat delay), always in the same order, so open floor gives a regular staircase and a wall ends it; it replaces the old alternating-wish staircase wherever a route is valid. Cardinal taps stay exactly one tile. The sim, keyboard, gamepad and "Off" are untouched: `GateAssist.diagonal` is set only for the touch device (`InputManager.gateAssist`), so a key, a D-pad or a stale stick diagonal cannot trigger it. Visible consequence: a diagonal FLICK is now two tiles when a route is valid (was one main-axis tile). | `input/moveAssist.ts` `planDiagonal`, `input/moveGate.ts` `startRoute` / `finishRoute`, `input/InputManager.ts`, `tests/mobile-diagonal-corner.test.ts` |
| MI1 | **Touch presses are kept through a lock.** A press made while the player cannot act (dazed, pothole stumble, oil slide, trapdoor arrival, a vehicle entrance) is no longer spent on a tick the simulation ignores: it is kept (newest press wins, at most 90 ticks from the press) and handed over on the first tick the player can step (measured before: a tap in a daze moved nobody; after: within 1-2 ticks of the daze ending). Touch device at the full assist level only; keyboard and gamepad behave exactly as before (`tests/mobile-input-reliability.test.ts` section 7). | `input/moveGate.ts` `LOCK_BUFFER_TICKS`, `input/InputManager.ts` `locked`, `sim/systems/queries.ts` `movementLocked` |
| MI2 | **Newest direction wins over an owed diagonal step.** A touch press whose direction is neither step of the owed MV3 route cancels the route (the first step is already committed; the second is dropped), so a flick followed quickly by the opposite tap performs the tap at the end of the first step (10 ticks) instead of losing it or waiting 20 ticks. A thumb lifting does NOT cancel (a flick is a lift). The second step is still re-validated (position, solid, danger) immediately before it is handed over; the reverse order (horizontal first) is still tried when the vertical-first route is blocked. | `input/moveGate.ts` `press` / `finishRoute`, `input/moveAssist.ts` `planDiagonal` |
| MI3 | **The direction-switch settle stays at 2 ticks.** Measured: it costs one tick (about 17 ms), a 1-tick settle already blocks a 1-tick swing through a third direction but not a 2-tick one, so 2 is the shortest value that covers a normal swing; it was never the cause of a freeze. It is skipped only for an emergency (MI4). | `input/moveAssist.ts` `SWITCH_SETTLE_TICKS`, `tests/mobile-input-reliability.test.ts` section 8 |
| MI4 | **Emergency escape.** A player standing in danger (a generator's blast footprint, a flame, a warned hazard) skips the settle, an owed route is ended by a thumb that has left its directions, and the assist may step THROUGH a generator's footprint (never onto a lethal tile, never to a tile nearer an armed generator): before this the assist refused every tile of the footprint and switched itself off exactly when escaping (a diagonal flick under your own generator went one tile; it now goes two). | `input/moveAssist.ts` `hazardSets` / `walkable`, `input/moveGate.ts` |
| MI5 | **Input trace.** `?debug` or `?trace` records, in a bounded ring, the raw touch event, the quantised direction and diagonal wish, the gate's decision with its reason (`buffered-while-locked`, `route-cancelled-by-newer-press`, `settle-bypassed-emergency`, `bump-guard` ...) and what the simulation did with it (stepped, blocked, ignored because the player could not act); `__WAHALA__.inputTrace().dump()`. Debug only; the simulation never reads it. | `input/trace.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts` |
| CB1 | **Three different close attacks.** Public names: Punch (Street Jab, `push`), Shove (`shove`), Daze Punch (`daze`). Each is data in `config.skills.attacks`: startup, whiff recovery, hit recovery, per-target lockout, displacement, daze (ticks). Punch: 4, 12, 60, 180, no push, 30 (0.5 s daze). Shove: 12, 36, 120, 180, 2 tiles, no daze. Daze Punch: 18, 30, 240, 240, no push, 60 (1 s daze, then 2 s immunity). Before, Push and Shove were the same code with 4 s and 3 s cooldowns (Shove strictly better). | `sim/config.ts`, `sim/systems/skills.ts`, `sim/data/skills.ts` |
| CB2 | **Rules shared by the three.** B starts the attack; it takes effect after its startup against whoever is adjacent THEN (a rival who stepped away is a whiff); a whiff costs only the whiff recovery, a hit the hit recovery (the attacker's global recovery: any OTHER rival is hittable after it); nothing roots the attacker (walking, placing, everything else stays free); a Shove that cannot move the rival at all (a wall, crate or generator right behind) is a whiff, which is the "exposed" cost; a hit that dazes or displaces cuts the victim's pending wind-up short (a fast jab beats a slow heavy punch); a daze-only attack cannot start on a rival still immune to a daze. `npm run combat-balance` measures them (alone / fleeing / duels / positional): Punch wins plain duels, Daze Punch holds a rival inside a lane that burns, Shove is the only one that drives a rival onto a lane two tiles away (a plain duel is its worst case: its value is the map). No attack dominates another on any axis (`tests/combat-attacks.test.ts`). | `sim/systems/skills.ts`, `scripts/combat-balance.ts` |
| CB3 | **Per-target lockout.** The same attacker cannot affect the same victim again for `targetLockoutTicks` after a hit (longer than the attacker's own recovery, so a chain on one rival is impossible); other rivals, and other attackers, are unaffected. B next to a locked-out or immune rival is not a melee press (it falls through to the next B action). | `Player.meleeLock`, `sim/systems/skills.ts` `attackable` |
| CB4 | **Bots and menus follow.** Bots hold their tile during a melee startup (a step away would make it miss) and judge a shove by where it lands (two tiles); the fighter screens, tutorial text and the B label (PUNCH / SHOVE / DAZE) are generated from the same config. | `ai/skills.ts`, `ai/tactics.ts`, `ui/menu/fighters.ts` |
| SK1 | **Fixed initial kill-streak loadout (no selection menu).** For the nth credited elimination of the current life: 3 QUICK HANDS, 5 VEHICLE GENERATOR, 7 GLOBAL KNOCKDOWN. Earned once per threshold (several kills in one tick pay every threshold crossed, in order), consumed once, and a death clears the streak and every unused reward (QUICK HANDS stops and its extra capacity goes with it). Match kills, deaths and assists are never reduced; the respawn is the normal one (spawn protection, base upgrades). On in every City Rumble ruleset (`RUMBLE_ECONOMY`), off in Classic. Assists advance nothing and pay nothing (documented future work; the audit's per-assist charge is NOT implemented). The existing elimination ladder (`config.rewards`) is unchanged and runs alongside. | `sim/config.ts` `streaks`, `sim/systems/streaks.ts` |
| SK2 | **QUICK HANDS.** 18 s (tuning start: 15-20 s), +5 generator charges while it lasts (base 2 -> 7), a held A lays a generator on every tile the fighter steps onto (`Intent.placeHeld`, touch label GENERATOR / HOLD: LAY), a placement press that cannot be done (dazed, tile taken, no charge) is kept 1.2 s and done as soon as it can be (never silently ignored), the fighter's other upgrades are untouched, the normal fuse / flames / owner vulnerability apply, so mistakes still kill. Without it a held A places nothing extra and a press in a daze is spent as before. | `sim/systems/streaks.ts` `quickHandsPlacement`, `input/InputManager.ts`, `input/specialHold.ts` |
| SK3 | **Why not the weak versions.** Not a "three-generator frenzy", not a short armour substitute, not an invulnerability, not cosmetic: QUICK HANDS is more charges, no lost presses and hold-to-lay for 18 s; the owner can still blow themselves up. | this table |
| SK4 | **HUD.** Acquisition, active and consumed states are shown in the HUD rail beside the board (`StreakStatus`, toasts with the reward icon): name, icon, seconds left and a draining bar and generator charges for QUICK HANDS, the vehicle name for a VEHICLE GENERATOR, READY for GLOBAL KNOCKDOWN, kills to the next one; notices EARNED / USED / OVER / STREAK LOST, and what the others see (a vehicle with a generator, LOCAL CHAMPION). The action buttons name the action (GENERATOR + HOLD: LAY; the vehicle's name + GENERATOR; KNOCKDOWN), never a letter. Nothing over the board, timer, portraits or controls (`e2e/streaks.spec.ts`, day and night). | `app/hud.ts` `buildStreakHud`, `ui/StreakStatus.tsx`, `ui/specialLabel.ts`, `ui/TouchControls.tsx` |
| VG1 | **Vehicles are public, not per character** (founder direction 2026-11-02; amends the Bible's "exactly three signature vehicles" and "no Okada attack vehicle"). `CharacterDef.vehicle` is gone. A voucher draws its vehicle when picked up (`Player.voucherKind`) and a VEHICLE GENERATOR when earned, from `config.vehicles.allowed` with the match RNG: danfo, taxi, keke everywhere, okada where the arena allows it (the shared Rumble stage config does). The Okada has its own spec (6 ticks per tile, no crates, gone after one rival), horn / engine / launch / pass sounds (synthesised) and a model. The Okada RIDER SHIELD (the mounted pickup) is unchanged. Tutorials always use a Danfo. | `sim/types.ts` `VehicleKind`, `sim/config.ts` `vehicles`, `sim/systems/vehicles.ts`, `sim/arenas/rumbleKit.ts` |
| VG2 | **VEHICLE GENERATOR timeline.** The vehicle's route is chosen by the same deterministic scorer as a voucher (never over the owner); the existing 1.2 s lane warning runs; when the vehicle starts driving a live generator is mounted on it and its WHOLE 3 s fuse starts (180 ticks); at 2 s (120 ticks) the carrier lets go and the generator is thrown at the rival the route passes nearest (re-picked at release if they are gone; never the owner or a teammate; a throw is at most 10 tiles), flight 40 ticks, a landing footprint shown from the release; it goes off 3 s after launch (measured: release 120 ticks, boom 180, every vehicle). A carrier that leaves the board or crashes releases at once; the fuse does not change. The carrier never rams (it is a carrier: a slower step per vehicle) and uses no generator capacity. | `sim/systems/vehicleGenerator.ts`, `sim/systems/vehicles.ts` |
| VG3 | **Readability.** The carrier is a vehicle with a pulsing pink roof beacon and a live countdown generator on its roof; its warning lane is pink (never the ram vehicles' amber / red, never ambient traffic, which is off the board); the release is a projectile arc and a pink ring plus a footprint on the ground, a horn / launch sound and a throw whoosh, and an "INCOMING" notice for everyone else. | `render/views/VehicleViews.ts`, `GeneratorViews.ts`, `OverlayViews.ts` |
| VG4 | **Owner safety.** The route never covers the owner, the rival is never the owner or a teammate, the landing prefers a tile whose footprint spares the owner, and the generator's flames are `ownerSafe`: the owner is never caught by this blast even if they walk into it (every other fighter is; the kill is credited to the owner). A plain voucher vehicle can still hit its owner (unchanged). | `sim/systems/lethality.ts`, `sim/systems/explosions.ts` |
| GK1 | **GLOBAL KNOCKDOWN / LOCAL CHAMPION.** One press throws an object from the building side at EVERY rival (never the owner or a teammate): 36 ticks of flight (visible, with a landing ring and a whistle), then each rival is hit on the head and lies flat for 2 s (120 ticks; tuning range 1.5-3 s is tested) - cannot move, place a generator, attack or use B - while the owner can do all of it; nobody dies of it. A rival who has got up is immune to another knockdown for 4 s (no stacking); one use; lost on death. Measured follow-up (150 matches per row, Standard bots, `npm run streak-measure`): 2 s is worth +0.27 extra kills per use over the same match without it (0.57 vs 0.31 in the next 10 s), 9 % of uses kill two and none kill three; 1.5 s +0.16, 2.5 s and 3 s +0.33; the thrower dies in 16 % of 2 s uses (21 % at 1.5 s, 7 % at 3 s). Strong (a fallen rival loses two of the generator's three seconds) but never an automatic multi-kill. | `sim/systems/knockdown.ts`, `render/views/KnockdownViews.ts`, `scripts/streak-measure.ts` |

## CR - The seven City Rumble communities (branch `claude/wahala-city-rumble-map-only-v1`, from `8ebf3f4`) - founder correction of 2026-11-02

Source: _WAHALA Canonical Lagos City Rumble Map Build Bible v2_ (30 October 2026) and its image manifest. This is a map-only change: no vehicle, input or combat rule is touched. Report: `docs/CITY_RUMBLE_RESEARCHED_ARENAS.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | -------- | ----- |
| CR1 | **City Rumble has exactly seven arenas: Ojuelegba (the reference, untouched) and the six researched areas of Bible v2 - Oshodi (kept at 63 x 47), Mushin, Fadeyi, Lagos Island, Lekki, Ebute-Metta.** Menu order: Ojuelegba, Oshodi, Mushin, Fadeyi, Lagos Island, Lekki, Ebute-Metta. The saved default Rumble stage stays Oshodi. The nine standard (2-4 player) arenas are not replaced and share no layout with them (tested). | `sim/arenas/index.ts`, `tests/rumble-researched.test.ts` |
| CR2 | **The generic Rumble maps are removed, not hidden:** the original abstract "City Rumble" district (`cityRumble`), the generic Apapa docks (`rumbleApapa`; Apapa is not one of the six approved areas and the brief forbids a generic port map), the generic mirrored "Lagos Island Markets" grid (replaced in place under the same id, as Oshodi was in LA1) and the generic "Lekki Nightworks" roadworks grid (replaced by the researched Lekki under the new id `rumbleLekki`). Their art, previews, screenshots, menu moods, obstacle styles, audio keys and tests are gone or re-keyed. Tests that used `cityRumble` as a generic fixture now use a researched board (`RUMBLE_FIXTURE`). | `docs/CITY_RUMBLE_RESEARCHED_ARENAS.md` |
| CR3 | **Communities are drawn from one source.** `sim/arenas/community/painter.ts` paints a board from named object letters; the same canvas gives the layout ('#' under each letter) and the `CoverRect` list the renderer draws (`ArenaDef.cover`, presentation data the sim never reads), so cover and layout cannot drift (tested for every '#' tile). `community/arena.ts` adds the shared Rumble rules, explicit summoned-vehicle lanes, deterministic starts (a farthest-point search over the preferred start's neighbours, kept-clear plazas allowed), supplies mirrored per half, and the two mandatory endgame trap waves inside the core. | `sim/arenas/community/*` |
| CR4 | **Boards (not mirrored, six or five named districts each, Bible ids):** Mushin 53 x 39 (M1-M6), Fadeyi 51 x 33 (F1-F5, deliberately the compact one), Lagos Island 55 x 39 (L1-L6), Lekki 57 x 39 (K1-K6, Phase 1 + Ikate only), Ebute-Metta 55 x 39 (E1-E6). Hazards use existing styles only (traffic and delivery-truck waves on the arterials, a BRT-lane wave and a bay departure at Fadeyi, the railcar at Ebute-Metta between solid portals with open walkway beyond them, shopfront goods, a mild landward wash at the water edges, construction debris at Lekki). No new rule, hazard style or sim system. | `sim/arenas/rumble*.ts` |
| CR5 | **Cultural and heritage protection:** the Lagos Island cathedral is a backdrop outside the board, the Freedom Park wall, the heritage frontage and the monument are permanent cover that cannot be blasted, the Ebute-Metta terminus and heritage house are permanent cover; no market fire, no victim or event re-enactment, no real brand: every sign is fictional (place names and plain station wording only), no person appears. Future one-player boss slots are reserved metadata only (`ArenaDef.futureBoss`, nothing reads it). | `render/arenas/*Art.ts` |
| CR6 | **Spawn spread:** the generic Rumble bar (8 / 16 / 32 starts at least 12 / 8 / 4-5 tiles apart) holds for every community except Fadeyi (12 / 7 / 3): the highway takes 40% of its open floor, and the Bible says it "should not be inflated". Written into `tests/rumble-stages.test.ts`, not hidden. | `tests/rumble-stages.test.ts` |
| CR7 | **Music and voices:** nothing was added. The founder's Lagos Island and Oshodi tracks keep their ids; the Lekki Night assignment moves to `rumbleLekki`; the retired Apapa track stays in the manifest, unassigned, until the founder assigns it; Mushin, Fadeyi and Ebute-Metta use the default playlist and join no voice zone until the founder assigns them (voice-zone membership of the retired ids was removed). | `public/audio/music/manifest.json`, `public/audio/voices/voices.json` |
| CR8 | **Day and night:** every researched community ships a night look on the same geometry (`?tod=night`, `community/looks.ts`, floor of ground light tested at 30% of the day's); Ojuelegba keeps its single look. | `render/arenas/community/looks.ts` |
| CR9 | **Integration: the seven Rumble stages keep the public-vehicle list.** Merging the streaks / vehicles branch (VG1) onto the map-only branch exposed that every Rumble arena replaced the shared stage's whole `vehicles` config with `{ lanes }`, so the Okada (`vehicles.allowed`) silently vanished from all seven stages. The stages now spread the shared stage config's `vehicles` and add their own lanes (Ojuelegba, Oshodi, the five community stages). No layout, lane or hazard changed. `tests/vehicle-generator.test.ts` asserts every Rumble stage allows the Okada and has lanes. | `sim/arenas/community/arena.ts`, `rumbleOshodi.ts`, `rumbleOjuelegba.ts` |

## AS - Anti-snowball balance patch (branch `claude/wahala-balance-patch-v1`, from `5c19a4d`) - founder brief of 2026-11-03

Experimental patch, no map change, no redesign. Perks: `src/sim/systems/lifePerks.ts`; the ladder in `src/sim/config.ts`; tests `tests/life-perks.test.ts`. Movement: `src/input/moveGate.ts`, `tests/mobile-corner-assist.test.ts`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AS1 | **Kill-earned power is a temporary life perk.** Every upgrade the elimination ladder pays lasts **12 s** (`rewards.perks.lifeTicks` = 720 ticks) from the kill that granted it. A newer grant of THAT perk (a second range step, the same reward again) moves its expiry; nothing else is refreshed, and a perk the reward did not pay keeps its own clock. When a perk ends, exactly the steps it gave are undone (range, generators, speed, tool, Square Blast, Armour or the Okada, the Remote Detonator; generators still waiting for the remote trigger go off on an ordinary 3 s fuse). A shield (Armour / Okada) that took its hit frees its slots at once. A death clears every perk (respawn modes reset the numbers too); kills, deaths and assists on the scoreboard are never touched. Survival uses the same rules (it has no respawn, so a death is final). | `lifePerks.ts`, `rewards.ts`, `respawn.ts`, `tick.ts` |
| AS2 | **Five slots, one perk per category, one large upgrade per reward.** A fighter has at most **5 life slots**; **Armour, the Okada and the Remote Detonator are major and take 2**, every other perk takes 1. Categories: range, capacity, speed, tool, Square Blast, shield (**Armour OR Okada, never both**) and detonation: one perk each, a second grant in a category is another step of the same perk up to its ceiling. Ceilings from rewards: **bomb range at most 2 steps above the base** (base 2 -> 4), **speed 1 step**, **Square Blast 1 tier**, **Armour 1 plate**, **3 active generators** (`generator.maxCapacity` 3 in Rumble: base 2 + one Extra Socket). A new perk that does not fit pushes out the OLDEST perk (never one the same reward just paid, and a perk pushed out is not paid again in that reward), so the newest kill always pays and the total never passes five. **One large upgrade per kill reward** (Armour, Okada, Remote Detonator, Square Blast) and **never a Remote Detonator together with a Vehicle Voucher**: enforced when a reward is resolved, and the shipped ladder was reshaped to match (rungs 5-7: `remote, socket` / `okada, petrol` / `voucher, skates, socket`). The Vehicle Voucher is a one-use item, not a perk (no slot, no expiry). The Remote Detonator does not lift the generator cap (it never did: remote generators count as active, and a test pins it). | `lifePerks.ts`, `rewards.ts`, `config.ts` |
| AS3 | **Placement interval.** The least time between two generators laid by the same fighter is **10 ticks (0.17 s)** in City Rumble (`generator.placeIntervalTicks`, 0 in Classic): a walker (10 ticks a tile) is not held back, an Okada rider (8 ticks a tile, 7 with Roller Skates) lays a generator every other tile at most. **QUICK HANDS is exempt** from the interval and from the three-generator cap (its +5 charges, SK2, are its own 18 s exception; say so if it should be capped too). | `generators.ts`, `config.ts` |
| AS4 | **Touchscreen movement: Standard and Beginner Assist** (new setting `moveStyle`, default Standard; keyboard and gamepad ignore it). Open space is cardinal: **no staircase, no diagonal route**, a lean alone never turns a walk. **Standard** = cardinal movement + limited corner help: only when the stick's main direction is BLOCKED (a wall, crate or obstacle) and the thumb leans round it, the two legal orders of the two-step corner are tried (the first valid one wins; whole route validated first, safe tiles only), the second step is taken only while the thumb is still down on the route's directions, a newer direction cancels it; plus the early-turn carry while the turn is still held. **Beginner Assist** adds: a press kept while dazed / sliding (MI1, 90 ticks), the longer 14-tick press buffer, a tapped turn remembered 24 ticks after the finger lifts, a slide along a wall to the single opening and corridor-bend following (never against a lean), and the emergency rules (MI4: a press settles at once and a step may cross a generator's blast area when the player already stands in it). Nothing, in either style, moves the player without a current directional input, picks a path with no direction held, or moves away from a generator by itself. Amends MV3 (broad diagonal routes), MI1 and MI4 (now Beginner Assist only) and the wish alternation of MV1 (removed). The 2-tick direction-switch settle stays in both styles (it only filters a swipe through the stick centre). | `moveGate.ts`, `InputManager.ts`, `moveAssist.ts`, `app/settings.ts`, `ui/menu/SettingsScreen.tsx` |
| AS5 | **Armour and the Okada ("caddy") are defensive extra lives, not temporary perks** (founder correction 2026-11-04; amends AS1, AS2, SK1, SK2 and GK1/VG). A shield absorbs ONE otherwise lethal explosion or hit (the Okada first, then Armour, one layer per tick - unchanged `applyLethalHits`), is spent, and leaves the fighter ALIVE; spending it is not a death and touches nothing else (other perks, streak rewards, scoreboard). A shield never expires (no 12 s clock), takes no perk slot and is never pushed out by newer perks; one shield at a time as before (Armour OR Okada from rewards). Only an ACTUAL death (`eliminate`, in every kill-economy mode) ends the temporary perks: extra generators, range, speed, tool, Square Blast, the Remote Detonator (its waiting generators go off on an ordinary fuse) and the Vehicle Voucher. The earned one-use kill-streak rewards (VEHICLE GENERATOR, GLOBAL KNOCKDOWN) are KEPT through a death until used or the match ends (QUICK HANDS: see AS7); a reward already held is not paid again when the streak is earned a second time, and a respawn pays nothing. Kills, deaths and assists are never touched. Slots: only the Remote Detonator is major (2); the five slots hold the offensive perks. | `lifePerks.ts` (`endLifePerksOnDeath`), `lethality.ts` `eliminate`, `streaks.ts` `clearStreakRewards` / `earn`, `tests/shield-and-death.test.ts` |
| AS6 | **Sudden Death: shields decide, two blasts, then the FINAL anti-stalemate blast.** The round is unchanged up to the first blast (60 s after the title card, an arena-wide hit through spawn protection): a contender without Armour or the Okada is eliminated, one with a shield survives and the shield is spent (and keeps everything else). If more than one side still stands, blast 2 follows after **15 s** (was 30 s) under the same rule. If both sides still stand after blast 2, the round enters its FINAL lethal phase: **no respawns, no pickups and no kill rewards**, floor pickups are swept, and the next blast (15 s later) is `terminal` - it kills through Armour and the Okada - so the round ends by the third blast: one clear kill leader wins, a still-shared lead is the draw `suddenDeathExhausted`. One side left at any point wins. Sudden death also stops respawns for the whole round. | `suddenDeath.ts` (`finalPhase`), `config.ts` `suddenDeath.blastDelayTicks`, `respawn.ts`, `pickups.ts`, `rewards.ts`, `tests/sudden-death.test.ts`, `tests/shield-and-death.test.ts` |
| AS7 | **Every earned kill-streak reward survives death until it is used or the match ends** (founder correction 2026-11-04; amends AS5, SK1, SK2). QUICK HANDS was modelled only as a timed buff that started the moment it was earned (`quickHandsUntil`), with no stored "unused" state, so a death lost it. It now has one: the earned reward is 18 s of QUICK HANDS time. It still starts when earned (the locked SK2 behaviour is unchanged). A death PAUSES a running buff on purpose (its +5 generator capacity goes with the death) and stores the unspent time in `StreakRewards.quickHandsBank`; the respawn resumes exactly that remainder (`streakRewardResumed`, HUD "QUICK HANDS BACK"), never a new 18 s, and time already used is not refunded. A QUICK HANDS that had fully run out before the death has nothing to give back. VEHICLE GENERATOR and GLOBAL KNOCKDOWN stay as they are. A reward already held (running, stored or unused) is never paid a second time (this also fixes a double +5 capacity when it was re-earned while running), the HUD does not offer a held reward as the "next" one, and a respawn pays nothing. Surviving through a shield is not a death: the buff keeps running untouched. `streakRewardsLost` is gone (no reward is lost any more). | `streaks.ts` (`clearStreakRewards`, `updateStreaks`, `resumeQuickHands`), `types.ts` `StreakRewards.quickHandsBank`, `app/hud.ts`, `tests/streak-rewards.test.ts` |
| AS6a | **The third Sudden Death blast is the final anti-stalemate rule.** After blast 2, if both sides still stand: no pickups, no rewards and no respawns return (floor pickups are swept, kill rewards and the pickup collector are off, the fallen stay out), and the third blast, 15 s later, kills THROUGH Armour and the Okada, so the round ALWAYS ends: one clear kill leader wins, a still-shared lead is the `suddenDeathExhausted` draw. Blasts 1 and 2 follow the shield rule (a shield is spent and the fighter survives); only the third ignores shields. | `suddenDeath.ts` `finalPhase` / `terminal`, `tests/sudden-death.test.ts`, `tests/shield-and-death.test.ts` |


## MS - Campaign mission runtime (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `mission-sim`)

A mission is an ordinary match (`GameOptions.mission`); `tick` calls the runtime only when `state.mission` exists, so normal matches are unchanged (hash-pinned in `tests/mission-regression.test.ts`). Report and numbers: `docs/MISSION_SIM.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | -------- | ----- |
| MS1 | **A mission is a new `GameKind` `'mission'`**, not a Rumble mode: the Rumble economy (radial blasts, two generators, elimination rewards, streaks) on the district's City Rumble stage, no Wahala Crush, no random hidden pickups (the marked `$` supply crates keep their basic upgrade, as on every Rumble stage), respawn on but granted only to the human, a crate density of 0.28 (the Rumble stages use 0.40-0.46) and a 3 x 3 open patch around every node and post so objectives are never walled in. Stage hazards and the mandatory endgame traps stay on (they are the arena's identity). The match end belongs to the mission runtime (boss, lives, clock), never to kills or the Rumble clock. | `modes.ts`, `mission/build.ts`, `mission/state.ts`, `systems/match.ts` |
| MS2 | **Strikes are telegraphed and never unavoidable.** A lethal mission strike warns at least 2.5 s (150 ticks), a rail window's train at least 3.5 s (the `train` stage style); it is only called when the human could walk off its tiles in `warn - 12` ticks (`escapeSteps`, the stage.ts departure rule); at most 3 are alive; a landing is postponed (bounded: 5 x 24 ticks) while the human cannot move (daze, knockdown, slide, trapdoor). Replay modifiers can never take a warning under the floor. | `mission/strikes.ts`, `mission/tuning.ts`, `tests/mission-oshodi.test.ts` |
| MS3 | **Strikes are real flames owned by the boss seat** (`ownerSafe`, plus a `BlastEvent`), so lethality, Armour / Okada, spawn protection, elimination credit and the renderer's fire are the existing systems; the boss and crew are team 1 against the human's team 0 (friendly fire off), so a strike only ever hurts the human. | `mission/strikes.ts`, `mission/build.ts` |
| MS4 | **Bot levels.** The crew plays at `MissionSpec.difficulty` (default Rookie on the first clear); the boss at that level but never below Standard. | `mission/build.ts` |
| MS5 | **Crew roles are directives, not new rules.** The sim turns an NPC role into an optional `Observation.directive` (guard / block / chase / run / escort / support, plus `flee` for the exposed boss); `ai/directive.ts` only changes the hunt field, its weight (at least 0.5 per tile) and a leash penalty, so a directed bot obeys the same rules and sees only `observe()` output. Normal matches pass no directive and take none of the new code paths. | `mission/directives.ts`, `ai/directive.ts`, `ai/brain.ts`, `ai/goals.ts` |
| MS6 | **The shielded boss is a mission flag, not spawn protection.** `lethality.ts` skips a hit on the boss while `boss.shielded`; `protectedUntil` is not used because the respawn rules end spawn protection when its owner places a generator (the boss places generators all the time). | `mission/rules.ts`, `systems/lethality.ts` |
| MS7 | **Boss window and retry loop.** The third phase's goal exposes the boss for 25 s; eliminating it while exposed wins (`defeat: 'eliminated'`). A window that runs out re-shields the boss and phase 3 starts again (`phasesCleared` back to 2); at most 3 windows in all, then the mission is lost. The exposed boss flees the human. A boss defeat on the same tick as the human's last life is a win (the locked rule "simultaneous final deaths = draw" is for standard matches). | `mission/runtime.ts` |
| MS8 | **Lives and the crew.** The human has 3 lives (spec `lives`): each elimination spends one, they respawn after 3 s with spawn protection (upgrades reset, as in the respawn modes) until the last is gone (mission lost). The crew never respawns; explicit reinforcements (3 s warning on the entry tile, at the start of phases 2 and 3 and after a missed boss window, at most 3 per mission) bring back a fallen **runner or escort** only. | `systems/respawn.ts`, `mission/runtime.ts` |
| MS9 | **Only the human earns elimination rewards** in a mission (`missionRewardEligible`): a boss or crew bot that eliminates the human gets credit and stats but no Armour / perks / streak rewards, so the exposed boss cannot soak the finishing blast and crew power cannot snowball. Perk, shield and death rules for the human are unchanged. | `systems/rewards.ts`, `systems/streaks.ts` |
| MS10 | **Replay modifiers** are one numeric tweak of their own mission each: rushHourDispatch pulse x 0.75; marketDay restock x 0.7 and one extra runner; tollSeason hold x 1.5; expressTimetable window x 0.7; auditWeek one more ledger per phase and the clock x 0.85; longBlackout sequence one longer per phase. Each is tested to change its number; a modifier of another mission is ignored. | `mission/tuning.ts`, `tests/mission-runtime.test.ts` |
| MS11 | **Clocks and stars.** Clocks 7 min (Ebute-Metta 8 min), exposure 25 s, par 4-5.5 min. Stars: 1 cleared, 2 at most one life lost, 3 also within the par time. A timeout is `draw / timeout`; a lost mission is a `win` for the boss seat. First values, untuned against real play. | `mission/defs.ts`, `mission/runtime.ts` |
| MS12 | **Six verbs, three rising phases, no waves.** Oshodi JAM relays (progress drains when you leave; live relays call convoy strikes; jams re-arm, runners re-arm from phase 2; phase 3 = all jammed inside 40 s), Mushin DESTROY parts stacks with blasts (market runners restock), Fadeyi / Somolu HOLD junction zones (contested by any marshal inside; held ones stop their strikes and open routes), Ebute-Metta HIT dispatch switches only inside rail windows, Lagos Island COLLECT and DELIVER ledgers (slower while carrying; a daze or fall drops it), Lekki SWITCH OFF in the signalled order (a wrong switch calls a strike and resets). Only existing intents: standing on a tile is the interaction. | `mission/objectives/*`, `docs/MISSION_SIM.md` |
| MS13 | **Ebute-Metta's windows are its trains.** The arena's own scheduled `train` hazards are switched off for the mission; each window cycle pushes the arena's real `train` `StageEvent` (so the existing stage code runs it and the renderer draws it), checked escapable like the stage departures, and the dispatch window opens when the lane is clear. | `mission/objectives/windows.ts`, `mission/state.ts` |
| MS14 | **Node tiles are data, validated.** Every node and post is an open '.' / '_' tile of the district's layout (no road, track, crate or concrete) reachable from the human spawn (`tests/mission-nodes.test.ts`); the runtime clears crates on and around them at the start. | `mission/defs.ts`, `tests/mission-nodes.test.ts` |
## FC - Fighters, the attack catalogue and Local Champion (worker `fighters-combat`, from `321cc5a`) - lead brief of 2026-11-04

Reference: `docs/FIGHTERS_AND_ATTACKS.md`. Measured with `npm run combat-balance` and `npm run streak-measure -- --only local`. The deployed rules are unchanged by default (the three fighters keep one attack with the CB1-CB4 numbers, the ladder stays 3 / 5 / 7, shields and death rules are untouched).

| ID | Decision | Files |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FC1 | **Step grace; retry and lockout audited.** A step commits its destination at once, so a rival whose step began during an attack's startup was a whiff while the board still showed them on the tile; the existing "pick another adjacent rival" rule cannot help a single rival (one orthogonal step never keeps adjacency). An attack now still connects while the rival has not visually crossed `skills.stepGrace.maxShare` (0.5) of the step; past half the step they are missed, so dodging still works (the Daze Punch's 18-tick startup stays dodgeable, tested). Diagonal-adjacent forgiveness was NOT added (the step grace covers the case; no new rule). A shove from the grace position pushes from where the rival now is. Rapid retry: the Punch's whiff recovery is 12 ticks (0.2 s, 3x its startup): already short, not changed (a test pins it is not below the telegraph). Lockout audit: `targetLockoutTicks` is per (attacker, victim) for every attack and at least the attacker's own hit recovery, so one attacker cannot chain a victim (tested for all eight attacks: an unbroken 30 s B stream lands at most `ceil(30 s / lockout) + 1` hits; the victim loses at most 20 s of 60 s); different attackers can each hit the same victim (by design); daze and slow immunities cap overlapping effects. | `systems/skills.ts` (`reachDir`, `pickTarget`), `config.ts` `skills.stepGrace`, `tests/attack-catalogue.test.ts` |
| FC2 | **`AttackSpec` records the full trade of an attack** (range `reach`, speed `startupTicks`, damage `0`, displacement, daze, recoveries, per-attack `cooldownTicks`, vulnerability `plantedStartup` / `whiffRootTicks` / `hitRootTicks`, `slowTicks` / `slowExtraStepTicks` / `slowImmunityTicks`, `counterDazeTicks`, `interrupts`) and the simulation honours every field: a root stops the attacker taking a step (not placing, not pressing B, not an oil slide), a slow adds step ticks (`playerStepTicks`), a cooldown is spent at the press (a whiff or an interrupted attack uses it too). Every attack is non-lethal: its value is control (`controlValueSeconds`). The original three attacks have plain values for the new fields, so they behave exactly as before (`tests/combat-attacks.test.ts` untouched and green). | `config.ts`, `data/attacks.ts`, `systems/skills.ts`, `systems/queries.ts`, `systems/movement.ts`, `types.ts` |
| FC3 | **Five more attacks, balanced around the three.** Kick (reach 2, shoves 1, 24-tick stagger, 14-tick planted wind-up, 54 whiff, 150 hit), Bottle Strike (9-tick planted wind-up, 128-tick daze, 10 s cooldown spent at the press, 60-tick stuck after a miss), Leg Sweep (15-tick wind-up, no daze, 3 s at 16 instead of 10 ticks per tile, 2 s slow immunity), Headbutt (8-tick wind-up, 55-tick daze, 30-tick recoil after a hit), Elbow Check (11-tick wind-up, 16-tick daze, shoves 1, +60 ticks of daze on a PLANTED or REELING rival). Numbers were tuned with the duel harness (`scripts/lib/duel.ts`); two horizons are asserted: in a 5 s ENGAGEMENT every attack beats one and loses to one, in a 60 s SUSTAINED duel no attack wins all (the Shove wins none: its value is the map, as CB2 said), and no attack is better than another on every spec axis (`dominates`). The original numbers were not touched. Early versions failed the matrix (the Kick lost every duel, the Bottle won every one, a harness bug let fighters overlap): the table in the doc is the final run. | `config.ts` `skills.attacks`, `tests/attack-catalogue.test.ts`, `scripts/combat-balance.ts` |
| FC4 | **B resolution order with secondary attacks (still two buttons).** Adjacent rival: the primary if ready, else the secondary if it is ready and can hit them (a DIFFERENT attack of the loadout waits only `skills.followUpGapTicks` = 30 after the last attack; each attack keeps its own recovery in `attackCooldowns`, a one-attack fighter is unchanged), else B is spent on nothing (never a vehicle or a reward). Nobody adjacent: a ready reach-2 attack on a rival exactly two tiles away in the faced line (open ground between). Then Vehicle Generator, Global Knockdown, Local Champion, Vehicle Voucher, nothing. A press never produces two effects (a 1500-tick mixed-input test). Bots use the same resolver (`chooseAttack`) for a secondary press from a tile safe for its startup. | `systems/actions.ts`, `systems/skills.ts` (`chooseAttack`, `attackReady`), `ai/tactics.ts`, `ai/brain.ts` |
| FC5 | **Fighter definitions; secondaries OFF by default; experimental roster is data only.** One `FighterDef` per fighter (look, cultural styling, movement, attacks, ability, trait, text-only Pidgin lines, introduction, stage, `arena:<id>` soundtrack reference, costumes, finisher id). `loadoutOf(player, config)` reads it live (the character's primary, the secondary only behind the switch); `PlayerSetup.attacks` / `Player.attacks` override it (missions, tests, balance runs). The three deployed fighters keep their ONE attack until `config.skills.secondaryAttacks` is switched on: giving them Kick / Bottle / Sweep changes the deployed game and needs the founder (not decided here). Three more fighters (Danfo Conductor, Market Mama, Jetty Boatman: fictional archetypes) are `status: experimental` data reusing an existing rig; they are not `CharacterId`s, so nothing can select or start them, and `fighterRoster(true)` lists them only behind the `experimentalContent` flag. No audio, TTS, music or recording was added. | `data/fighters.ts`, `state.ts`, `tests/fighters.test.ts` |
| FC6 | **LOCAL CHAMPION: a streak reward separate from the Global Knockdown.** B starts an 8 s window: every 1.5 s the crowd throws at the nearest 2 rivals within 5 tiles of the owner (visible projectile, 48-tick warning, at least the knockdown's 36, landing on a FIXED tile: stepping off it dodges), a hit is a 36-tick daze (no kill, no knockdown, no push, no credit), one hit per rival per use and 5 s immunity after any hit, the owner keeps acting, a death ends the window, an unused reward survives death (AS7). Not on the default ladder: `grantStreakReward(state, id, kind)` (campaign missions) or `streaks.localChampion.ladder` (default OFF) which makes it the 7-kill reward instead of the knockdown. Measured with the same bots and seeds (100 matches): +0.06 extra kills per use against +0.16 for the Global Knockdown, so it is the weaker, dodgeable alternative. Events `localChampionStarted / Throw / Hit / Missed / Ended`; throws share the knockdown projectile list (`landX/landY/throwTicks`) so the renderer needed a 6-line change; bots press it when a rival is within the radius and nobody is adjacent. The Global Knockdown toast now reads GLOBAL KNOCKDOWN (it said LOCAL CHAMPION). | `systems/localChampion.ts`, `systems/streaks.ts` (`grantStreakReward`), `systems/knockdown.ts`, `render/views/KnockdownViews.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts`, `ai/tactics.ts`, `tests/local-champion.test.ts`, `scripts/streak-measure.ts` |
| FC7 | **Scope notes.** Not done and listed in the doc: HUD card / icon / B label for Local Champion and for secondary attacks (HUD worker), art for the five new attacks and the finishing sequences, the experimental fighters' models and costume meshes, any dodge behaviour for bots against the Local Champion's objects. Audio: the five new events reuse existing synthesised cues (`audio/eventRouter.ts`); no new sound exists. | `docs/FIGHTERS_AND_ATTACKS.md` sections 6 and 8 |
## HC - Combat HUD (branch of `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, from `321cc5a`) - presentation only, no rule changed

Model: `src/app/killFeed.ts`, `src/app/combatNotices.ts`, `src/ui/combatHudModel.ts`, `src/ui/missionPanelModel.ts`. Tests: `tests/app/killFeed.test.ts`, `tests/ui/combatHud.test.ts`, `tests/ui/combatHudContrast.test.ts`, `tests/ui/hudRail.test.ts`, `e2e/hud-combat.spec.ts`. Layout and wording: `docs/HUD_COMBAT.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| HC1 | **One kill feed, in the rail, never a toast.** Every elimination and assist notice is a line of the kill feed: "YOU ELIMINATED X", "X ELIMINATED YOU", "A ELIMINATED B", "X WAS ELIMINATED" (nobody credited), "ASSIST ON X", each with a cause word (BLAST, VEHICLE, CRUSH, HAZARD, CROWD, SUDDEN DEATH, OWN BLAST, FRIENDLY FIRE, SIDELINE). Newest first, at most 4 lines, 4 s for the local player's lines and 2.6 s for others'. The old centre toasts ("YOU TOOK OUT", "GOT YOU", "ASSIST ON") and the Rumble panel's own list are gone. With more than 4 competitors (City Rumble) the local player's kills, deaths and assists always show and push other fighters' lines out first; other fighters' lines are low priority and let in at most one per 1.2 s. Lines come from the state (`eliminatedTick`, `elimination`, `eliminatedBy`), assists from the `playerEliminated` event's `assistIds`: no sim change. Placement: standard matches = the rail beside the board; City Rumble = the top-left panel under the counter (the follow camera's centre is the play area); a landscape phone with the touch controls = a strip right of the board, under the right portraits, above the action buttons (`railBoxRight`). Throttle and expiry constants are `KILL_FEED_*` in `killFeed.ts`. | `app/killFeed.ts`, `ui/KillFeed.tsx`, `ui/HudRail.tsx`, `ui/RumbleHud.tsx` |
| HC2 | **A shield that absorbs a lethal hit is announced, and is not a death.** `protectionBroken` (local player) gives "OKADA SAVED YOU" / "ARMOUR USED" (with "N LEFT" while plates remain) as a rail toast; no elimination line is ever produced for it (the feed reads eliminations only). The inventory row carries a shield chip counter "SHIELD xN" (Okada mount + Armour plates, the held layers) that drops with each use. The old floating "OKADA SAVED YOU!" over the board was removed (a spark stays): no text over the board. | `app/combatNotices.ts`, `ui/combatHudModel.ts` `shieldChip`, `ui/Hud.tsx`, `render/GameRenderer.ts` |
| HC3 | **QUICK HANDS is RUNNING, STORED or RESUMED on screen (read-only on AS7).** The rail card says RUNNING with seconds and generators; while the fighter is down in a respawn ruleset it stays as a STORED card with the unspent seconds (`StreakHud.quickHandsStored` from `quickHandsBank`); a death toasts "QUICK HANDS STORED - Ns" and the respawn "QUICK HANDS RESUMED - Ns" (`streakRewardResumed.ticksLeft`). Nothing is "stored" in last-standing rulesets (a buff there would never resume). | `app/hud.ts`, `ui/StreakStatus.tsx`, `ui/combatHudModel.ts` `quickHandsCard` |
| HC4 | **Sudden Death says what it does.** The old title-card line ("Only an Okada or Armour plate survives it") was wrong for the final blast. The card now reads the sim schedule: blasts 1-2 respect an Okada or Armour layer (one each), blast 3 ignores shields. The rail clock is one line, "BLAST 1 OF 3 - SHIELDS RESPECTED - 0:42" (blast 3: "SHIELDS IGNORED"), plus one line about the local contender's own shield. Counts come from `config.suddenDeath.blastDelayTicks` (2 shield blasts + the final one); nothing is hard-coded in the UI. | `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx`, `ui/combatHudModel.ts`, `app/hud.ts` `SuddenDeathHud` |
| HC5 | **Mission panel in the rail.** `HudSnapshot.mission = missionView(state)` (null in every normal match: the panel renders nothing). `missionPanelModel(view)` turns the sim's `MissionView` into phase pips (3), the objective verb, progress bar and text, the callout, the boss status (SHIELDED / EXPOSED with a countdown / DEFEATED), lives left, time and up to 3 warnings (soonest first, seconds, urgent at 3 s or less). The panel is the rail's first combat block (its warnings must never be clipped); the combat block is clipped above the touch move pad, the rest of the rail keeps its height (the arena rule must fit whole). | `ui/MissionPanel.tsx`, `ui/missionPanelModel.ts`, `ui/HudRail.tsx` |


## ON - Online, voice, progression, ads and seasons scaffolds (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `online-scaffold`)

Everything is disabled by default and never required by the static build. Documents: `docs/ONLINE_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/VOICE_CHAT_DESIGN.md`, `docs/PROGRESSION.md`, `docs/ADS_AND_SEASONS.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | -------- | ----- |
| ON1 | **Disabled by default, mock by URL, server by build.** `?mockonline` enables only the in-memory mock; the server transport needs `WAHALA_ONLINE_ENABLED=true` AND `WAHALA_ONLINE_URL` at build time (the build constant `__WAHALA_SERVER_ONLINE__`, computed in `vite.config.ts` by the same rule as `resolveFlags`, false under Vitest). A `'server'` request in a build without the transport returns `null`, never a silent mock. A server build deliberately fails `check:static` and must not go through the Pages publisher. | `platform/flags.ts`, `online/index.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, `tests/online/build-guard.test.ts` |
| ON2 | **The adapter is unreachable from a default build, proved twice**: the dynamic import sits behind the constant (the bundler drops the branch and emits no adapter chunk), `check:static` scans `dist` for socket / WebRTC / microphone / `wss://`, and an import-graph test from `src/main.tsx` asserts nothing reaches `src/online/server/**` or any file naming those APIs. The mock and preview UI are a lazy chunk, so a default user does not download them. | `tests/platform-static.test.ts`, `scripts/check-static-build.mjs` |
| ON3 | **Server-authoritative by construction.** Clients send intents, ready flags and commands only. The wire schema rejects client-supplied positions, kills, results, rewards, XP, ratings, seeds and team numbers (`forbiddenField`); a client can only ASK to switch team. Snapshots are public views (no seed, hidden pickups or rng). Validators are for UX; a real server must re-validate everything (the mock does). | `online/schema.ts`, `online/validate.ts` |
| ON4 | **The mock runs the real simulation behind `MatchService`** (`createGame` + `tick` + `createBot`), logs every accepted input, and a replay from the seed and the log reproduces the live state hash (this is what makes "resync from snapshot + input log" real). Time moves only through `pump` / `step` on an injected clock. | `online/mock/match.ts`, `tests/online/mock-match.test.ts` |
| ON5 | **Reconnect.** A dropped seat is held for a grace window (default 30 s); the resume token is secret, seat-bound and single use (rotated); after the window a bot takes the seat for good (`reconnectExpired`). | `online/mock/match.ts`, `tests/online/mock-reconnect.test.ts` |
| ON6 | **Rooms fill with bots when the ready check starts** (party rooms) or at once (queue rooms); the pure team rules run on the filled roster; teams lock at the ready check; classic rooms hold 4, City Rumble rulesets 8. | `online/mock/lobbies.ts`, `online/lifecycle.ts` |
| ON7 | **Preview "play it yourself" hands a locally simulated final state to the mock server** (`control.adoptLocalMatch`, PREVIEW ONLY) so the normal match path stays untouched; the saved menu settings are never written (an in-memory patch, restored). A real server never accepts a client result. The preview can also let the mock server play the match (8x speed, a bot driving the seat) to show the authoritative flow and the reconnect demo. | `app/online.ts` |
| ON8 | **Progression never touches the local game.** Pure reducers over a profile persisted through the `KeyValueStore` port; recorded only by the online preview / a future server; nothing in sim / ai / input / render / audio / match path / session imports it (tested). XP, rank and season tables are PLACEHOLDER tuning, unbalanced. Campaign rewards are a read-only mirror. | `progression/`, `docs/PROGRESSION.md` |
| ON9 | **Voice: interfaces and policy only.** Default muted, microphone never auto-prompted, team-only voice in team matches, match-wide in free-for-all only with a server flag, ends with the match, none in missions. Only `NullVoiceProvider` ships (always disabled); the UI shows "Voice chat is not available yet" and only when `WAHALA_VOICE_ENABLED` is set. Not production ready. | `voice/`, `docs/VOICE_CHAT_DESIGN.md` |
| ON10 | **Ads: default-deny placement gate, no SDK.** Allowed only on the results screen, between matches and as an opt-in reward; never in a live match, countdown, pause menu, tutorial, campaign briefing, boss intro or cutscene; `adFree` and `adsEnabled` win; `NullAdProvider` shows nothing. | `ads/`, `tests/ads/ads.test.ts` |
| ON11 | **Seasons and cosmetics are data.** Six to eight week seasons with event themes (Christmas, Eid ...), deterministic daily / weekly challenges, fictional cosmetics with rarity and unlock rules; no store, no currency, the renderer does not read the catalogue. | `seasons/`, `cosmetics/` |
| ON12 | **Settings shows the flag states read-only** (Online off / mock / server, Voice, Campaign); the Home "Online (preview)" entry, the menu screen and the controller exist only with `flags().online` (a stale menu state naming the screen falls back to home). | `ui/online/BuildFlags.tsx`, `app/menu.ts` `onlineScreenReady` |

## RR - Raid presentation (branch of `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `raid-render`) - presentation and input only, no rule changed

Documents: `docs/RAID_PRESENTATION.md`. Code: `src/render/views/RaidViews.ts`, `DamageViews.ts`, `ProjectileViews.ts`, `raidStyle.ts`, `damageModel.ts`, `src/render/arenas/raid*.ts`, `src/input/dpad.ts`, `inputMode.ts`, `src/ui/DpadControl.tsx`, `src/app/raidNotices.ts`. Tests: `tests/render/raid-views.test.ts`, `tests/input-raid-cross.test.ts`, `tests/app/raidNotices.test.ts`, `e2e/raid-render.spec.ts`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RR1 | **Raid world markers are read from `raidView(state)` and the events, nothing else.** Cargo, belts, shipments, doors and the exit are drawn from `RaidView`; one-shot reactions (a crate punches on `cargoHit`, debris on `cargoDestroyed`, a door opens on `raidDoorOpened`, the exit grows on `raidExtractionOpened`) from `SimEvent`s. `RaidViews` takes the source as a constructor argument (default: `state.raid` present and `sim.raidView` exported), so it is tested with hand-made fixtures and is a no-op in every other match. | `render/views/RaidViews.ts` |
| RR2 | **Cheap by instancing.** All cargo of a kind is one `InstancedMesh` (plain crate, marked cargo, barrel, shipment crate, pallet) and every crack decal one more per level and shape; a belt is one instanced mesh with a scrolling texture (one full tile per `stepTicks`, locked to `tick + alpha` so a pause stops it); the shipment telegraph, marked rings and door frames are `TileOverlay` batches. A warehouse with 80 pieces of cargo is the same few draw calls as one with 3 (tested). | `RaidViews.ts`, `TileOverlay.ts` |
| RR3 | **Colour carries state, shape carries kind, no text in the world.** Marked cargo = dispatch-yellow crate with a black target stencil and a pulsing gold floor ring; plain crate = weathered board; barrel = red drum with a hazard-stripe band; shipment = blue-grey crate on a pallet, its next two tiles hatched red while `warnTicks > 0`; locked main door = amber frame, steel shutter, chain and gold padlock; optional-room door = teal; open = green / light-teal open frame; exit = green pulsing pad, rising chevrons and a beam, only once `raidExtractionOpened` / `extraction.open`. Wave, door, room and exit words are rail notices (`app/raidNotices.ts`), never text over the board. | `raidStyle.ts`, `raidTextures.ts`, `app/raidNotices.ts` |
| RR4 | **Damage is explained by a cue per outcome.** `campaignHit.result` has its own look: damaged = red flash (cream on an enemy), ring, flinch, a hit on the player shakes the camera a little; dazed = yellow flash (the stars and the Dazed pose are `PlayerViews`' own, from `dazedUntil`); blockedArmour / blockedOkada = a white-blue spark and a second blue ring with NO flinch and NO shake (the shield took it); invulnerable = silent; `campaignDefeated` = a comic-star poof. A flinch is a 0.2 s shove along the hit direction (`PlayerViews.jolt`, off for `prefers-reduced-motion`); camera shake goes through `fx.shake` and its existing off modes. | `damageModel.ts`, `DamageViews.ts`, `PlayerViews.ts` |
| RR5 | **Enemy health pips are thin and conditional.** Pips (one per segment, scaled past 8) show above an enemy only when it is straight ahead of the player within 3 tiles ("targeted"), was hurt in the last 3 s, or is within 3 tiles; never for the player (their meter is the HUD's), never for a boss or lieutenant (their bar is the rail's, story-hud), never for the dead. Data: `Player.vitals` and `enemyKind` (the HUD snapshot carries the same numbers; reading the state keeps the renderer free of the app layer). One instanced draw, camera-facing (the view gets the scene camera in `FrameContext`). | `damageModel.ts` `pipReason`, `DamageViews.ts`, `views/types.ts` |
| RR6 | **Projectiles: cue, path, object, result - from events only.** `projectileLaunched` raises a launch cue (flash and ring at the thrower, the object held overhead, the thrower's Throw clip) and previews the path for `warnTicks`; the object starts moving at `max(launchTick, eventTick + warnTicks)` (the contract does not say whether `launchTick` includes the telegraph, so the telegraph can never be skipped), travels at `stepTicks` per tile with a spin, a shallow arc and a shadow, and stays visible until `projectileHit`, whose `result` picks the landing: wall = shatter (glass sparkle, stone debris), shield = cold spark, hit = impact flash, expired = thud ring. A strong projectile is cued red-hot and drawn 1.5 times bigger. No simulation state is read besides the events. | `ProjectileViews.ts`, `damageModel.ts` |
| RR7 | **The raid cross is movement only and cardinal only.** On a touch device in a raid the left control is four arrow buttons (`ui/DpadControl.tsx`, `input/dpad.ts` `CrossPad`): one finger per arrow, the newest held arrow wins, a finger sliding onto another arrow switches to it, lifting hands back to the arrow still held; the output is one of four directions or none. Left and right sit side by side (no empty centre cell) so the control is two buttons wide and fits the 36 vh movement strip of a landscape phone beside the board's near row; arrows are at least 56 px (`crossLayout`, tested against the action column and the camera's board on 640x360, 740x360, 844x390, 932x430, desktop and portrait). The two action buttons are untouched. On roomy screens the cross stays modest (8 percent of the short side, 56 to 84 px) so it never covers the near row. | `input/dpad.ts`, `ui/DpadControl.tsx`, `ui/DpadControl.css`, `ui/TouchControls.tsx` |
| RR8 | **`cardinalOnly` input mode, selected by the app from the game kind.** `InputManager.setCardinalOnly(on)` (from `inputModeFor(state).cardinalOnly`, true for `settings.game === 'raid'`): every device sends at most one cardinal direction, the move gate runs without `legal` or assist (no corner buffer, route, sidestep, bend or settle), the touch wish is dropped, the gamepad stick snaps to four cardinals and a thumb resting on a diagonal gives nothing (`snapStick(..., strict)`). Keyboard (arrows / WASD) already sent only the newest key. Normal modes are unchanged (all existing input tests green). | `input/InputManager.ts`, `input/gamepad.ts`, `input/inputMode.ts` |
| RR9 | **Raid arena art: one generic look, three variants, drawn around the board.** Warehouse interior (racking, roller doors on a glare of yard light, strip lights, forklift, pallets, "DISPATCH DEPOT" / "BAY n" boards), rail / goods yard (two tracks, a boxcar and a flat wagon, a semaphore signal, a floodlight tower, sheet fence, tarpaulins, drums, sleepers), under-bridge storeroom (a flyover deck and fat piers overhead, padlocked lock-up stores, a house storeroom, bare bulbs, tyre stacks). All read the board size from the set context; everything is off the board, tall things north, the camera side below a metre; every material is lit and weathered by the grit patch like every set. Names and signs are fictional. Registered by arena id in `newArenas.ts` (`RAID_VARIANT_BY_ARENA`; any sim arena of kind `raid` not listed gets the warehouse). Day and night are the same art (not required). | `render/arenas/raid*.ts` |
| RR10 | **No new quality budget.** The raid layer adds no `QualityProfile` field: it is a handful of instanced draws and the arena sets are baked into a few merged meshes like every other set. The existing ladder (shadows, particles, street life) still applies to them. | `docs/RAID_PRESENTATION.md` |


## CC - Campaign combat core: bomb geometry by ruleset, health and damage, enemy durability (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `combat-core`)

Plan: `docs/CAMPAIGN_EXPANSION_PLAN.md`. Reference: `docs/CAMPAIGN_COMBAT.md`. Multiplayer, Rumble and Classic Battle behaviour is unchanged: tests record the seeded Survival, Deathmatch, Team and Classic state hashes on the base commit `187401d` and require them to be identical (`tests/blast-rules.test.ts`).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CC1 | **Campaign health is a separate layer on top of the deployed lethal rules, and only for players that carry `Vitals`.** `applyCampaignHit(state, hit, events)` resolves one hit in a fixed order: recovery frames, then Okada, then Armour (exactly the deployed shield rules: one layer absorbs one lethal event and is consumed, with `okadaBroken` / `protectionBroken`), then health (1 point = 1 segment), then the ordinary `eliminate()`. Every call pushes exactly one `campaignHit` (source, result, hpAfter) and a defeat also pushes `campaignDefeated`. A hit that only dazes or shoves (damage 0) removes no segment. Protection is spent before health and health before campaign lives (the mission runtime's `livesUsed`, unchanged): a defeat is an ordinary elimination, so the mission counts the life; the respawn gives full health and the existing spawn protection, shown as recovery frames. The new elimination cause `{ kind: 'campaign' }` carries the source and owner for credit and the kill feed ("HIT"). | `sim/systems/campaignDamage.ts`, `sim/systems/lethality.ts`, `sim/systems/respawn.ts` |
| CC2 | **Bomb geometry belongs to the ruleset; the player-facing choice is gone.** `modeConfig` applies `BLAST_RULE_BY_GAME[settings.game]`: radial (the unchanged Rumble economy) for survival, deathmatch, team and field missions; cross (`blastMode: 'plus'`, or the arena's own full-line widening such as the Power Yard) for classic and raid. `GameSettings.rumbleBlast`, `MatchSettings.rumbleBlast`, the Setup "Blast shape" control and the "experiment" Ready line are removed (a stale saved key is silently dropped by `sanitizeSettings`). Supersedes RE-L2. The Ready screen still states the radial rule as plain text read from the table; no screen has a control. | `sim/modes.ts`, `app/settings.ts`, `ui/menu/SetupScreen.tsx`, `ReadyScreen.tsx`, `tests/blast-rules.test.ts`, `tests/ui/blast-controls.test.ts` |
| CC3 | **Damage table (config.campaign).** The human has 4 segments (the setup may give 3). A blast flame or a vehicle takes 2 (two blasts to die, the same tension as "Okada + one plate survive two lethal hits, die to the third"; a guard or thrower falls to one blast, a heavy needs two); a stage hazard or trap takes 1; the closing city (crush) and the Sudden Death blast are terminal and kill through everything (the campaign has no closing city, so crush is n/a in practice). Recovery frames last 1 s after a wound or an absorbed lethal event, and an event that touched the player (even ignored) can hurt only once, so one explosion never takes two segments. One lethal tick is ONE campaign hit (the largest wound of the tick), like the deployed "one layer per tick". A `strong` hit is lethal-class (meets the layers first) and pierces recovery frames only when the hit that started them was not strong. | `sim/config.ts` (`CAMPAIGN_CONFIG`), `sim/systems/lethality.ts` |
| CC4 | **Close attacks take segments only from campaign victims.** Punch 1, Shove 0 + two tiles, Daze Punch 0 + 1 s daze, Kick 1 + push, Bottle Strike 2, Leg Sweep 1 + slow, Headbutt 1, Elbow 1 (`config.campaign.playerDamage`); an enemy's close attack takes at most `enemyMeleeDamage` (1) from the human (enemies are individually weaker). The daze and the displacement go through the same `applyCampaignHit`, so the player is told what each hit did; a multiplayer victim keeps the deployed daze-only behaviour (tested). Existing cues are reused for sound (a wound is a dull `push`, a defeat adds `bottle_smash`); no new recording. | `sim/systems/skills.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts` |
| CC5 | **Enemy durability by kind (data).** agbero 1 (punch only), guard 2, thrower 2 and slower (+3 ticks per tile), heavy 3 (the setup may raise it to 4) and slower (+2), lieutenant 8 and boss 14 with a boss bar. A regular enemy's bar is a pure view (`enemyHealthView.showBar`): only when the human targets it (adjacent or faced within 2 tiles in a clear line), within 3 tiles, or for 3 s after it lost a segment; never permanent for everyone. `playerVitalsView` gives the HUD health, armour, Okada, recovery frames and lives. | `sim/data/enemies.ts`, `sim/systems/campaignView.ts` |
| CC6 | **The campaign tuning is not part of normal matches' serialised state.** `GameConfig.campaign` is optional: set by the mission and raid rulesets, by a `maxHp` / `enemyKind` seat, or by a test override; `campaignConfig(config)` falls back to the defaults. This keeps the multiplayer state hashes byte-identical to the base commit. | `sim/config.ts`, `sim/state.ts`, `sim/modes.ts` |


## EA - Campaign enemy attacks (branch of `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `combat-attacks`)

Details, tables and event semantics: `docs/CAMPAIGN_ATTACKS.md`. Numbers: `config.enemyAttacks`. Tests: `tests/enemy-attacks.test.ts`, `tests/enemy-tactics.test.ts`, `tests/enemy-balance.test.ts`; `npm run enemy-balance` re-measures.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EA1 | **A throw is a cue, a path, then a collision.** `projectileLaunched` carries the full tile path, `warnTicks` of telegraph (never below `minWarnTicks` = 0.5 s, whatever the data says) and `stepTicks`; nothing moves during the cue and the thrower is planted. The object then crosses one path tile per `stepTicks`. A rival hit is one standing on the path tile when the object arrives; stepping off in time is a dodge. A wall, hard block or crate stops it (`wall`), an ally of the thrower stops it harmlessly (`blocked`: nothing passes through its own team), the end of the range is `expired`; dazing or downing the thrower during the cue cancels the throw. Common objects (bottle, stone, brick) take 0-1 segment with a short daze or nudge; the strong one (tool) takes a segment, flies 1.7 times slower than a bottle and cues 1 s. Every object is slower than a walking human (12-20 ticks per tile against 10), so walking away along the line outpaces it. | `systems/projectiles.ts`, `config.enemyAttacks.projectiles` |
| EA2 | **Enemies use the one B press and no generators.** For an enemy seat B punches an adjacent rival (the catalogue attack's animation plus the enemy's own slower wind-up, so the player's 4-tick Punch cuts it short), else a thrower throws along a clear cardinal line `minRange`..range tiles away, else nothing. `Intent.place` is ignored for an enemy seat in `tryPlaceGenerator` (and the enemy bot never sends it); humans keep exactly two buttons. `createBot` plays any seat with `enemyKind` with the enemy controller (ordinary profile with placement, pickups, traps, vehicles and generator tools off). The usual "do not loiter next to a rival" tile penalty is off for enemies: their job is to stand next to the target. | `systems/enemyActions.ts`, `systems/actions.ts`, `systems/generators.ts`, `ai/enemyBot.ts`, `ai/goals.ts` |
| EA3 | **Rate limits, so movement is never impossible.** Per-enemy cooldown (2.3-4.5 s, always longer than cue plus flight); a group (team) throws at most once a second, so enemies never all throw in one tick; at most 3 objects in flight per group and 5 overall; at most 2 punch wind-ups at once per group, 0.7 s apart; after any enemy hit lands, that victim is untouchable by enemies for the daze plus one more second (`victimFreeTicks`; the result shows as `invulnerable`, never silent). Combat-core's recovery frames and shield rules apply on top (only a strong hit meets Okada / Armour). | `config.enemyAttacks`, `systems/projectiles.ts`, `systems/enemyActions.ts` |
| EA4 | **Group tactics are directives, not powers.** `enemyGroupDirective` (read by bots only through `observe().directive`, mission roles win when present): `surround` (each melee enemy of a group takes a different open tile next to the target, the second ring only when the four neighbours are taken), `standoff` (throwers and lieutenants take distinct clear cardinal-line tiles 2+ tiles away, 3 preferred), `block` (a melee enemy on a runtime-marked chokepoint; never the whole group, a thrower or the boss; players do not collide in this engine, so a blocker threatens rather than walls), `hold` (nobody within 11 tiles), `reinforce` (an open call and far from the fight). Lieutenants and bosses announce a call (`reinforcementCalled`, bounded: lieutenant 1, boss 2, 20 s apart, only when at half health or alone with a rival in sight); the sim never spawns anyone, the mission / raid runtime answers. | `campaign/tactics.ts`, `data/enemyAttacks.ts`, `mission/types.ts` `NpcDirective` |
| EA5 | **Measured balance (standard bots, Ojuelegba, 1 human vs 4 enemies, 60 s).** A scripted human who watches the screen (leaves a line on the cue after 18 ticks, steps out of a punch in its last 12 ticks, keeps 3 tiles away, never fights back) loses 1.9-2.9 segments a minute to melee and mixed squads and 0 to four throwers (daze only); a human who never moves is not defeated before 4.8 s with 3 segments. Test bands: 0.3-6 per minute, none before 3.5 s. Across every thrower kind, 4 directions, distances 2/3/5 and ~1500 arrangements on the Danfo board, a human who reacts to the cue (steps out of the line, else away) is never hit, and one who simply walks away along the line stays ahead while there is room. The player's Punch with equal health beats every enemy kind in every measured duel (about 6.5 s). First tuning (enemies 2 tiles from the target, a naive "step away when adjacent" script) was misleading in both directions: the pull-back penalty kept enemies from reaching their slots and the script did not dodge wind-ups; wind-ups were lengthened (0.37-0.7 s) and group punch tokens added after measuring 16-22 segments a minute. | `scripts/enemy-balance.ts`, `tests/enemy-balance.test.ts` |
| EA6 | **Audio: existing synthesised cues only.** `projectileLaunched` = a soft `crowd_throw_whistle` (lower and louder for the strong tool); `projectileHit` = `bottle_smash` / `gen_drop` (+ `daze`) on a rival, `okada_break` / a high `gen_drop` for a blocked hit, a faint thud on a wall, silence for blocked / expired / invulnerable; `reinforcementCalled` = `crush_warning` and a gasp. No music, voice or recording added. | `audio/eventRouter.ts`, `tests/audio/engine.test.ts` |
## SN - Short campaign text, narration model and the health HUD (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `story-hud`)

Reference: `docs/CAMPAIGN_TEXT_NARRATION.md`. Front-end only; no audio, voice or TTS was added.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SN1 | **Every briefing and story scene is a card: a summary of at most two sentences (240 characters), an objective line of at most twelve words, an optional Read more (collapsed by default) and a Continue control.** Nothing is removed: the long text moves into Read more. Tests enforce the limits on all shipped content, that Read more holds everything else, and that no card lacks an objective. | `campaign/cards.ts`, `tests/campaign-cards.test.ts` |
| SN2 | **Short copy precedence: content field, then the `CARD_COPY` table, then derivation** (first sentences, first objective / boss phase / reward). The optional fields were added to the content types without touching the content files (the table avoids merge conflicts with `field-sections`); sections are carded generically from `summary` / `cardSummary` / `objectiveCard`. | `campaign/types.ts`, `campaign/content/cardCopy.ts` |
| SN3 | **A story scene is one card with Continue in the footer; the typewriter log and the per-line Next / Skip pair are gone** (two buttons with the same effect on a single card). Enter / Space / A continue unless another button has the focus. The broker stays a message card inside Read more, never a face. | `ui/campaign/CardView.tsx` |
| SN4 | **Narration is data plus an optional provider that fails safely; the catalogue ships empty.** `NarrationProvider` never throws or rejects, the file provider loads only what a `recorded` catalogue entry names, and a missing, blocked or throwing audio path leaves the text untouched. `tts-future` is reserved and never played; no TTS, generated voice or sound file is added (founder rule). | `campaign/narration/**`, `app/narration.ts` |
