| SN5 | **Listen is on every card, disabled with "Narration not recorded yet" when there is no clip, and pressing it never errors.** `campaignListen` (default on) can hide it; `campaignNarration` (default off) auto-plays a clip when one exists. Continue ends the scene at once and stops narration first. | `ui/campaign/listenModel.ts`, `app/settings.ts` |
| SN6 | **A future premium gate is an interface only.** `EntitlementPort.has('fullNarration' \| 'extendedLore' \| 'altPerformance')`, default `FreeEntitlements` (grants nothing premium); premium-tier clips need `fullNarration`. No payment code, account or network. The core mission meaning (summary, objective, Read more) is always free. | `campaign/narration/entitlements.ts` |
| SN7 | **The campaign health meter reads sim views only** (`playerVitalsView`, `enemyHealthView`) into `HudSnapshot.campaign` / `.enemies`; null without `Player.vitals`, so normal matches are unchanged. 3-4 segments, filled block vs empty outline (not colour alone), a 0.6 s flash on the segment lost, in the HUD rail (Rumble: the right column), never over the board. | `app/campaignHud.ts`, `ui/CampaignHealth.tsx` |
| SN8 | **Protection is visibly consumed.** Armour plates, Okada, lives and the recovery frames are chips; a used layer stays, dashed and struck through, for the mission; the cause ("HIT BY BOTTLE") shows 3.5 s; every `campaignHit` outcome has a rail notice except the recovery frames, and an Armour / Okada outcome is never worded twice when `protectionBroken` carries it. | `ui/campaignHealthModel.ts`, `app/campaignHud.ts` `campaignHitNotice` |
| SN9 | **Boss and lieutenant kinds get a boss bar in the rail** (name, kind, up to ten segments, at least one while alive); other enemies only expose `showBar` for the renderer. New HUD colours are contrast-tested on the day and night plate (AA text, 3:1 shapes). | `ui/campaignHealthModel.ts`, `tests/ui/campaignHealthContrast.test.ts` |

## RD - Classic raids (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `raid-sim`; the brief called these CR1-CR12, but CR is taken by Community Rumble above)

Format, mechanics and layouts: `docs/CLASSIC_RAIDS.md`.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RD1 | **A raid is an ordinary match with a `state.raid` runtime, like a mission.** `GameOptions.raid` / `GameState.raid` exist only in raids (the key is absent elsewhere); `tick` calls `updateRaid` / `raidAfterEliminations` and `evaluateMatchEnd` calls `evaluateRaid` only when it exists. `tests/raid-core.test.ts` re-runs the two pinned state hashes of `tests/mission-regression.test.ts` and they do not move. The ruleset is `GameKind 'raid'`: cross blast (`BLAST_RULE_BY_GAME`), no Wahala Crush, no random crates (`softDensity` 0, every crate is authored), no hidden pickups, no elimination rewards or kill streaks, no spectators, no stage hazards or endgame traps (the board is a closed room; belts bring its own telegraphed hazard). | `sim/modes.ts`, `sim/raid/build.ts`, `sim/state.ts`, `sim/tick.ts`, `sim/systems/match.ts` |
| RD2 | **Raid boards are `ArenaKind 'raid'` and are NOT in `ARENAS`.** They live in `RAID_ARENAS` (`getArena` finds them), so the arena pickers, menus and every "all standard / Rumble arenas" test keep seeing exactly what they saw. The arena layout is derived from the `RaidDef` (walls and doors `#`, every destructible `x`), so `createGame` needs no raid special case for tiles. Boards are at most 17 x 13 and the camera is the fixed whole-arena one. | `sim/arenas/raidKit.ts`, `arenas/index.ts`, `raid/layout.ts` |
| RD3 | **Cargo, crates and barrels are soft tiles with hit points.** The tile blocks walking and absorbs a flame arm like any crate; `destroySoftBlocks` asks `raidOnSoftHit`, which takes ONE hit per separate blast (the blast's `softHit` map already counts a chain once, as vehicle damage does) and leaves the tile standing until hp is 0. Only flames damage cargo (nothing else in the sim touches it). Marked cargo hp 2, unmarked cargo 1, sturdy crate 2, barrel 1, plain crate 1 (the existing soft block). | `sim/raid/cargo.ts`, `systems/explosions.ts` |
| RD4 | **Barrels explode once, 6 ticks after they are destroyed:** a plus of flames, reach 1, 30 ticks, stopped by walls, credited to whoever set it off, through the ordinary flame rules (so campaign damage applies) and the same cargo hits (a barrel next to a barrel chains one fuse later). Generators standing in the flames go off. | `sim/raid/cargo.ts`, `raid/tuning.ts` |
| RD5 | **Conveyors carry a standing player one tile per `stepTicks` on the global clock** (cardinal, blocked ahead = stays). A belt never traps: walking against it wins, and every belt tile must be within two steps of floor that is not a belt (layout test). Generators are not carried. | `sim/raid/belts.ts` |
| RD6 | **Shipments are telegraphed and never unavoidable:** a shipment shows on the belt's first tile and waits `warnTicks` (>= 75 ticks = 1.25 s, validated per raid, and kept when a launch is delayed by `maxLive`) before it moves one tile per `stepTicks`; `raidShipmentWarning` and `RaidView.warnings` carry the telegraph. It hits each player on its tile once through `applyCampaignHit` (source 'hazard', knockback along the belt), is stopped by a wall or crate at the end of the belt and removed 0.7 s later, and a flame on its tile destroys it. Because the belt carries a rider at shipment speed, a shipment only catches someone who is held (the foot of the belt) or walks into it. | `sim/raid/belts.ts`, `raid/tuning.ts` |
| RD7 | **Waves are pre-allocated dormant seats** (`alive` false, `enemyKind`, `capacity` 0: enemies never lay generators). A wave starts on its trigger (start, a number of marked cargo, an earlier wave cleared, a door opened, a time), shows its spawn spots for 1.5 s (`raidWaveStarted`, `RaidView.warnings` kind 'wave'), then the enemies appear (30 ticks of spawn protection; a blocked spot waits up to 3 s and is then shifted to the nearest free floor). Enemies never respawn. Until `combat-attacks` brings its group tactics, `raidDirectiveFor` makes an enemy hold its spot (radius 2) and chase once the player is within its aggro range. | `sim/raid/waves.ts`, `raid/directives.ts`, `sim/observe.ts` |
| RD8 | **Doors are walls until their condition holds, and stay open (route changes are permanent).** Conditions: N marked cargo destroyed, one named cargo / crate / barrel destroyed, a wave cleared, the nth floor switch stepped on (by the human), the lieutenant down, another door opened. A side room's door also raises `raidRoomOpened`. | `sim/raid/doors.ts` |
| RD9 | **Side rooms are optional by construction:** the objective, the exit and the wave numbers never mention them; the bonus (one health segment, one Armour plate up to the rules' cap, or a star token) is taken by walking onto its tile (`raidBonusTaken`); a room may hold a small enemy group that wakes when the door opens (it does not count in the wave numbers). `RaidOutcome` counts rooms opened and total. | `sim/raid/doors.ts`, `raid/view.ts` |
| RD10 | **Lives and stars:** the human has 3 lives (`RaidDef.lives`); a fall spends one, the last ends the raid `failed` (`failedBy: 'lives'`), the clock ends it `failed` (`failedBy: 'time'`). While lives last he returns to the entrance 1.5 s later with spawn protection and full health (upgrades and Armour are kept: `respawn.resetOnDeath` false). The health he enters with is `RaidSpec.hp`; `RaidOutcome.hpLeft` hands it back. Stars: completed 1, no life lost +1, inside the par time +1, a star token +1, capped at 3; failed or abandoned 0. | `sim/raid/runtime.ts`, `raid/build.ts` |
| RD11 | **Every layout is proven solvable from its data** (`analyseRaid`): a fixed-point closure over reachable floor, bombable destroyables (a straight clear line within range from a reachable tile that can be left before the 3 s fuse), waves and door conditions must destroy every marked cargo and reach the exit, every side room, every switch and every enemy spot. The same test pins data validity (glyphs, doors, spots, rooms, belts, board size) and that a deliberately broken layout is reported. | `sim/raid/analysis.ts`, `tests/raid-layouts.test.ts` |
| RD12 | **Audio is existing synthesised cues only:** cargo hit / break, barrel burst, door, room, wave warning, shipment bell, bonus and the star chime reuse `vehicle_hit_gen`, `block_crumble_2`, `explosion_2`, `gen_kick`, `ui_go`, `pickup_*`, `crush_warning`, `rail_bell`; `raidOutcome` adds only a star chime because `matchEnded` already plays the win or lose sting. Two events were added to the contract for this: `raidShipmentWarning` and `raidBonusTaken`. No recording, voice or music. | `audio/eventRouter.ts`, `tests/audio/engine.test.ts` |

## FS - Campaign sections, field missions and doors (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-online-v1`, worker `field-sections`)

Report: `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTIONS.md`.

| ID | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FS1 | **Every mission district has three sections** (approach field + door, middle field or standalone raid, the existing boss). The boss mission is playable only after sections 1 and 2; its rewards, unlocks, modifiers and sim are unchanged, and a district clear is still the boss clear. | `campaign/content/sections.ts`, `sectionState.ts` |
| FS2 | **A section field mission is a `state.mission` with `field`**, selected by `MissionSpec.fieldId`; the boss missions never run that code (boss seat `-1`, `ObjectiveKind` gains `fieldRoute`, the objectives table has an inert entry). Boss tests and state hashes are unchanged. | `sim/mission/field*.ts` |
| FS3 | **A route of legs, not phases**: reachMarkers, clearGroup, escapeRoute, door, returnRoute (always last). Same lives and clock as the boss missions. | `sim/mission/field.ts` |
| FS4 | **Escape is fair by construction**: the pursuer starts at least 8 tiles from the player and 4 tiles farther from the exit than the player (data test); a catch needs 40 ticks adjacent, costs a life, resets the leg to the checkpoint with 2 s protection. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `tests/field-nodes.test.ts` |
| FS5 | **A door raises `pendingRaid` after a 1 s hold and the field waits**; `resolveRaid` answers. Failed = a life lost and the door stays open; abandoned = free; completed = done (and the leg advances). A three-star raid is a bonus that can earn the mission's third star. | `field.ts` |
| FS6 | **Save version 2**: sections and missions are stored, only `cleared` is trusted, everything else derived; v1 saves migrate by marking the sections of every cleared district cleared (rewards paid), nothing lost. The save key keeps its name. | `campaign/save.ts` |
| FS7 | **Section rewards are separate** (`sectionRewards`, titles) from boss `rewards`/`unlocks`, so the boss reward logic and its tests are untouched. | `sectionState.ts` |
| FS8 | **A boss win clears the district's sections** (direct results, old saves); the gate lives in the start path (`bossAvailable`), not in `applyMissionResult`. | `progress.ts`, `app/campaign.ts` |
| FS9 | **No mid-mission save.** Only finished results reach the reducers; resume = the map opens on `nextMission`. | `sectionProgress.ts` |
| FS10 | **The field state is kept alive and paused while a raid is played** (`MatchController.playRaid` / `resumeField`); the raid's outcome is `state.raid.outcome` with a match-end fallback. | `app/MatchController.ts`, `app/raidOutcome.ts` |
| FS11 | **Raid pause menu**: Resume, Retry raid, Leave raid (door stays open), Back to campaign map. | `ui/Overlays.tsx`, `app/campaign.ts` |
| FS12 | **Raid reuse is placeholder**: only six raid ids exist, so Fadeyi and Ebute share `junctionToolShed` and Mushin's bonus door reuses `partsStoreroom` until more layouts ship. `src/sim/raid/build.ts` here is a stub of raid-sim's signature. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `sim/raid/build.ts` |

## CK - Checkpoint clarity in field missions (worker `checkpoint-ux`, from `a4fe457`) - founder playtest of Oshodi Terminal Approach

Report: `docs/CHECKPOINT_CLARITY.md`. Presentation and wording only: no rule, timing or tile changed (the checkpoint still completes the instant the human stands on the current one, the door still needs `DOOR_HOLD_TICKS` = 1 s and drains 2 per tick).

| ID | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CK1 | **Walk-through and hold are different objects.** Checkpoints, escape exits and the return marker are beacons (column, flag, floor ring, ripple; never a filling ring); doors are a square cyan hold pad with a door icon and a hold ring. | `render/views/CheckpointBeacons.ts`, `MissionViews.ts` |
| CK2 | **Only the current checkpoint is loud**: bright gold, breathing, with a waypoint arrow drawn over roofs and buses (depth test off, the one exception in the mission markers); later ones of the leg are a faint grey stub; later legs are hidden. | `checkpointStyle.ts` |
| CK3 | **A reached checkpoint flashes white-green for 1.2 s of render time, then stays as a small green flag with a tick**; the flash is timed by the renderer when it sees the state change, so a resumed mission does not replay it. | `CheckpointBeacons.ts` |
| CK4 | **The objective is a tag + target block, first in the right column**: GO TO / HOLD / DEFEAT / ESCAPE / RETURN, the target, "1 OF 3", short notes (NO WAITING, ALL MUST GO DOWN, FIGHTS OPTIONAL). First so a phone's short column never clips it (it did before). | `ui/FieldGoal.tsx`, `app/fieldGoal.ts` |
| CK5 | **The door hold is an explicit bar in that block**, "HOLD 0.4 / 1.0 s", green while filling, amber while draining, with what to do (STAY ON IT / STEP BACK ON). Never on the board. | same |
| CK6 | **Reach notices come from a view diff, not from events**: "CHECKPOINT A ✓ · NEXT: CHECKPOINT B"; the last checkpoint of a leg emits no `missionNode` event (the leg changes first), and debug stepping drops events, so comparing two `MissionView`s is the only reliable source. The memory survives a raid (no view) so "RAID DONE ✓ · NEXT: ..." shows on return. | `app/fieldGoal.ts` (`fieldNotices`), `MatchController.publishHud` |
| CK7 | **Read-only view additions only** (`MissionNodeView.kind` / `optional`, `MissionView.fieldGoal` / `route`); the compass follows `fieldGoal.nodeId` (and prefers required nodes over optional bonus doors), the minimap rings the current one. A field mission shows no boss row and no boss bar (it has no boss). | `sim/mission/field.ts`, `types.ts`, `ui/missionCompassModel.ts`, `ui/minimapModel.ts`, `ui/campaignHealthModel.ts` |
| CK8 | **Leg text says exactly what to do**: "Walk through the 3 checkpoints. No need to stop.", "Stand on the depot door for 1 second to raid it.", "Defeat the 2 hawkers ...", "Escape to the footbridge before the trailer hand catches you.", "Return to ORITA GATE to finish."; counts are tested against the data. Oshodi's third marker is "CHECKPOINT C" (it was "YARD GATE", also Ebute-Metta's return gate). | `sim/mission/fieldDefs.ts`, `campaign/content/sections.ts` |


## EF - Campaign enemy combat fairness (worker `enemy-combat`, from `a4fe457`) - founder playtest: enemies interrupt, dodge every generator, the player flees instead of fighting

Investigation, measurement method and all tables: `docs/ENEMY_COMBAT_FAIRNESS.md`. Harness: `npm run enemy-fairness` (`scripts/enemy-fairness.ts`, `scripts/lib/fairHuman.ts`: a scripted human with ordinary intents against active enemy bots on a classic 15 x 11 grid and on the Dispatch Depot raid). Tests: `tests/enemy-fairness.test.ts`, `tests/app/campaign-enemy-level.test.ts`. Every rule is behind `Player.enemyKind` / `vitals`; multiplayer is pinned byte-for-byte (EF8). Numbers below: Casual bots (the Normal baseline), 12 seeds, before -> after.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EF1 | **Enemies read generators like people, not like the multiplayer planner.** Root cause: an enemy seat ran the ordinary `Brain` (exact fuses from the tick a generator lands, full escape search), so 81-88 % of basic enemies standing in a fresh cross walked out of it. Now the enemy bot shows its planner a generator it does not heed yet as an obstacle with an unreachable fuse: calm (no rival within `focusTiles`) it heeds it after `reactTicks` (0.27-0.5 s); brawling only when the fuse is down to `alertTicks` (agbero 0.4 s, guard 0.5 s, thrower / heavy 0.6 s, lieutenant 1.3 s, boss 1.8 s), sometimes later (`slipChance` x `slipTicks`, the bot's seeded RNG, once per generator); then it re-plans at once with the full escape search. Nothing is added to the observation. Measured (pure bomber): seconds per bomb kill agbero 21.7 -> 17.0, guard 17.8 -> 14.5, throwers killed 9/12 -> 12/12, mixed agbero + guard + thrower group won 7/12 -> 11/12 with deaths 4 -> 1; at Standard the same group 2/12 -> 7/12. A calm guard still walks out of a generator it has had time to see (tested). | `ai/enemyBot.ts`, `config.enemyAttacks.awareness`, `Brain.alert` |
| EF2 | **Commitment: the punishable window.** A resolved enemy punch (hit or miss) plants the enemy for `recoveryRootTicks` (0.17-0.67 s by kind); a throw plants the thrower for its cue plus `throwRecoveryTicks` (thrower 0.6 s). The bot never starts a punch or a throw inside a cross it heeds. Before, an enemy could walk the tick its punch or cue ended. | `systems/enemyActions.ts` `enemyAfterPunch`, `systems/projectiles.ts`, `config.enemyAttacks.melee[*].recoveryRootTicks` / `throwRecoveryTicks` |
| EF3 | **Ordinary punches finish basic enemies.** The human's attack on an enemy seat has its per-target lockout capped at `campaign.vsEnemy.targetLockoutTicks` (1.1 s instead of 3 s) and lands through a running daze immunity (the segment without a new daze). Guard (2 segments) 6.0 -> 3.6 s alone, two guards 4.4 -> 2.6 s; the equal-health duel 6.3-6.9 -> 2.5-3.0 s. Multiplayer lockouts and every attack on the human are unchanged. | `systems/skills.ts` (`humanOnEnemy`), `CAMPAIGN_CONFIG.vsEnemy` |
| EF4 | **Heavies need positioning.** `ENEMY_KINDS.steadfast` (heavy, boss): the player's jab still takes the segment but neither dazes nor interrupts the swing. With EF3 alone the heavy was out-jabbed; now 8-10 of 12 heavies fall to generators, and a slower player who trades blows loses 5 segments a minute. | `data/enemies.ts`, `systems/skills.ts` |
| EF5 | **No repeated interruption: kept, now pinned.** The shared `victimSafeUntil` already covers a punch followed by another enemy's throw (daze + 1 s free); chained interruptions measured 0 in every run before and after. Interruptions a minute in the depot 3.8 -> 1.0 (slower player 2.5 -> 0.8). | `tests/enemy-fairness.test.ts` |
| EF6 | **Field pursuers give up.** An escape-leg pursuer chases only while the human is within `pursuitGiveUpTiles` (14, Chebyshev), otherwise it holds where it stands; it still hunts from the moment the leg starts. Mandatory `clearGroup` legs only get easier (EF1-EF3). | `mission/field.ts` `fieldDirective`, `config.enemyAttacks.pursuitGiveUpTiles` |
| EF7 | **A field mission's seat 1 is an enemy, not a boss.** `MatchController.startMission` lifted seat 1 to Standard (the boss floor) even when it was an ordinary field enemy; the floor now applies only to a seat without `enemyKind`. | `app/MatchController.ts` |
| EF8 | **Normal baseline = Casual enemy bots; multiplayer untouched.** Casual plans properly but late, ignores chains and margins and errs 20 % of the time: the depot is completed 12/12 by the fighting scripted human (deaths 9 -> 0, segments lost a minute 3.37 -> 0.84) and 11/12 by a 0.5 s player (deaths 5 -> 1). Standard is the candidate Hard, Rookie / Beginner Easy (the save still chooses; the difficulty branch maps). A bot-vs-bot digest recorded on `a4fe457` (`scripts/lib/mpDigest.ts`) is pinned. | `docs/ENEMY_COMBAT_FAIRNESS.md`, `tests/enemy-fairness.test.ts` |

## CG - Classic grid: the raid boards as Bomberman mazes (worker `raid-geometry`, from `a4fe457`)

Report: `docs/CLASSIC_GRID.md`. Founder playtest: the raids lacked the maze structure (open halls, so a cross bomb never trapped and enemies always escaped).

| ID | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CG1 | **A raid board is a pillar maze, enforced as data validation.** `raidProblems` runs `raidShapeProblems`: no open 3x3 square of walkable floor anywhere (rooms included); at most 4 open 2x2 squares per raid, each inside a side room, touching a belt tile, or touching the entrance / exit; >= 85 % of the (even, even) interior tiles outside rooms and belts are walls. A layout that breaks it cannot be built. 85 % leaves room for a door or pocket on a lattice tile; the redrawn raids measure 94-100 %. | `sim/raid/shape.ts`, `sim/raid/layout.ts`, `tests/raid-classic-grid.test.ts` |
| CG2 | **The four open raids are redrawn on the classic lattice, every mechanic kept** (cargo counts and ids, doors and conditions, rooms and bonuses, switches, waves and spot letters, belts and shipment specs, lives / time / par, board sizes). Depot: rack columns, the belt moved one column west into its own aisle (x 13), switch (12,7), thrower `d` (12,5). Rail yard: buffer-stop pillars between straight belts, (14,4) left open for the belt corner. Storeroom: shelving posts on the standard parity, barrel aisle on row 5, office drums side by side inside the door. Vault: alcove corridors, the archive posts on row 4, closet (13,4)-(13,5) behind door 4 at (12,5). | `sim/raid/defs/*.ts` |
| CG3 | **Deliberate pockets only:** the vault's spawn pocket (2,10) (otherwise the corner entrance of a two-row hall has one way out) and the rail yard's belt corner (14,4) (otherwise (15,3) cannot be left within two steps). | `recordsVault.ts`, `railYardGoods.ts` |
| CG4 | **Fairness checks with the shape rule:** a bomb on the entrance has a safe tile through two different first steps; every enemy spot is floor with an exit and joined to the entrance on the enemy's own board (crates, cargo and closed doors block; the doors open when that seat appears come from `analyseRaid().doorsAtWaveStart` / `doorsInOrder`), since enemies never lay generators (RD7). The compact template moves one marked crate ((1,5) -> (3,3)) for this; the storeroom keeps no crate that seals a guard spot. | `tests/raid-classic-grid.test.ts`, `sim/raid/analysis.ts`, `defs/compact.ts` |
| CG5 | **The bot board and the sim agree on every tile** (`World.blocked` / `walk` from `observe()` == `!canPlayerEnter`), at the start, with doors open, cargo gone, a generator down and mid-raid. They already agreed; no sim or AI change. | `tests/raid-classic-grid.test.ts` |
| CG6 | **No corner clipping found, so no input or movement change:** on the real depot board, keyboard, gamepad, the touch stick (full assistance, swept round the clock, a flick across a pillar corner) and the cross never move the player diagonally, round a pillar or into one; the controller turns cardinal-only on for raids; random moves on every raid never move a player more than one orthogonal tile or onto a solid tile. | `tests/input-raid-grid.test.ts` |
| CG7 | **Cross-bomb behaviour on the maze is pinned:** pillars and walls stop an arm, the first crate breaks and shields what is behind it, marked cargo takes one hit; a bomb at a corridor mouth traps a rival with no side exit (enemies have no contact ability, so a generator blocks them), a rival at a junction escapes round the corner. | `tests/raid-classic-grid.test.ts` |
| CG8 | **Tests follow the new coordinates without weakening:** the scripted depot playthrough (both rooms, 3 cargo, extraction) and the real-controller slice walk to the new switch; belt tests use x 13; the pressure test stands the human on the floor tile nearest the centre (the centre (8,6) was, and is, a wall). | `tests/raid-*.test.ts`, `tests/app/campaign-slice-real-raid.test.ts` |
| CG9 | **Raid pillars are drawn lower (72 % height), presentation only.** On the full lattice the person-tall procedural pillar hid most of the aisle north of it under the fixed camera (judged on the screenshots); at 72 % every aisle reads and a pillar is still clearly taller than a crate. Raid arenas get `RAID_STYLE` (same models and breakables as the default). | `render/assets/obstacleStyles.ts`, `tests/render/obstacleStyles.test.ts` |

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |

The interim non-combat "Baba Junction" figure was removed so no unfinished identity is canon. | `campaign/content/ojuelegba.ts` unchanged vs release |

## EW - Ebute Metta West (branch `claude/wahala-lagos-enhancement-v1`, report `docs/EBUTE_METTA_WEST.md`)

The City Rumble arena Ebute-Metta grew about 33% by adding genuine new territory to the west of the original board. The original is Ebute Metta EAST in character and is kept tile for tile. No rule, hazard style or sim system changed; `src/sim` stays pure and deterministic, every number below is board data.

| ID | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EW1 | **One constant, one mapping.** The board is 75 x 39 (was 55 x 39): `EBUTE_WEST_DX = 20` new columns on the WEST, and every old coordinate goes through `ebuteX(x) = x + EBUTE_WEST_DX` (the commuter railcar lanes, the core, every old anchor, the six old district rects and links, the campaign Ebute mission and its field mission). The old west border (old x 0-1) is replaced by the new territory, whose own east edge (x 20-21) is a facade with gateways; the old lane at old x 2 (now x 22) stays a lane. The original 55-column board is pinned as a fixture (`tests/ebuteEastOriginal.ts`) and the test requires the east of the new board to equal it tile for tile (crates and supply marks included). Rejected: scaling the old map by 1.3 (would change every street the founder likes). | `sim/arenas/ebuteWest.ts`, `sim/arenas/rumbleEbuteMetta.ts`, `tests/ebute-west.test.ts` |
| EW2 | **Four new districts, ten in all** (`abuleIjesha` Abule Ijesha Lanes x 2-21 y 2-12, `apapaRoad` Apapa Road x 0-21 y 13-22, `ilogbo` Ilogbo Quarter x 2-21 y 23-31, `otumara` Otumara Waterfront x 2-21 y 32-36), rectangles disjoint from the six old ones, each with at least two links; the new link tiles join the old board along its former west border through 14 gateway rows (y 5-6, 11-13, 22, 26-32, 36). Total open tiles 1,329 -> 1,774 (+33.5 %). | `sim/arenas/ebuteWest.ts` |
| EW3 | **Real names, compressed and partly fictional.** Used: Apapa Road, Ilogbo (the community off Apapa Road, not Ilogbo Elegba in Ojo), Abule Ijesha (Yaba side, compressed to the north-west), Otumara (Ilaje Otumara, the riverine community on the lagoon in Ebute Metta West). Not used as a district: Abule Ijero (no source found for a place of that name; only an "Ijero" church school), Iddo (stays a sign on the viaduct), Costain (the direction the trucks come from). Every sign and shop is fictional or generic wording; no real business, brand or person. Otumara is shown as a working waterfront (canoes, slips, timber, drains), never as a slum or a demolition scene. | `docs/EBUTE_METTA_WEST.md` |
| EW4 | **Roads and hazards extend, nothing new.** Murtala Muhammed Way carries on west as Apapa Road: the same four road rows from x 0 to x 74, the traffic and truck sweeps run the full width (edge to edge, 2 s / 2.5 s warnings), the summoned-vehicle lanes sit at the new west edge. The railcar and its portals move with the board (3.5 s warning, one at a time); the track-free route (north of the north portal, south of the south portal) still joins west and east, tested by blocking either crossing. The disused Ilogbo rail spur is floor paint plus one signal post (scenery: no sim tile, no hazard, no ballast, no lane lights). Two new mild lagoon washes at Otumara join the foreshore ones. | `sim/arenas/rumbleEbuteMetta.ts`, `render/arenas/ebuteMettaArt.ts` |
| EW5 | **Team Rumble halves.** The split is by x at the new centre column 37 (old x 17), so the west half (new ground + old x < 17) holds 795 open tiles and the east half 962 (west 17 % smaller; the old board's open floor is concentrated east of its own centre, so any westward expansion of +30 % leaves the west half smaller). The stage test tolerance for this arena is 0.20 (Oshodi has 0.12 for the same reason); the supply crates are balanced exactly (13 west, 13 east, 1 on the centre column) and cover share per band matches its neighbour on the old board. Rejected: filling the west to 65+ % open (empty combat space, +40 % tiles) or moving old supplies (would change the east). | `tests/rumble-stages.test.ts`, `tests/ebute-west.test.ts` |
| EW6 | **Economy.** 9 new supply crates (27 on the board; the "at most 24 marks" cap of `rumble-economy-evolution` is 30 for this arena), 6 new floor traps, 2 new barrier crates, 2 new wash zones, all on open floor and away from spawns; the old anchors are snapped on the ORIGINAL layout and shifted, so none of them moved; the Survival core is the original one, shifted. Spawns (32, balanced half and half) are recomputed by the existing generator. | `sim/arenas/rumbleEbuteMetta.ts` |
| EW7 | **The east keeps its look.** The cover list is the original objects (same order, variants and numbering, shifted) followed by the west's own, so every old shop sign and house is identical; the west uses its own palette and signs (`THEME_WEST`, applied by `forWest`) on the shared families and six new families (`oldHouse`, `compound`, `trailer`, `hoardingShop`, `boatSlip`, `signalPost`) next to the shared `workshop`. Only the old west border facade pieces were dropped (they stood where the west now is), so the `building` family variants of the east facade renumber. | `sim/arenas/community/arena.ts` (`cover` override), `render/arenas/ebuteMettaArt.ts` |
| EW8 | **No new map anchors.** The identity rule "exactly three visual anchors per area" stands (`tests/render/map-anchors.test.ts`); the west's identity is carried by cover families, floor paint (APAPA RD road words and zebra, drain slabs, the rusted spur) and the lagoon set pieces. | `render/arenas/anchors.ts` (unchanged) |
| EW9 | **Campaign Ebute is the same mission on the same tiles.** Dispatch-windows nodes, crew posts, boss post and the Yard Approach field mission are authored in the old coordinates and go through `ebuteX`; objectives, windows, tuning and the lives / respawn logic are untouched. | `sim/mission/defs.ts`, `sim/mission/fieldDefs.ts` |
| EW10 | **Performance.** The expanded board adds about the same number of static objects as the board's area; numbers (static scene budget and per-frame draw calls and triangles, low / medium, desktop and 844 x 390 phone, before / after) are in `docs/EBUTE_METTA_WEST.md`; no new post-processing, light or texture; the west reuses the shared materials and the merged cover builder. `scripts/ebute-perf.mjs` reproduces them. | `scripts/ebute-perf.mjs` |

## LK - Lekki Admiralty Way paving (branch `claude/wahala-lagos-enhancement-v1`, `docs/LEKKI_ADMIRALTY_WAY.md`; first written as "Amarite Way", a transcription error for Admiralty Way confirmed by the founder)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LK1 | **Procedural, original zig-zag interlocking pavers** painted into the arena's one floor canvas; the founder photographs are references only and are not shipped. No new mesh, texture or draw call. | `render/arenas/community/pavers.ts` |
| LK2 | **Only Admiralty Way is paved.** The photographs match the commercial N-S boulevard (the straight run of Admiralty Way); its two straight segments (60 tiles) are paved, Freedom Way, the expressway and both junctions stay asphalt. The road choice was an ASSUMPTION for founder review, since confirmed (LK6); `ADMIRALTY_WAY_PAVED` is the single switch. Coverage superseded by LK6. | `render/arenas/lekkiAdmiralty.ts` |
| LK3 | **Geometry is untouched**: the median (grass, black-and-white kerb stripes) and the lane dashes are floor paint, not obstacles. | `lekkiAdmiralty.ts` |
| LK4 | **Blocks are drawn 0.36 x 0.18 tile**, larger than life, because the floor canvas is about 45 px per tile on Lekki and real-size blocks would shimmer as sub-pixel noise; joints low-contrast. | `pavers.ts` |
| LK5 | `communityFloor` gains an optional `paved` hook run after the asphalt; no other arena uses it. | `community/floor.ts` |
| LK6 | **Founder clarification (integration branch `claude/wahala-campaign-integration-v1`): the road is Admiralty Way, and its whole carriageway is interlocking paving.** The identifiers, file names, script and doc are renamed from Amarite to Admiralty (`ADMIRALTY_WAY_PAVED`, `ADMIRALTY_PAVERS`, `paintAdmiraltyWayDetails`, `lekkiAdmiralty.ts`, `scripts/admiralty-shots.mjs`, `docs/LEKKI_ADMIRALTY_WAY.md`). The paving now covers every Admiralty Way road tile, both directions, from the plaza (y 10) to the expressway (y 29), including the two approach rows (y 17, y 21) that were asphalt: 68 tiles (was 60). Freedom Way's lanes (y 18-20, the crossing included) and the expressway stay asphalt: they are other roads. Tested: every Admiralty Way road tile is paved, no Freedom Way or expressway tile is, and the sim road tiles are unchanged (524). | `lekkiAdmiralty.ts`, `tests/render/lekki-admiralty-pavers.test.ts` |
| LK7 | **Junction transitions like a real block road.** Where the paving meets asphalt (Freedom Way at y 18 and y 21, the expressway at y 30): a pale cast-concrete edge restraint, a soldier course of blocks laid across the road just inside it, and a white stop line on the lane that approaches the junction (drive on the right). The painted median now ends in rounded, dark-nosed kerbs short of each junction instead of a square cut. Floor paint only: no mesh, collision or draw call. | `lekkiAdmiralty.ts` |
| LK8 | **Block tone a touch darker** (`[146,147,143]`, joints `rgba(84,86,84,.88)`; was `[158,160,156]`): in daylight the first pass read almost as pale as Lekki's pavements; it now reads as a medium-grey carriageway, still clearly not asphalt. Shape, size and layout unchanged. | `community/pavers.ts` |

## CI - Campaign integration (branch `claude/wahala-campaign-integration-v1`: classic grid / combat + Lagos enhancement)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CI1 | **The campaign level picks the active enemies' bot level: Easy = Beginner, Normal = Casual, Hard = Standard.** The fairness work measured and tuned the enemy rules at Casual (EF8, "the Normal baseline") and left the mapping to the difficulty branch; the difficulty branch left the bot level to the separate "Crew level" (save default Rookie), so a Normal campaign actually fought Rookie enemies. Now field-mission and raid enemies (every seat with an `enemyKind`) play at `CAMPAIGN_LEVEL_DEFS[level].enemyLevel`, one bot level either side of Casual for Easy / Hard, on top of the player-side numbers of CL1-CL7. A boss mission keeps the crew level and the Standard boss floor (MS4), and the briefing says so. No save change: an old save (crew level Rookie, no campaign level) loads as Normal and fights Casual enemies. Multiplayer, Rumble and the bot-vs-bot digest never read it. Depot harness (`npm run enemy-fairness -- --only raid`, 12 seeds, Normal player-side numbers; fighting / slower player): Beginner 10/12 / 11/12 won, 2 / 1 deaths; Casual 11/12 / 10/12, 1 / 1; Standard 8/12 / 11/12, 2 / 1. The bot level alone is a gentle step (every failure is the scripted player's stand-off at the 4-minute limit); the player-side numbers carry most of the Easy / Hard difference. | `sim/campaign/levels.ts` `campaignEnemyLevel`, `app/campaign.ts`, `ui/campaign/BriefingScreen.tsx`, `tests/app/campaign-enemy-difficulty.test.ts` |
| CI2 | **Easy and Hard keep the new player-on-enemy punch lockout.** The gameplay branch added the required `CampaignConfig.vsEnemy` after the difficulty branch wrote `levelConfigLayer`; the layer now spreads it like `damage` / `bar`, so a level never drops it (the merge's one semantic conflict, caught by tsc). The awareness, recovery-root and pressure knobs (docs/ENEMY_COMBAT_FAIRNESS.md "Knobs") are not scaled per level yet: unmeasured, left for a tuning pass. | `sim/campaign/levels.ts` |

## RF - Campaign respawn freeze hotfix (branch `claude/wahala-respawn-freeze-hotfix-v1`)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RF1 | **Root cause: a campaign defeat dealt by an enemy attack was never counted.** `applyCampaignHit` -> `eliminate()` runs inside an earlier system of the tick (an enemy's thrown object in `updateProjectiles`, a punch in `updateAttacks`, a raid belt) and returns to the caller; only blasts, vehicles and hazards go through `applyLethalHits`, whose returned list alone fed `scheduleRespawns` (the respawn timer) and `missionAfterEliminations` (the life). So after a brick / punch defeat the human was down with `respawnAt === null`, `livesUsed` unchanged (HUD "Health 0/4, Lives 3/3") and the HUD's `respawnIn ?? 1` said "Back in 1" for ever; on the last life the mission never ended. The first hotfix (a4fe457, spectator UI) was right but its test killed the human with a blast, the one path that worked; deaths by blast were fine and enemy-attack deaths froze, hence "sometimes". Boss and field missions were affected; raids were not (`raid/runtime.ts countFall` already counts any fall once). | `sim/tick.ts` |
| RF2 | **Fix: one idempotent bookkeeping rule, no timer or HUD workaround.** `withCampaignDefeats` adds the campaign human (a player with `vitals` and no `enemyKind`, `alive === false`, `eliminatedTick === state.tick`) to the tick's eliminated list when `applyLethalHits` did not already list them. The existing `scheduleRespawns`, `missionAfterEliminations` (life, ledger carry), checkpoint respawn, drops and game-over rules then run unchanged and exactly once. Seats without `vitals` (every multiplayer fighter) and enemies are never added, so Rumble, classic and enemy-defeat handling are byte-identical (the bot-vs-bot digest tests pass). Not done, on purpose: hiding the DOWN bar, extra lives, a restart, a watchdog timer. | `sim/tick.ts` |
| RF3 | **Pause / visibility rules are unchanged and now tested.** A hidden tab pauses the game (`session.ts onVisibility`), so the countdown does not run while away and does not resume by itself; resuming completes it. A long gap between frames is clamped by the controller (`MAX_FRAME_MS`), so it neither skips nor freezes the respawn. | `tests/app/campaign-respawn-controller.test.ts`, `e2e/campaign-respawn-freeze.spec.ts` |
| RF4 | **Tests cover every way to die, repeated, in the real loop.** Sim: field, boss and raid x projectile, punch, self blast, enemy blast; the 1 -> 0 tick boundary; one life per death even under continued fire; three deaths ending the mission; checkpoint kept. Controller (controlled clock): HUD countdown 3 -> 2 -> 1 equals the sim timer, pause, hidden-tab gap, three deaths. Browser (desktop and phone, real time): two consecutive deaths with real fighter reactivation, health, lives, checkpoint and a working generator press; hidden tab; last life. The sim and controller sets fail on the unfixed code (11 + 5 failures); the browser spec reproduces the frozen "Back in 1" there. | `tests/campaign-respawn-lifecycle.test.ts` and the two above |


## LI - Lekki Phase 1 interlocking road network (branch `claude/wahala-lekki-interlock-redesign-v1`, `docs/LEKKI_INTERLOCK_ROADS.md`; supersedes LK1, LK2, LK4, LK6-LK8 for the road surface)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LI1 | **The pavers are drawn by the floor shader, not painted into the floor canvas.** The canvas (about 45 px per tile on Lekki, capped at 2560 px) cannot hold a real block; it now marks paved tiles with a reserved key colour, and a patch of the floor material samples a seamless, mip-mapped 512 x 512 paver texture in world tile coordinates wherever the canvas shows the key. Markings painted over the key (kerbs, lane lines, stop lines, the median, road words) differ from it and show through unchanged. One lattice for the whole network: no reset or seam at tile, segment or junction boundaries. No mesh, no draw call; one texture (1 MB, 1.3 MB with mips) per Lekki floor, owned and disposed with it. | `render/arenas/paving/material.ts`, `paving/floorKey.ts`, `views/ArenaView.ts` |
| LI2 | **The block is the I-shaped (dog-bone) road paver of the founder's close-ups**: 2:1, flared ends and a waisted middle, laid in courses offset by half a block so each flared end sits in the waist beside it. Original procedural texture (the photographs are references only, never shipped): thin sand-filled joints about 0.66 of the block tone, a soft chamfer that darkens each block edge, aggregate speckle and faint mottling, block-to-block variation of +/- 6.5 % (no checkerboard), medium grey `[118,120,122]`. Pure and tested (`paving/interlock.ts`). | `paving/interlock.ts` |
| LI3 | **A block is 0.22 tile long** (was 0.36 painted): about 18 x 9 screen px at the desktop gameplay camera, so the road reads as many small fitted blocks, not big tiles; its own texture gives about 190 texels per tile, so the blocks stay crisp up close and the GPU mip chain fades them to an even grey further away (no moire). The repeat (2.64 tiles) is hidden by a few percent of world-space tone drift in the shader. All courses run east-west across the whole network (one orientation, continuous through every junction). | `paving/material.ts` |
| LI4 | **Coverage from the map, not a list of rectangles**: every road tile (`=`) outside the Expressway Gateway rows (y 30-34) is a local road (Admiralty Way, Freedom Way, their crossing) and is paved; the open floor of the Freedom Way Estates district (role "street": the residential streets between the walled compounds) is paved; the expressway keeps its asphalt (the regional Lekki-Epe road, outside the Phase 1 street network); pavements, forecourts, the promenade plaza, the court, the sandy coastal lanes and the gateway pavements are not road and are not paved. One switch per class (`LEKKI_PAVED`). | `render/arenas/lekkiRoads.ts` |
| LI5 | **Edges like a real block road**: kerbs where paving meets a non-road surface already exist on road edges (generic pass); estate streets get a pale cast-concrete edge restraint where they meet open ground that is not road; Admiralty Way ends at the expressway with an edge restraint and a stop line; stop lines stay at the Freedom Way crossing; the soldier courses and junction cut-offs of LK7 are gone because the paving is now continuous through the crossing. Asphalt cracks are no longer drawn on paved tiles (the floor's random sequence is preserved, so the rest of the floor is unchanged). | `paving/floorKey.ts`, `lekkiAdmiralty.ts`, `community/floor.ts` |
| LI6 | **Only Lekki changes** (`PAVED_ARENAS` has one entry); every other arena's floor, and the sim layout, collision and routes of Lekki (524 road tiles), are unchanged (tested). | `paving/arenas.ts`, `tests/render/lekki-interlock-roads.test.ts` |

## CP - Oshodi Mission 1 combat pilot (branch `claude/wahala-oshodi-combat-pilot-v1`, from the release `b55e95f`; `docs/OSHODI_COMBAT_PILOT.md`)

An experiment for founder playtesting, gated to the Terminal Approach field mission. Not merged, not deployed. Every number is in `src/sim/config.ts` (`OSHODI_COMBAT_PILOT`, `OSHODI_PILOT_ENEMY_ATTACKS`, `OSHODI_PILOT_LEVELS`); before / after measured with `npm run combat-pilot` on the real mission.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CP1 | **One switch, one mission.** `FIELD_COMBAT_PILOTS` maps `oshodi.approach` to the pilot; its config layer adds `config.combatPilot` and the pilot's enemy-attack tuning. Every other match (other field missions, raids incl. the Dispatch Depot, bosses, City Rumble, Classic, multiplayer) has no `combatPilot` key and runs exactly as before (tested; the regression hashes and the multiplayer digest are unchanged). Removing the pilot = deleting the entry. | `sim/mission/combatPilot.ts`, `sim/mission/build.ts` |
| CP2 | **One pilot punch against enemies, whatever the fighter.** Why: Lekki Boy's Daze Punch and Campus Queen's Shove took 0 health from enemies (2 of 3 playable characters could not defeat anyone by punching), and the Punch landed once a second. Startup 5, 1 segment, hit recovery 24 (0.4 s), whiff recovery 16. Multiplayer attacks are untouched. | `systems/pilotCombat.ts` |
| CP3 | **Enemy durability: guard 3, thrower 2, heavy 5; a blast takes 3 from an enemy; enemy frames 6 ticks, and a pilot punch neither waits for frames nor starts them.** Why: the 1 s recovery frames after every hit made a punch protect an enemy from the blast that followed. One blast still drops any ordinary enemy (generators stay the big weapon), and a heavy needs a blast plus two punches. | `campaignDamage.ts`, `lethality.ts` |
| CP4 | **Every accepted press swings at once; a miss is visible.** Why: the swing clip played only on a hit and B with nobody adjacent did nothing, so presses looked ignored. Reach adds the diagonals and the own tile (forgiving under the high camera). Wind-up / counter-window / stagger rings on the board, the enemy swing stretched over its wind-up, existing synthesised sounds only. | `render/views/CombatPilotViews.ts`, `PlayerViews.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts` |
| CP5 | **Input buffer and refusal notice.** A B or A press the human cannot act on yet (hit-stun, the punch's own swing / recovery) is kept and done the first tick it can be: through a hold-up of up to 0.75 s, plus 0.25 s. A generator that cannot be laid says why (NO SOCKET / TILE TAKEN / WAIT). Why: presses during a daze were dropped silently. | `systems/pilotCombat.ts` `pilotIntents` |
| CP6 | **Readable enemy attacks with a counter window**: guard wind-up 0.57 s, planted 0.6 s after; heavy 0.57 s, planted 0.83 s, 1-tile knockback; thrower planted 0.8 s after a throw, bottle / stone cue 0.7 s, 3 s cooldown; shorter hit-stun on the human (guard 0.3 s, heavy 0.4 s, bottle 0.33 s). | `OSHODI_PILOT_ENEMY_ATTACKS` |
| CP7 | **Groups take turns**: one wind-up at a time per group, 0.6 s apart. After an enemy hit, 1.25 s pass before another enemy can hurt the human. | same |
| CP8 | **Stagger without stun-lock.** A punch staggers an ordinary enemy for 0.2 s and cuts its wind-up short, then 0.9 s of stagger immunity (damage still lands). The interrupted swing's cooldown is given back, so the enemy swings back 2 ticks after the stagger. The heavy reels only on every third punch. Tuned so a fast player beats a lone guard cleanly and a slower one sometimes eats the counter (measured: with 0.33 s punches and no refund, guards never hit anyone). | `systems/pilotCombat.ts` |
| CP9 | **Enemies keep off the human's tile; an enemy punch reaches a human on its own tile.** Why: guards walked onto the player's tile, where nothing could hit them; one press under fire started a punch 3 times in 80. | `movement.ts`, `skills.ts`, `enemyActions.ts` |
| CP10 | **The enemy AI checks the real radius footprint** before committing to a wind-up near a generator it heeds (it used the plus shape on this radius-blast map). Pilot matches only. | `ai/enemyBot.ts` |
| CP11 | **Pilot roster**: a second Park Warden by the first (two ordinary enemies) and a Bottle Boy thrower by the yard gate (a mixed group), seated after the original three; fights stay optional. | `FIELD_COMBAT_PILOTS.extraEnemies` |
| CP12 | **Levels**: Easy gives a longer stagger (20), a slower counter-swing (12) and a longer free window. Hard gives a 6-segment heavy, a shorter stagger, two wind-ups at once and a quicker guard. Both sit on top of the ordinary level differences. Normal adds no layer. | `OSHODI_PILOT_LEVELS`, `pilotLevelLayer` |

## Integrated release (branch `claude/wahala-integrated-release-v1`: release 0c200a7 + Oshodi combat pilot + the sections below; notes in `docs/integrated-release/`)

### SD-B - Sudden Death banner lifecycle

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SD-B1 | **The blast clock announces, then folds.** When a countdown begins (the round's first, and the next one after every blast) the clock shows its full announcement for `SUDDEN_DEATH_ANNOUNCE_TICKS` (180 ticks, 3 s), then folds into a one-line chip: SUDDEN DEATH · BLAST n OF 3 (plus SHIELDS IGNORED on the final blast) · the countdown. The chip still pulses in the 5 s warning window; it does not expand again over the fight. The long lines (your shield, the ranking) stay in the chip's accessible label. | `app/hud.ts` (`SuddenDeathHud.announce`), `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx`, `ui/SuddenDeath.css` |
| SD-B2 | **Counted in simulation ticks, read from the sim state.** `announce` is derived from `transitionEndTick` / `lastBlastTick` (presentation only, no new sim field, no rule change), so a paused game keeps the banner as it was, a resume carries on, a rematch (a new state) starts with no banner, and a fresh round started mid-match (the last contenders fell together) gets its own card and its own announcement. | `app/hud.ts` `buildSuddenDeath` |
| SD-B3 | **The title card keeps its sim lifecycle.** It shows exactly while input is held and disappears on the first tick of play; it is `pointer-events: none` and has no controls. Not shortened by wall time: the sim holds input for those ticks whatever the device's frame rate. | `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx` (unchanged rule), `tests/ui/sudden-death-banner.test.ts` |
| SD-B4 | **Nothing on the board takes a touch.** The card, the chip, the rail and the blast flash are all `pointer-events: none`; the e2e checks a tap where the chip is reaches the game, and the fighter moves (keys on desktop, the touch pad on a phone) with the chip up. | `e2e/sudden-death-banner.spec.ts` |

### SD-T - Sudden Death test match (temporary local playtest tool; founder request)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SD-T1 | **A dedicated local test, opened from the Training Academy screen ("Sudden Death Test"; first on the Home list, then moved: a sixth Home row pushed the portrait-phone main action off the screen and failed the menu-fit and Settings specs in CI).** The human plus exactly three bots on the standard Danfo Bus Park board (Last Player Standing ruleset), straight into the sudden-death round on the first playing tick: all four are contenders, the SD-B title card (3 s here) and blast clock behave exactly as before. The board is an open floor (no crates) so the ring and the front stay readable. The menu's saved setup is read (fighter, bot level), never written; a rematch replays the test. Not offered in Classic, City Rumble, the campaign or online play. | `app/names.ts` `suddenDeathTestSetup`, `app/MatchController.ts` (`variant`), `ui/menu/AcademyScreen.tsx`, `sim/suddenDeathTest.ts` |
| SD-T2 | **All extra rules sit behind `config.suddenDeath.lab`, absent everywhere else.** `SUDDEN_DEATH_TEST_CONFIG` carries it; an ordinary match's state and rules are byte-for-byte what they were (a test checks). Removal list in `suddenDeathTest.ts`. | `sim/config.ts`, `sim/suddenDeathTest.ts` |
| SD-T3 | **The outer arena collapses over 30 s through the ordinary Wahala Crush.** When the countdown starts the existing crush order (outside in, around a 7 x 5 core that never closes) is scheduled evenly so the last tile lands 30 s later. Same telegraph (`crushWarning`, red floor tile and closing rings) and the same lethal landing and elimination path as Wahala Crush; the core is where the contenders are seated. | `sim/systems/suddenDeathCollapse.ts`, `sim/state.ts` |
| SD-T4 | **Fairness: every killing tile is warned 1.8 s first** (`config.crush.warnTicks`, longer than the 1.2 s of a normal match; a walker leaves a tile in 10 ticks). Tests check each crush elimination has an earlier warning of its tile at least that long. A protected fighter is shoved to the nearest free tile, as in Wahala Crush. | `sim/suddenDeathTest.ts`, `tests/sudden-death-test.test.ts` |
| SD-T5 | **Each blast is a front that crosses the floor, in the sim.** It leaves the middle of the area and strikes one square ring (Chebyshev distance) per 14 ticks (about 1.6 s across the board). A contender takes the ordinary sudden-death lethal hit (shield layers, the shield-proof final blast, AS6, SD1-SD6 unchanged) on the first tick the front has reached the ring they stand in; once it has crossed the whole board every contender still standing has been struck, so the blast stays full-area. The result of the blast is reported once the front has passed. The 5 s HUD warning still precedes it and the floor pulses red over every open tile in that window. | `sim/systems/suddenDeathFront.ts`, `render/views/suddenDeathFrontLook.ts` |
| SD-T6 | **What the player sees is what strikes.** The renderer draws the sim's own front (`SuddenDeathFront`, `frontRing`) as hot tiles on the rings struck, cooling behind it, with only a faint non-lethal hint half a ring ahead. | `render/views/OverlayViews.ts` |
| SD-T7 | **Timing (test only).** Title card 3 s, collapse 30 s from the start of the countdown, blast 1 at 36 s, blast 2 12 s later, the final (shield-proof) blast 12 s after that; the full-length match clock is set to 10 min so no endgame trap wave or timeout can interfere. New `suddenDeathStarted` cause `test`, new event `suddenDeathFront`, `suddenDeathBlast.spread`. | `sim/suddenDeathTest.ts` |

### SD-U - Sudden Death test, second pass: a real City Rumble stage, two bots, findable (founder feedback)

The founder found the SD-T setup wrong: it was the classic open-space arena with three bots and a hard-to-find button. SD-T1 (arena, four players, Academy entry) and SD-T3 (7 x 5 core on a 15 x 11 board) are superseded by the rows below; SD-T2, SD-T4-SD-T7 and SD-B1-SD-B3 (the announcement) stand unchanged.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SD-U1 | **The test is played on a real City Rumble stage: Ojuelegba Underbridge (45 x 33), not a standard arena.** It is the smallest Rumble stage (7 stages: 45 x 33 to 75 x 39), so the ring is the shortest to close; its heart, the open pier court (x 14-30, y 11-21), is a compact readable footprint of 17 x 11 tiles that the test keeps open; it has real outer buildings, blocks and barriers around it. The ruleset is Last Player Standing (`survival`) with the Rumble blast and economy; the stage's own traffic, lethal events and floor traps are off for this match only (`stageHazards: false`, the tutorial/test switch) so the only things that can kill are the closing ring and the blasts being judged. | `sim/suddenDeathTest.ts` |
| SD-U2 | **Exactly the human and two bots (three competitors).** `SUDDEN_DEATH_TEST_PLAYERS = 3`; the test settings are `play: 'rumble'` on the test stage with `rumbleMixed: false` (all bots at the player's chosen level). The saved menu setup is read, never written. The match opens on the whole-stage view so the shrink and the blasts are in frame (`O` still switches to the follow camera); this is set in the store and renderer only, never saved, and put back when the player leaves to the menu. | `app/names.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| SD-U3 | **The whole stage closes toward the middle in about 30 s.** The ordinary Wahala Crush order (outside in, ring by ring, clockwise) over every non-concrete tile outside the 17 x 11 open area, scheduled evenly so the last tile lands 30 s after the countdown starts (`collapseTicks`); cadence is therefore scaled to the stage's tile count (835 closing tiles, roughly 30 per second). Barriers and crates outside the open area stay and are crushed by the ring; the open area is cleared of crates and supply crates. The lab schedule is now built whatever mode the arena forces (a Rumble stage forces `crush`). | `sim/systems/suddenDeathCollapse.ts`, `sim/state.ts` |
| SD-U4 | **Fair, telegraphed eliminations (unchanged rule, re-checked at stage size).** Every closing tile is marked `crush.warnTicks` (1.8 s) before it lands, with the same `crushWarning` / `crushLanded` events and red floor; a fighter on a landing tile is eliminated (or shoved by a protection layer) exactly as in Wahala Crush, with no credit. Tests check each landing was warned at least 1.8 s earlier. | `tests/sudden-death-test.test.ts` |
| SD-U5 | **The blast front covers the stage that is left, and no longer idles past it.** The front still leaves the middle of the area one ring per step, but its last ring is now the farthest tile that is not concrete (or a fighter still standing), not the far corner of the whole 45 x 33 board, and its pace is derived from the open area so it crosses it in about 3 s (`FRONT_CROSSING_TICKS`; 20 ticks per ring on the 17 x 11 area). Strike rule, shield layers, the shield-proof final blast and the 5 s HUD warning are unchanged. | `sim/systems/suddenDeathFront.ts`, `sim/suddenDeathTest.ts` |
| SD-U6 | **One clearly visible entry: a "Test" button in the action bar of the City Rumble mode screen** (Home -> City Rumble -> bottom bar: amber outlined button with a TEST chip, "Sudden Death Test", "You + 2 bots"). One tap launches (the usual loading screen, 3 s title card, countdown). It is the screen's action bar, not a fifth mode card and not a sixth Home row, so the Home list keeps its height on portrait phones (the SD-T1 failure) and the mode cards are unchanged. The Training Academy copy is removed: one place only. | `ui/menu/ModeScreen.tsx`, `ui/menu/flow.css`, `ui/menu/AcademyScreen.tsx` |
| SD-U7 | **Unchanged on purpose**: the Sudden Death announcement (title card, clock card, chip, SD-B1-SD-B3), ordinary matches, the campaign, online play and every other Rumble start. All lab rules stay behind `config.suddenDeath.lab`. | |

### LI7-LI9 - Lekki: interlock paving on Admiralty Way only (founder revision)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LI7 | **Founder revision: only Admiralty Way is interlocking paving, through its own crossing with Freedom Way.** Surface classes from the map: `admiraltyWay` (road tiles in `ADMIRALTY_WAY`, x 20-23 y 10-29: paved), `freedomWay` (other road tiles outside the expressway rows: asphalt), `estateStreet` (estate open floor: not paved), `expressway` (rows 30-34: asphalt); `LEKKI_PAVED = {admiraltyWay}`. Supersedes LI4's coverage; LI1-LI3 (shader, block, size) and LI6 (only Lekki) unchanged. | `render/arenas/lekkiRoads.ts`, `tests/render/lekki-interlock-roads.test.ts` |
| LI8 | **Paving meets asphalt with an edge restraint on both sides of the crossing box** (rows 18-20 at x 20 west and x 23 east), in the existing restraint style; paint only, no geometry. | `render/arenas/lekkiAdmiralty.ts` |
| LI9 | **Estate streets are un-paved, not turned into asphalt**: they are walkable open floor in the sim, and dark asphalt reads as a traffic lane on this map. Flagged for founder review. | `render/arenas/lekkiRoads.ts` |

### NV - Forgiving navigation and touch controls

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NV1 | **Held-turn continuation (keyboard and gamepad).** A perpendicular turn asked for while it is blocked, with the way the player walks STILL held (a second key or D-pad button), keeps the player walking that way tile after tile; the turn is re-checked every step and taken on the first legal tile. Both directions are the player's own current input, so there is no reach limit and no danger veto. It ends when either is released; a reversal is never continued; Settings > Assisted movement Off keeps the old gate exactly. **Not on the touchscreen**: the thumb's lean is the approved 2-step corner route (MV3 / AS4) and must never become a slide along a wall (a regression test pins that); touch keeps its 2-tile early turn and corner route. | `input/moveGate.ts` `alongHeld` |
| NV2 | **Gamepad D-pad: newest button wins** and every held D-pad direction is reported (as with keys), so rolling the thumb from UP onto UP+RIGHT asks for RIGHT while UP stays held (NV1). Raids still send one direction (the newest). The analogue stick is unchanged. | `input/gamepad.ts`, `input/InputManager.ts` |
| NV3 | **Settings > Control size (Normal / Large)**: Large scales the floating stick by 1.15 (radius cap 83 px; dead / release zones keep their share) and the A / B cluster by 15 %. On a landscape phone the cluster stays inside its 36vh strip (no tile is covered); there A grows 23 -> 26vh and B 20.5 -> 23vh, still diagonal and apart. Default Normal (unchanged layout). The raid cross keeps its own sizing. | `ui/TouchControls.tsx`, `ui/styles.css`, `input/joystick.ts` |
| NV4 | **Settings > Vibration (On / Off)**: a 12 ms tick when a touch action button goes down, 40 ms when the local player is hurt or dazed, a short pattern when knocked out. Only through `navigator.vibrate` (Android Chrome; iOS and desktops have none), only for touch play, never for keyboard / gamepad. Presentation only. | `platform/haptics.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts` |

### CL-L - City Rumble Combat Test Lab (temporary, `docs/COMBAT_TEST_LAB.md`)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CL-L1 | **Local City Rumble only, off until picked.** `setCombatLabLevel` refuses every match that is not a City Rumble ruleset or that has a campaign mission or raid; the app calls it between ticks from the pause menu. Online code is untouched. A match starts with the lab off (today's rules), so no level is preselected. The picked level carries over to rematches and later local Rumble matches in the same page session. | `sim/systems/combatLab.ts`, `app/MatchController.ts` |
| CL-L2 | **One combat system, four data presets.** `COMBAT_LAB_PRESETS` scales and caps every catalogue attack's startup, hit / whiff recovery and same-target lockout from the match's own numbers (never slower), and switches the reach shape, aim help, press buffer and air swing. Each fighter keeps their attack (push / shove / daze) and its daze, displacement and immunity. | `sim/config.ts`, `combatLab.ts` |
| CL-L3 | **Reach shapes never pass walls.** Level 2 adds the forward corners (one open side tile needed), Level 3 the tile two ahead (open tile between), Level 4 the tiles two ahead and one aside (an open route). Aim help (Levels 2-4) turns the fighter to a rival in reach on another side. | `combatLab.ts` `labReach`, `skills.ts` |
| CL-L4 | **Every accepted press swings.** A punch draws its swing at the press (`punchSwing`); with nobody in reach and nothing else for B to do, the press is a visible swing at the air and a miss. | `skills.ts` `beginAirSwing`, `actions.ts` |
| CL-L5 | **Presses are kept, not ignored.** A B press during recovery or a short daze is kept for 10 / 12 / 15 / 15 ticks and done the moment it can be; one that outlives the buffer is counted as dropped. | `actions.ts` `labSpecial`, `retryKeptSpecial`, `tick.ts` |
| CL-L6 | **No stun-lock, no loops.** The victim's daze immunity is untouched at every level; Level 4 mashed every tick for 10 s keeps the victim dazed under 50 % of the time (tested). Hit counts come from sim ticks, never frame rate. | `tests/combat-lab.test.ts` |
| CL-L7 | **B hold stays the Remote Detonator.** No hold-to-repeat: the input layer decides tap vs hold before the sim; rapid punching is rapid tapping, protected by the buffer. | `input/specialHold.ts` (unchanged) |
| CL-L8 | **Visible but out of the way.** A small "LAB n Name" chip under the minimap; the picker and the press diagnostics live in the pause menu; nothing is drawn over the board. Bots use the same rules through `chooseAttack`. | `ui/CombatLab.tsx`, `app/hud.ts` |
| CL-L9 | **Hit confirmation and a look per level; no rule or number changed.** The founder found levels 1 and 2 almost identical: both drew the same white arc and ring and the chip said only the name, while the measured differences are small (startup 1 tick, a recovery hidden behind the same-rival lockout, an equal Punch miss recovery; see the Findings in `docs/COMBAT_TEST_LAB.md`). Presentation only, from existing events and only behind `config.combatLab`: a coloured swing fan sized by the reach, a coloured ring / flash / dust / flinch / bounded shake on a hit, a grey thin arc on a miss, a thud from the existing `gen_drop` recipe, a plain-words "what this level changes" line in the pause menu and a second line on the chip, all from `COMBAT_LAB_PRESETS`. The four presets, their order and default, the campaign, raids, enemy fairness values and the Oshodi pilot are untouched; a retune of level 2 is only proposed in the doc. | `render/combatLabLook.ts`, `render/views/CombatPilotViews.ts`, `render/fx/Effects.ts` `swing`, `audio/combatLabCue.ts`, `app/combatLabText.ts`, `ui/CombatLab.tsx` |

### PS - Fighter select and story-scene presentation

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PS1 | **The fighter screen says plainly that its roster is the playable heroes**: a "Playable fighters · 3" label (the count is the registry's) and the group label "Playable fighters". The fine print adds that the district bosses appear in the campaign and are not playable. No layout redesign; the three approved portraits stay. | `ui/menu/FighterScreen.tsx`, `ui/menu/flow.css` |
| PS2 | **RADIO lines are short radio-message cards**: a green-edged dispatch card with a three-bar signal mark and "on the radio". It is distinct from the broker's blue phone card and has no portrait. CSS only, no new asset. | `ui/campaign/lines.tsx`, `speakers.ts`, `campaign.css` |
| PS3 | **Read more beside a picture: the text column scrolls by itself.** With Read more open (`reading` class) on a wide screen or in phone landscape, the story card fills the screen and only its text column scrolls. The picture stays in view and the last line can be read. Collapsed cards and the phone-portrait single column are unchanged. | `ui/campaign/CardView.tsx`, `storyHero.css` |
| PS4 | **A playable hero speaks beside their approved menu portrait** (48 px, the existing `fighter-*-360.webp`), recognised by the registry name. No current line uses it; this is the path for approved hero dialogue. | `ui/campaign/speakers.ts`, `lines.tsx` |
| PS5 | **No boss portraits and no cropping.** Boss and other speakers stay named text lines. The boss introduction art is a group arrival scene and is never cropped into a character picture. The Gemini group portrait is concept art and is not in the game. | `speakers.ts` |
| PS6 | **Left for separate approval**: new Gemini / Google Flow art, final character artwork, hero dialogue lines, the revised storyline and a multi-panel comic sequence. Today a scene is one card with one optional picture (`STORY_IMAGES`, one key per card), and the Read-more lines are its panels. A panel sequence would extend the registry to several keys per card; nothing here blocks that. | n/a |

### ML - Immediate match loading and orientation feedback (`docs/MATCH_LOADING.md`)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ML1 | **A tap is acknowledged before any heavy work.** Every match entry (Quick play, Start Match, Rematch, campaign mission, raid) goes through `MatchLoader.run`, which publishes the loading screen and waits for it to paint (two animation frames, 250 ms timer fallback) before `createGame` / the scene build runs. | `app/matchLoader.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| ML2 | **Real states only.** Starting / models (real "done / total" from the boot preload) / building the arena / first frame / failed. No progress bar, no invented percentage. | `matchLoader.ts` `loadStep`, `ui/MatchLoading.tsx` |
| ML3 | **Never an endless wait.** The model wait stops after 20 s (every model has a drawn fallback); the first-frame wait after 12 s; the screen is then removed regardless. | `DEFAULT_LOADER_OPTIONS` |
| ML4 | **Held from the tap until the new match is drawn.** The controller does not step the simulation while the loading screen is up (a field mission whose door asked for a raid does not play on behind it; a failure screen keeps it held until Retry / Back / leaving), and resets its frame clock on release, so shader compiles do not eat the countdown. Render-side only; `src/sim` is unchanged. | `app/MatchController.ts` `setLoadHold` |
| ML5 | **One start at a time.** Taps while a start is in flight or the failure screen is up are ignored; Quick play is no longer disabled during the boot preload (the count is shown instead). | `matchLoader.ts` `busy`, `ui/menu/HomeScreen.tsx`, `ReadyScreen.tsx` |
| ML6 | **A failed start says why, with Retry and Back.** Retry runs the same start again; Back returns to the title menu (or the campaign map for a mission / raid). | `matchLoader.ts`, `ui/MatchLoading.tsx` |
| ML7 | **Upright phone: one screen, both messages.** The loading screen leads with the turn-sideways instruction, loading line underneath; the ordinary rotate notice is suppressed while it is up and takes over afterwards. | `ui/MatchLoading.tsx`, `ui/App.tsx` |
| ML8 | **Audio never blocks the start.** The audio unlock stays synchronous in the tap; the loader never awaits audio. Lightweight screen: menu fonts and colours, the WAHALA! mark and a CSS pulse (reduced-motion aware), no artwork. Resume from Pause, `resumeField`, the Training Academy and the attract match are not wrapped (no set-up to wait for / no tap to acknowledge). | `app/session.ts`, `ui/matchLoading.css` |

## VF - Follow-camera view: notices off the centre, scenery fades off the fighter (branch `claude/wahala-polish-vis`, presentation only)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| VF1 | **City Rumble notices hang on the left edge, not top-centre.** Checkpoint steps, streak progress (QUICK HANDS ...), BACK IN / spawn protection, stage alerts, the arena rule, status toasts and subtitles are one column under the Rumble panel, as wide as the panel, clipped above the play zone (and the touch pad). Texts are unchanged. Standard arenas keep their rail beside the board. | `ui/HudRail.tsx` (`rail-edge`), `ui/hudZones.ts`, `ui/HudRail.css` |
| VF2 | **The play zone is a rule, not a guess.** `playZone` is the screen region around the followed fighter (slightly below the middle, scaled with the smaller screen side); `noticeColumn` never lets the column enter it, and in portrait stops it above the fighter. Tested for desktop, phone landscape and portrait sizes. | `ui/hudZones.ts`, `tests/ui/hudZones.test.ts` |
| VF3 | **The Sudden Death announcement is untouched.** It keeps its own top-centre column (`.rail-top`, same geometry and contents as before); only the toasts moved out of it. | `ui/HudRail.tsx` (`hud-rail-sd`) |
| VF4 | **Scenery that hides the followed fighter fades, found by a line test.** The old rule dissolved only the obstacle on the tile(s) straight south (cover: up to a fixed reach), so a tall building a few columns over, or taller than its declared reach, still hid the fighter under the 41 degree follow camera. Now the line camera -> fighter (feet, torso, head) is tested against each cover group's world box and the per-tile pillars / breakables near the line. Only objects the line crosses (with a 0.2 margin) fade; the old rule stays as it was. | `render/assets/viewClear.ts`, `render/views/ArenaView.ts`, `render/arenas/boardCover.ts` (`bounds`) |
| VF5 | **No flicker, no lingering dots.** Hysteresis: a fading object stays faded inside a 0.5 margin, a new one needs 0.2. It dissolves in over 0.12 s and is solid again within about 0.05 s once the line is clear. Same dither as the pillars (foot stays solid, shadows and depth correct, no transparency sorting). Runs every third frame, on bounding boxes and a handful of tiles. | `ArenaView.ts` (`HIDE_RELEASE_TAU`, `VIEW_CLEAR_EVERY`) |
| VF6 | **Quality: `viewClear` is a live budget.** 0 on the two Very Low rungs (only the old cheap rule runs), 1 elsewhere. A manual preset is never changed by Auto. | `render/quality.ts`, `GameRenderer.applyQuality` |

### VF-B - The follow camera never hides the fighter or what is around it (branch `claude/wahala-fu-vis`, presentation only)

Founder report: tall buildings in City Rumble (Lekki above all) still block the view of the player. The VF4-VF6 line test had been checked at one Lekki position at desktop size. Causes found with the real-camera harness (`scripts/view-clear-matrix.mjs`: a real Rumble match, the real follow camera, the fighter placed on a grid of tiles, 844 x 390 and 390 x 844 phones and desktop, quality Auto / lowest / highest; every metric is measured from the camera's own pose by ray-casting the scenery triangles and by comparing a fighter-only render with the frame):

1. **The fade was off exactly where phones run.** `viewClear` was 0 on the two Very Low rungs, and Auto starts there on a software renderer, a Mali below G52, Adreno 2xx-5xx, PowerVR, <= 2 GB, <= 2 cores, WebGL 1 (`render/perf/capabilities.ts`), and drops there under load. Only the old straight-south rule ran.
2. **One line to the fighter was the wrong question.** A building beside or in front of the fighter hides the floor around it (rivals, flames, blast lanes), and a big roof can sit between the camera and the fighter although neither the "south tile" rule nor its reach catches it. The old Lekki matrix at phone size (Auto, 33 positions): the fighter completely hidden at two positions (a roof and a building face, only the "YOU" tag showed) and the floor disc around it hidden at 7; at the fixed 0.72 dissolve a faded building was still a mesh over the fighter.
3. Smaller: the test used the tile position, not the interpolated render position, and a quarter of a second could pass between a step and the fade.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| VF-B1 | **The decision is made in screen space, from the real camera.** What must stay readable: the fighter (torso, head, feet), a disc of walkable floor around it (rings at 0.9 / 1.7 / 2.5 tiles, 12 points each, only points on floor and inside the frame by the real view-projection matrix), and live rivals / generators within 4 tiles (at most 10). A box that stands between the camera and any of these points fades: that is "its projection overlaps the point and it is nearer than the point". The floor disc only counts for a box that stands in front of the fighter (its closest point to the camera is at least 0.25 nearer than the fighter): a hut right behind the fighter hides the floor behind itself and stays solid (found in the staged blast, where the first version dissolved it). Rivals and the fighter's own lines have no such rule. Low boxes (breakables, 1.1 high) only protect the inner rings, so a street of crates does not dissolve. The point is the render position (interpolated), not the tile. | `render/assets/viewClear.ts` (`viewPoints`, `pickOccludersFor`, `regionTiles`), `render/views/ArenaView.ts` |
| VF-B2 | **Cheap by construction.** Bounding boxes (cover groups, pillars and crates in the corridor between fighter and camera), about 50 points, an allocation-free slab test and a hull pre-filter: no raycast against meshes. | same |
| VF-B3 | **On every quality rung.** `QualityProfile.viewClear` (0 / 1) is replaced by `viewClearStride` (frames between tests): 2 on the four lowest rungs, 1 above. It is never switched off; a manual preset is never changed by Auto. Amends VF6. | `render/quality.ts`, `GameRenderer.applyQuality`, `tests/render/quality-ladder.test.ts` |
| VF-B4 | **Stronger dissolve for what hides the fighter.** An object picked by the view test dissolves to 0.88 (`VIEW_HIDE`), the pillar rule keeps 0.72: at phone resolution the fighter has to read through it. Foot stays solid, dither and shadows as before. | `render/assets/occlusionFade.ts`, `ArenaView.updateOcclusion` |
| VF-B5 | **No flicker, back at once.** Hysteresis: a fading object keeps fading inside a wider margin (0.3 for the body lines, 0.08 for floor points, which sit at the foot of the wall next to a lane) and over an outer halo ring (2.8 tiles, 0.3 beyond the disc); a new one needs the narrow margin. A debounce (`holdMisses`): an object is released only after missing two tests in a row, so a sub-tile step along an edge never blinks it (a first version with a 3.3 halo kept a building dissolved for a fighter standing still 3.6 tiles from it, found by the e2e spec). Released objects go solid in about 0.05 s. | `viewClear.ts`, `ArenaView.ts` |
| VF-B6 | **Unchanged on purpose.** The old straight-south rule (for every live player, generator and pickup), the camera, the HUD notices, traffic, combat. Surrounding scenery outside the board cover is not made fadeable: the matrix found nothing there that covers the fighter (see the report). | - |

Real-camera results (SwiftShader frames, Auto = rung 0 "Very Low"; "hidden" = scenery covers at least 40 % of the fighter's torso / head lines, or of the floor-disc lines; the wait per position is 8-14 drawn frames, never seconds: a first run with a fixed 1.4 s wait measured the fades mid-way and overstated the old failures, so it is not quoted):

| Matrix (positions) | Fighter hidden before -> after | Floor disc hidden before -> after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lekki, phone landscape 844 x 390, Auto (33) | 2 -> 0 | 7 -> 0 |
| Lekki, phone portrait 390 x 844, Auto (33) | 2 -> 0 | 7 -> 0 |
| Lekki, desktop 1280 x 720, Auto (11) | 0 -> 0 | 3 -> 0 |
| Lekki, phone landscape, High (4) | 0 -> 0 | 1 -> 0 |
| Oshodi / Ebute Metta, phone landscape (12 / 13) | 0 / 1 -> 0 / 0 | 3 / 4 -> 0 / 0 |
| Fadeyi, Lagos Island, Ojuelegba (8 / 12 / 6), after only | - | 0 / 0 / 0 (fighter never hidden) |
| Mushin (12), after only | - | 1: a row of crates beyond the inner rings, by design |
| Walk along Lekki row 9, phone landscape / portrait, 30 fps | 37 of 132 frames (28 %, longest 400 ms) / 37 of 131 (367 ms) -> 0 / 0 | 29 / 26 frames -> 0 / 0 |
| Walk along Lekki row 25, desktop | 26 of 138 frames (133 ms) -> 0 | 19 -> 0 |

The two worst old positions (a roof and a building face right in front of the camera, only the "YOU" tag visible) and a staged blast are in `docs/screenshots/view-clear-2/` (`*_before.jpg`, `*_after.jpg`). Nothing outside the board cover or the arena's pillars blocked the fighter at any of about 250 positions (no hit on the surrounding scenery), so the surroundings stay as they are (VF-B6).

Debug: `__WAHALA__.viewClear()` returns `{ stride, groups, tiles, points, fading }` (what the fade is doing now). Scripts: `scripts/view-clear-matrix.mjs` (grid of positions), `scripts/view-clear-walk.mjs` (a walk at a fixed 30 fps game clock, per-frame), `scripts/view-clear-fight.mjs` (a staged fight and blast); screenshots in `docs/screenshots/view-clear-2/`.

### TN - Classic raid touch controls and guidance (branch `claude/wahala-polish-touch`)

Founder report: the raid's four-arrow cross felt inconsistent and frustrating next to the open-field thumbstick. Causes found (all in the input / HUD layers, none in the sim): (1) the arrows were four small fixed targets in a corner, so a thumb that drifted off an arrow released the direction and a turn meant sliding across a seam or lifting; (2) a raid ran the move gate with no `legal` and no assist, so a turn pressed one tile before an opening was spent on the wall (a bump) and the player stopped, where the open field carries it one tile (decision WA2); on a pillar grid with an opening every second tile that is most turns; (3) a press made during a daze, slide or arrival was dropped (the lock buffer, decision MI1, needs Beginner Assist) and a press waited only 10 ticks for a step to finish; (4) nothing said that a move had been refused; (5) the raid HUD named no objective at all (the sim's `RaidView.objective` was only used by the world markers).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TN1 | **A raid uses the floating thumbstick, like every other match; the arrows are gone.** The stick already quantises the thumb to one cardinal direction with a dead zone, hysteresis and a following origin; in a raid `setTouchDir` drops the diagonal wish, so the grid stays strict (one cardinal tile per step, no diagonal, no corner route, no slide). Nothing moves: A and B stay where they are. `ui/DpadControl.*` was removed; `input/dpad.ts` (the `CrossPad` model, `crossLayout`) stays for its tests and for a future D-pad. | `ui/TouchControls.tsx`, `input/InputManager.ts` |
