| TN2 | **A thumb on a raid grid gets the open field's plain grid-turn forgiveness; keys and gamepad are untouched.** Only while the last device is touch: the one-tile corner-turn carry (`legal`, `TURN_BUFFER_TILES` = 1: a turn held while walking is taken at the next opening, never a skipped tile or a diagonal), a press made while the fighter cannot act is kept for `LOCK_BUFFER_TICKS` and walked once they can, and a press waits `ASSIST_BUFFER_TICKS` (14) instead of 10 for a step to end. There is still no assisted route, sidestep, bend or settle in a raid (RR8). | `input/InputManager.ts` (`gridTouch`), `input/moveGate.ts` |
| TN3 | **A refused move is shown as a short bump, presentation only.** On the tick a raid request is handed over and `moveWouldAct` says the simulation will not act on it (and the fighter is neither mid-step nor locked), the app raises a cue (direction + counter); the renderer shoves the drawn fighter about a tenth of a tile towards the obstacle and back (the damage flinch curve, 0.2 s). A held request repeats at most every 30 ticks, a new press or direction cues at once, reduced motion and pause switch it off. No sim change, no new event, no text, nothing on the board. | `app/blockedMove.ts`, `render/views/bumpModel.ts`, `render/GameRenderer.ts` |
| TN4 | **The raid rail names what to do next.** First block of the HUD rail (`ui/RaidGoal.tsx`, the field-goal plate): tag (DESTROY / EXIT), target (MARKED CARGO / REACH THE EXIT), the count ("1 OF 3 DOWN"), an eight-way arrow with the distance in tiles to the nearest standing marked cargo or to the open exit, and at most two notes (next wave within 10 s, enemies on the board, the locked main door and what opens it, a bonus room open). Distance is the straight grid distance (walls can add some). Pure function of `RaidView` and the fighter's tile; null once the raid is over. | `app/raidHud.ts`, `app/hud.ts` (`raid`), `ui/RaidGoal.tsx`, `ui/HudRail.tsx` |

Tests: `tests/input-raid-touch.test.ts`, `tests/ui/raidGoal.test.ts`; specs `e2e/raid-render.spec.ts`, `e2e/campaign-expansion-smoke.spec.ts` (the cross checks became stick checks). Supersedes RR7 (the cross) and the "no corner-turn buffer" clause of RR8 for the touch thumb only.

## CC-F - Classic Battle close-combat feel (branch `claude/wahala-fu-classic`, from the release `453b1a0`)

Founder: Classic combat "still does not feel good"; the open-field (Level 2 Balanced) feel is better and should inform it; movement stays grid-based and the cross blast stays. Smallest effective change, no new tuning pass.

**Findings** (real sim, `danfoBusPark` 15 x 11, a player tapping B every 6 ticks next to a rival who stays put, 30 s; 60 ticks = 1 s). Classic ran with the lab absent (Off): Punch startup 4 / hit recovery 60 / same-target lockout 180, a B press made while recovering is ignored, a miss does nothing, impact = a white ring.

| Measure (Street Baller Punch unless said) | Classic today (Off) | Level 2 feel |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gap between two hits on one rival | 186 ticks (3.1 s) | 115 ticks (1.9 s) |
| Hits in 30 s | 10 | 16 |
| Lekki Boy Daze Punch: gap / hits in 30 s | 258 ticks (4.3 s) / 7 | 182 ticks (3.0 s) / 10 |
| B at nobody in 10 s | 0 swings, nothing shown | 55 visible swings |
| Press made while recovering | ignored | kept 12 ticks, thrown once |
| Hit confirmation | white ring (a miss shows nothing) | cyan fan at the press, ring, flash, flinch, thud; a miss is a thin grey arc, dust, quiet swish |
| Aim help | an adjacent rival on another side was already faced; nothing else to help | same (cardinal only), see CC-F3 |

Why it felt worse: a 3 s recovery between hits on the same rival, a press during it silently lost, and no sign of a hit or a miss (the white ring was the only cue and a whiff had none).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CC-F1 | **A local Classic Battle (`game: 'classic'`, started by `MatchController.start`) runs the Level 2 feel as part of its ruleset.** The same mechanism as the lab (`config.combatLab` + scaled `config.skills.attacks` + the kept-press runtime), pinned to a preset derived from `COMBAT_LAB_PRESETS[2]` (so it cannot drift; no lab value was edited). Applied by `applyClassicCombat` right after `createGame`; `createGame` itself, tests' sandboxes, City Rumble with the lab off, missions, raids, online and the Sudden Death test match never get it. Not switchable, not shown as LAB (no chip, no pause-menu section: `combatLabAllowed` stays false for Classic, `CombatLabState.classic`). | `CLASSIC_COMBAT_PRESET` (config.ts), `applyClassicCombat` (systems/combatLab.ts), `MatchController.start`, `hud.ts` |
| CC-F2 | **Timing and buffer = Level 2**: Punch 3 / 24 / 108 (startup, hit recovery, lockout), Shove and Daze Punch scaled the same way, a kept B press of 12 ticks, a visible swing at the air. One press is one punch (the buffer is a single slot, cleared when thrown). | `labAttackSpec` |
| CC-F3 | **Reach stays the four cardinal neighbours.** Level 2's forward-corner reach is a classic exception (`forwardDiagonals: false`): no diagonal, no two-tile reach; aim help only turns to a rival on another of the four sides. Movement and the cross blast are untouched. | `CLASSIC_COMBAT_PRESET` |
| CC-F4 | **Hit confirmation is Level 2's**: the swing fan at the press (one tile, narrow: no corner reach to show), cyan ring / flash / dust / flinch on a hit, grey arc on a miss, `gen_drop` thud and quiet swish (all synthesised), camera jolt bounded by `render/shake.ts` (off on touch and with reduced motion). | `CombatPilotViews.ts`, `eventRouter.ts` |
| CC-F5 | **Raids and field missions: presentation only, no number changes.** The human's punch gets the same fan at the press, the ring / flash / flinch on a hit, the grey puff on a miss and the hit / miss sounds, driven by the existing `attackStarted` / `attackResolved` events (`feelPresetFor`: only a seat without `enemyKind`, and never in the Oshodi combat pilot). No `config.combatLab` there, so the human's punch timing, `config.enemyAttacks`, `config.campaign.*` and the enemy fairness work (docs/ENEMY_COMBAT_FAIRNESS.md) are unchanged. Enemy attacks keep their own wind-up rings. | `feelPresetFor`, `CombatPilotViews.hasCombatPilot` |
| CC-F6 | **Touch**: B already fires on the press when no remote generator is live (`input/specialHold.ts`); with a live remote the tap waits for release by design (tap vs hold). The kept press is a sim buffer, so it adds no touch-specific path and cannot double-punch. Exactly two action buttons. | unchanged input layer |
| CC-F7 | **Digests.** The bot-vs-bot multiplayer digest (`scripts/lib/mpDigest.ts`, pinned in `tests/enemy-fairness.test.ts`) builds games through `createGame` and so does not see Classic's feel: both pinned values are unchanged (no deliberate digest change). Online seats and bot batches in `ai/headless.ts` are unchanged for the same reason (a Classic bot batch with the feel needs `applyClassicCombat`). | - |

Tests: `tests/classic-combat-feel.test.ts`.

## Tactical Trap pilot (branch `claude/wahala-tactical-trap-v1`, docs/TACTICAL_TRAP_PILOT.md, decisions TT1-TT10)

Why: campaign enemies escaped generators too reliably (EF1: a planner that sees every generator) while punches finished them faster than generators. One isolated, reversible pilot makes the generator the tactical weapon. It exists only in the Training Academy "Generator Tactics" scenarios (`config.tacticalTrap`, absent everywhere else: tested for every ruleset, mission, raid and Rumble mode).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TT1 | **Seen, not omniscient.** With the key, an enemy knows a generator only if it is within its kind's vision radius and the grid line of sight is clear (two Bresenham traces between tile centres, ends excluded; one clear trace is enough; any non-floor tile and any other living body blocks; a body on the generator's tile hides it; the engine has no low cover tile). Remote, triggered, disarmed, thrown, carried and own generators stay always known. The planner sees the rest as obstacles with an unreachable fuse (the EF1 mechanism). | `systems/sight.ts`, `ai/trapSight.ts`, `ai/enemyBot.ts` |
| TT2 | **Memory.** A seen generator is taken seriously `reactTicks` after it was first seen and remembered (position, real fuse) while out of sight for `memoryTicks`; a ticking generator with <= `memoryHoldFuseTicks` of fuse is held until it blows (so with the 3 s fuse a seen generator is never forgotten); one that moved out of sight is forgotten. Walking in front of it never erases the awareness. | `TrapSight` |
| TT3 | **Hearing = caution.** A generator laid within `hearingTiles` (Manhattan) is heard on the tick it appears: no new wind-up or cue for 36 ticks, reaction to newly visible things halved, one investigating step along the sound's dominant axis. Never its tile, never its blast zone. | `TrapSight`, `EnemyBot.investigate` |
| TT4 | **Readable commitment.** `enemyAttackPhase` (idle / windup / attack / recovery) from the sim state. Pilot recovery 48 ticks (0.8 s) for agbero, guard, thrower, heavy (lieutenant 30, boss 24); throw recovery 48 / 40 / 24 / 18. Every cooldown exceeds wind-up + recovery + 36 ticks (asserted), so no punch chain. A stagger already cuts a throw's cue short (existing rule). | `systems/tacticalTrap.ts`, `TACTICAL_TRAP_ENEMY_ATTACKS` |
| TT5 | **Slow stacking.** A landed human punch on an enemy: 1st 25 % slower for 90 ticks, 2nd raises it to the 40 % cap and keeps it 90 ticks from that hit, a 3rd does nothing, the chain ends 150 ticks after its first hit, then 120 ticks of resistance. Integer extra step ticks of the enemy's own step: base 10 -> +3 (13 ticks, 23.1 %) and +7 (17, 41.2 %); a 13-tick thrower +4 / +9. Reuses `Player.slow` / `slowImmuneUntil`; no new event. | `trapSlowOnHit`, `slowExtraStepTicks` |
| TT6 | **A punch cannot finish.** The human's punch takes an enemy down to `max(1, ceil(maxHp x share))` and no further (share 0 for agbero, guard, thrower, heavy; 0.5 for lieutenant and boss): it still staggers and slows. Only a blast (or another lethal source) finishes. Chosen over "0 damage" because the health bar still moves and a wounded enemy is a worse swimmer in a blast. | `trapPunchDamage`, `skills.ts campaignMelee` |
| TT7 | **The planner knows how slow it is.** With the key `ai/world.ts` adds the slow and the enemy kind's step penalty to the step time and the root (`rootedUntil`) to the ready tick, so a slowed or planted enemy plans escapes it can really walk. Without the key nothing changes. | `ai/world.ts` |
| TT8 | **Enemies keep off the human's tile** (as the Oshodi pilot, CP9, through `tacticalTrap.enemiesKeepOffHuman`): one standing on the generator tile would see it. `pilotBlocksStep` now reads either key. | `pilotCombat.ts` |
| TT9 | **Measured, not claimed** (tests/tactical-trap-evasion.test.ts, `scripts/lib/trapDrill.ts`): the same scripted trap hits the pilot's enemies in 18 of 48 (cover) and 48 of 48 (cover + two punches) runs against 0 of 48 for both perfect knowledge and the EF1 baseline; a free enemy that saw the generator is hit in at most 40 % of runs; one planted by its own swing in every run; leaving at once (no trap) catches nobody. Hits are not guaranteed. | tests |
| TT10 | **Isolation and removal.** Key absent everywhere but the three Academy lessons (new lesson ids only, `wahala.academy.v1` unchanged). `TutorialController` seats the real enemy bot for `Actor.enemyKind` seats only; the older lessons seat none. Remove the pilot by deleting `tacticalTrap` (type, `TACTICAL_TRAP_PILOT`, `tacticalTrapLayer`), `systems/sight.ts`, `systems/tacticalTrap.ts`, `ai/trapSight.ts`, `lessons/tactics.ts` and the guarded lines in `skills.ts`, `world.ts`, `enemyBot.ts`, `pilotCombat.ts`. | docs/TACTICAL_TRAP_PILOT.md |

## Generator Parts progression slice (branch `claude/wahala-stitch-uiux-v1`, docs/CAMPAIGN_GAMEPLAY_BIBLE.md section 2, decisions PR1-PR8)

Why: the founder asked for one simple campaign resource and the smallest useful progression slice, first on Oshodi, without touching competitive balance or player saves.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PR1 | **Parts are derived, not stored.** Earned = sum over eligible missions of `partsForStars(bestStars)` (0 / 3 / 4 / 5 for 0-3 stars). A first clear pays 3, each better best-star step pays 1 once, a replay at the same or fewer stars pays nothing: no farming by construction, and an older save gets exactly what its stars are worth. | `campaign/upgrades.ts` |
| PR2 | **Oshodi only.** `CAMPAIGN_PROGRESSION.districts = ['oshodi']` (approach, rail edge, boss: 9 to 15 Parts). Other districts earn nothing and apply nothing until verified; `enabled: false` turns the slice off without touching a save. | `campaign/upgrades.ts` |
| PR3 | **Seat bonus, mission matches only.** `PlayerSetup.loadoutBonus` raises the seat's base range and capacity (`systems/loadout.ts`). `createGame` drops it unless the ruleset is `mission`, so Classic, City Rumble, raids, tutorials and online can never carry one; enemies never get one; a zero or absent bonus leaves options and state byte-identical (tested). | `sim/state.ts`, `sim/systems/loadout.ts` |
| PR4 | **Two upgrades, cap 1 each, 6 Parts each.** Capacity +1 (the seat's capacity cap rises with it, so the Extra Socket still adds one) and range +1 (Petrol / Diesel build on it, radius cap unchanged). Finishing Oshodi at all (9) buys one; an average of two stars (12) buys both. Bought only between missions (refused during a mission, like the campaign level, CL4). | `campaign/upgrades.ts`, `app/campaign.ts` |
| PR5 | **No save-version change.** `CampaignSave.upgrades` is optional; repair clamps levels to the caps and refunds (range first) down to what the earned Parts pay for; a save that bought nothing keeps its exact shape. Rolling back to an older build keeps all progress and only forgets the bought levels (Parts are re-derived). | `campaign/save.ts` |
| PR6 | **Pickups stay temporary.** Respawn resets range and capacity to the seat's upgraded base (never below it); death drops compare against that base. | `systems/respawn.ts`, `systems/drops.ts`, `systems/pickups.ts` |
| PR7 | **Raids not included.** Door and standalone raids keep their own loadout (`raid/build.ts`) in this slice. | `app/campaign.ts` |
| PR8 | **Result feedback.** Section and boss results show the Parts the result paid (or how to earn more); the two Oshodi field missions show one "Ring weakened" line (`SectionMissionDef.ringWeakened`); a cleared district's map card shows its consequence once its existing consequence flags are set. | `ui/campaign/*` |

Tests: `tests/campaign-upgrades.test.ts`, `tests/app/campaign-upgrades-flow.test.ts`.

## Generator Parts across the campaign (branch `claude/wahala-csa-progress`, docs/CAMPAIGN_GAMEPLAY_BIBLE.md sections 2 and 5, decisions PR9-PR15)

Why: the founder asked to extend the Parts foundation (the only campaign currency) over the whole campaign in a controlled way, with visible world-state feedback, without touching competitive play, saves or the farming guard.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PR9 | **Every campaign mission earns, from content data.** `CAMPAIGN_PROGRESSION.districts = 'all'`: every section mission in `content/sections.ts` (field, standalone raid, boss) pays by best stars as in PR1 (3 / +1 / +1, at most 5). 18 missions today: 54 Parts at one star everywhere, 72 at two, 90 at three. A mission added to the content (the Ojuelegba finale) earns with no code change; a boss mission's stars are read from `districts[missionRef]`. The Oshodi-only list still works as a rollback config. For the finale: an Ojuelegba boss mission needs its stars in `districts.ojuelegba.bestStars` (today the hub is stored as cleared with 0 stars), field missions need only their `missions` record. Saves made under PR2 gain exactly what their other districts' stars are worth. | `campaign/upgrades.ts` |
| PR10 | **Level 2 at 14 Parts, cap +2 each.** Costs `[6, 14]` per upgrade, 40 for everything. Arithmetic at two stars (12 per district): 12 after Oshodi (both level 1), 36 after district 3 (short of 40), 40 on the first mission of district 4 (Ebute Metta): both level-2 upgrades are reachable around district 4 at average play. One star everywhere: 36 after district 4, enough during district 5; three stars: during district 3. The 50 Parts above 40 at a perfect run are deliberately unused (headroom for the finale missions and the proposed rooms, PR15). Radius cap and the seat's capacity cap rules are unchanged (PR4). | `campaign/upgrades.ts` |
| PR11 | **Bonus in every district's field and boss missions; raids still excluded.** `loadoutBonusFor` answers for any eligible district; the controller asks only in `startMission` (boss) and `startField`. Door and standalone raids keep the Classic raid loadout (`raid/build.ts`, ruleset `raid`, and `createGame` drops a bonus outside `mission` anyway): raids are compact Classic mazes with cross blasts and their own tuned loadout (PR7), and the bonus has never been measured there. Classic, City Rumble, online and the Academy never receive one (sim test per ruleset and per lesson, static source check that only `app/campaign.ts` mentions a bonus outside the sim and the campaign). | `app/campaign.ts`, tests |
| PR12 | **Free respec.** "Refund all (free)" on the Workshop returns every level (the `upgrades` field is removed); Parts are derived, so nothing is lost and buying again costs the same. Refused during a mission, like buying. No confirmation: it is harmless. | `refundUpgrades`, `CampaignController.refundUpgrades` |
| PR13 | **Save: no version change.** No new stored field. Repair still clamps levels to whole numbers within the caps; when the earned Parts cannot pay, it now refunds one level at a time, the highest level first (range on a tie), so a level 2 goes before a level 1. A pre-slice save and a slice save with both Oshodi upgrades load byte-identical; a v1 save migrates as before. The standalone raid result now reports the Parts it paid (it showed none). | `campaign/save.ts`, `upgrades.ts`, `app/campaign.ts` |
| PR14 | **World-state feedback, derived from the save.** Every section mission (all 18, boss sections included) has a short "Ring weakened" line (`SectionMissionDef.ringWeakened`; 16 lines written for this milestone, the two Oshodi field lines unchanged), shown on the section and boss result with the Parts paid, the Parts to spend (and a Workshop nudge when an upgrade is affordable) and an "Up next" line (`nextMission`, or the finale). The map node shows sections reclaimed (n/3) or FREED; the district card lists what the Ring lost there and keeps the PR8 consequence line. No world-state engine, no rule change. | `content/sections.ts`, `ui/campaign/*` |
| PR15 | **Bonus Parts for rooms and door raids: PROPOSED, not built.** A three-star door raid already earns its field mission's third star (+1 Part through PR1) and raid side rooms already pay their own bonus (health, Armour, a star token), so paying Parts again would count the same play twice; it would also need a stored one-time ledger. Left for a founder decision with the finale's design. | docs |

Tests: `tests/campaign-progression-wide.test.ts`, `tests/app/campaign-progression-wide-flow.test.ts`, `tests/ui/progressionScreens.test.ts`, updated `tests/campaign-upgrades.test.ts`, `tests/app/campaign-upgrades-flow.test.ts`, `e2e/campaign-progression.spec.ts`.

## First-visit onboarding (branch `claude/wahala-stitch-uiux-v1`, decisions OB1-OB5)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OB1 | **Immediate startup display** is static HTML inside `#root` (WAHALA!, Lagos City Rumble, Loading...): visible before any script, replaced by React's first render; no animation, nothing waits for it. | `index.html` |
| OB2 | **One offer on a first visit**: START TUTORIAL (the Academy's first beginner lesson, `openTutorial(0)`) or SKIP TO MENU, over Home; the adaptive-runtime line reads `render/perf/brand.ts` (FlowFrame today; approved W-AR art and name replace it there in one edit). | `ui/menu/FirstVisitCard.tsx` |
| OB3 | **Remembered locally** in `wahala.onboarding.v1` (localStorage; no cookies, no tracking). Returning players go straight to Home; blocked storage just shows the card again next time. | `app/onboarding.ts` |
| OB4 | **Automation sees Home as before** (`navigator.webdriver`) unless the URL carries `?onboarding`; the existing browser suite is unchanged, `e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` forces the card. | `app/onboarding.ts` |
| OB5 | **Home labels** follow the approved navigation names without moving anything: "Learn to Play" (Training Academy) and "Settings & Sound"; test ids unchanged. | `ui/menu/HomeScreen.tsx` |

Tests: `tests/onboarding.test.ts`, `e2e/onboarding.spec.ts`.

## Enemy telegraphs and looks (branch `claude/wahala-csa-looks`, docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md "Enemy telegraphs and looks", decisions SV1-SV5)

Why: founder testing - enemy punches were impossible to anticipate from the elevated camera on a phone. Presentation only: no rule, timing or sim field changed.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SV1 | **Telegraphs in every match with campaign enemies** (field and boss missions, raids, the Academy's Generator Tactics lessons), never in multiplayer (no `enemyKind` seats). The Oshodi pilot's enemy rings moved into the new view; `CombatPilotViews` keeps the human's swing feedback only. | `render/views/EnemyTelegraphViews.ts`, `render/GameRenderer.ts` |
| SV2 | **Read from sim state only, never from hard-coded durations**: a punch fills from the press to the LIVE `attack.resolveTick` (joined late: `resolveTick - windupTicks`), a throw over `launchTick..launchTick + warnTicks` of its projectile, the open window from `enemyAttackPhase` + `rootedUntil`, the stagger from `dazedUntil`. **Shape and motion carry the meaning** (solid ring + arrow + framed tile + head clock = punch; sight + marching chevrons + X = throw; broken cyan ring = open; thin ring + stars = stagger), every glyph has a baked dark outline, colours are not tone-mapped, tile marks and the head clock draw over bodies. One instanced draw per glyph (hidden while unused), shared geometry, no per-frame allocation; **not gated by quality** (Very Low draws the same). A missed punch plays the Stumble clip with dust. | `render/views/enemyTelegraph.ts`, `telegraphShapes.ts` |
| SV3 | **Wind-up warning sound**: synthesised `enemy_windup` (rising growl, punch) and `enemy_aim` (two rising clicks, throw) on the warning bus, each at most every 0.35 s and any two 0.15 s apart (a group winding up makes one warning); the throw whistle now waits for the flight (`warnTicks`). Humans and multiplayer bots keep the old breath. No recording. | `audio/recipes/gameplay.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts` |
| SV4 | **Variant looks** on the cast rigs: two-tone cloth palette, headwear, bone-attached props, silhouette scale, a gold leader ring for lieutenants and bosses (table in the doc). The Ring crews wear an invented plum-and-lime palette; nothing copies a real agency's uniform or insignia. One shared material, one merged mesh per dressed bone cached per variant and rig; the fallback model gets headwear and scale. **Temporary** until dedicated 3D production. | `assets/enemyLooks.ts`, `render/assets/enemyKit.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts` |
| SV5 | **No "armoured: only a blast finishes" glyph yet**: no general sim field says it (only the Academy pilot's `punchFloor`). Requested from the AI worker as `Player.punchProof` or `finishableByPunch(state, p)`. | docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md |
| SV6 | **Awareness marks** (branch `claude/wahala-csa-polish`): a brief billboard where the head clock sits, from the territorial layer - the `enemyCue` alert / investigate / lost events AND the `state.territory.minds` state changes (sight lost in a chase and a search that found nothing have no event): an orange warning TRIANGLE with a dark "!" (spotted you; pops, holds the alert beat clamped to 0.6-1.2 s, fades), a pale-blue ROUND plate with "?" (going to look; sways 1.1 s, fades), a grey round plate with three dots that sinks and fades in 0.9 s (lost you, going back). Shape carries the meaning; drawn as outlined geometry (no text, no texture), so it is not HUD text over the board. Never together with the wind-up clock (the clock wins); timed in ticks (a pause holds it); per-instance fade in the instanced glyph batch; reduced motion keeps only the fade. Not gated by quality. Pursuit has no mark (the chase itself reads). | `render/views/enemyAwareness.ts`, `EnemyTelegraphViews.ts`, `telegraphShapes.ts` |
| SV7 | **Awareness sounds**: synthesised `enemy_alert` (a dry two-note "hey!" stab rising a fourth, warning bus) at most every 0.6 s, so an ally's call that alerts a crew in one tick is one sting; `enemy_query` (a soft breathy glide up, effects bus, ambient priority) at most every 0.8 s and never within 0.4 s after a sting. `lost`, wind-up, recovery and stagger cues stay silent in the router (their sounds belong to `attackStarted` / `campaignHit` / `attackInterrupted`). No recording, voice or music. | `audio/recipes/gameplay.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts`, `public/audio/README.md` |
| SV8 | **Armour cue** (supersedes SV5, now that `enemyHealthView(...).blastOnly / punchFloor` exist): a steel-blue heater SHIELD as a badge at the left end of the name tag of an enemy only a blast can finish, shown with its pips; at its punch floor (`hp <= punchFloor`) the shield turns amber, pulses and gets a red-orange STARBURST behind it ("needs a blast"; a shape change, not colour alone). A boss / lieutenant (bar in the HUD rail, no world pips) shows it over its head only at the floor or just hurt. Two instanced draws at most. The rail's boss bar is unchanged (its snapshot lives in `src/app`, outside this branch). | `render/views/damageModel.ts` (`armourCue`), `DamageViews.ts` |

Tests: `tests/render/enemy-telegraphs.test.ts`, `tests/render/enemy-looks.test.ts`, `tests/audio/engine.test.ts` (wind-up warnings).

SV6-SV8 tests: `tests/render/enemy-awareness.test.ts`, `tests/audio/engine.test.ts` (awareness sounds), `tests/audio/soundAnalysis.test.ts`, `e2e/campaign-combat-readability.spec.ts`.

## SA - Campaign sector AI: the campaign tactics layer (branch `claude/wahala-csa-combat`, `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md`)

Why: founder direction 2026-11-18 (authorised campaign-wide, replacing the PROPOSED status of Gameplay Bible 1.4):
generators are the primary weapon, melee assists without a ritual, enemies are intelligent but readable and fair. SA1 and
SA2 are the sector contract (SA2: the variant roster in `src/sim/data/enemyVariants.ts`, inert until assigned).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SA3 | **One optional layer, campaign field and boss missions only.** `GameConfig.campaignTactics` (`CAMPAIGN_TACTICS`, Easy / Hard in `CAMPAIGN_TACTICS_LEVELS`, enemy commitment in `CAMPAIGN_TACTICS_ENEMY_ATTACKS`; `campaignTacticsLayer(level)`). `missionConfigLayer` adds Normal to every mission, `buildMissionGame` the Easy / Hard layer. Absent in raids, the Academy (its Tactical Trap pilot unchanged), Classic, City Rumble, multiplayer and online; their pinned digests are unchanged. The Oshodi combat pilot is SUPERSEDED: removed from the campaign; its code is kept as an archived replay behind `MissionSpec.archivedPilot` (its tests, `npm run combat-pilot`) because the views still read the key; its e2e spec was removed. Campaign behaviour changes deliberately (the Normal field mission now carries a config layer; Terminal Approach seats its own 3 enemies, not the pilot's 5). | `config.ts`, `campaign/levels.ts`, `mission/build.ts`, `mission/combatPilot.ts` |
| SA4 | **Perception, no omniscience at any level.** The pilot's grid sight (walls, crates, other bodies; a body on the tile hides it), memory and hearing, campaign-wide; a generator never seen is ABSENT from the planner's observation (not even an obstacle), one seen but not yet reacted to is an obstacle; hearing only ever yields one of 8 coarse direction bands (`noiseBand` / `investigateSpot`, tested with two hidden tiles per band); boss-mission generator bots perceive too (`seatPerception`). | `ai/trapSight.ts`, `ai/enemyBot.ts`, `ai/seatPerception.ts`, `systems/territory.ts` |
| SA5 | **Trap sense, never information.** Tiers none / basic / keen / expert: reaction scale, tunnel vision while brawling, a calm 90-degree view cone with a short `calmVisionTiles`, watching a placement in sight (keen+ on Normal, basic+ on Hard, never while staggered), the escape planner one or two bot levels sharper, no random hesitation. No random failure as a difficulty mechanic. | `config.ts` `trapSense`, `ai/enemyBot.ts` |
| SA6 | **One territorial state machine for every variant.** patrol / watch / investigate / alert / pursue / return in the sim, overlays wind-up / recovery / stagger / defeat; the leash is measured from the territory's anchors with hysteresis; giving up starts a watch facing the human (throwers keep throwing); a hit re-engages it with no leash for `hitChaseTicks`; allies within the variant's call radius are alerted; a watching guard sweeps its view; deterministic (no RNG). Through the directive pipeline (new kinds `patrol`, `investigate`, `return`). | `systems/territory.ts`, `ai/directive.ts`, `mission/field.ts`, `mission/fieldState.ts` |
| SA7 | **Readable commitment.** Ordinary wind-ups 60 / 48 / 38 ticks (Easy / Normal / Hard), recovery 48 / 42 / 36, cooldown > wind-up + recovery + 36 for every kind and level; 1 / 2 / 2 simultaneous wind-ups per group (gap 60 / 48 / 30); throw cue 54 / 42 / 36 + recovery; a thrower never throws while dodging, on the move (12 ticks settle) or from a calm state. One new event `enemyCue` (windup with tile and direction, recovery, stagger, alert, investigate, lost), emitted only under the layer; the audio router treats it as a no-op. | `config.ts`, `systems/enemyActions.ts`, `systems/projectiles.ts`, `systems/enemyCues.ts`, `types.ts` |
| SA8 | **Melee vs generator without a ritual.** The human's landed hit on an enemy, whatever the fighter: 1 segment, stagger 24 / 18 / 14 ticks (cuts the wind-up; then immune 48 / 54 / 60), 1-tile knockback when free, ONE 25 % slow for 90 ticks (never stacked or refreshed, then resisted 120), per-target lockout 60 / 72 / 84, the human's recovery capped at 30. Ordinary enemies (agbero 2, guard 3, thrower 2) can be punched out slowly; one blast defeats any of them; ARMOURED kinds (heavy 4, lieutenant 8, boss 14) stop at a punch floor (1, half, half) and lose 3 to a blast: "only a blast finishes armoured enemies" (`enemyHealthView.blastOnly`). Enemy frames 6 ticks (punch + blast both land); enemies keep off the human's tile, same-tile reach both ways. | `systems/skills.ts`, `systems/lethality.ts`, `systems/campaignDamage.ts`, `systems/campaignView.ts` |
| SA9 | **Difficulty is strategy, not health.** Easy: clockwork patrols, short pursuit, no ally calls, no group tactics or cover, slower senses, longer warnings and windows. Normal: varied patrol pauses, clear tells, meaningful recovery, group tactics. Hard: sharper senses, longer pursuit and search, ally calls x1.25, cover, reversing patrols, faster but fair reactions, placement-watching; never information about unseen generators. Enemy health is the same on every level. | `config.ts` `CAMPAIGN_TACTICS_LEVELS` |
| SA10 | **Variant personalities as data, first assignment on the 11 field missions.** `EnemyPersonality` (trap sense, pursuit bonus, notice scale, vision delta, ally-call radius, cover, preferred range, recovery bonus); `FieldEnemyDef.variant` / `.territory`; a variant's base kind replaces the role's kind. Pursuers keep their thrower kind (escape legs keep their pace) and their mission logic. Patrol routes are cleared of crates by the builder. The sector worker re-authors these. | `data/enemyVariants.ts`, `mission/fieldDefs.ts`, `mission/build.ts` |
| SA11 | **Telegraph contract** for the views: `Player.attack`, `rootedUntil`, `dazedUntil`, `slow`, `state.territory.minds[*]`, `enemyMindView`, `enemyHealthView.blastOnly / punchFloor`, and the events listed in `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md` section 8. | `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md` |
| SA12 | **Measured** (`npm run sector-ai`, `tests/campaign-tactics-drill.test.ts`): on Normal the corner ambush traps 15/16 and the bait 15-16/16 of basic enemies; every enemy that SAW a badly placed generator escapes it (16/16 at every level); Hard stops the under-the-feet bait (0/16 except the reckless area boy) where Easy allows it (16/16). Honest limits: measured on the Academy pillar board, not the irregular field maps; the counter-jab strategy is weak (0-7/16); boss-fight balance with perception is unmeasured. | `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md` section 9 |
| SA13 | **An enemy never plans an escape through the human it may not step on.** Under the campaign tactics layer (where the sim keeps enemies off the campaign human's tile, `pilotBlocksStep`), the enemy planner's walk map marks that tile closed, so it goes round instead of staying in a blast. Found by the district playthroughs (DB). Hunting still targets the human's tile (`blocked` unchanged); multiplayer bots, the Academy's Tactical Trap lessons and the archived Oshodi pilot plan exactly as shipped (tested). Campaign field missions retuned after it. | `ai/world.ts`, `tests/campaign-tactics-keepoff.test.ts` |

Tests: `tests/campaign-tactics-layer.test.ts`, `-perception`, `-combat`, `-territory`, `-drill`, `tests/architecture.test.ts` (perception), updated deliberately: `tests/oshodi-combat-pilot.test.ts` (archived replay), `tests/field-missions.test.ts`, `tests/combat-lab.test.ts`, `tests/audio/*`.

## District sectors (branch `claude/wahala-csa-sectors`, docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md "District-sector architecture", decisions DS1-DS10)

Why: field missions sat in a corner of a whole City Rumble district: too much map to wander, a camera free to show the city, a scene built for 10x the ground the route uses. Each field mission now plays in a recognisable part of its district, closed by street objects.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DS1 | **Keep the district, stamp a sector (option c).** The layout is untouched; `applySector` turns the barricade tiles to concrete and sets `GameState.sector` right after `placeSupplies`, before floor hazards, endgame traps and the stage. No random number is drawn; without a sector the key does not exist (every other match and the multiplayer digests unchanged). Field missions only (`FieldMissionDef.sectorId`); a boss sector later needs only a `MissionDef.sectorId` read in `missionSectorOf`. `?nosector` (debug) plays the whole district. | `sim/mission/sector.ts`, `sectors.ts`, `state.ts` |
| DS2 | **Barricades are concrete drawn as real objects, never soft crates, never invisible.** A blastable `x` would open the sector. Five kinds (danfo rank, jersey barrier + crowd rail + bollards, steel gate wall, rail buffer fence, Ring checkpoint kiosk + boom) on the open tiles just outside the rects, built as a board cover merged with the arena's (`mergeCovers`), so the occlusion and view-clear fades treat each as one object. | `render/views/SectorBarricades.ts`, `render/arenas/boardCover.ts` |
| DS3 | **Sizing.** Every sector holds 180+ walkable tiles and under half its district; two missions of a district share under 15 % of their tiles; every authored tile lies inside and every opening of the rects is closed (`sectorLeaks`, flood test). No marker, post, exit or door was moved and no enemy entry edited. | `tests/mission-sectors.test.ts` |
| DS4 | **Hazards stay whole or go.** Policy `inside`: a scheduled hazard runs only when every tile of its routes / zones is inside (a half-inside route would stop at a barricade; trimming would change the arena's rules); player-targeted strikes and drops fit; drops land inside only; a manhole with its far end outside carries nobody (the shared partner cache untouched); respawn spots are in-sector. In the eleven sectors every sweep is off. | `state.ts` `hazardFitsSector`, `systems/stage.ts`, `systems/respawn.ts` |
| DS5 | **Vehicles enter only through a border entry inside the sector** (data, not an arena-id branch; the sector is public in `Observation.sector` so bots see the same entries). None of the eleven sectors contains a lane mouth, so a voucher calls no vehicle there; field missions rarely give one. | `systems/entries.ts`, `observe.ts` |
| DS6 | **A sector's route never needs a blast.** Between consecutive route stops the path with the fewest crates (bucket Dijkstra, a crate costs 8 steps, fixed neighbour order) is cleared of crates at creation; crates elsewhere stay for cover and traps. Only with a sector. Why: a small area cannot offer the detours the whole district did (the Oshodi pilot's scripted walk was boxed in by crates). | `mission/fieldState.ts` `clearRoute` |
| DS7 | **Generator-sensitive legs.** `destroyTargets`: Ring equipment on soft tiles with hit points, one per separate blast set off by the HUMAN; vehicles, the closing city, enemy blasts and melee leave it standing; a piece wrecked before its leg counts when the leg comes. `holdArea`: time accrues while the human is inside and no enemy is (contested or away = frozen, never lost); a wave group holds its post until its time, then guards the zone. New events `fieldTargetHit` (cargo cues) and `sectorGateOpened`. Not assigned to existing missions (district worker). | `mission/equipment.ts`, `mission/field.ts`, `audio/eventRouter.ts` |
| DS8 | **Camera, minimap, render clip.** The follow shot carries the sector box; the rig keeps the HUD-free band within box + `max(viewMargin, its HUD margin)` (sides, south) / `viewMargin` (north), centring on a narrow axis; the overview frames the sector. The minimap crops to the sector clip. The renderer builds nothing outside the clip + 6 rows north / 3 sides / 2 south (cover objects, pillars, crates, ambient traffic / crowds / lights). Presentation only. | `render/cameraRig.ts`, `cameraDirector.ts`, `ui/minimapModel.ts`, `render/sectorClip.ts`, `views/ArenaView.ts` |
| DS10 | **The set's backdrop releases what it built.** Measured while checking sector reloads: every match start (any mode) left ~118 geometries on the GPU because `createBackdrop`'s dispose only freed its tracked parts. It now disposes every geometry in its tree and its per-match canvas boards (never the shared texture cache). About 48 library model textures per start remain (pre-existing, to be traced in the asset library). | `render/assets/backdrop.ts`, `textures.ts` `isSharedTexture` |
| DS9 | **Gates between legs.** A sector gate is a barricade that opens (concrete to floor, its object hidden) when a given leg is completed. Mechanism and tests only; no sector uses one yet. | `sector.ts` `openSectorGates`, `SectorBarricades.ts` `sync` |

Tests: `tests/mission-sectors.test.ts`, `tests/field-sector-legs.test.ts`, `tests/render/camera-sector.test.ts`, `tests/render/sector-render.test.ts`, `e2e/campaign-sectors.spec.ts`.

## BX - Campaign bosses: generator-first boss loop (branch `claude/wahala-csa-bosses`, `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md` "Part 3. Bosses")

Why: founder direction 2026-11-18: preserve the six boss mechanics and identities; generators must be essential and the
vulnerabilities understandable; bosses must not rely on repetitive melee. Measured before (commit `4f2d2a9`, the same scripted
player with the old verbs, real crew bots): a generator player won 4 of 36 boss fights (Easy / Normal / Hard), most stuck
in phase 1-2; the objectives were "stand on it", so a pure punch player cleared all three Lekki phases and reached
every Lekki exposure; the exposed boss simply fled (the four wins were lucky blasts on a fleeing boss).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| BX1 | **Every broken phase exposes the boss (a boss RECIPE, data).** `MissionDef.recipe` (`BossRecipe`, `src/sim/mission/boss.ts`): hits 3, `exposeSec` per level, `maxExposures` 5, `anchor` (post / node broken last), walk grace 12 s, restore radius 1, interrupt 1.5 s x 2, reel 1.5 s, crew call, escort cover. Exposed, the boss walks to his anchor (`block` directive) and is COMMITTED there (rooted until the window ends: his planner lays nothing he cannot walk away from); the countdown waits up to the walk grace while he walks. A window that runs out re-shields him and rebuilds the SAME phase (was: exposure only after phase 3, three windows, phase 3 repeated). Oga Sunmola, Marshal Kobo, Ikenna Wayside and Barrister Quill restore at their post; Madam Ronke and Halcyon come to the node broken last. | `mission/boss.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `directives.ts`, `defs.ts` |
| BX2 | **A blast costs the exposed boss one hit; the third eliminates him.** `rules.missionBossAbsorbs` (asked by lethality after the shield check): any lethal event that reaches him while exposed (a blast, a vehicle, a train) is one hit per window; he reels, re-shields (`bossShielded` `phase`) and the next phase starts (`bossHit` event); the last hit is the ordinary elimination (`bossDefeated`). His own generator never costs a hit. The boss stays a generator bot without `Vitals`. | `rules.ts`, `systems/lethality.ts`, `runtime.ts` |
| BX3 | **Melee interrupts, never decides.** A landed punch of the human's on the exposed boss lengthens the window by 1.5 s, at most twice a window (`bossInterrupted`); it never takes a hit (measured: punching for a whole window leaves him at 3 hits). Crew can still be punched (stagger, push) as before. | `runtime.ts updateExposure` |
| BX4 | **Every boss objective node is broken by the human's BLASTS** (one blast counts once, a crew blast never): relays are jammed by a blast; Fadeyi junctions have a toll box that breaks to a blast while no marshal stands in its zone (a marshal inside shields it); Ebute switches throw to a blast only inside the open rail window; Lagos ledgers sit in cabinets a blast opens, then are picked up by stepping on and carried; Lekki systems are blasted in the signalled order (a blast on a wrong one calls the contingency strike and resets). Standing on a node does nothing. Re-tuned for the fuse: Oshodi runners re-arm in 4 s (was 1.5), Ebute's phase-3 reset is 40 s (was 25). | `objectives/*.ts`, `defs.ts` |
| BX5 | **Toll Season** became "every toll box takes one more blast" (the stand-in-the-zone hold it multiplied no longer exists). The other five replay modifiers are unchanged. | `tuning.ts` |
| BX6 | **Levels.** Window 26 / 20 / 15 s (Easy / Normal / Hard; travel plus a 3 s fuse always fits), crew called back when a window opens 0 / 1 / 2 (telegraphed reinforcement on the seat's own post, outside the mission's budget), escorts body-block the line to the human on Hard only. Read from `config.campaignTactics.level`; never information about unseen generators. | `boss.ts`, `state.ts` |
| BX7 | **Lagos Island pacing:** a dropped ledger returns to its (open) cabinet; the phase-2 press moved from (30,21), one step from the boss's post, to (31,28) outside his guard ring; phase 3 asks 3 ledgers (was 4; Audit Week still adds one); clock 480 s (was 420), par 320 s (was 280). | `defs.ts`, `objectives/evidence.ts` |
| BX8 | **Readable crews and boss attacks.** Every boss-mission crew seat names a variant (`CrewDef.variant`, `config.campaignTactics.seatVariant`): its trap sense (reaction, placement watching) and vision for the generator-bot perception, never information. The enemy telegraph view also reads boss-mission seats (`telegraphKindOf`: the boss as a boss, the crew by variant), so a boss or crew punch shows the same wind-up as any enemy's; a restore ring marks the anchor while the boss is exposed. HUD: "BLASTS LEFT n/3" and a short hint (BREAK THE OBJECTIVE TO EXPOSE / RUNNING TO RESTORE / RESTORING: BLAST NOW). | `build.ts`, `ai/seatPerception.ts`, `render/views/enemyTelegraph.ts`, `MissionViews.ts`, `ui/missionPanelModel.ts` |

Measured after (`npm run boss-audit`, 3 seeds per cell, the scripted generator player vs the real bots): Easy 17/18,
Normal 17/18, Hard 18/18 fights won; lives lost in all 18 fights Easy 1, Normal 7, Hard 9; a pure punch player broke no
objective in 12 Normal fights (0 exposures, 0 hits). Tests: `tests/mission-bosses.test.ts`,
`tests/mission-bosses-fights.test.ts`; updated deliberately: `tests/mission-oshodi.test.ts`, `mission-objectives`,
`mission-runtime` (Toll Season), `mission-crew`, `render/mission-markers`, `ui/combatHud`, `audio/helpers`.

## DA - District encounters, pass A: Oshodi, Mushin, Fadeyi/Somolu (branch `claude/wahala-csa-districts-a`, docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md "Districts: Oshodi ...")

Why: the six field missions of the first three districts played in 15-50 s (measured with real bots) as walks past a few posted enemies, with no objective a generator was needed for. Founder goals: different encounter geometry per mission, territorial enemies on the district's real lanes, generator-sensitive objectives, 3-6 minute missions, Easy / Hard that differ, and an open-field / Classic split of about 70 / 30. Data only (`fieldDefs.ts` entries, `sections.ts` text); no rule, AI or sector code changed.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DA1 | **Rosters by district theme, at most 8 enemies.** Oshodi: agbero park wardens, bottle-thrower touts, heavy lane blockers and trailer crews; Mushin: market-enforcer hawkers and loaders, area-boy runners, thrower lookouts, a heavy minding the cages; Fadeyi/Somolu: route-marshal crews (the corner marshal calls the others), thrower lookouts, heavies. Every field enemy has a variant; patrols run along the district's lanes (the stall lane and the kiosk loop at Orita, the market cross streets, the bay aisle, the approach road, the junction mouths). 8 enemies is the 2-9 competitor limit of `field-nodes`. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DA2 | **Terminal Approach keeps its legs.** It is the checkpoint-clarity reference (CK1-CK8), the archived pilot and several app flows and e2e specs walk it. Only the roster changed: a warden loops round the ticket kiosk, a warden crosses both streets down the stall lane, a ticket tout watches checkpoint C, a heavy walks the depot lane across the door. Par 180 -> 90 s (measured 27 s scripted). | `fieldDefs.ts`, `tests/oshodi-combat-pilot.test.ts` (the archived replay's kinds) |
| DA3 | **Rail Edge: defeat, destroy, keep, escape.** 2 hawkers -> 3 relay boxes (3 blasts each; a heavy crew minds the nearest; a warden walks round the mast block) -> keep the rail plaza 25 s (two trailer-crew waves) -> escape the trailer hand to the footbridge -> Park gate. Oshodi's `destroyTargets` mission. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `tests/field-missions.test.ts`, `tests/field-checkpoint-clarity.test.ts` |
| DA4 | **Spare-Parts Row: walk, destroy, raid.** 2 checkpoints -> 3 parts cages in the loading bay (3 blasts each, a heavy crew, an area-boy runner on the bay aisle, a lookout over the storeroom) -> the storeroom door -> Row gate. Mushin's `destroyTargets` mission. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DA5 | **Aisle Sweep: defeat, walk, keep (interrupt the restock).** 3 runners -> 2 top-street checkpoints -> keep the lorry bay 40 s against three loader waves -> Market gate; the optional back-stall door is unchanged. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DA6 | **Toll Approach: defeat, destroy, raid.** 3 marshals -> 3 toll boxes by the shed yard (3 blasts each, a heavy crew, a thrower lookout) -> the tool-shed door -> Toll gate. Fadeyi's `destroyTargets` mission. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DA7 | **Junction Run: walk, capture, escape.** 3 junction posts -> keep the centre junction 40 s against three crossing-crew waves (the junction-capture beat that leads to the boss's `junctionAccess`) -> escape the marshal to the slip road (a heavy stands on the way) -> Route office. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DA8 | **Equipment takes 3 blasts; waves are ordinary enemies; nobody camps in a zone.** Three separate blasts per piece make a destroy leg a generator job, not a single drop. A steadfast heavy in a hold wave that sees every generator can contest a zone for minutes (measured: Junction Run stalled), so heavies are crews or guards, never waves; no post (wave or pursuer) lies inside its zone (tested). | `fieldDefs.ts`, `tests/field-districts-a.ts` |
| DA9 | **Pacing and clocks from scripted measurements.** Par = about 2.2 x the scripted Normal mean (the raid pars are 2.2-2.9 x the scripted raid player on the same raids), clocks 360-600 s (at least 2.6 x the slowest scripted Hard run). Estimated human time = scripted x 2.2-2.9 + raid par: every mission lands in 3-6 minutes. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DA10 | **No sector gate opens in these missions.** A gate that reveals a second area would put route stops behind it: `clearRoute` (DS6) and the containment tests treat a closed gate as concrete, so they would need to learn gate order first (shared sector code, owned by the sector worker). The verbs come from the legs instead. | handover |
| DA11 | **The measurement harness.** `scripts/lib/districtPlay.ts`: the real mission at a level against `createBot` enemies seeded like `MatchController.startMission`; a generator-first scripted human (escapes real footprints, jabs or sidesteps wind-ups, punches adjacent enemies, lays when a target is in the footprint and a way out exists, bombs crates that shut his way, closes in on a dodger after two blasts that hurt nobody); doors answered with `resolveRaid`; `seenOnMission` stages the drill's "seen" case on the mission board. `npx tsx scripts/district-play.ts [play|raids|seen]`. | `scripts/lib/districtPlay.ts`, `scripts/district-play.ts`, `tests/field-districts-*.test.ts` |

## Districts B: Ebute Metta, Lagos Island, Lekki Phase 1 field missions (branch `claude/wahala-csa-districts-b`, from `31cb5dc`; docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md "Districts: Ebute Metta, Lagos Island, Lekki Phase 1", decisions DB1-DB9)

Why: the founder's district goals (recognisable encounter geometry, territorial patrols the player can watch and trap, generator-only objectives, 3-6 minute missions, a measured Classic / open-field split) applied to the five field missions of Ebute Metta, Lagos Island and Lekki. Mission ids, door raids, door / return logic and the standalone `ebuteMetta.railYard` raid are kept.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DB1 | **Equipment survives the board preparation (bug fix).** The crate clearing round an enemy post and along a patrol route (and the route clearing) treated a piece of `destroyTargets` equipment as a crate: a piece beside a post became an empty tile while its target stayed standing, so no blast could ever finish the leg. Equipment tiles are now kept by every clearing step and are never a seat's snap tile. Route clearing (DS6) may also cross a sector gate (closed at creation, open by the time the route crosses it). Regression test in `tests/field-districts-ebute-lagos-lekki.test.ts`. | `mission/fieldState.ts` |
| DB2 | **Ebute Metta Yard Approach reveals the sidings.** The sector gains a second rect (the wagon lanes, the signal cabin and the track end north of the yard streets) behind the internal wall on row 25, whose east end is the first real sector gate (`sidingsGate`, opens after the switch-cabinet leg; decision DS9 had the mechanism only). Murtala Muhammed Way, the cabin street and the timber yard close the new rect. The train sweep stays off (its lane is not wholly inside: DS4). | `sectors.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DB3 | **Ebute Metta encounter.** Destroy 4 switch cabinets (two take 2 blasts) minded by the switchmen crew (agbero + bottle thrower) while an agbero and a runner (area boy variant) patrol the middle and north streets; the gate opens; defeat the 2 freight guards (heavy + thrower) on the wagon lanes; keep the signal cabin 25 s against two waves (a relief enforcer, then what is left of the street patrols); the signal-shed raid; return. The yard **lieutenant** walks the lock-up yard and is never a required kill: as a required clear or a hold-zone defender he produced endless stalemates in the scripted runs (expert trap sense + standoff range), which a player would feel as a wall. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DB4 | **Lagos Island Ledger Row.** Defeat 3 auditors (two Ring security walking loops round the office blocks, a heavy on the side street); keep the traders' counter hall 25 s against three waves (Ring security, a thrower, a runner); destroy 3 stamp machines (2 blasts each) minded by the annexe heavy; the records-annexe raid; return. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DB5 | **Lagos Island Press Run: physical, not paperwork** (Story Bible 7). The "walk to the desks to collect audit slips" leg is gone: destroy the 5 receipt presses (machines that print the forged licences) minded by two pressmen while Ring security walks the middle street; defeat the 2 guards blocking the press lane (Ring security + a heavy across its narrow end); escape down the press lane before the auditor catches you; return. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `content/sections.ts` |
| DB6 | **Lekki Gate Approach.** Keep the gatehouse road (a 5 x 5 zone on the closed stretch of Admiralty Way and its verge) 25 s while the gatehouse guard and a thrower come from the east block; destroy 4 standby feeders; the standby-plant raid; return. Two Ring security patrol the garden wall and the pool edge, a heavy stands in the garden. Admiralty Way's paving, traffic rules, camera and building visibility are untouched; no traffic is added (its sweeps stay off under the sector's `inside` policy). | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DB7 | **Lekki Estate Sweep reveals the east lawn.** Defeat the 4 drive guards (two Ring security walking the north drive out of step, the drive-gate heavy, the terrace thrower); the inner estate gate (`lawnGate`, column 40) opens; destroy 5 control points minded by two grid techs; the hounds are loosed from their kennels in the lawn's east corners: escape west to the SERVICE LANE (moved from the lawn corner so the escape is a run, not a step) before a hound catches you; return. | `sectors.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DB8 | **Clocks and pars from scripted measurements.** Par is about twice the scripted Normal field time (a person reads patrols, hesitates and misses; the script does not), the clock at least 3.5 times it and never under 420 s: Ebute 540 / 300, Ledger Row 420 / 210, Press Run 480 / 240, Gate Approach 480 / 240, Estate Sweep 420 / 180. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| DB9 | **Scripted full-mission playthrough harness.** `scripts/lib/generatorPlaythrough.ts` plays a field mission exactly as the campaign starts it (level, enemy bot level, 3 lives, sector, seed) against the real enemy bots with ordinary intents only: it reads the danger it can see (real radial footprints, flames, wind-ups, thrown paths, oil slicks), wrecks equipment by blasts, jabs wind-ups, lays under its feet when an enemy is staggered or armoured, holds a zone without chasing out of it, and reacts to each cue after 0.3-0.6 s (a hash of seed and cue: no clock, no Math.random). A door raid is answered with `resolveRaid` (the scripted Oshodi precedent); its time is the raid's par time (an estimate, stated). `seenInMission` is the "seen" drill on the real boards. `scripts/field-play.ts` prints the table. | `scripts/lib/generatorPlaythrough.ts`, `scripts/field-play.ts` |

Tests: `tests/field-districts-ebute-lagos-lekki.test.ts` (data, regression, scripted playthroughs per level, the seen drill), gate-aware walks in `tests/field-nodes.test.ts` and `tests/mission-sectors.test.ts`, gate objects in `tests/render/sector-render.test.ts`.

## FN - The Ojuelegba finale: missions 19-21 (branch `claude/wahala-csa-finale`, `docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md` "Part: Ojuelegba finale")

Why: founder direction 2026-11-18: implement the planned three-part Ojuelegba finale (docs/STORY_BIBLE.md 5.2) as the last
campaign chapter on the existing mission, checkpoint, sector, boss-recipe and generator-objective systems; the enforcer's
name and art are unapproved (working title "The Landlord", provisional); the Chairman stays unseen and escapes after an
operational victory; the jetty escape moves after mission 21 and the Lekki ending becomes an interim beat (the Story
Bible's PROPOSED reconciliation, now authorised).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FN1 | **The finale is the seventh mission district, the hub stays the hub.** `MissionId` gains `'ojuelegba'` (`DistrictMissionId` = the six); `MISSION_IDS` keeps meaning the six district bosses; `CAMPAIGN_MISSION_IDS` = the six then the finale; `FINALE_MISSION_ID`. The hub content (`districts.ojuelegba`, boss null, prologue briefing) is unchanged; the chapter is `CampaignContent.finaleChapter` (boss, briefing, interim) and `SECTIONS.ojuelegba`. `bossFor('ojuelegba')` is the finale's enforcer; `bossList()` stays the six. | `sim/mission/types.ts`, `campaign/content/finale.ts`, `content/index.ts` |
| FN2 | **Three missions, in order, after Lekki.** `ojuelegba.return` (field), `ojuelegba.junction` (field), `ojuelegba.boss` (boss), sections `ojuelegba.s1`-`s3`, opening when all six district bosses are cleared; `nextMission` points to 19 after Lekki. No door raid: every existing raid layout names another district and faction, so none fits honestly. | `finaleDefs.ts`, `sectionState.ts` |
| FN3 | **Three distinct sectors on the UNCHANGED reference arena, gates between legs.** Return: the shop lane and the corner (218 tiles); junction: the pier court and the road (190); boss: the danfo pocket and the repair court (333); no shared tile. Mission 19 uses two sector gates (`laneBarrier` after leg 1, `cornerGate` after leg 2); route clearing plans across a closed gate; the shared data tests flood with gates passable. Fix of DS7: equipment tiles are claimed and stood again after the crates around posts and patrols are cleared (a post beside a piece used to clear it). | `finaleDefs.ts`, `fieldState.ts`, `tests/mission-sectors`, `field-nodes` |
| FN4 | **Save: no version change.** The finale boss's attempts, clears, stars, time, intro and modifiers live on the hub record `districts.ojuelegba` (status stays `cleared`); missions and sections are keyed records; optional `interimSeen`. Repair: the finale counts only once the six are cleared, never mission 20 without 19, only the finale's own modifier; `completed` needs the finale won, so a save that watched the old Lekki ending loads with the chapter open (not lost, not auto-cleared). Parts are content-driven (21 missions, 105 at most); the seat bonus applies in 19-21. | `save.ts`, `progress.ts`, `sectionState.ts`, `upgrades.ts` (unchanged) |
| FN5 | **The boss verb `controlPoints` on the recipe.** Control points broken by the human's blasts only (1 / 1 / 2 blasts by phase, goals 2 of 3, 3 of 4, 3 of 4); a shut point clears its `opens` crates; lockers re-lock a shut point (never / 6 s / 5 s); live points call telegraphed lockdown lanes. Recipe: `anchor: 'lastBroken'`, walk grace 14 s, call roles escort / runner / melee guard; default windows and hits. Measured 6/6 wins on Easy, Normal and Hard; pure punching 0/3 (nothing broken). | `objectives/controlPoints.ts`, `finaleDefs.ts` |
| FN6 | **Replay modifier Curfew (provisional):** the boss's exposure window x 0.8. Reward "Open Road" (a title, provisional). | `tuning.ts`, `content/finale.ts` |
| FN7 | **A boss mission may have a sector.** `MissionDef.sectorId` (looked up in `BOSS_SECTORS`) and `MissionDef.start` (the human's tile when the arena spawn lies outside); `missionSectorOf` reads it; boss and crew snap inside the sector. Only the finale uses it; the six bosses are byte-identical. The departure-strip hazard stays on inside the boss sector, the road traffic is off. | `sectors.ts`, `mission/state.ts`, `defs.ts` |
| FN8 | **Provisional canon.** "The Landlord" is a working title shown plainly (title "Enforcer of the Locked Routes (working title)"); no biography beyond the LOCKED role. New optional `provisional` flags on `StoryLine`, `BossDef` and `SectionMissionDef` mark every new line. His look reuses the existing Boss variant kit through `MissionDef.boss.look` (`Player.enemyVariant` without `enemyKind`: presentation only, no rule reads it). Bayo: two lines inside his LOCKED arc (he helps hold the junction; he tells the street what he did); no broken promise, no Tayo. Faction "Underbridge Lockmen" (fictional, provisional). | `content/finale.ts`, `types.ts`, `assets/enemyLooks.ts` |
| FN9 | **Story order.** After the Lekki boss: the INTERIM beat (the old finale's Lekki opening and radio roll call, then "the estates go dark, but Ojuelegba is still locked"), from the Lekki result ("Home to Ojuelegba") or once on Continue. After mission 21: the FINALE scene, operational victory first (routes open, danfo released, stall and junction lit, Bayo faces the street), then the Chairman's existing lines and the jetty escape; the ending summary names Ojuelegba, the teaser is unchanged; the Lekki consequence line says Ojuelegba is still locked. `campaignComplete` now means the finale's boss fell (`finaleOpened` marks the Lekki clear). | `story.ts`, `content/finale.ts`, `lekki.ts` (one line), `progress.ts`, `app/campaign.ts` |
| FN10 | **Map and screens.** The hub node shows the final chapter (locked / available / cleared, "Final n/3", "Freed", stars); the hub card lists the chapter's liberation, sections and keeper; briefing "The finale · Ojuelegba" with the chapter's own briefing; result buttons "Home to Ojuelegba" and "Watch the finale"; up-next lines name the finale; the Complete screen lists Curfew. No menu (src/ui/menu) change. | `ui/campaign/*`, `app/campaignState.ts` |

Measured (`npx tsx scripts/finale-audit.ts --seeds 6`, real enemy bots, crates and hazards on): mission 19 6/6 wins on
every level (median 51-62 s), mission 20 6/6 (85-130 s), the boss 6/6 on Easy / Normal / Hard (227 / 225 / 287 s), pure
punching 0/3. Tests: `tests/campaign-finale-data.test.ts`, `campaign-finale-playthrough.test.ts`, `campaign-finale.test.ts`,
`ui/campaign-finale-screens.test.ts`, `e2e/campaign-finale.spec.ts`; updated deliberately: `campaign-progress`,
`campaign-sections`, `campaign-progression-wide`, `field-nodes`, `field-checkpoint-clarity`, `campaign-tactics-layer`,
`mission-sectors`, `render/sector-render`, `render/enemy-looks`, `ui/progressionScreens`, `app/campaign-flow` (counts, the new finale order, the boss look).

## DT - District tuning after SA13 (branch `claude/wahala-csa-tune`, from `301005b`; docs/CAMPAIGN_SECTOR_AI.md "After SA13")

Why: SA13 (the enemy planner treats the campaign human's tile as closed) is correct, but two district tests failed after it: Lekki Estate Sweep won 3 of 6 on Normal, and the Fadeyi/Somolu Easy cost rose above Normal. Measured with the two scripted harnesses (6 seeds per mission and level), the cause was in the scripted players, not in the AI or the mission data: they had leaned on the old planner bug. No mission data, rule or AI code changed; no test threshold changed.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DT1 | **The bait against an armoured enemy at its punch floor.** Estate Sweep's timeouts were all in leg 1 with the drive heavy at 1 health (its punch floor): the script laid a generator next to it and walked off, and the heavy, which sees every generator once engaged, walked out of the 3 s fuse (it used to plan its escape through the human and stay in the blast). The scripted human now does what the sight rules allow a person to do (Part 2, rule 1: a body on the generator's tile hides it; placements are watched only by keen / expert enemies, and by basic ones on Hard): next to a free heavy at its floor, or a few steps from one that is coming for him (its "!" up) and has no clear line to him, he lays under his own feet and STAYS on the generator until the heavy commits to a wind-up (rooted for the wind-up and its recovery, about 100 ticks on Normal), then steps out of its reach first and out of the footprint; if it never commits he leaves while the way out is still long enough. Ordinary intents only, no AI state read beyond the visible telegraphs and awareness markers. Estate Sweep Normal 3/6 -> 6/6 (Hard 2/6 -> 6/6, Easy 6/6). | `scripts/lib/generatorPlaythrough.ts` (`holdBait`, `layBait`) |
| DT2 | **Boxed in by stage crates: blast out.** Press Run Easy seed 5 respawned between two crates with no tile outside its own footprint and stood there to the end of the clock (the script would only lay with a way out). After 2 s boxed in he lays anyway and takes the knock (a blast costs 2 health segments), as a person would. | `scripts/lib/generatorPlaythrough.ts` (`walk`, `boxedIn`) |
| DT3 | **The district-A human: a true fewest-crate search and oil slides.** Junction Run Easy seed 1 stood 250 s in front of a crate: `crateOnWay` (a roughly sorted queue) returned a crate-costing way while a crate-free one existed, and he could not safely blast it from an oil slick. It is now a 0-1 search by cost buckets. Seed 2 walked onto an oil slick, slid back and tried again for 40 s: `route` now lands a step on a slick where the slide ends (`hazards.oilSlideTiles`), as `generatorPlaythrough` already did. Fadeyi/Somolu cost (6 seeds, field s + 60 per life): Easy 1320 / Normal 1084 / Hard 1557 -> 1046 / 1142 / 1523 (Easy <= Normal x 1.05, Hard > Normal x 1.05 as required). | `scripts/lib/districtPlay.ts` |
| DT4 | **No mission retune was needed.** With DT1-DT3 every one of the eleven district field missions wins Normal 6/6, Easy at least as often, and Hard costs more; the clocks and pars of DA9 / DB8 still hold (the slowest Normal mean, Ebute Metta 158 s, is under its 300 s par). Hard became easier to win for the script (Estate Sweep 2/6 -> 6/6, Press Run 2/6 -> 6/6, Ebute 2/6 -> 5/6) because the same bait and crate fixes help there; Hard still costs the most damage and time (over the five Districts-B missions: 80 vs 7 health segments lost per 30 runs against Easy). | handover |

## RC - Campaign combat reliability (branch `claude/wahala-combat-reliability-v1`, from production `0830ee8`; docs/CAMPAIGN_COMBAT_RELIABILITY.md)

Why: founder playtest, "trapping enemies with generators is still too difficult and not smooth": no readable patrol routes, and an enemy that notices you chases at nearly your speed for as long as it likes, so you cannot disengage, reposition and time a trap. Measured with scripted human-like play (a human model with 6-24 tick input latency, +-20 tick timing error, no inside knowledge) on real field missions, not argued.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RC1 | **The human outpaces every ordinary enemy.** Under the campaign tactics layer an enemy's step time is data: ticks per tile Easy 13 / 13 / 14 / 15 / 12, Normal 12 / 12 / 13 / 14 / 11, Hard 11 / 11 / 12 / 13 / 11 (agbero / guard / thrower / heavy / lieutenant) against the human's 10 (9 / 8 / 7 with Roller Skates): a 17 % lead on Normal, 9 % on Hard. It replaces `stepPenaltyTicks` in the sim (`playerStepTicks`) AND the bot planner (`stepTicksOf`), so the planner plans the pace it walks. A variant may shave `stepBonusTicks` (area boy 1), never below `enemyMinStepTicks` (12 / 11 / 11). Outside the layer nothing changes (tested). | `config.ts`, `systems/enemyPace.ts`, `queries.ts`, `ai/world.ts` |
| RC2 | **A calm walk is a walk, and every roamer has a readable route.** A calm enemy (patrol, watch, walking back) adds 3 / 2 / 1 ticks per tile (`patrolStepExtraTicks`); pause 48 / 42 / 36, jitter 0 / 6 / 12, Hard no longer reverses laps. Every ordinary roaming variant of all 13 field missions owns a route of at least 2 waypoints and 12 tiles of circuit, inside the sector, off every objective tile and floor trap (tested): 12 new or rewritten routes (loops round blocks where the geometry allows), the oil-slick waypoint of Mushin's Late Loader fixed, Fadeyi's marker-3 waypoint moved. A campaign enemy's route planner treats a crate as a wall (a stage-dropped crate froze a patroller for good), and a patrol stuck 720 ticks goes on to its next waypoint. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `finaleDefs.ts`, `directive.ts`, `territory.ts` |
| RC3 | **A chase breaks off.** The leash and `hitChaseTicks` stay. A pursuer also breaks off when the human has been farther than 5 / 6 / 8 walking tiles for 45 / 60 / 75 ticks, or it has chased 420 / 600 / 900 ticks without landing a hit (a landed hit restarts the clock): it investigates where it last saw him (`lost` cue), searches, walks back, resumes its route; a ranged unit watches and keeps throwing; 150 / 90 / 60 ticks without a new notice by sight (a human within `closeTiles` or a hit still engages it at once). A walk back that never arrives completes after 480 ticks (a mission-held guard stayed in `return` forever). | `territory.ts`, `config.ts` |
| RC4 | **Trap reliability is tuned by measurement, never by blindness.** The hidden-generator rules, sight, hearing, telegraphs, stagger / knockback / slow and the 3 s fuse are untouched. Tuned: brawler recovery 72 / 60 / 48 ticks in the first round (second round: RC9; was 48 / 42 / 36: the planted window of a swing is when a generator at its feet cannot be walked away from); calm notice range of the human 4 / 5 / 6 for an agbero (guard 5 / 6 / 7, thrower 6 / 7 / 8, heavy 3 / 4 / 5, lieutenant 7 / 8 / 9; engaged enemies keep `noticeTiles`); brawl reaction to a generator under a rival's feet x2.4 (no trap sense) / x2 (basic), was x2 / x1.5, for field enemy seats only (`enemyBrawlReactScale`; the boss missions' crews keep their old scale: the retune lost the Ebute and Lagos boss fights). Normal bait: production 42-58 % first try, now 40-70 % first try, 70-80 % within two (12-20 seeds, 4 missions). | `config.ts`, `territory.ts` |
| RC5 | **Fair difficulty.** Easy slower, a short chase (7 s), longer recoveries, nobody calls for help; Hard closer in speed (still 9 % slower than the human), a 15 s chase, 8-tile gap, quick re-notice (60 ticks), shorter reaction, and it still watches a generator being laid (basic trap sense too: the bait under your feet stops working; the ambush and the counter-jab do). No level knows about a generator it did not see or hear. | `config.ts` |
| RC6 | **The probe's two flaws.** The first disengage runner consumed one direction per tick (it dithers at corners: 32 % "caught" whatever the enemy) and "hurt" counted damage from any enemy. `scripts/readability.ts` keeps both (`RUNNER=legacy`, `hurtByIt`) and defaults to a runner that keeps to its plan. | `scripts/lib/readability.ts` |
| RC7 | **A human-like trap harness is the acceptance test.** `scripts/lib/humanTrap.ts`: input latency 6-24 ticks, timing error +-20, no inside knowledge; ambush, lure, bait and the deliberately bad plain setup on real missions; success = the target dies from the human's blast; first attempt and within two. Fairness: a generator laid in plain sight is walked away from in 32 of 32 runs. | `scripts/lib/humanTrap.ts`, `tests/campaign-reliability-traps.test.ts` |
| RC8 | **What was not made reliable, stated.** Normal bait reaches 70 % first try on one of four missions and 90 % within two on none; the ambush needs room; Hard has no reliable generator strategy in the harness. No bigger lever (a longer recovery, a pursuer that does not leave a known blast) was taken: it makes the enemy stupid. | `docs/CAMPAIGN_COMBAT_RELIABILITY.md` |

Second round (same worktree; measured with the human-like harness, n = 20 per cell, four missions; docs/CAMPAIGN_COMBAT_RELIABILITY.md "RC9-RC12"): the first round left Normal bait at 40-70 % first try (production 50-58 % with the first human) and Hard at 0-8 %. Reading 134 bait misses: 98 the enemy was free and walking after the human at the blast, 16 the human was killed by the crowd first, 20 other.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RC9 | **A longer commitment after a swing.** Brawler (agbero, guard) recovery Easy 72, Normal 72 (was 60), Hard 56 (was 48); a swing that finds nobody plants it `attack.whiffRecoveryExtraTicks` = 12 ticks longer (layer only); cooldowns 192 / 180 / 150 so every cooldown still exceeds wind-up + recovery + miss + 36. Normal bait first try 60 / 70 / 40 / 50 % -> 80 / 80 / 25 / 50 % (approach / railEdge / market / junctions). | `config.ts`, `systems/enemyActions.ts` (`enemyAfterPunch`), `skills.ts` |
| RC10 | **Tunnel vision for a committed Hard brawler.** A basic / no-trap-sense Hard enemy that is winding up, swinging or planted in its recovery does not watch the ground under its rival (`TrapSenseSpec.watchesWhileBrawling: false`); it reacts to a generator it sees by the Normal brawl scale (x2 / x2.4). Calm, pursuing, and the keen and expert tiers, still watch placements (the first version covered pursuit too and made the Hard missions cheaper than Normal for the scripted playthrough: `field-districts-fadeyi` failed). A later reaction alone (x3.2 / x3.4, measured) did not do it: the enemy that sees the placement walks out when it is free. Hard bait first try 0-10 % (three missions) -> 70-80 %. | `config.ts`, `ai/enemyBot.ts` |
| RC11 | **Hard speed 12 / 12 / 13 / 14 (lieutenant 11),** as Normal, so the human leads by 17 % on every level above Easy: Hard probe "reached an adjacent tile" 10.4 % -> 8.0 %. | `config.ts` |
| RC13 | **Hard breathing room.** Hard heavy wind-up extra 32 (was 26), free window after an enemy hit 66 (was 54). Needed because RC10-RC11 left the Hard Oshodi flow at 5-8 of 12 seeds; now 16 of 20, with the district cost test (Hard dearer than Normal) holding. A longer agbero / guard wind-up was tried and dropped (Hard missions became cheaper than Normal). | `config.ts` |
| RC12 | **The human of the harness does what a player does:** picks the lone patroller (fewest enemies within 8 tiles of its route) and leaves the generator when the second swing has started and the fuse is no longer than the 1.8 s a swing commits it (deadline 40 ticks); an intended strategy, not a game change. Final Normal first try / within two: 95 / 100, 85 / 95, 100 / 100, 85 / 95 %; Hard 70 / 90, 70 / 80, 70 / 90, 80 / 85 %; Easy 100 / 100, 85 / 100, 100 / 100, 100 / 100 %; production with the same human Normal 75 / 95, 85 / 95, 70 / 90, 80 / 85 %, Hard 0 / 0, 20 / 20, 80 / 90, 0 / 0 %. A generator laid in plain sight kills in 1 of 80 (Normal) and 0 of 80 (Hard) runs. | `scripts/lib/humanTrap.ts` |

## Six-screen UI redesign (branch `claude/wahala-ui-redesign-v1`, from `0830ee8`; docs/SIX_SCREEN_UI.md, decisions SX1-SX10)

Why: the founder approved the six-screen Gemini direction (Home, Choose How to Play, Match Setup, Story Campaign, Learn to Play, Pause / Exit) after an audit found too many competing Home actions, no unmistakable Play path, Classic hidden behind setup, a 16-fighter Quick Play, and no confirmation when leaving a match. This implements the plan in docs/MENU_ARCHITECTURE_PLAN.md (steps 1, 2, 3 and 6) without touching any rule, save or sim code.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SX1 | **Home is one PLAY button, STORY CAMPAIGN / CONTINUE STORY, LEARN TO PLAY, and two small utilities** (Settings, Sound). The City Rumble / Classic rows, the saved-setup card and Quick play leave Home. The Campaign button says CONTINUE STORY once a district is cleared. The three fighter portraits and the key art (with a dusk wash) are the backdrop. | `ui/menu/HomeScreen.tsx`, `redesign.css` |
| SX2 | **Choose How to Play (`play` replaces the `mode` screen):** QUICK MATCH (START HERE), CLASSIC BLAST, CITY RUMBLE, all visible at once (a 3-column grid, no carousel; one column in portrait). Story Campaign is not repeated. | `app/menu.ts`, `ui/menu/PlayScreen.tsx` |
| SX3 | **Quick Match = an ordinary Classic Blast match:** 1 human + 2 Rookie bots (the lowest level), Danfo Bus Park (a standard arena), crowd throws off, started through `commands.start()` and the normal loading lifecycle. It writes those `GameSettings` (so the next Setup shows them; fighter and preferences are never touched). CUSTOMISE applies the same values and opens Match Setup. | `app/quickMatch.ts` |
| SX4 | **Match Setup carries the battle style:** Classic Blast / City Rumble chips (and the three City Rumble rulesets, keeping the `mode-*` test ids), the fighter chip, REVIEW (the old Ready/Edit flow is kept) and START MATCH (`commands.start`, guarded by the loader: a second tap while a start is on its way starts nothing). Ready's "Mode" row is "Battle style" and edits on Setup. The `mode` screen and its Sudden Death button are gone from the flow. | `ui/menu/SetupScreen.tsx`, `ReadyScreen.tsx` |
| SX5 | **Names:** "Classic Battle" is "Classic Blast" wherever the player reads it (the pending naming decision, plan step 3). The campaign screen is titled "Story campaign". | `app/setupSummary.ts`, UI strings |
| SX6 | **Campaign "now" strip** above the map from `nextMission()`: current district, next mission, objective, the style (Open-field 3D mission / Classic Blast raid / Keeper fight) and the first-clear reward from the content. No new data; the Continue button reads "Continue story". The finale is named only when `nextMission` returns it. | `ui/campaign/currentMission.ts`, `CampaignMap.tsx` |
| SX7 | **Learn to Play** keeps every lesson and the saved progress, grouped into Quick Start (the five Beginner lessons), Generators, Classic Blast, City Rumble, Combat & Specials and Advanced Training (the Generator Tactics pilot). There is no Movement section because the Academy has no further movement lessons. The Sudden Death Test lives under Advanced Training with a pointer to the Combat Test Lab (which stays in the City Rumble pause menu). | `ui/menu/academyModel.ts`, `AcademyScreen.tsx` |
| SX8 | **Pause:** Resume, Restart, Controls (opens Settings on the Controls tab), Settings & Sound, EXIT MATCH. EXIT MATCH always asks "Leave this match?" (KEEP PLAYING resumes; EXIT MATCH leaves; Esc closes the question and the match stays paused). Campaign copy never promises a mid-mission save: only cleared missions are stored. | `ui/Overlays.tsx` |
| SX9 | **Test ids** (`home-rumble`, `home-classic`, `mode-*`, `quit`, `mode-sd-test`) are kept on the controls that replace them; helpers `playSavedSetup`, `toSavedSetup` and `exitMatch` carry the new route. | `e2e/helpers.ts` |
| SX10 | **Not changed:** the splash, first-visit onboarding, FlowFrame branding (one file, `render/perf/brand.ts`), match loading, controls, any rule, save format or campaign data. The unfinished W-AR identity is not integrated. | |

## Mission guidance: briefings, objective HUD and target pins (branch `claude/wahala-cg-guidance`, from `0b89d09`; docs/MISSION_GUIDANCE.md)

Why: founder feedback (campaign guidance problem A): players entered missions, Classic Blast raids above all, without understanding what to destroy, capture, activate or reach; text and arrows were not enough. Presentation and wording only: no rule, timing, position or save field changed. Everything reads the mission, leg, boss-recipe and raid data (never a mission id or coordinate in UI / render code), so the district re-authoring flows through.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MG1 | **A short visual briefing before EVERY campaign mission.** Field missions and the standalone raid get a briefing screen (`missionBrief`) opened by the map's Start / Replay and by Continue story; its START MISSION plays the mission, Back returns to the map. The boss briefing shows the same card above its story card. A replay (mission / district cleared) shows the compact card (the picture and one line). No match exists under the screen.
