Retry from a result restarts at once (just played). `continue()` now opens the briefing instead of starting the field mission: `tests/app/campaign-flow.test.ts` and `campaign-sections-flow.test.ts` call `startBriefed()` after it, and the two e2e specs that click a section's Start press START MISSION. | `ui/campaign/MissionBriefScreen.tsx`, `app/campaign.ts` (`openBrief`, `startBriefed`), `app/menu.ts` |
| MG2 | **The briefing is a pure model of the real data.** ONE main objective: a field mission's first `destroyTargets` leg, else its door, hold area, group, escape, checkpoints (the Ring equipment is what the mission is for); a boss's first phase with the goal the sim resolves and every phase as a step; a raid's `M` tiles. The picture is the in-game object rendered once offscreen with the game's own builders (equipment cabinet, marked crate, door, boss node icons); checkpoints, areas, groups and exits (light and floor effects, no still object) and a missing WebGL use the objective glyph. | `app/missionBrief.ts`, `render/briefPreview.ts` |
| MG3 | **A door raid's instructions come at the SAFE hand-over.** The field is already held when the door raises its request; the raid is built and HELD (`MatchController.holdBriefing`, like the load hold: no tick) under the raid briefing until START RAID; leaving, retrying or going to the map drops it and releases the hold. Compact on a replay of a cleared field mission. | `app/campaign.ts` (`enterRaid`, `startRaidFromBrief`), `ui/campaign/RaidBriefOverlay.tsx` |
| MG4 | **One glyph per kind of objective, the same in the briefing, the rail and the world; the shape carries the meaning, never colour alone.** A small pin over every LIVE target of the current objective (raid marked cargo, floor switches smaller, the open exit; the current leg's equipment, door, area, escape exit, return marker), drawn over walls, about half a tile, above the object, faint when the fighter is next to it. Ordinary crates and later legs' targets wear none. No `QualityProfile` field: a dozen sprites at most. | `render/objectiveGlyphs.ts`, `render/views/ObjectiveMarkers.ts`, `objectivePins.ts` |
| MG5 | **Counters are done / total.** Destroy legs "1/3" (was "(2 LEFT)" in the target text), hold areas "12/25 s" (was "12.0 / 25.0 s" in the target text), raids "0/3" (was "0 OF 3 DOWN"); checkpoints keep "1 OF 3" (which one you are on). Four assertions in `field-sector-legs`, `field-checkpoint-clarity` and `ui/raidGoal` were updated to the new format. | `app/fieldGoal.ts` (`fieldCounter`), `app/raidHud.ts` |
| MG6 | **The direction indicator follows the walkable route.** A BFS over the tiles (a solid target from a neighbour): open floor first, else through crates ("BLAST A PATH"), else a direction with "NO OPEN ROUTE" (never a route implied through a wall). The arrow is the direction of the first three steps, the number the route's length. Recomputed only when the human's tile, the target or the half-second revision changes (about 0.1 ms per run on a district board). The field compass points along the same route. | `app/routeGuide.ts`, `app/hud.ts`, `ui/MissionCompass.tsx` |
| MG7 | **Success feedback names the count and the next step.** "MARKED CARGO DOWN 1/3 ✓ · NEXT: MARKED CARGO", the last "... · NEXT: REACH THE EXIT"; "RELAY BOX WRECKED 1/3 ✓ · NEXT: DESTROY RELAY BOX" (the leg's last piece keeps the leg notice); in the world the pin pops, a wrecked piece throws a gold sparkle and ring. No new sound (no audio added by rule). | `app/raidNotices.ts`, `app/fieldGoal.ts`, `FieldEquipmentViews.ts` |
| MG8 | **Pause shows the same briefing card, compact** (the raid's during a raid). | `ui/campaign/MissionPauseInfo.tsx` |
| MG9 | **The blast pattern is said from the ruleset (`BLAST_RULE_BY_GAME`):** field and boss missions "ROUND BLASTS" (radial), raids "CROSS-SHAPED BLASTS: a generator blasts along its row and column, walls stop it." with the range; a raid never says round / radial (tested). "USE GENERATOR BLASTS" appears exactly where only blasts work: destroy legs, every boss, every raid. | `app/missionBrief.ts` |
| MG10 | **On a landscape phone the hold readout and the route cue share one row of the dock.** The cue added a line to a door leg, and at 667 x 375 the phase row fell out of the dock (13 px, found by `e2e/mobile-hud-fit.spec.ts` in CI; the walk to the door overflowed too, which no test covered). Under 520 px tall: the cue while walking to the door (the bar is still empty), the bar once on it or just off it (the cue would read HERE; STEP BACK ON says the rest). Taller screens show both. | `ui/FieldGoal.css` (`hold-<state>` class), `e2e/mobile-hud-fit.spec.ts` (both states) |
| MG11 | **On a short screen the district briefing's visual brief heads the right column.** Stacked over the story card it pushed the card's own objective below the fold at 844 x 390 (`e2e/story-visuals.spec.ts` in CI). Under 520 px tall the `.cp-brief` grid places the brief over the boss card and the story card in the left column (one element, grid areas); portrait and desktop are unchanged. | `ui/campaign/BriefingScreen.tsx`, `ui/campaign/storyHero.css` |

## CF - Campaign camera, minimap discovery and asset lifecycle (branch `claude/wahala-cg-camera`, from production `0b89d09`; docs/CAMPAIGN_CAMERA.md)

Why: the founder found that missions still open on too much of the environment; the player should see their immediate surroundings and be followed. Presentation only: no rule, timing, collision or save changes.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CF1 | **A `campaign` camera mode chosen from the match, not the arena id:** any campaign mission (`state.mission`: field, boss, finale, the pilot) or classic raid uses it, so a campaign-only map needs no registration. Standalone Classic keeps `arena`, City Rumble keeps `stadium`; `?cam=stadium` still overrides. The O key / camera button toggles any follow camera. | `render/cameraModes.ts` `cameraModeForMatch` |
| CF2 | **The campaign follow framing: focus at 0.2 of the HUD-free band, distance 1.15 x the action camera.** Measured at 740 x 360: 6.6 tiles north, 5.0 south, 9 each side of the fighter (the stadium framing showed 7.2 / 2.8 / 7.0); desktop 6.9 / 5.1 / 7.8; tiles about 40 px on the phone. Chosen from six measured pairs as the nearest to "about 5-6 tiles on every side" with the least pull-back. Dangers widen it only inside a 7 x 5 box round the fighter; rivals do not pull it. | `render/cameraDirector.ts` `CAMPAIGN_FRAMING`, `campaignShot` |
| CF3 | **No establishing overview; no jump at an edge.** The countdown opens on the fighter from 1.3 x the play distance and glides in by mid-countdown. The view is bounded by the sector box (or the board plus 2; a raid's plus 1.2) with a soft clamp (1.6 world units of easing, never past the limit), and the smoothed pose is held inside the bounds while the distance is still easing. A respawn wait and a loss stay on the fighter's spot (the stadium camera cut to the whole district); a win ends on the winner (0.8). | `render/cameraRig.ts` `softClamp`, `boundsBox` |
| CF4 | **The player's camera setting is respected:** with the whole-view setting (`cameraView: 'overview'`) a campaign mission shows its bounded overview (the sector, a raid's board), never the city. Campaign forces nothing else; the default stays the follow view. | `campaignShot` |
| CF5 | **A raid follows only when it does not fit the screen:** the fixed whole-board framing (identical to Classic) is kept when its tiles are at least 95 % of the standard 15 x 11 board's on the same screen, or at least 25 px (the standard board's tile on a 740 x 360 phone: Classic's size on the smallest phone), or at least the follow camera's; otherwise the follow camera, bounded to the board. Result: 13 x 9, 15 x 11, 15 x 13 fixed everywhere; 17 x 11 and 17 x 13 follow at 740 x 360, fixed at 844 x 390 and on desktop. Decided per screen (a rotation re-decides). A following raid's HUD rail is left to the guidance worker (docs/CAMPAIGN_CAMERA.md section 9). | `CameraRig.boardFitsFixed`, `shot.fixedIfFits` |
| CF6 | **Rigged copies release their bone textures.** The "48 textures per start" leak was 42 skeleton bone textures (one per `SkeletonUtils` copy), not the library's GLB maps; `GameRenderer.unload` now calls `releaseSkeletons` on the scene. Also released per match: the PMREM sky scene, the sun's shadow map, the arena floor and contact-shadow meshes, the loaded slab-atlas maps, each raid door's boxes (18 geometries a warehouse start) and the crowd zones' pose geometries (6 a boss start). Restarts are now flat. Shared library and cached textures are never disposed. | `render/assets/library.ts`, `GameRenderer.ts`, `lighting.ts`, `views/ArenaView.ts`, `views/RaidViews.ts`, `life/CrowdZones.ts` |
| CF7 | **The campaign minimap shows what has been discovered:** the sector, tiles within 6 of where the human has stood, and the route to the current objective (recomputed when it changes); undiscovered tiles draw flat with no crates, hazards, generators or rivals; objectives, warnings and the closing city always show. A per-match bitset in the UI (a `WeakMap` on the state), never in the sim. City Rumble unchanged. | `ui/minimapDiscovery.ts`, `ui/Minimap.tsx`, `ui/minimapModel.ts` |
| CF9 | **On a campaign-only area the minimap reveals each named part on entry, not the whole sector** (integration of CF7 with WX1): an area's sector is its whole board, so revealing the sector up front would show the entire mission map at the start. On a campaign-only board (`ArenaDef.campaignOnly`) each sub-area (`ArenaDef.districts`) becomes known when the human first stands in it; the radius and objective-route rules are unchanged; district maps still reveal their sector. Tests: `tests/render/minimap-discovery.test.ts` (an Oshodi area starts with only its first part plus the surroundings and route, a part is revealed on entry; the boss case now checks the exact rule tile by tile). The camera tests' first-frame share is 0.4 of a district board, 0.5 of a smaller campaign-only area plus at most 75 % of its width. | `ui/minimapDiscovery.ts` |
| CF10 | **The asset cache across districts is bounded and the leak test checks repeat cycles.** Measured at integration (all 13 field maps): the first visit to a district keeps its shared library assets (19 textures for Mushin; +39 textures / +37 geometries after every campaign map), then every restart and every further visit is flat. `e2e/campaign-camera.spec.ts` now starts A A B A B A: a restart and a second B -> A cycle must add at most 3; the first visit's cache may add up to 30 textures (it was a 20 bound on A B A only, which the measured 19-26 sat on; the old leak added about 84 per cycle). | `e2e/campaign-camera.spec.ts`, docs/CAMPAIGN_CAMERA.md section 5 |

## WX - Campaign world: campaign-only areas, framework + Oshodi (branch `claude/wahala-cg-world`, from production `0b89d09`; docs/CAMPAIGN_WORLD.md)

Why: the district sectors only clipped the City Rumble maps; the founder wants each district made of several coherent places, campaign-only ones included, different missions in different places, only the current mission's area loaded, and the Rumble map kept as the compact competitive version.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WX1 | **A field mission may play on its own campaign-only arena** (`FieldMissionDef.arenaId`; absent = the district's Rumble map). Campaign arenas live in `CAMPAIGN_ARENAS`, never in `ARENAS` (no menu, picker, preview, Classic / Rumble / Team list or multiplayer test sees them), are marked `campaignOnly`, keep `kind: 'rumble'` (the follow camera and the Rumble-type presentation flags field missions already used) and join the follow-camera list. One resolver (`mission/arena.ts`) answers "which board" for the sim, the app, the tools and the tests. | `sim/arenas/campaign/`, `sim/mission/arena.ts`, `render/cameraModes.ts` |
| WX2 | **A campaign area is data:** district, missions, place and neighbours, sub-areas (the arena's districts), the play area as a `MissionSectorDef` on the area's board (camera bounds, progressive gates), entry, exits, landmark anchors. Encounters, doors, equipment and checkpoints stay in the mission data; tests check both agree. A campaign area's sector covers its whole board (only the mission's area is built), so the district sectors' "less than half the map" rule applies to district maps only. | `sim/mission/areas.ts`, `campaignAreas.ts`, `tests/campaign-world.test.ts`, `tests/mission-sectors.test.ts` |
| WX3 | **Boards are painted with the district's own object kit** (Oshodi: the Transport Metropolis cover letters) and drawn by the district's cover builder on the area's letter map, with the district's looks (day and night), floor materials by sub-area role, environment and a set from the set kit placed by landmark anchors; no raw concrete and no invisible wall (the kit refuses `#`). A new area needs its painted board and anchors only. | `sim/arenas/campaign/kit.ts`, `render/arenas/campaign/` |
| WX4 | **Determinism and isolation:** boards are built at import from data (seeded authoring marks only); Classic, City Rumble and multiplayer digests are unchanged and the Rumble Oshodi layout hash is pinned. Endgame trap waves are part of every campaign board (the mandatory rule), inside a rect away from the entries. | `tests/campaign-world.test.ts`, `tests/enemy-fairness.test.ts` |
| WX5 | **Oshodi's two field missions move to new boards; ids, legs, labels, doors, raids and groups unchanged.** Tests that copied the old coordinates now stage the human on the mission data's own tiles (`tests/oshodi-route.ts`, the debug handle's `fieldMissionDef` in e2e). | `fieldDefs.ts`, tests, e2e |
| WX6 | **Sector gates are the sector transitions:** Orita's steel depot gate opens after the checkpoints, Railside's station barrier after the plaza; the district data flood counts gates as open (they open in leg order). | `areasOshodi.ts`, `tests/field-districts-a.ts` |
| WX7 | **Terminal Approach on the Orita board:** the depot gate on the yard's south side (entering through the heavy's lane cost the Hard flow 3 runs in 20), a kept-clear loading apron in front of the depot doors, a fare tout (thrower post) in the forecourt instead of a roaming marshal (a fifth roamer cost the Hard flow 3-6 runs in 20). Par 90 -> 120 s for the longer route (the scripted Normal run is 48 s, was 21 s). | `oshodiOrita.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WX8 | **Rail Edge on the Railside board:** the trailer hand waits at the market corner below the plaza, 8 tiles from it and 4 tiles further from the footbridge than the player (the escape fairness rule); there the escape costs more on Hard, which keeps the district cost test (Hard / Normal 1.08) without touching a threshold. The archived combat pilot replays frozen data (Terminal Approach in its old Rumble sector) so its reference tests keep measuring what it was tuned on. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `archivedFieldDefs.ts` |
| WX9 | **The Oshodi boss stays on the Rumble map, bounded to its dispatch corridor** (rows 10-22: the main road, plaza, junction, railway edge, market front) with 20 Ring cordons and a start tile; its recipe, nodes and strike lanes are tied to the main road, so a new board was not authored. Every scheduled hazard runs inside and stays on. Boss fights still win on every level (lives a little higher in the tighter space). | `sectors.ts`, `defs.ts` |

## WM - Mushin campaign areas and the community-district campaign kit (branch `claude/wahala-cg-mushin`, from the integration branch at `77e128f`; docs/CAMPAIGN_WORLD.md)

Why: five community districts (Mushin, Fadeyi/Somolu, Ebute Metta, Lagos Island, Lekki) need campaign-only areas drawn as the same district as their City Rumble boards; one shared kit keeps that data-only and the four district workers consistent.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WM1 | **One community-district campaign kit.** Sim: `campaignArena` takes `cover` (`Board.cover(LEGEND)` of the painted board) with `legend: communityLegend(LEGEND)`, the district's existing `CoverLegend`; `checkCover` refuses a cover that drifts from the letters. Render: `communityCampaignKit(art)` turns a district's `CommunityDistrictArt` (its Rumble looks, cover theme / families / reach, ground and a tint per sub-area role, env kinds and beds, a backdrop and its set pieces BY LANDMARK KIND) into the `KITS` entry: the community cover factory, floor and env on the area's own board, a set placed from the area's landmark anchors. `CampaignLandmark.kind` is open (a district's own piece names; an unknown kind is skipped). The community cover / floor / env take an optional board resolver (default `getArena(id)`: the Rumble output is unchanged, layout and cover hashes of the five community Rumble boards pinned). A district exports its pieces from its `*Art.ts` without changing them (Mushin: `MUSHIN_THEME`, `MUSHIN_FAMILIES`, `MUSHIN_REACH`, `MUSHIN_GROUND`, `redLineStation`). | `sim/arenas/campaign/kit.ts`, `render/arenas/campaign/communityKit.ts`, `mushinCampaign.ts`, `register.ts`, `tests/campaign-community-kit.test.ts` |
| WM2 | **Spare-Parts Row plays on its own board, `campaignMushinPartsRow`** (48 x 26, 684 walkable tiles, old sector 349): the Row Gate side street (entry and return), the spare-parts row, the engine back aisle and the supplier yard. Same legs, labels, door / raid, groups, clock and par; the cages move from the Rumble map's loading bay to the yard's loading apron and the storeroom's side. | `sim/arenas/campaign/mushinPartsRow.ts`, `sim/mission/areasMushin.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WM3 | **The supplier yard's steel gate is the sector transition of mission 1** (opens after the checkpoints, like Orita's depot gate); the enemies get loops instead of shuttles: the hawker 18 tiles round the middle stall blocks, the stall guard 32 round the back aisle's lock-ups, the bay runner 34 round the apron; the heavy and the thrower keep posts in cover. | `areasMushin.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WM4 | **Aisle Sweep plays on its own board, `campaignMushinMarketHall`** (50 x 26, 734 walkable tiles, old sector 369): the covered market, the provisions aisles, the warehouse interiors and the service lane with its lorry bay. The hall's steel gate opens once the runners are down; the far stall is in the warehouses, the "top street" is the service lane. Same legs, labels, optional door / raid, groups, waves, clock and par. The return is the MARKET GATE in the hall's north shop row (the start is south-west), so the mission crosses the hall twice. | `sim/arenas/campaign/mushinMarketHall.ts`, `areasMushin.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WM5 | **Mission 2's loaders keep to walkable floor posts:** the service-lane loader shuttles the kerb row (y 4) beside the lane (posts and waypoints are open floor, never the lane's `=` tiles); the loaders' yard area boy loops the tyre heap (16 tiles). Briefing texts name the supplier yard, the covered market and the service lane. | `fieldDefs.ts`, `campaign/content/sections.ts` |
| WM6 | **The Mushin boss is NOT bounded.** Its stacks lie across the whole district map in every phase (Ladipo, Idi-Oro, Daleko; the courtyards to Timber Row) and the traffic / truck sweeps run the main road edge to edge: no coherent part holds them without moving stacks, which would change the recipe. It plays the whole Rumble map as before. Tests that used Mushin's field missions as "a field mission on a district map" use a fixture sector on the Mushin Rumble map (sector-render) and the archived Oshodi pilot replay (minimap discovery). | `defs.ts` (unchanged), `tests/render/sector-render.test.ts`, `tests/render/minimap-discovery.test.ts` |

## WF - Fadeyi/Somolu campaign areas (branch `claude/wahala-cg-fadeyi`, from the community campaign kit `662f8e2`; docs/CAMPAIGN_WORLD.md "Fadeyi/Somolu")

Why: the two Fadeyi/Somolu field missions played clipped bands (309 and 302 walkable tiles) of the compact City Rumble junction; the founder's Fadeyi/Somolu is junction approaches, access-control streets, checkpoint corridors, Ring-controlled facilities and the side routes linking them, with each mission in a different place.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WF1 | **Two new campaign-only boards painted with the community kit as is** (`campaignArena` + `communityLegend` + `Board.cover`, the `KITS` line `fadeyiSomolu: communityCampaignKit(FADEYI_CAMPAIGN_ART)`). The letters are the Rumble `FADEYI_LEGEND` unchanged plus `H` = the community `warehouse` family for the Ring's sheds and stores (`FADEYI_CAMPAIGN_LEGEND`, district data; no kit API change). `fadeyiArt.ts` exports `FADEYI_THEME`, `FADEYI_FAMILIES`, `FADEYI_REACH`, `FADEYI_GROUND` without changing the Rumble output (layout and cover hashes pinned). Set pieces by landmark kind: `ikoroduRoad` (the highway with its BRT lanes beyond the north barrier), `footbridge`, `signBoard`, `edgeStalls`, `danfoRank`, `floodlight`. | `sim/arenas/campaign/fadeyiKit.ts`, `render/arenas/campaign/fadeyiCampaign.ts`, `register.ts` |
| WF2 | **Toll Approach on the Kobo Toll Corridor (48 x 26, 697 walkable tiles):** the Ikorodu Road slip (entry, the TOLL GATE under the highway barrier), the Kobo checkpoint street between two jersey-barrier lines with kiosk gaps, the toll plaza corridor (lanes between booth islands, the Ring toll office north) and, behind the compound wall, the tool-shed yard. The yard's toll bar is a Ring checkpoint gate that lifts once the toll boxes are smashed (leg 1). Legs, labels, door / raid (`shed` / `junctionToolShed`), groups and clock unchanged. | `fadeyiTollCorridor.ts`, `areasFadeyi.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WF3 | **Toll Approach encounters:** the three marshals loop the slip shop block and the blocks either side of the checkpoint street (16-18-tile loops round cover); the toll boxes stand by the kiosk gap and north / south of the plaza's booth islands with the heavy minding the nearest; the lookout (thrower post) now covers the tool-shed door from inside the yard, so the door leg is an encounter and not a walk. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WF4 | **Junction Run on the Somolu Side Routes (50 x 27, 731 walkable tiles):** the Ring route office (entry, the ROUTE OFFICE return), the Somolu back lanes with junction posts west to east and the kept-clear CENTRE JUNCTION where the north-south lane crosses the route, the walled Kobo vehicle pound (the crossing crews' base) and the Onipanu slip road behind a route checkpoint that lifts once the junction is held (leg 1). The pursuer waits in the pound, 10 tiles from the junction and further from the slip road than the player: posted at the north lane mouth it stood in the only lane to junction 2, and the scripted Easy run stalled there (546 s on one seed). | `fadeyiSideRoutes.ts`, `areasFadeyi.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WF5 | **The Fadeyi/Somolu boss stays on the whole Rumble map, unchanged.** Its recipe's nodes span x 6-47, y 5-27 of the 51 x 33 compact junction and its traffic, BRT and departure sweeps run the full board width, so a cordon that keeps every node and every hazard would only trim the outer rows: no coherent bounded part exists without moving the recipe, which the brief forbids. The Rumble Fadeyi is already the deliberately compact board ("should not be inflated"). | `defs.ts` (unchanged) |

## WL - Lekki Phase 1 and the Ojuelegba finale on campaign areas (branch `claude/wahala-cg-lekki-finale`, from the community kit at `662f8e2`; docs/CAMPAIGN_WORLD.md "Lekki Phase 1", "The Ojuelegba finale")

Why: the founder wants every district made of several coherent places with each field mission on its own map; Lekki's two missions and the finale's two field missions still played clipped corners of their City Rumble maps (225, 402, 218 and 190 walkable tiles).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WL1 | **Lekki uses the community campaign kit as it is:** `lekki: communityCampaignKit(LEKKI_CAMPAIGN_ART)`. Its letters are the Rumble `LEKKI_LEGEND` plus one campaign-only object the Rumble map never needed, the estate substation (`'T'`, `lekkiLegend.ts`), drawn by a Lekki family in `lekkiCampaign.ts`: a small backward-compatible addition through the existing `families` / `reach` data, no kit API change. `lekkiArt.ts` exports its theme, pieces, reach and ground without the Rumble board's positional touches (the seawall, the plaza fountain, the Admiralty Way plates); the Rumble output, layout and cover hashes are unchanged (pinned). Neither area includes the boulevard, so Admiralty Way's interlocking paving (LI7) is not used: every road on them is asphalt. | `sim/arenas/campaign/lekkiLegend.ts`, `render/arenas/lekkiArt.ts`, `render/arenas/campaign/lekkiCampaign.ts`, `register.ts` |
| WL2 | **Gate Approach on the Seabreeze Estate Gate** (48 x 28, 834 walkable tiles): the garden walk, the gate road (a palm avenue up to the gatehouse booths), the Coral Close service road with the staff quarters, and behind the checkpoint boom the back service road and the standby plant yard. The boom is the sector gate (a Ring checkpoint) and lifts once the road is held; the hold zone stands on the checkpoint apron, kept-clear slabs (an authored tile must be open floor, not road). Legs, labels, groups, door and raid (`plant` / `standbyPlant`) unchanged; the door's return tile moved with the plant (the district test's expectation follows it). | `lekkiEstateGate.ts`, `areasLekki.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WL3 | **Lekki's lone patrollers are hired muscle, not Ring Security.** The human-like bait is measured on the most isolated patroller, and Ring Security's keen trap sense walks off a generator laid at its feet by design (both old Lekki missions: 0/8). The garden walk's first loop is a gate tout (agbero), the back service road's a Ring road marshal (route marshal: he now walks round the estate office, coverage 50 % -> 75 %), the water works' a tanker tout (agbero); Ring Security keeps the pool walk, the gatehouse and the substation. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WL4 | **Estate Sweep on Palmgrove and the Coralview Substation** (48 x 28, 854 tiles): the north drive between villa compounds, the estate water works, the substation behind the inner estate gate (`lawnGate`, still opening after the drive patrol), the estate service lane. The clear-group text names the water works ("Defeat the 4 guards on the drive and the water works."), the destroy text the substation. The drive's walks are two tiles wide and its north footpath has no palms: in a one-tile pocket the seen-generator drill cornered the human beside the marshal, who never saw the generator. | `lekkiInnerEstate.ts`, `areasLekki.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts`, `sections.ts` |
| WL5 | **Estate Sweep's escape:** the hound kennels in the substation's north-east corner, the SERVICE LANE marker at the lane's east end past the back fence; the fence's barrier crate is the short way (blast it), the inner gate the long one. From every control point the exit is at least 4 tiles nearer to the player than to a hound (the escape fairness rule). | `fieldDefs.ts`, `lekkiInnerEstate.ts` |
| WL6 | **Ojuelegba follows the Oshodi pattern, not the community kit:** it has its own cover builder, so its campaign letter kit is the Underbridge cover map's letters (`OJUELEGBA_KIT`, tested letter for letter) and `ojuelegbaCampaign.ts` draws an area with the reference arena's builder on the area's letter map: `buildOjuelegbaCover(board, { map, deck })`, `buildDeck(..., span)` and `flyover(ctx, L, span)` take optional data whose defaults are the reference arena's (its output unchanged). The deck span comes from the area's pier letters. The areas use the reference look; it has no night look, so neither do they. The finale's old field sectors of the reference arena are retired; its layout hash is pinned. | `sim/arenas/campaign/ojuelegbaKit.ts`, `render/arenas/campaign/ojuelegbaCampaign.ts`, `ojuelegbaCover.ts`, `ojuelegbaDeck.ts`, `ojuelegbaLandmarks.ts` |
| WL7 | **Return to Ojuelegba on the South Streets** (48 x 28, 821 tiles): south of the junction, the way home the story opens on: the Ijoko motor park where the danfos drop you, the Kekere shop street and its back street, the vulcanisers' yard behind the lane barrier, and the corner under the flyover's south ramp behind the corner gate (both Ring gates kept, opening after the barrier locks and the gate locks). The lock guard walks the tyre racks instead of standing (coverage 75 % -> 100 %). | `ojuelegbaSouthStreets.ts`, `areasOjuelegba.ts`, `finaleDefs.ts` |
| WL8 | **Continuity with the opening:** both finale areas are the reference arena's pieces (hammerhead piers with the deck carried on over them, packed danfos, shops, workshops, tyre racks, the bus stop), its late-afternoon look and its floor materials, in streets the Rumble map does not have; the Rumble arena is untouched. | `ojuelegbaCampaign.ts` |
| WL9 | **Hold the Junction on the Underbridge Interchange** (48 x 28, 863 tiles): the west approach road, the bus stop island under the deck, the under-bridge market between the piers, the Ring control yard. A wave holds its post until it is sent (`field.ts`), so when the hold is the first leg no wave member ever walks a route (old coverage 0 / 5): the waves are now the yard's area boy and thrower (18 s) and a roof thrower (26 s), and the old north wave's agbero and market enforcer are the junction's street muscle (group `street`), walking their rounds from the start and coming for the bus stop when they see you on it. The pier control box stands on an open corner by the south-west pier, the lieutenant beside it (between a pier and a stall the Hard flow timed out twice). | `ojuelegbaBusInterchange.ts`, `areasOjuelegba.ts`, `finaleDefs.ts` |
| WL10 | **The finale boss is unchanged:** it already plays in its bounded sector of the reference arena (`ojuelegba.danfoYard`, FN7) with its recipe, nodes, crew and the departure strip inside; extending its routes was not needed, so it was left alone. | `finaleDefs.ts` |

## WE - Ebute Metta and Lagos Island campaign areas (branch `claude/wahala-cg-ebute-island`, from the community-kit commit `662f8e2`; docs/CAMPAIGN_WORLD.md "Ebute Metta and Lagos Island")

Why: the founder wants each district's field missions on their own, larger campaign-only places (Ebute Metta / Oyingbo: freight corridors, railway crossings, goods yards, cargo storage, industrial compounds; Lagos Island: commercial lanes, market access routes, records facilities, infrastructure compounds, secondary streets), two different places for Lagos Island's two missions, with the shared community kit (WM1).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| WE1 | **Yard Approach moves to the Iron Lane Freight Yard** (`campaignEbuteFreightYard`, 50 x 30, 879 walkable tiles against the old sector's 462): cargo streets, the Alafia level crossing (the freight line under two bridge portals, walkable track, no train), the Oyingbo goods sidings, the Ajao works compounds. Mission id, legs, labels, groups, door and raid (`shed` / `junctionToolShed`), clock and par unchanged; the old `ebuteMetta.yardApproach` sector is removed. Tests that pinned the old tiles follow the data (door return tile, gate id; `tests/ebute-west.test.ts` now checks the mission is on its own board). | `ebuteFreightYard.ts`, `areasEbuteMetta.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WE2 | **The sidings gate is a sector gate in the yard fence** (a steel gate, opens after the switch cabinets), so the freight guards stay on the sidings until their leg and the hold waves come through it. | `areasEbuteMetta.ts` |
| WE3 | **Ebute encounters on real loops:** the street patrols loop the lock-ups by the gate (24 tiles) and the cargo block north of the road (42 tiles; a shorter loop round one lock-up let the area boy never look at a generator laid two tiles from him, 0 of 6 seeds seen, so the drill "ordinary enemies escape a badly placed generator they saw" failed), the relief enforcer and the lieutenant loop the goods shed and the signal shed (32 tiles each); the switchman and freight guards hold posts in cover. | `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WE4 | **One small, backward-compatible kit option:** `CommunityDistrictArt.floor.extra` (the district's own floor marks after the kit's); Ebute Metta draws ballast, sleepers and rails on every north-south `=` run of 6+ tiles and stripes the level crossing. The Ebute and Lagos Island art export their theme, families, reach and ground (`EBUTE_*`, `LAGOS_ISLAND_*`) without changing the Rumble output (layout and cover hashes pinned). The parked trailers, the pump house tank and the substation gantry are a few procedural boxes (no new model or texture). | `communityKit.ts`, `ebuteMettaCampaign.ts`, `lagosIslandCampaign.ts` |
| WE5 | **Ledger Row moves to the Ledger Lanes** (`campaignLagosLedgerLanes`, 52 x 28, 831 tiles against 224): the cloth lanes and the market road lead to the counter hall court and the Ledger House records yard. Ring security walks within reach of each other (they defend in pairs); the lone patroller a player picks off is the row runner (an area boy) looping the cloth lanes, which made the human-like bait reach 7/8 first try and 8/8 within two (the old sector: 1/8 on a Ring security target). The row heavy holds the arcade's mouth beside the road (a post on the road tile itself is not open floor). | `lagosLedgerLanes.ts`, `areasLagosIsland.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WE6 | **The records gate opens after the counter hall is kept** (a sector transition): the stamp machines and the annexe are behind it, so the hold comes before the yard as the legs say; the hold waves are all outside it. | `areasLagosIsland.ts` |
| WE7 | **Press Run moves to the Press Yards** (`campaignLagosPressYards`, 50 x 28, 736 tiles against 198), a different place from Ledger Row (back streets and infrastructure compounds). The press lane's Ring checkpoint lifts after the presses (the lane guards wait behind it). Two encounter changes, groups and legs kept: the "lanes" street walker is a hired agbero walking the middle street end to end instead of Ring security (with Ring security as the lone patroller the bait stayed at 0-1 of 8: it reads traps keenly); the pressmen are a heavy and a thrower instead of two market enforcers (a crew stands by its presses for the whole destroy leg, so market enforcers there never counted as patrolling: 2 of 4 roamers, under the 70 % bar). The bureau auditor waits in the substation office, far behind the player when the escape starts. | `lagosPressYards.ts`, `fieldDefs.ts` |
| WE8 | **Both bosses stay on their whole district maps, unchanged.** Ebute Metta's switches are in the station column, but its traffic, goods strikes and foreshore washes span rows 7-36 and the full width: a corridor that keeps every hazard firing is nearly the whole map. Lagos Island's ledgers and presses spread over the whole map in three phases. Bounding either would switch hazards off or cut nodes: never half-move a boss. | docs |

## RA - Classic raid enemy AI (branch `claude/wahala-cl-raid-ai`, from production `514be88`; docs/CLASSIC_RAID_AI.md)

Why: the founder found Classic raid enemies unreadable and untrappable: they changed direction all the time and escaped almost every generator. Verified causes: the competitive bot's random wander inside a 2-tile guard radius (31-66 reversals per enemy-minute, all churn; Hard stood still), no perception (every generator dodged through walls after 6-30 ticks), the planner planning slow enemies at the player's pace, and enemies as fast as the player.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RA1 | **Raids carry the campaign tactics layer WITHOUT its combat model.** `raidTacticsFor(level)` = the field level's layer + `RAID_TACTICS_LEVELS` + `classicRaid`; `raidConfigLayer` adds Normal, `buildRaidGame` Easy / Hard (Normal adds no options config). Every combat hook reads `tacticsCombat(config)` (undefined with `classicRaid`): enemy health, the tactics hit, armour, enemy frames, keep-off-human, same-tile reach, whiff extra and variant recovery bonus stay the raid's, and `config.enemyAttacks` is not layered. Field, boss, Academy, Classic, Rumble and multiplayer digests are unchanged (`tests/raid-ai-isolation.test.ts`). A raid seat with a mind follows its territory; the old hold/chase rule only without the layer. | `config.ts`, `raid/build.ts`, `raid/directives.ts`, combat systems |
| RA2 | **Routes are derived from the board when an enemy wakes** (deterministic): agbero / guard a one-way loop round 1-4 pillar blocks through the spot (prefer 12 tiles, a lap longer than the fuse so a corner can be ambushed) else a corridor run of up to 7 tiles; lieutenant a wider loop; heavy a 3-tile beat; thrower / boss a post facing its longest lane. Never a belt, within 3 of the entrance or another route's tile; corner pauses 48 / 36 / 24 ticks, no jitter, no reversed laps (a reversal only at a run's end). | `raid/routes.ts`, `config.classicRaid.routes` |
| RA3 | **Raid pursuit and pace:** notice 3-5 tiles calm, 5-7 engaged; pursuit 4 / 5 / 7 tiles from the territory, max chase 360 / 480 / 720 ticks, then search and walk back. Ordinary enemies 12 / 11 / 11 ticks a tile chasing (thrower, heavy slower), +3 / +2 / +1 on a calm walk: always slower than the player; the planner now plans the sim's step time and readiness (the mismatch fixed for raid enemy seats). | `RAID_TACTICS_LEVELS` |
| RA4 | **Raid generator awareness:** hearing a generator being laid 1 / 2 / 3 tiles (the field's 4-6 covered a whole raid loop), calm vision 2 / 3 / 3 in the cone (a raid is a small maze), Hard reacts 22 ticks (guard); a KNOWN generator is avoided for its whole fuse (no bot lookahead: no waiting beside it to step out last); while brawling the reaction is x4 / x3.5 / x2.5 (tunnel vision, the field's x2). Unseen generators are absent from the plan. | `RAID_TACTICS_LEVELS`, `ai/enemyBot.ts` |
| RA5 | **Commitment:** no random wander or hesitation for raid enemies (the field's rule), and a new territory order (next waypoint, pause over, chase begun or ended) triggers an immediate re-plan, so patrol timing is exact clockwork. | `ai/enemyBot.ts` |
| RA6 | **Raid roles:** only the thrower keeps its distance (`RaidRouteStyle.keepsDistance`, read from the kind when it pursues: `territory.ts` `keptRange`); a lieutenant closes in, never takes a group throwing slot, and plans at its level's bot (+1 on Hard), so a raid does not stall on a lieutenant holding a door. A wave called by the player's own action (crate, door, cleared wave) wakes alerted and hunts him for at most 480 / 600 / 720 ticks with no leash, gap or max-chase break-off (`EnemyMind.calledUntil`, raid-only). | `config.ts`, `raid/waves.ts`, `systems/territory.ts` |
| RA7 | **One sight rule, readable by presentation:** `enemySeesPerson` (cone when calm, grid line of sight, range) is the only person-sight check of the territory; a light term extends its range, never its line rule, with numbers in the config. `Player.facing` is the cone's direction; `enemyLookout(state, id)` gives facing, calm and notice range for a torch beam. | `systems/sight.ts`, `systems/territory.ts` |

Measured (`scripts/raid-ai.ts --seeds 8`, before = `514be88`): churn 65.5 / 30.8 / 0 -> 0 / 0.2 / 0 reversals per enemy-minute, predictable calm steps 61 / 71 % -> 100 %; a corner ambush on a route traps 100 % / 72 % / 25 % (no routes before); completion 48 / 47 / 47 -> 48 / 48 / 47 of 48. The fair-fight harness (`scripts/lib/fairHuman.ts`) now, in raids, walks up to an enemy it cannot fire at and hunts the last wave when the open exit is behind its gate; both trees were measured with it.

## NL - Classic lighting: readable raids and one night raid (branch `claude/wahala-cl-night`, from production `514be88`; docs/CLASSIC_LIGHTING.md)

Why: the founder found some Classic environments excessively dark and asked for two categories only - normal stages properly lit and readable (restrained, not washed out), and deliberately dark stages only where a working flashlight and selective light are the gameplay. Not "brighten everything".

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NL1 | **The raid art is registered under the raid's SIM arena id.** The table was keyed by raid id (`warehouseDispatchDepot`) while `GameState.arenaId` is `raidWarehouseDispatchDepot`, and its fallback looped `ARENAS` (which excludes raid boards), so every raid rendered with the Danfo Bus Park look, set and floor (exposure 1.04, smog brown, lamps dropped on Low) instead of its designed warehouse / yard / storeroom. Now the variant is chosen per raid id (`RAID_VARIANT_BY_RAID`) and registered for every id in `RAID_ARENA_IDS`; tested on a real game of every raid. Measured on desktop Low (floor mean luminance 0-255, before -> after): Dispatch Depot 61 -> 84, Parts Storeroom 54 -> 76, Junction Tool Shed 53 -> 128, Rail Yard 52 -> 98, Standby Plant 51 -> 102; the fighter's contrast against the floor about doubled (0.25-0.45 -> 0.61-0.80). The raid looks themselves needed no retune once shown (judged on Low and High: readable, gritty, the yard bright but not blown, p90 155). | `render/arenas/raidArt.ts`, `tests/render/raid-art.test.ts` |
| NL2 | **Readability is measured, not guessed.** On the rendered picture (`scripts/classic-lighting-shots.mjs`: floor mean / p10 / p90, obstacle and fighter / enemy contrast, sampled with the renderer's camera) and in code (`render/readability.ts` `readabilityIndex`, which mirrors Low's lamp drop and x1.3 fill and the fog at the fixed camera's distance). The test keeps every lit raid at least as readable as the approved Danfo Bus Park on the same preset, under a wash-out ceiling, its fog off the board, and Low within 75% of High. Standard arenas are measured and reported, not changed (competitive art). | `render/readability.ts`, `tests/render/classic-readability.test.ts` |
| NL3 | **Gameplay overlays ignore fog.** `TileOverlay` (blast danger lanes, vehicle lanes, raid warnings) is `fog: false`: a warning is never washed towards the fog colour in any arena. They were already unlit, so no light or darkness changes them. | `render/views/TileOverlay.ts` |
| NL4 | **One deliberately dark raid: the Records Vault night break-in, behind its data.** `RaidDef.lighting` (`mode: 'night'`, lamps, torch kinds) is additive sim data; no other raid changes. Chosen because its four chambers behind a corridor of doors make light stop at real walls and a door opening reveal a dark room, the 5-tile beam explores one chamber at a time, and its wardens / guards / chief clerk carry torches while the file thrower stands in the dark. It keeps the storeroom set and floor and wears `RAID_STOREROOM_NIGHT_LOOK` (the look of what light falls on: no point lamps, fog past the board). | `sim/raid/def.ts`, `sim/raid/defs/recordsVault.ts`, `render/arenas/raidArt.ts`, `raidStoreroom.ts` |
| NL5 | **Lit tiles are a pure sim function, and the darkness is a mask over the grit patch.** `sim/raid/light.ts`: the fighter's cone (5 ahead, 3/3/5/5/7 wide) in `facing`, the 8-tile personal radius, guard torch cones (4 ahead, 1/3/3/3 wide, never the carrier's own tile), the lamps and burning flames, all occluded on the grid (a straight line to the tile must cross no solid tile); deterministic integer levels, reused buffers. The renderer writes the three channels into a one-texel-per-tile `DataTexture`, eased per tile; the grit patch every lit material already has scales the lit colour by a cold shade where dark and by the light's tint where lit. No extra pass, no shadow map, no new dynamic light; one uniform test in every other match. | `sim/raid/light.ts`, `render/nightMask.ts`, `render/grit.ts`, `render/views/NightLightViews.ts` |
| NL6 | **Generators keep part of their own light in the dark** (`userData.nightK` 0.45 on the generators group): a generator body stays a findable shape; its blast lanes are unlit, fog-free overlays drawn above the mask, always legible. | `render/GameRenderer.ts`, `render/grit.ts` |
| NL7 | **What the dark hides follows the rule.** An enemy whose tile (or the tile its step started from) is not revealed (`tileRevealed`: lit by any light, or within the personal radius) is drawn like one off screen (model, ring, name) and gets no health pips. Awareness marks and attack telegraphs always show (they are warnings about the fighter). A marked crate's floor ring glows only as far as light reaches it; the objective pins stay as guidance, at 55% over a dark target. | `render/views/{PlayerViews,DamageViews,RaidViews,ObjectiveMarkers}.ts` |
| NL8 | **No flashlight toggle.** Exactly two action buttons on every device, no third permanent button: the flashlight is always on and follows movement (no aiming). `?nonight` is a measurement flag only, not a setting. | `render/views/NightLightViews.ts` |
| NL9 | **Quality never changes what is lit.** The mask, the eased levels, the lit tile sets and the hiding never read the preset (tested headless and in a browser: Low and High light the same tiles); only the beam cone's segment count does (10 on Low / Very Low, 16 above). Light-based detection (P1b) reads the same tile sets (implemented after the raid AI merge: NL10-NL13). | `render/GameRenderer.ts`, `docs/CLASSIC_LIGHTING.md` |
| NL10 | **Light-based detection is a night raid's data and config, on the raid AI.** `RaidDef.lighting.detection` (the Records Vault: on) makes the raid add `config.nightSight` (`NIGHT_SIGHT_LEVELS`); `systems/nightSight.ts` `nightNotice` replaces only a CALM enemy's sight and contact checks inside `updateTerritory`. Without `config.nightSight` it returns null and the territory's own path runs, so every other match is byte-identical (isolation digests, mission regressions, and new full-raid digests of the five lit raids at every level). Alerted, pursuing and investigating enemies keep the raid AI's all-round sight and memory. `detection: false` is the off switch (night visuals with the AI's own sight). | `sim/systems/nightSight.ts`, `sim/systems/territory.ts`, `sim/config.ts`, `sim/raid/build.ts` |
| NL11 | **A calm torch guard sees the fighter only on its own lit torch tiles, after a reaction.** The same `lightCone` the renderer draws (4 ahead, 1/3/3/3 wide, grid occlusion) intersected with the AI's `enemySeesPerson` (RA7: the light term narrows the area and sets the range it passes, reach + 1 because the cone is measured along the facing and the sight range is Euclidean; the line rule is untouched). The alert needs 15 consecutive ticks at 1-2 tiles rising to 30 at the cone's reach (Normal); leaving the cone resets the count. | `sim/systems/nightSight.ts`, `sim/config.ts` |
| NL12 | **The fighter's beam costs exactly one tile; the dark only contact; difficulty only the reaction.** A guard with a line of light to a lit floor tile of the fighter's beam in its calm view cone gets a torch cone one tile longer (5, never 6, never through a wall; the extra row holds the fighter only in his own light). Out of a torch (or a torchless enemy, the thrower) a calm enemy notices him only at Chebyshev 1 (instead of the lit raid's Manhattan 2) or by the unchanged hearing. Easy 20-36, Normal 15-30, Hard 12-24 reaction ticks. | `sim/config.ts` `NIGHT_SIGHT_LEVELS` |
| NL13 | **Shipped on: the measurements say it is fair.** Scripted fair-fight player, 8 seeds a level, night vs no night detection: completed 8/8 everywhere; health lost Easy 3.00 / 2.75, Normal 1.63 / 2.25, Hard 1.00 / 1.25; detections per run 5.3 / 5.6, 10.1 / 11.5, 5.6 / 5.5. The beam does not give the fighter away and the raid stays beatable. `scripts/raid-ai.ts --nonight` and a detections column measure it. | `scripts/raid-ai.ts`, `scripts/lib/raidAi.ts` |
| NL14 | **The dark keeps the maze readable.** Render constant only: the mask's dark shade went `0x2a3a52` -> `0x6a7c9c` (about 20% of the lit light, linear) and the night look's exposure 1.32 -> 1.5, so pillars, walls and corridors outside the light read as dim cold silhouettes (Classic play plans escapes on the grid) while enemies in the dark stay hidden (the rule, NL7). Measured in the picture (desktop / phone Low): unlit floor median 15 -> 23-24 / 255 (sRGB), lit floor 57-64, dark / lit 0.37-0.49 on screen; the solid-vs-floor number on top faces stays low (0.01-0.15) because the blocks separate by their sides, which the pictures show. | `render/nightMask.ts`, `render/arenas/raidStoreroom.ts` |
| NL15 | **Night blast lanes are a saturated red.** In a night raid only, the generator danger lanes are drawn by a separate alpha-blended, non-tone-mapped overlay in full red (an additive red over the cold dark floor read pale magenta). Lit matches draw their lanes exactly as before (same overlay, same colours). The whole cross shows: the vault's entrance lanes are short because walls clip the blast (range 2), not because tiles were missing. | `render/views/OverlayViews.ts` |

## RI - Release integration: Classic raid AI / night lighting + the 18-character roster (branch `claude/wahala-release-integration`, from production `514be88`; docs/QA_ENTRY.md)

Why: the founder authorised one development release with both workstreams (`claude/wahala-classic-ai-lighting-v1` `50666d2`, `ccr-5b343a94-mqq1vh` `9ae45c6`). The roster branch's own CI had failed one gate (`e2e/campaign-camera.spec.ts`, restart textures +10, limit 3); the cause was found and fixed in render code, not in the test.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RI1 | **The match loading screen waits, bounded, for the on-demand character models its first frames asked for** (`AssetLibrary.lazyPending`, `MatchLoaderDeps.modelsPending`, at most `modelWaitMs` 6 s, then the frames that draw them). The match no longer opens on the fallback look and swaps enemy / boss models in front of the player; a slow connection still plays on with the fallback after 6 s. Loading only: no rule, timing or input change. | `app/matchLoader.ts`, `app/session.ts`, `render/assets/library.ts`, `tests/app/match-loader.test.ts` |
| RI2 | **A seat re-dressed mid-match releases its old skeleton, and every new character is drawn once before the follow camera may cull it.** Measured on `oshodi.approach` (desktop Low): the fallback body of each enemy whose model arrived after the first frame kept its bone texture for the whole session (9 a first start), and the re-dressed seats off camera were never drawn until they walked on screen (a first-draw shader compile mid-play). Restart textures 160 -> 170 before, 140 -> 140 after; first visit to Mushin +21 (was +39 with the leak), then flat. The CF10 thresholds are unchanged. | `render/views/PlayerViews.ts` (`destroy`, `Visual.fresh`) |
| RI3 | **Temporary founder QA entry:** `?qaraid=<raidId>[&qalevel=]` adds one Home button that plays that raid on its own through the real campaign controller (Restart / Leave in the pause menu) and never writes the save; a raid that belongs to no mission (QA, debug) shows its result without saving it. To be removed after the founder's phone tests (docs/QA_ENTRY.md "Removal"). | `app/qaEntry.ts`, `app/campaign.ts`, `ui/menu/HomeScreen.tsx`, `tests/app/qa-entry.test.ts`, `e2e/qa-entry.spec.ts` |
| RI4 | **Raid enemies drive the dedicated models' clips:** the raid AI's territory minds (RA1) are what the roster's `Walk` / `Run` / `Alert` choice reads (`mindOf`, `enemyCue`), so raid guards now walk their routes and alert instead of always running. Pinned in `tests/render/raid-enemy-animation.test.ts`. No code change was needed. | `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `render/views/enemyAwareness.ts` |
| RI5 | **A figure created for a seat that is already down starts hidden** (`initialDeadAt`): a raid wave not yet on the board, or any down seat re-dressed when its on-demand model arrives, used to replay the 1.7 s death tumble, puff and white flash where nothing happened; with the models arriving during loading it ran on into the countdown, and in the night raid it showed where hidden waves wait. Appearing still plays the revive effect. Records Vault phone Low: the three pale figures gone; depot draw calls 274 -> 192. | `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `tests/render/player-views-dead-seat.test.ts` |

## AM - Classic raid combat correction (ambushes): patrols, generator ambushes, pursuit, escapable punches (branch `claude/wahala-classic-combat-v2`, from production `acaeae7`; docs/CLASSIC_RAID_COMBAT.md)

Why: the founder did not accept the RA raid AI on a real phone: enemies too fast and unpredictable, several chasing at once, generator ambushes practically impossible, punch warnings not escapable. Measured on the live raids with a scripted ambusher (it learns a guard's lap by watching, plants out of sight, retreats) and the fair-fight player, before and after. Classic raids only; every other match byte-identical (`tests/raid-ai-isolation.test.ts`).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AM1 | **Clockwork patrols.** A calm patrolling raid enemy steps towards its waypoint the moment it can (shortest walk, fixed tie-break) unless a generator it knows of or a flame is about; the bot planner's own cadence had left it standing up to 0.5 s on a different corridor tile each lap, so a learned route could not be timed. | `ai/enemyBot.ts` `patrolStep` |
| AM2 | **At most 1 / 2 / 3 ordinary enemies hunt at once** (Easy / Normal / Hard). Without a slot an enemy that notices him, hears a call or is called in watches from its ground and returns to its route; a hit or the player at arm's length always turns it. Called waves wake calm on Easy and hunt 4 / 8 s on Normal / Hard (RA6 amended). Lieutenants and bosses uncapped. | `systems/territory.ts`, `classicRaid.maxChasers`, `RAID_CALLED_CHASE_TICKS` |
| AM3 | **Slower, shorter pursuit.** Ordinary chase pace 15 / 13 / 12 ticks a tile (player 10), calm walk +3 / +3 / +2; max chase 4 / 6 / 9 s, leash 3 / 4 / 6, walking gap 3 / 4 / 5 for 0.5 / 0.75 / 1 s, re-notice after giving up 5 / 3 / 2 s; notice 4 / 5 / 6 tiles (calm 3 / 4 / 5) after 0.75 / 0.5 / 0.25 s. | `RAID_TACTICS_LEVELS` |
| AM4 | **Escapable, committed punches.** Ordinary wind-ups 54-60 / 44-50 / 34-40 ticks (a phone reaction plus half a step is about 21); the strike lands only on the telegraphed tile (or a player still under half-way off it), never on another neighbour or along a line; one wind-up at a time (two on Hard), group gap 90 / 60 / 40 ticks, no enemy hit for 150 / 120 / 90 ticks after a hit. | `RAID_ENEMY_ATTACKS`, `enemyAttacks.committedMelee`, `skills.ts` `committedTarget` |
| AM5 | **Calm generator vision 1 / 2 / 3 tiles**, taken in 1.5 / 1.2 / 1 x slower: an unseen, well-timed generator on a guard's route gets it; one in plain view in time is escaped. | `RAID_TACTICS_LEVELS.trapSense` |
| AM7 | **The punch warning shows exactly what can be hit.** In a raid the wind-up telegraph marks only the committed tile (the arrow and its framed, filling tile); the corner brackets on the enemy's other neighbours (and a reach-2 tile) are drawn only where the old catalogue reach applies. | `render/views/enemyTelegraph.ts`, `tests/render/enemy-telegraphs.test.ts` |
| AM6 | **One blast defeats an agbero, guard or thrower in a raid at every level** (Easy's blast 1 for the human had also spared a 2-health guard); a heavy, lieutenant or boss loses at least 2. | `systems/lethality.ts` `raidBlastDamage`, `classicRaid.blastDefeats` |

Measured (24 runs a level, before -> after): ambushes that killed their guard 17 / 35 / 57 % -> 71 / 94 / 67 %; enemy punches landed on the ambusher 20 / 19 / 15 -> 1 / 2 / 2; most ordinary enemies hunting at once 3 / 4 / 3 -> 2 / 3 / 3; every raid completed before and after. Pinned tests deliberately updated: `campaign-tactics-layer` (raids now carry `RAID_ENEMY_ATTACKS`, it asserted the unlayered table) and the lit-raid digests of `raid-night-detection` (re-recorded: every raid changed on purpose).

## CV - Classic combat correction v3: rear approaches, one-blast kills, room to breathe (branch `claude/wahala-classic-raid-v3`, from production `ee939a3`; docs/CLASSIC_RAID_COMBAT.md "v3")

Why: on a real Android phone the founder still found raid guards noticing him while they faced away, a rear approach to lay a generator impossible, ordinary enemies seeming to survive direct blasts, gangs pressing without a break, punches hard to evade and the opening raid too demanding. Diagnosed in the sim: "too close" was 2 tiles Manhattan whatever the facing; the eight tiles round a guard (including the three behind) were always in view; a calm guard punched anyone next to it, its back included (the punch turned it and alerted it); laying a generator was heard 1-3 tiles away; flames tested only the tile a slow enemy had just stepped onto while it was still drawn on the one it was leaving; a guard that knew a generator could stand beside it and step out at the last moment; the lieutenant (8 health, 4 blasts) hunted on top of the chaser cap. Classic raids only: every rule is reached through `classicRaid` / `RAID_ENEMY_ATTACKS`; every other match byte-identical (`tests/raid-ai-isolation.test.ts`).

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CV1 | **No eyes in the back of the head.** A calm raid guard does not see the three tiles behind it (Normal, Hard; on Easy only the three ahead); "too close" is one tile and needs the player in front or at its side; a calm guard swings only at someone it can see. Engaged (hunting) it still sees all round. | `systems/sight.ts` `inViewCone` ring, `territory.ts`, `nightSight.ts`, `ai/enemyBot.ts`, `classicRaid.nearRing`, `awareness.closeTiles` 1 |
| CV2 | **A laid generator is a quiet click; a generator is seen in front.** Laying one is heard 0 / 0 / 1 tiles (Easy / Normal / Hard; a blast stays loud); a calm guard sees one only in its view cone and the three tiles ahead (Hard: and the two beside it). | `classicRaid.placeHearingTiles`, `classicRaid.trapRing`, `ai/trapSight.ts` |
| CV3 | **The blast hits what the player sees.** A raid enemy in the first half of a step is also caught by fire on the tile it is leaving (where it is still drawn): a blast never seems to pass through a guard. | `systems/lethality.ts` `collectFlameHits` |
| CV4 | **Calm generator reaction:** Easy takes it in 2.1 x slower (was 1.6), a guard at its post turns a quarter every 4 / 2.5 s (Easy / Normal; was 2 / 1.5 s), so a player can watch it and pick his moment. Normal's reaction is unchanged (1.3 / 1.2 x). | `RAID_TACTICS_LEVELS` |
| CV5 | **An aware guard acts at once.** A known generator's cross is left by the shortest way the moment it is known (no waiting beside it), and a guard outside every known cross never steps into one. An unaware guard still walks into a generator it never saw. | `ai/enemyBot.ts` `clearStep` |
| CV6 | **Room to breathe; a fair opening raid.** On Easy and Normal the lieutenant takes one of the 1 / 2 hunting places (it no longer hunts on top of the cap); blasts take 4 / 3 / 2 from a lieutenant or heavy (a lieutenant falls to 2 / 3 / 4 blasts, was 4 at every level). | `classicRaid.capsLieutenant`, `classicRaid.blastVsTough` |
| CV7 | **Longer punch wind-ups on Easy and Normal:** +8 ticks for every kind (an agbero / guard 68 / 62 and 58 / 52 ticks); Hard unchanged. | `RAID_ENEMY_ATTACKS` |
| CV8 | **A post guard's shoulder is seen, not felt (Easy, Normal).** The instant arm's-length notice and a calm guard's swing need the player in front (`classicRaid.closeRing` 'front'; Hard keeps 'frontSides'); a player at its shoulder is still noticed by sight after the usual reaction (0.5 s on Normal). Why: the founder passed the isolated rear approach on Easy but found it inconsistent in the full raid on Normal. Measured in the real depot (`scripts/raid-rear.ts`): the tile behind post guard C is unwatched by every guard 13 % of the time on Normal (25 % on Easy) in 1-2 s windows, because C sees its shoulders as it turns a quarter every 2.5 s and guard B's beat ends at that tile; a player laying a generator there just before C's turn was caught on that very tick (4 of 36 timed attempts, 0 with this change; 32 of 36 kill C before and after; Hard unchanged: the click is heard). No other perception, pace or pressure change. | `classicRaid.closeRing`, `systems/territory.ts`, `ai/enemyBot.ts` |

Measured (before = production `ee939a3`, same scripts; `scripts/raid-first.ts` added). Opening raid, 8 seeds a level, fair (0.3 s) and slow (0.5 s) scripted players: every run completed before and after; time with nobody hunting the fair player 54 / 54 / 45 % -> 69 / 59 / 51 % (Easy / Normal / Hard); most enemies hunting at once 2 / 3 / 3 -> 2 / 2 / 3 (slow player Easy 1). All raids, fair-fight player (24 runs a level): enemy time calm 62 / 40 / 32 % -> 76 / 52 / 29 %; punches landed 1 / 12 / 5 -> 4 / 4 / 1; wind-up minimum 48 / 40 / 32 -> 56 / 48 / 32 ticks. Ambusher (24 runs a level): ambushes planted 17 / 17 / 12 -> 33 / 17 / 12, killing their guard 71 / 94 / 67 % -> 73 / 100 / 100 %; damage per run 1.00 / 0.83 / 1.58 -> 0.46 / 0.21 / 1.13; punches landed on him 1 / 2 / 2 -> 8 / 1 / 1 (Easy rose: it now walks up behind guards that then turn); mean spell with nobody hunting 11.8 / 10.3 / 9.3 s -> 18.9 / 15.3 / 9.7 s. Acceptance test G (`tests/raid-combat-acceptance.test.ts`): a player who waits out of sight, sees the post guard face the wall and its neighbour walk away, walks up behind it, lays a generator and walks back kills it unseen on Easy and Normal; on Hard the click is heard and the guard gets clear. Pinned tests deliberately updated: the raid-ai chase test (the player two tiles ahead, not beside it), the plain-view negative control (a full fuse: it is seen, no longer heard), `seenDrill` (the tile ahead only), the Easy reaction test (now Easy vs Hard), raid-core / raid-maze-combat set-ups facing the guard towards what it must see, the called-wave test (at most the cap hunts), the night adjacency test (beside him, not behind) and the lit-raid digests. Scripted players are not people: founder phone testing remains the acceptance gate.

## IV - iOS viewport: no persistent page zoom in a match (branch `claude/wahala-ios-viewport`, from production `acaeae7`; docs/IOS_VIEWPORT.md)

Why: on an iPhone 17 (Safari and Chrome, both WebKit) a landscape match occasionally became enlarged and could not be brought back; Android was fine. WebKit ignores `user-scalable=no` / `maximum-scale` for the player's own zoom, resets `touch-action` to `auto` at every scroll container, and a zoomed page whose game surface is `touch-action: none` cannot be pinched back out. Render / UI / input-surface only: no sim, AI, camera, rule, input-timing or assist change. Real-iPhone confirmation pending.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IV1 | **Layered gesture blocking, only while a match is played.** Safari `gesture*` events, two-finger `touchmove`, one-finger page pans outside a real scroller, `dblclick` and a second touch release within 350 ms (except on click controls) are cancelled; the viewport tag stays as it was but is no longer relied on. Menus, pause and results keep the browser's behaviour and fast scrolling (the non-passive listeners exist only during play). | `platform/viewport.ts`, `app/session.ts` |
| IV2 | **No double-tap zoom anywhere:** `:where(*) { touch-action: manipulation }` (zero specificity: explicit values win; Android's effective values unchanged), the Rumble roster list `pan-y`. | `ui/styles.css`, `ui/RumbleHud.css` |
| IV3 | **Refit after every viewport change and after WebKit's late sizes:** `resize`, `orientationchange`, `visualViewport` resize/scroll, `screen.orientation`, `fullscreenchange`; re-read on the next frame and at 120 / 350 / 800 ms, re-sending `resize` once when the sizes moved; a scrolled page is put back during a match. | `platform/viewport.ts` |
| IV4 | **A page zoomed anyway is counter-scaled, not reflowed:** during a match the game root is translated to the visual viewport and scaled by 1 / zoom, so the whole match shows at its normal size and nothing re-lays out (vw/vh, canvas, HUD unchanged; no game state touched). Measuring code maps client coordinates into the root (`rootRect`, `clientToRoot`, `rootSize`; identity when unzoomed). Chosen over resizing the root to the visible area, which would leave every `vw`/`vh` control size and the camera's control strips mismatched. | `platform/viewport.ts`, `ui/styles.css`, `app/session.ts`, `ui/{HudRail,MissionDock,MissionCompass,Minimap,TouchControls}.tsx` |
| IV5 | **Escape:** when the page is zoomed and no match is being played (pause, results, menus), `html, body, #root` allow `pinch-zoom` so the player can pinch back out; the counter-scale undoes itself. | `ui/styles.css` |
| IV6 | **Accessibility:** `-webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%` (never `none`) stops the landscape text inflation and keeps the browser's text / page zoom; menus keep their pinch behaviour. `shrink-to-fit=no` was not added (Chromium logs it as a console error); the e2e checks that the page never overflows instead. | `ui/styles.css`, `index.html` (unchanged) |

## PI - Player identification marker (branch `claude/wahala-player-marker`, from production `ee939a3`; docs/PLAYER_MARKER.md)

Why: with three or four rivals around, the founder sometimes lost track of which fighter they control. The old cue was a 0.48-tile slot-coloured chevron plus the "You" name tag, in the same style and colour family as every rival's name tag and ring; behind a stack of bodies it was the least visible thing on screen.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PI1 | **Three live styles, `indicator` is the default.** `original` is exactly the old chevron + name tag; `indicator` replaces both with one "YOU" tag over the local human's head; `indicatorGround` adds a faint floor-contact tint with a thin crescent. The style is render-only state (`setPlayerMarkerMode`, read every frame), the Test Lab and `__WAHALA__.playerMarker(mode)` switch it live. | `render/playerMarker.ts`, `render/views/youMarker.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts` |
| PI2 | **Only the local human, never a bot or a rival; hidden while down.** The tag follows `localPlayerId` (none in attract or bot-only matches), is hidden with the figure on death, off screen or culled, and returns with the respawn. It stays steady (does not blink) during spawn protection. | `youMarker.ts`, `PlayerViews.ts` |
| PI3 | **Contextual prominence.** Alone: 0.78 scale, 0.62 opacity. Rivals within about 2 tiles (full at 1.2, none at 2.4), or standing in the player's screen column 2.2 tiles in front of or behind them, raise it to 1.12 scale and full opacity; two close rivals reach the top. Eased (fast up, slow down), so it never pops. Only living rivals count; the formula is pure and tested. | `playerMarker.ts` |
| PI4 | **Drawn over bodies, readable day and night.** No depth test or depth write, render order 40 (above name tags 31, chevron 32, stars and badges 33), fog and tone mapping off; a dark plate with a dark outer halo and a hairline in the fighter's colour, so it reads on a pale floor and in the dark Records Vault. One sprite, one cached texture per colour, no lights, no per-frame allocation. | `textures.ts` (`youTagTexture`), `youMarker.ts` |
| PI5 | **The tag points at the head and replaces the local name tag** in the indicator styles (the tip rests just over the head top; a mounted rider is lifted with the bike). Rivals keep their name tags; the tag sorts above them. | `youMarker.ts`, `PlayerViews.ts` |
| PI6 | **`indicatorGround` is an unapproved experiment.** It is reachable only through `setPlayerMarkerMode`, never a default, and kept understated (opacity 0.34-0.54 against the ring's 0.98). The `original` style and the existing rings of every fighter are untouched. | `playerMarker.ts`, `youMarker.ts` |

## BH - Boss health bar and fight readability (branch `claude/wahala-boss-hud`, from production `b6b2367`; presentation only)

Why: the founder played the Oshodi boss on Easy and Normal and could not tell how much health he had left. Cause: the district bosses are generator bots without `Vitals` (BX2), so the hit-point bar (`enemyHealthView`) never had a boss to show; the only bar was the mission fallback "BOSS DEFENCES", which counted PHASES (3 of 3 defences up -> one flashing segment the moment the shield dropped -> 2 after the hit), so it jumped without damage and never showed the three hits the boss really has. The sim already exposes the truth (`MissionView.boss.hitsLeft / maxHits`, the `bossHit` / `bossDefeated` events); the bar just did not read it. The mission panel also said the same things again in small print ("BLASTS LEFT 2/3", "BOSS SHIELDED") further down a column that is clipped on a phone.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| BH1 | **The bar's value is the sim's hits left, nothing else.** `missionBossBar`: one segment per hit he has (`maxHits`, capped at 10), `filled = hitsLeft`, text "2 / 3", name above. Opening the window drains nothing; the shield closing again after an expired window drains nothing. The bar leads the rail / dock block of a boss mission. | `ui/campaignHealthModel.ts`, `ui/CampaignHealth.tsx`, `CampaignHealth.css` |
| BH2 | **Shielded and exposed are states beside health, never damage.** SHIELDED: blue plate, blue-hatched segments, "BLASTS DO NOTHING". EXPOSED m:ss: gold plate, a window timer under the segments, "BLAST HIM NOW". A blast that reaches the shielded boss has no sim event, so the view recognises it from the flames on his tile (the lethality tile test, ignoring flames of the blast he is already immune to): the bar flashes blue with "BLOCKED / NO DAMAGE" and the board shows a cold spark, once per blast. | `render/views/bossFeedback.ts` (`shieldBlockingFlame`), `app/bossBar.ts`, `MissionViews.ts` |
| BH3 | **A hit drains its segment only on the sim's event.** `app/bossBar.ts` remembers the last `bossHit` / `bossDefeated` (tick and hits left after it) and the last blocked blast; the bar flashes the lost segment white -> red -> hollow, shakes, and shows "HIT -1" for 0.9 s, only while the health on the bar equals the event's `hitsLeft`. A bar lower than the events say simply shows its (sim) value without the flash. At zero: all segments empty, "DEFEATED". | `app/bossBar.ts`, `MatchController.ts`, `HudSnapshot.bossFeedback` |
| BH4 | **Rail words for the beats.** `BOSS HIT - n/3 LEFT`, `BOSS DOWN!`, and `TOO SLOW: SHIELD IS BACK UP` when a window runs out (the one bad boss beat). The panel no longer repeats the boss label / blasts-left in a boss mission (its hint line stays: what to do now). | `app/bossBar.ts` (`bossNotice`), `MissionPanel.tsx` |
| BH5 | **Boss beats on the board, from events.** A hit: gold flash, two rings, sparks, bounded shake, the boss's ring swells white; the shield closing / dropping: a ring in its colour; the defeat: the biggest flash and rings plus a comic puff and dust. The defeated boss is flung higher than a rival, lies down circled by stars and only then shrinks away (`bossDefeatPose`). No text over the board. | `bossFeedback.ts`, `MissionViews.handleEvents`, `PlayerViews.poseDead` |
| BH6 | **Telegraph audit: no change needed.** The boss of a mission attacks with generators (38 laid in an Easy fight, no melee): their blast lanes are the existing `previewBlast` danger lanes for every owner, and a crew or boss punch already reads through `telegraphKindOf` (BX8, tested). The restore ring, strike hatching, reinforcement blue and the stagger of the reeling boss agree with the sim. A defeated boss leaves no warning, window or ring (`missionView.warnings` is empty after the win). | `tests/render/mission-markers.test.ts` |

## Founder Test Lab (temporary; docs/TEST_LAB.md)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TL1 | **`?testlab=1` replaces the main menu with the Test Lab** (two sections: Full Playtests, Isolated Scenarios) so the founder can launch real gameplay at once on a phone. Without the query the game and Home are unchanged. The Test Lab supersedes `?qaraid=` (RI3): its Home button, `qaEntry.ts` and `commands.qaStartRaid` are removed; the controller's no-save raid path (`CampaignController.qaStartRaid`, `finishRaidMission(null)`) is kept and reused. | `testlab/`, `ui/testlab/`, `ui/App.tsx` |
| TL2 | **Save isolation by replacing `window.localStorage`** with an in-memory store before any game module is evaluated (`testlab/boot.ts` is the first import of `main.tsx`). That covers every persistence path at once (campaign save, unlocks, progression, economy, achievements, settings, audio prefs, Academy / onboarding flags, learned graphics rung). Only the feedback key `wahala.testlab.feedback.v1` passes through to the real storage. If the swap fails the lab refuses to start tests. Exit Test Lab is a full reload on the URL without `testlab`. | `testlab/isolation.ts`, `testlab/boot.ts` |
| TL3 | **Real modes only; setup hooks, never scripted outcomes.** Full Playtests start through the normal paths (campaign controller, `commands.start`). Scenarios are real raids built by `buildRaidGame`, then prepared once through an optional `RaidMeta.prepare(state)` hook (stand extra enemies down, choose start tiles, swap an enemy kind); enemies still wake through the sim's wave code and decide with the real bots. No sim or AI rule or tuning changed. The boss gate is opened only by `CampaignDeps.testLabUnlocked`. | `app/MatchController.ts`, `app/campaign.ts`, `testlab/scenarios/` |
| TL4 | **Feedback is the founder's alone:** Pass / Needs improvement / Failed / Not tested plus a note, set only by a tap, kept under its own key, summarised as compact plain text for pasting (Clipboard API with a textarea fallback). | `testlab/feedback.ts` |
| TL5 | **Player marker contract:** `render/playerMarker.ts` (`original` / `indicator` / `indicatorGround`, default `indicator`) is owned by the render worker; the lab only calls `setPlayerMarkerMode` (pause menu, entry card, HUD button of that scenario). | `render/playerMarker.ts` |

## OX / SU - Occluded buildings you still collide with, and the Sudden Death banner and victory (branch `claude/wahala-visuals-sd`, from production `b6b2367`)

Founder reports: a building that fades out because it hides the fighter still blocks, so he walks into what looks like empty space; the Sudden Death announcement sometimes stays across the middle of the arena; after a timeout whose Sudden Death took both fighters down, the winner (on score) lay dead on the victory screen. Render / UI only: no collision, rule, tuning or camera change.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OX1 | **A faded structure is a restrained ghost, never invisible.** The dither cap of what hides the followed fighter (`VIEW_HIDE`) was 0.88 (12% of a building's pixels left, on a pale floor as good as gone); it is now `OCCLUSION_LOOK.farHide` 0.76, and 0.62 while the fighter is next to it or pushing at it. The fighter stays readable (the dissolve is still stronger than the 0.72 of a pillar that merely stands in front, and the x-ray silhouette is unchanged). Collision, the view-clear test, its hysteresis and the camera are untouched. | `render/assets/occlusionFootprint.ts`, `occlusionFade.ts` |
| OX2 | **The blocked tiles are drawn on the ground while a structure is faded.** A cover group's footprint is exactly the tiles it claims in the sim layout (`CoverBounds.tiles`, a test checks every arena's cover claims only `#` tiles and no tile twice); a per-tile pillar is its own tile. Look: a worn-ochre rim on the OUTER boundary only (a shared edge inside one building has no line, so it reads as one object), a dark line just outside it (readable on a pale floor as on asphalt) and a barely-there dark patch; the north / south rim is wider because the camera squeezes it; low opacity, fog off, under the telegraphs. Breakable crates get none (low, solid foot). No bright cartoon outline. | `render/views/OcclusionFootprints.ts`, `boardCover.ts` |
| OX3 | **More legible when near or pushing.** Proximity is the distance from the fighter to the footprint's nearest edge (full at 0.7 tile, none at 2.2); `pushing` is a settled fighter facing a blocked tile next to it. Near / pushing lowers the cap to 0.62 and raises the rim from 62% to full strength. Read from sim state in the view (`Player.facing`, `stepRemaining`); no new sim field. | `occlusionFootprint.ts` (`proximity`, `isPushing`, `hideFor`, `outlineStrength`) |
| OX4 | **Smooth, and several structures at once.** One exponential ease for the dither and the rim (in 0.16 s, out 0.10 s; was 0.12 / 0.05, frame-rate independent, no overshoot, no pop). Overlapping structures each keep their own cap and rim; the nearest to the fighter keeps the full cap, each one behind it holds 0.04 less (at most 4 steps, never below 0.3) so a whole street does not vanish. A group takes the building's own cap on every tile (the pillar rule of the tile in front of a player no longer overrides it). | `occlusionFootprint.ts` (`ease`, `stackedHide`), `ArenaView.ts` |
| OX5 | **Quality ladder.** New live budget `QualityProfile.occlusionFootprints` (footprints drawn at once, nearest first; 2, 2, 3, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8 up the ladder; never 0, each is at most three small transparent draws). The fade itself stays on every rung. | `render/quality.ts`, `tests/render/occlusion-footprint.test.ts` |
| SU1 | **The Sudden Death banner is brief, in the rail, and cannot outlive its moment** (amends SD5 and SD-B3). The title card is no longer a full-screen scrim with a large panel across the arena: it is a compact card at the top of the HUD rail beside the board (title, reason, up to 4 contenders and "+N more", the rule line except on short screens), `pointer-events: none`. It still ends with the sim's held input, and now also on its own clock: the card and the full blast announcement time out after 8 s of real time (`useBriefly`, keyed per round / per countdown; the normal sim-driven lifecycle is 4 s and 3 s, so a normal match never meets it), so a stalled simulation clock (hidden tab, held match) can no longer leave them up. The round's HUD is dropped when the match has ended (it used to ride on into the end moment and the result screen). | `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx`, `SuddenDeath.css`, `HudRail.tsx`, `app/hud.ts` |
| SU2 | **A winner who is down is shown standing on the result.** Root cause: the sim is as designed (SD1 / SD4 / AS6a: when the last contenders fall together, the credited kills of every player decide, a lead earned in the round wins even if the leader fell in that blast, a dead leader wins a timeout; only a still-shared lead is the `suddenDeathExhausted` draw; Classic keeps its draws), but the figure's pose followed `alive`, so the winner lay dead or was gone under the "Victory" result. Now, when the match has ended, a down winner is drawn upright in the victory pose on the tile where they fell (nearest open floor if a block landed there), facing the camera, with the revive shockwave; a team win with nobody standing raises only the last members to fall; a draw raises nobody. Presentation only: `alive` stays false, nothing is credited or resurrected, the rules are unchanged. | `render/views/victoryPose.ts`, `PlayerViews.ts`, `tests/render/victory-pose.test.ts` |
| TL6 | **City Rumble parity: the lab starts the normal match with the normal settings.** The lab's first City Rumble mapped Easy / Normal / Hard to Beginner / Casual / Standard bots (`campaignEnemyLevel`), while a normal match defaults to Rookie, the weakest level; that, not the engine (both go through `commands.start` and the same createGame options), is why the founder found it much harder.
