The City Rumble test now holds the fields a normal match reads (stage, mode, fighters 8 / 16 / 24 / 32, all seven bot levels, mixed roster, 3 / 5 min, friendly fire, fighter, camera and control prefs), starts from the player's real saved settings (read once, read-only, in `realSnapshot.ts` before the in-memory storage replaces them; the defaults when nothing is saved), has a **Use Current Rumble Settings** button, and lists the exact configuration before Start. `buildMatchOptions` (`app/names.ts`) is the one options builder for the menu's Start Match and the lab; tests prove identical `GameOptions`, initial state and state hash after 240 ticks with the real bots. No AI, tuning or default (still 16 fighters, Rookie) changed; nothing is saved. | `testlab/rumbleSetup.ts`, `testlab/realSnapshot.ts`, `testlab/labActions.ts`, `app/names.ts`, `tests/testlab/rumble-parity.test.ts` |
| TL7 | **Tests Requiring Review / Completed Tests.** Home shows the seven tests that need review first and in order (City Rumble parity, Occluded building visibility, Sudden Death banner and victory, Boss encounter, Classic rear ambush, One-hit lethality, Player-identification variants), each with a one-line objective and what to look for; the ten approved tests (the five isolated scenarios rear approach, patrol ambush, punch dodge, pursuit, flashlight, and the approved full playtests) sit in a collapsed Completed Tests list and stay launchable. No existing test id changed, so stored feedback stays attached; the copied summary groups by the same two lists and carries the City Rumble configuration or the chosen one-hit enemy. New entries launch real modes only: the Lekki Rumble stage, the game's own Sudden Death test match, the real first raid. | `testlab/registry.ts`, `ui/testlab/TestLab.tsx`, `testlab/feedback.ts` |
| TL8 | **Player marker switch: the wiring was right, the Ground style was invisible.** In a real browser every tap on the lab's switch, the pause menu and the HUD button reached the render module (one instance; `__WAHALA__.playerMarker()` followed each tap; Original showed the chevron and "You" name tag, Indicator the YOU plate), but Indicator + Ground differed from Indicator by a 30 % tint at 0.34-0.54 opacity with a one-pixel crescent, which the fixed camera cannot show (PI6 understated it past visibility). The floor mark is now a disc with a dark-outlined ring in the fighter's colour, 1.75 tiles wide (the fighter's own ring is 0.96) at about 0.6-0.85 opacity with a small slow pulse; still a flat decal, no light, never the default (`indicator` stays), render only. A unit test checks the three styles are three different sets of visible parts and the ground mark's size and opacity; the Playwright test compares real pixels around the player for the three styles. | `render/views/youMarker.ts`, `render/textures.ts` `groundMarkTexture`, `e2e/testlab.spec.ts` |
| TL9 | **One-hit lethality is tested on posts, not on walkers.** The first set-up put three enemies in a row and they walked away. Now ONE ordinary enemy (agbero, guard or thrower, chosen on the card) holds a post at the entrance (2, 11) facing east into the depot with its back to the player, who starts at (1, 9), two steps from the tile behind it. Under the real rules a calm raid enemy is blind behind it (CV1) and does not hear a generator laid there on Easy and Normal (CV2); the player walks, lays, steps out of the cross and the real blast kills it (3 s fuse). A post turns a quarter every 4 s (Easy) / 2.5 s (Normal), so there is a window of about 1.5 s on Normal to lay it; Hard hears the generator (by design, no kill promised). The posts were found by simulation, not by hand: the column-13 posts are on a conveyor belt and walk away. Nothing is forced: the scenario places entities only; `tests/testlab/one-hit.test.ts` plays each kind at Easy and Normal with intents only, checks the walk exists, the enemy never alerted and the kill is credited to the human. | `testlab/scenarios/defs.ts`, `tests/testlab/one-hit.test.ts` |
## BB - Boss balance: the Oshodi boss mission made fair per level (branch `claude/wahala-boss-sim`, from `b6b2367`, `docs/BOSS_BALANCE.md`)

Why: the founder played the real Oshodi boss mission on Easy and Normal and found both excessively difficult. Measured on
the real mission (`npm run boss-measure`: a scripted player held to a person's limits, 0.3-0.4 s reactions, a few
decisions a second, only what the screen shows, the real boss and crew bots, 8 seeds per cell): the SHIELDED boss left
his booth about a second into every fight and hunted the player along the corridor invulnerable (23-38 s of a fight more
than 5 tiles from his post; 82-86 % of his 24-35 generators within 3 tiles of the player); every boss-mission bot's Punch
wound up in 4 ticks (no phone player steps out of that, and the bots use it to hold a player inside a blast); the exposed
boss kept laying generators where the player had to stand; only 49-73 % of the player's generators that caught the
exposed boss cost him a hit; and nothing of the boss or the objective was different on Easy. Before -> after (fair /
slow player): deaths per fight Easy 0.3 / 0.4 -> 0.1 / 0.0, Normal 1.5 / 1.6 -> 0.6 / 0.4, Hard 1.5 / 1.3 -> 0.8 / 0.9;
wins Normal 7/8 -> 8/8, Hard 5/8 / 8/8 -> 8/8 / 8/8.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| BB1 | **The shielded boss defends his booth.** A boss recipe may name a boss balance (`BossRecipe.balance`, `config.ts` `BOSS_BALANCE`; only Oga Sunmola's does). Shielded, his guard directive carries a `ground` (Manhattan tiles from his post: Easy 3, Normal 5, Hard 8): his planner takes no goal beyond it (walk, pickup or generator) unless only an escape leads there (`ai/directive.ts` `groundBias`, a 1000-point penalty). He still bombs a player who comes into his ground. Why: he was invulnerable AND hunting the whole corridor; the guard's 1-point-per-tile leash lost to any placement he wanted. | `config.ts`, `mission/boss.ts`, `mission/directives.ts`, `ai/directive.ts`, `ai/goals.ts`, `ai/context.ts` |
| BB2 | **Readable wind-ups for every bot seat of the boss fight.** `campaignTactics.botMeleeWindupTicks` (Easy 36, Normal 30, Hard 24; written by `buildMissionGame` from the recipe's balance at the level): a generator-bot seat (no `enemyKind`) starts any close attack with at least this wind-up (`skills.meleeStartupTicks`, used by `beginAttack`); the bots plan their skill and their stand-still checks with the same number. The player's attacks and every other match keep the catalogue's. Why: a phone player needs ~15 ticks to read a wind-up plus up to half a step to move; the catalogue's Punch is 4. | `config.ts`, `mission/build.ts`, `systems/skills.ts`, `ai/skills.ts`, `ai/tactics.ts` |
| BB3 | **The window is an opening.** While exposed (on his way to or at his anchor) the boss may have at most `exposedGenerators` down (Easy 0, Normal 1, Hard 2 = his capacity): `rules.missionGeneratorCap` in `tryPlaceGenerator`, and the anchor `block` directive's `maxGenerators` for his planner. | `mission/rules.ts`, `systems/generators.ts`, `mission/directives.ts`, `ai/goals.ts` |
| BB4 | **His own fire never swallows the player's (all bosses, a correctness fix).** Lethality asked `missionBossAbsorbs` about the first lethal hit only; when his own generator's flames were listed first (his and the player's went off in the same tick), the player's hit was absorbed with them. It now asks about a hit that is not his own when there is one (`rules.bossHitOwner`). Changes nothing outside boss missions and nothing in a tick without both fires on him. | `systems/lethality.ts`, `mission/rules.ts` |
| BB5 | **A chain carries the player's fire.** With a balance (`chainCarriesFire`), a crew or boss generator that the player's blast set off (the blast's `seedOwnerId` is the player) burns the EXPOSED boss as the player's fire. Why: a crew generator between the player's and the boss stops the radial wave on its tile and its own flames were friendly fire: the player saw his blast go off beside the boss and nothing counted. | `mission/rules.ts` `missionChainOwner`, `systems/lethality.ts` |
| BB6 | **The objective's pressure per level.** `BOSS_BALANCE.objective` multiplies the authored phase numbers at the level (`tuning.ts`): convoy interval x 1.6 / 1.25 / 1, jam duration x 1.5 / 1.25 / 1, a runner's re-arm time x 2 / 1.5 / 1 (Easy / Normal / Hard). Hard plays the authored relays. Warning floors (MS2) still apply. | `config.ts`, `mission/tuning.ts` |
| BB7 | **Measured, and isolated.** `scripts/lib/bossHuman.ts` (the scripted person) and `npm run boss-measure`; acceptance `tests/boss-balance-fights.test.ts` (Easy won with at most one life lost, Normal won, exact end, determinism) and `tests/boss-balance.test.ts`. The balance fields of `BossTuning` exist only with a balance, so the other five bosses' and the finale's serialised matches are byte-identical; the pinned Oshodi boss digest in `tests/raid-ai-isolation.test.ts` was re-pinned deliberately (it is this mission). The recorded flee scene of `tests/mission-crew.test.ts` keeps the catalogue wind-ups. Unchanged: three hits, windows, crew calls, escort cover, the player's health and lives, speeds, perception. | `scripts/`, `tests/` |

## SDT / PM / TB - Sudden Death timer, permanent player marker, viewer-relative blast warnings (branch `claude/wahala-final-polish`, from production `00eff0a`; presentation and Test Lab only, no rule or damage change)

Investigation (SDT): the "banner in the middle of the screen" was NOT Test Lab-only. The Test Lab's Sudden Death entry starts the game's own `suddenDeathTest` match, and both it and a normal City Rumble that reaches Sudden Death render the SAME component (`ui/SuddenDeath.tsx` `SuddenDeathClock`) in the same place: the "full" blast announcement, a 420 x 89 px panel at the top-centre of the board (832 x 384: x 206-626, y 112-201) with the kill ranking, shown for the first 3 s of EVERY blast countdown, then a one-line chip that stayed at the same spot for the whole round. The Release 310 fix had moved only the title card (`SuddenDeathCard`) to the rail. The main match clock read 0:00 in a normal match and the spent match time (9:57 ...) in the Test Lab's test, i.e. it did not show the only real clock of the round. The simulation has a genuine countdown (`suddenDeath.nextBlastTick`: first blast 60 s after the card, then 15 s later; the Sudden Death test's own stage schedule), so no new timer is invented.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SDT1 | **The main timer is the blast countdown during a Sudden Death round.** `ui/timerModel.ts` (pure) reads the HUD snapshot: during the title card's held input it shows the first blast's delay, then each blast's own `nextBlastTick - tick` (rounded up, exactly the sim's), the plate turns red, the tag says SUDDEN DEATH, a narrow column beside the digits says BLAST n/3 (FINAL BLAST on the last), the bar is the fraction of that countdown left, and the digits pulse in the warning window. Outside a round it is the match clock as before. `SuddenDeathHud.countdownSeconds` is the one new HUD field. | `ui/timerModel.ts`, `ui/Hud.tsx`, `ui/styles.css`, `app/hud.ts` |
| SDT2 | **The announcement is brief and never over the board.** The full panel is now a compact rail card (blast number, whether shields count, what it means for you; the ranking is dropped, the title card already listed the contenders), shown for the sim-driven 3 s and at most `SD_ANNOUNCE_MAX_MS` (8 s) of real time, then gone: the persistent chip is retired (the timer carries the countdown). The blast flash keeps its own element. Rules, scoring and the Release 310 standing-winner presentation are untouched. | `ui/SuddenDeath.tsx`, `ui/HudRail.tsx`, `ui/SuddenDeath.css` |
| PM1 | **Indicator + Ground is the permanent player marker.** `DEFAULT_PLAYER_MARKER = 'indicatorGround'`: the overhead YOU plate AND the floor ring under the fighter in every match. The Test Lab's three-way switch (entry card, pause menu, HUD button, store field) is retired; the Original and Indicator styles remain only for unit tests and `?debug` (`__WAHALA__.playerMarker`). The ring is hollow-centred (the tile under the player stays visible), a flat decal drawn below the blast overlays. | `render/playerMarker.ts`, `testlab/*`, `ui/testlab/*` |
| TB1 | **A blast's safety is classified per viewer by the damage rule itself.** `flameHarms` (queries.ts) = `canHarm` (self always, a teammate only with friendly fire, an opponent / spectator / environment always) except that a VEHICLE GENERATOR never catches its own owner (the lethal-hit rule's own exception). `systems/blastSafety.ts` (pure) labels each generator that burns a tile by ITS owner (every flame carries its generator's owner, chain reactions included), so a teammate's blast that sets off a rival's generator is dangerous exactly on the rival link's tiles. A viewer who is down or a spectator is told nothing is safe. FFA, solo, Classic and campaign have no teams: every blast is dangerous and the lanes are drawn exactly as before. | `sim/systems/blastSafety.ts`, `sim/systems/queries.ts` |
| TB2 | **Geometry is the simulation's own; danger takes priority.** `previewBlastParts` (explosions.ts) is the one traversal (obstacles, radial footprint, chain links); `previewBlast` is its union. The overlay merges every generator's labelled tiles and `applyDangerPriority` removes any safe mark from a tile a dangerous blast covers (about to burn) or is burning on right now. Look (team viewers only): SAFE = steady emerald tile with a DASHED outline and a ring in the middle; DANGEROUS = the existing red glow plus a diagonal hazard hatch with a solid border and the existing pulse. Shape and pattern differ, not only colour. Same timing as the existing lanes (the last 1.7 s of a fuse, thrown vehicle generators from release) and, for a spared blast, while its flames burn. | `render/views/OverlayViews.ts`, `render/textures.ts` |
| TB3 | **The three layers are tested to agree.** `tests/blast-safety.test.ts` runs the real explosion for every owner x friendly-fire case, chains both ways, overlaps and many simultaneous generators and checks the viewer dies exactly when the label says dangerous; `e2e/team-blast.spec.ts` reads the live three.js scene (tiles drawn safe / dangerous, never both, none left after the blast, the marker parts). No combat, friendly-fire or damage rule changed; `lethality.ts` is untouched. | `tests/`, `e2e/` |
| TB4 | **Team Blast Test Lab scenes** (friendly fire OFF, ON, overlap): a small REAL Team Rumble (you, a teammate, two rivals; team ruleset and friendly fire through `buildMatchOptions`, the sim's own alternating team seating) on the Lagos Island stage; the other seats stand still and lay generators with the real `place` intent on a timer (a director, as the Training Academy uses); the stage's hazards and the crowd are off (`stageHazards` is the tutorials-and-tests switch). You lay your own with A. Waves: teammate alone, rival alone, side by side, a chain, three at once. | `testlab/teamBlast.ts`, `app/session.ts` `labRunScene` |

## ON - online integration (branch `claude/wahala-online-integration`, docs/ONLINE_INTEGRATION.md; server work package `claude/wahala-online-s`)

Ids follow the integration plan (ON1-ON12 in `docs/ONLINE_INTEGRATION.md`); the older "ON - Online, voice ... scaffolds" section above used the same ids for earlier work. Server rows only here; the client rows ON1 / ON2 / ON11 are appended by their owners.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ON6 | **Disconnect policy: stand, then a bot, never a pause; the same fighter comes back.** A human's connection drops mid-match: (a) for `RUMBLE_GRACE_SECONDS` (0..60, default 5) the seat simply stands where it is (no bot yet, it may be hit), (b) then, policy `RUMBLE_DROP_POLICY=bot` (default; `idle` = keep standing until the window ends), a replacement bot plays the fighter and the match never pauses for anyone, (c) until `RUMBLE_RECONNECT_SECONDS` (default 30, counted from the drop) the human can take back THE SAME fighter: reconnecting only flips the controller (`RumbleMatch.connect`), the `Player` object, position, step, lives, stats, protection and respawn timers are untouched, a fighter who fell while away returns as the same down / respawning fighter under the mode's own rules, (d) after the window the bot keeps the seat for the rest of the match. Grace is counted in simulation ticks (60 / s), so it is deterministic. A returning player gets a fresh bot brain on the next takeover. The lobby view carries an optional `botControlled` so the UI can say "a bot is playing for X" (the member row disappears when the window ends: the seat is a human seat with no connected member). | `server/src/core/RumbleMatch.ts`, `Lobby.ts`, `RumbleRoom.ts`, `options.ts`, `tests/match.disconnect`, `room.disconnect*` |
| ON7 | **Personal statistics count human-controlled time only; the match result is untouched.** Per human seat the server keeps `human*` and `bot*` kills / deaths / assists, `autopilotTicks`, `absentTicks` and `drops`, as per-tick deltas of what the simulation itself credited (`eliminate()` is its only writer), attributed by who controlled the seat on the tick the credit happened. `ResultsMsg.players[].kills / deaths / assists` are the human numbers; `botKills / botDeaths / botAssists / autopilotSeconds / absentSeconds / drops / controlledByBotAtEnd` are new optional fields (human seats only; server bots keep the simulation's totals and carry none). `kills + botKills` always equals the simulation's total for the seat. A tick on which nobody acts (grace, idle) belongs to the human. The simulation, team score, Sudden Death and the winner are NOT changed: every credited kill still counts for the match; `placement` is the simulation's. Whether bot time counts for rank / XP, whether a long absence forfeits and whether a returning player keeps the bot's team-score kills are NOT decided: listed as open founder questions in `docs/CITY_RUMBLE_ONLINE.md` section 8. | `RumbleMatch.attributeStats`, `results()`, `src/net/protocol.ts` (optional fields) |
| ON8 | **Policy values are server configuration, validated, with safe fallbacks.** `RuntimeConfig.graceSeconds` (`RUMBLE_GRACE_SECONDS`, 0..60, default 5), `reconnectSeconds` (`RUMBLE_RECONNECT_SECONDS`, 1..3600 default 30; 0.1.. in test-fast mode), `dropPolicy` (`RUMBLE_DROP_POLICY` = `bot` | `idle`, default `bot`); anything else falls back to the default, never to an error. A grace longer than the window means the bot only arrives when the window ends. The match itself defaults to grace 0 and accepts the legacy name `autopilot` (older tests). Metrics: `net.drops`, `net.botTakeovers`. | `server/src/core/options.ts`, `tests/options.test.ts` |
| ON9 | **Local development setup: one command, LAN only, no cost.** `npm run dev:online` (`scripts/dev-online.mjs`, no dependency) starts the Node server (`server`, `npm start`, 0.0.0.0:2567) and the Vite dev server (0.0.0.0:5173, strict port) with `WAHALA_ONLINE_ENABLED=true` and `WAHALA_ONLINE_URL=ws://{host}:2567` (`{host}` replaced by the page's own hostname at run time: no address in code). The script computes this machine's LAN addresses and passes the exact page origins (localhost, each `http://<lan-ip>:5173`, the machine name and `.local` name) to the server as `RUMBLE_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`; in development mode the server allows localhost plus that list, in production ONLY the explicit list (nothing by default, never implicit localhost or a LAN range, never a wildcard we add). The server prints its LAN URLs at start; `GET /health` is the phone-side reachability check. The dev server's base is `/` (vite.config.ts uses `/wahala-game/` only for build / preview), so the URL is `http://<lan-ip>:5173/?online`. HTTP-to-WS on purpose (an HTTPS page cannot open an insecure `ws://`); a secure option (mkcert, a free tunnel) is documented, not set up. | `scripts/dev-online.mjs`, `server/src/core/origins.ts`, `lan.ts`, `docs/CITY_RUMBLE_ONLINE_LOCAL.md`, `tests/lan.test.ts` |
## ON (integration) - City Rumble online behind two independent switches (branch `claude/wahala-online-integration`, docs/ONLINE_INTEGRATION.md; the ids ON1-ON12 are that document's and are distinct from the scaffold's ON1-ON11 above, hence the "[integration]" tag)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ON1 [integration] | **Two independent switches, and the endpoint comes from the build only.** `FeatureFlags.rumbleOnline` is true only when the BUILD has `WAHALA_ONLINE_ENABLED` truthy AND a non-empty `WAHALA_ONLINE_URL` (the existing "server build") AND the page URL has `?online`; `onlineEndpoint` is `WAHALA_ONLINE_URL` with `{host}` replaced by `location.hostname` (`resolveFlags(env, search, hostname)` stays pure), null in any other build, with an empty / malformed hostname, or when the scheme is not `ws://` `wss://` `http://` `https://` (then `rumbleOnline` is false too). The endpoint is never read from the URL, and no host, address or tunnel appears in `src/` as a default (a test reads the flags source for it). `?mockonline` is unchanged (local mock, no sockets) except that `?mockonline=0` / `off` / `false` / `no` now means "not set". The `?online` parameter on a default build changes nothing at all. `npm run build:online` = server build into git-ignored `dist-online/` with the TEMPLATE `ws://{host}:2567`. | `platform/flags.ts`, `online/rumbleEntry.ts` (`rumbleOnlineAvailable()`), `package.json`, `tests/platform-flags.test.ts` |
| ON2 [integration] | **One guarded edge into the online zone.** `src/net/**`, `src/app/rumbleOnline.ts`, `src/ui/online/rumble/**` and `server/**` must be unreachable from `src/main.tsx` except through a dynamic `import()` lexically guarded by `__WAHALA_SERVER_ONLINE__` (early return, `if (CONST && ...) {}` block, or `CONST ? / &&` in the same statement). A runtime flag (`flags().rumbleOnline`) is NOT a guard: it cannot keep code out of a public bundle. `import type` edges are erased and ignored. The match controller, `session.ts`, sim, ai, input, render and campaign never reach `src/net`, `src/online`, the controller or its screens (`remoteMatch.ts` is the types-only port). Only `src/net/client/connection.ts` names the Colyseus SDK. The detector is unit-tested on planted guarded / unguarded snippets and on two fixture entries. | `tests/online/importGuard.ts`, `tests/online/import-guard.test.ts`, `tests/platform-static.test.ts` |
| ON11 [integration] | **The public bundle is asserted, not assumed.** `scripts/check-static-build.mjs` (rules in `scripts/lib/staticBuildScan.mjs`) fails a default build that contains a socket / WebRTC / microphone API, `colyseus`, the room-code alphabet or `RumbleSession` (online chunk markers), `home-rumble-online`, any `ws://` / `wss://` literal, an http(s) URL or quoted host naming localhost / loopback / 0.0.0.0 / RFC1918 / `.local`, a private address with a port, or a tunnel host (ngrok, trycloudflare, loca.lt ...). Addresses are URL-shaped, never bare words, so vendored libraries cannot trip them; the only absolute URLs the libraries carry (`www.w3.org` namespaces, a `react.dev` error link, a `jcgt.org` citation) are listed with their reason. `tests/online/public-bundle.test.ts` builds the default app AND a server app (`--sourcemap --manifest`, temp dirs) and proves from the source maps / manifest that no online module is in any public chunk, that in the server build online code is a separate dynamic chunk absent from the entry chunk and everything it imports statically, and that the endpoint there is the `{host}` template, never a host. `e2e/online-public-offline.spec.ts` proves in a browser, against the default build, that `?online` and server-smuggling URLs show no entry, construct no WebSocket / EventSource, make only same-origin requests, and that an offline City Rumble starts and plays (also after the network is taken away). | `scripts/`, `tests/online/`, `e2e/` |
| ON13 [integration] | **A dropped connection is always retried, at any uptime.** The Colyseus SDK refuses automatic reconnection for a connection younger than `reconnection.minUptime` (5 s). A phone that drops while its match is still loading is exactly that case (found by the two-browser e2e: the guest never came back). `connection.ts` sets `minUptime = 0`; the server holds the seat for `RUMBLE_RECONNECT_SECONDS` either way. | `src/net/client/connection.ts`, `e2e/rumble-online.spec.ts` scenario 3 |
| ON14 [integration] | **Everything the match start needs is fetched while the network is known to work.** The arena's models are prefetched when the lobby names the arena (`FlowDeps.prefetch`, so the start is not a long first-frame stall), and the in-match layer (chip, reconnect banner, results; its own lazy chunk) is imported from the room screen, not at match start (a fetch started offline failed and left the player with no feedback). Online chunk only; the public game is unchanged. | `src/app/rumbleOnlineFlow.ts`, `src/ui/online/rumble/RumbleOnlineScreen.tsx` |
| ON15 [integration] | **Server liveness tolerance is configuration, not a constant.** The transport drops a socket after `pingMaxRetries` missed pongs at `pingInterval`: default 3 s x 4 (a page stalled by a first-frame compile or a busy CPU is not a dropped player); `RUMBLE_PING_INTERVAL_MS` (500..30000) and `RUMBLE_PING_MAX_RETRIES` (1..20) override it. A drop in the lobby or countdown is a lobby drop (the member leaves, "Try again" rejoins); only a playing match reserves the seat. | `server/src/core/options.ts`, `server/src/app.ts`, docs/CITY_RUMBLE_ONLINE_LOCAL.md |
## ON - online integration (branch `claude/wahala-online-integration` and its work packages; docs/ONLINE_INTEGRATION.md)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ON4 | **The local fighter's walking is predicted, as presentation only.** Each frame the driver starts from the replica's authoritative local player, replays the inputs the server has not applied yet with the sim's own `updateMovement` on a private view (a copy of the player, copied generators) through a model of the server's press latch, and draws the fighter at the tick an input sent now reaches the server (at most 40 ticks ahead, slewed). It is an in-place overlay of the seven step fields on the replica's local Player, reverted before every `replica.advance` (patches and hash checks see only authoritative data) and re-applied after; verify mode proves bit-identity after every revert. Holds (slide, manhole, vehicle entrance, inverted controls, SUDDEN DEATH card, daze, attack wind-up) draw the server's position; a correction <= 0.55 tile blends over 120 ms, a larger one snaps. Never sent, never in the HUD scores, kill feed or results; off by default in the driver, on in the browser unless `?nopredict`. Why: the step showed 167-600 ms late (p50 from rest, harness) under europe-like to bad-mobile links; with prediction 17-33 ms. | `src/net/client/prediction/**`, `src/net/client/driver.ts`, docs/ONLINE_PREDICTION.md, `tests/net/prediction*.test.ts` |
| ON5 | **No server ack: the input clock comes from the existing ping / pong.** `PongMsg.t` (the server tick when the ping was handled) gives the tick an input sent at the same client time arrives at; median of 5 pongs plus a bias from presses from rest, and a server step the latch model cannot explain credits the matching press. An ack would need a per-client message (frames are encoded once per room) and cannot foresee a lost input either. Measured estimate error p95: 0 ticks (LAN, europe-like), 1 (mobile), 16 (bad-mobile: retransmissions). Protocol and server unchanged. | `src/net/client/prediction/clock.ts`, `predictor.ts`; docs/ONLINE_PREDICTION.md section 5 |
## ON - online integration (branch `claude/wahala-online-integration`; docs/ONLINE_INTEGRATION.md)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ON10 | **The online City Rumble UI is the game's own menu flow, a lazy chunk that exists only in an online build.** Home gets `home-rumble-online` ("PLAY ONLINE", secondary style) only when `flags().rumbleOnline`; it calls `loadRumbleOnline()` then `open()`. The flow is one menu screen id `rumbleOnline` (`app/menu.ts`, guarded by `rumbleOnlineScreenReady`; MainMenu lazy-loads it behind `__WAHALA_SERVER_ONLINE__`) whose steps are entry -> connecting -> room -> loading (-> the game's match) -> results, or error; the fighter is the one already chosen in the game (a "Change" link opens the existing Fighter screen, which returns to the entry on Confirm), and the host's stage / mode / length / bot level reuse the sim registries and the menu's own difficulty slider. State lives in one Zustand store (`app/rumbleOnlineFlow.ts`, all outside parts injected so it is tested without a socket); the connection chip, the reconnect banner and the results over the frozen board are one overlay (`RumbleOnlineOverlay`) mounted by `App.tsx` only while `app/rumbleOverlayHost.ts` says an online match is showing, so nothing online is fetched before the player taps the entry. Rules shown (who may ready / start / change settings) mirror the server's checks in a pure `roomRules`, but the server decides; names are only ever React text; Esc / Back in a room asks "Leave this room?" (a second press cancels) instead of silently leaving a connection behind; every waiting state (connecting, loading) has Cancel / Back, and a dropped connection while loading offers Try again / Back to menu, never an endless spinner. Bot time is shown as the server reports it (`autopilotSeconds`, `botKills` ... read defensively) and bot kills are never presented as the absent player's own. Until `src/net/client/session.ts` lands the session is an in-memory FAKE (`app/rumbleOnlineFake.ts`, behind the `app/rumbleOnlineSession.ts` indirection, labelled "Test session: no real server" on every screen); it never opens a socket. | `src/app/rumbleOnline*.ts`, `src/ui/online/rumble/`, `src/app/menu.ts`, `src/ui/menu/{HomeScreen,MainMenu}.tsx`, `src/ui/App.tsx`, `tests/ui/rumble-online-*.test.ts`, `tests/app/rumble-online-flow.test.ts`, `docs/screenshots/online-ui/` |

## PIV - Post-integration visual review (branch `claude/wahala-pi-visual`, from production `9491d92`; presentation only, no rule or balance change)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PIV1 | **A Sudden Death round's match clock is spent for good: the main timer reads 0:00 after the winner is known.** Founder phone review (Test Lab, 750x336): the timer showed about 9:21 at the end. Cause, reproduced in a unit test and in a real browser: the Test Lab's `durationTicks` is a long dummy (600 s, `SUDDEN_DEATH_TEST_CONFIG`) that the round never reaches, so the raw `durationTicks - playTicks` is minutes long (9:23 when the lab's bots finish at tick 2418; 9:56 when stepped by hand); on the `ended` phase the HUD drops its Sudden Death snapshot (SU1), so `timerModel` fell through to `hud.secondsLeft`. A normal City Rumble was never affected (a round only starts once `playTicks >= durationTicks`, so the remainder is already 0). `buildHud` now reads the match clock through `matchSecondsLeft(state)`: 0 whenever `state.match.suddenDeath` exists (live round and after it), else exactly `secondsRemaining`. That fixes the main timer and the pause menu's "Time left", and `final30` follows. Not changed: the blast schedule, the live countdown (SDT1), the title card, the SU1 winner presentation, any rule. An ordinary match that ends before time keeps its frozen real remainder. | `app/hud.ts` `matchSecondsLeft`, `tests/ui/timer-ended.test.ts`, `e2e/sudden-death-test.spec.ts`, `e2e/sudden-death.spec.ts` |
| PIV2 | **Local fighter in a crowd: a stencil-gated silhouette, only where OTHER FIGHTERS hide it.** Every fighter body writes a stencil value as it draws (0 = none, 1 = rival, 2 = the local fighter); each mesh sets its value just before its own draw and restores 0 after (the character materials are the library's and shared by every copy, so the value cannot live on the material; a background person on the same material writes 0). The local fighter gets one extra translucent twin per skinned mesh (lighter tint of its slot / team colour, opacity 0.7, no depth or stencil write, render order 39 under the YOU tag) that draws only where the depth test fails (`GreaterDepth`) AND the stencil is 1. Walls, buildings, crates and props leave 0 and never trigger it; the fighter's own body (2) never tints itself; the older "hidden by scenery" silhouette (every fighter) now requires stencil 0, which is exactly the set it had before (it excluded stencil 1, the only fighter value then). World depth and positions are untouched; no pass over the screen; the overhead YOU tag and the floor ring (PI1-PI6, PM1) are unchanged. Quality ladder: `QualityProfile.crowdSilhouette`, false on Very Low and Very Low+, true from Low up, LIVE (a visibility switch on meshes built with the fighter; a manual preset is never changed by Auto). Measured at the 750x336 / 832x384 touch viewports with 16 and 32 fighters packed round the local fighter (software GL, not a phone): +5 to +6 draw calls (472 -> 478, 758 -> 764, 472 -> 477, 755 -> 762: one per skinned mesh of the local fighter, 6) and about +17.8k triangles (+1.2 % of 1.45 M, +0.9 % of 2.07 M); frame time off versus on is inside the noise of the software renderer (about 1.2-2.0 s per frame at dpr 2, alternating A/B rounds, no consistent sign). Not verified: a physical phone. Known limits: the silhouette is drawn over translucent faded scenery too (a faded building is not an opaque occluder), and the three-state stencil assumes the nearest opaque fighter wins the pixel (true for the draw-order-independent depth test; a rival drawn first and then covered by a nearer wall leaves a stale 1 under the wall, where the older wall silhouette already draws the fighter). | `render/crowdSilhouette.ts`, `render/assets/xray.ts`, `render/views/PlayerViews.ts`, `render/quality.ts`, `render/GameRenderer.ts` |
| PIV3 | **Options compared for PIV2 (what was and was not chosen).** | `scripts/crowd-silhouette-shots.mjs`, `docs/screenshots/post-integration/` |

| Option | Draws through walls / props? | Extra cost | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Draw the local fighter with no depth test (always on top) | Yes, through everything, and over blast overlays | 0 extra, but wrong | Rejected (the founder's explicit "not in front of every 3D object") |
| (a) Silhouette twin with a depth-fails test only (the existing wall twin, extended) | Yes, it is the existing see-through-scenery look | 1 draw per mesh | Not enough alone: it cannot tell a rival from a building |
| **(b) Stencil-gated twin: depth fails AND a rival fighter's body is the visible surface (chosen)** | **No: only fighter pixels (stencil 1) trigger it** | **+1 draw per skinned mesh of one fighter (6), no pass, +1 % triangles** | **Chosen, behind a ladder field** |
| (c) Fade the rivals that overlap the local fighter (alpha) | No | Needs per-fighter material copies (the materials are shared), sorted transparency for up to 31 bodies, hides what rivals are doing | Rejected: hurts reading the rivals and costs more |
| (d) Outline by an inflated back-face hull of the local fighter | Same gate needed (stencil) | 2 draws per mesh and a skinned vertex-shader patch | Rejected for now: more code and cost than the flat silhouette, which read well in the review pictures |
| (e) Full-screen outline / edge-detect post pass | Depends on the mask | A render target and a full-screen pass | Rejected: not acceptable on low-end phones |
| (f) Bigger / brighter YOU tag or ring only | No | 0 | Kept as is (PI1-PM1); a tag points at a head the rivals can hide, it does not show the body |

## PIS - online requirements verified against the code, and the server memory soak (branch `claude/wahala-pi-server`, docs/ONLINE_REQUIREMENTS_MATRIX.md; ids PIS1-PIS8 because PI1-PI6 are the player marker above)

Online stays dormant and development-only; nothing below enables it in the public build.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PIS1 | **The public matchmaking wait is 30 seconds, fixed by the server.** A public room starts 30 s after its FIRST human joined (a later joiner does not restart the clock; 32 humans start it at once), then bots named `BOT <name>` fill the empty seats. The default was 20 s in the code and the docs, and a client could choose its own `autoStartSec` on creation (start at once, or hold a room for 10 minutes); now `PUBLIC_WAIT_SECONDS = 30` is the single source of the server default and of the QUICK PLAY text, and only the test-only fast mode (`RUMBLE_TEST_FAST=1`) honours a client value. | `src/net/protocol.ts`, `server/src/core/options.ts`, `server/test/matchmaking.test.ts` |
| PIS2 | **Team Rumble online uses the offline rules, friendly fire included.** Teams are the simulation's `assignTeams` (16 v 16 over the 32 seats, sizes differ by at most one, humans spread because seats alternate sides), never a server invention. `RoomSettings.friendlyFire` (optional, default OFF, Team only, host-only) is the offline setting (WD2, TB1-TB4); the match passes it to the sim and the descriptor tells every client. No new damage rule. | `server/src/rooms/RumbleRoom.ts`, `server/src/core/RumbleMatch.ts`, `server/test/teams.test.ts` |
| PIS3 | **No manual team choice, no team preview in the lobby (for now).** Sides are decided at the match start by the sim; the room screen says so ("Two teams of 16, picked for you and kept even"). Public Team Rumble matchmaking (a mode picker that filters rooms) is NOT built: QUICK PLAY opens the default Free-for-All room. Founder decision needed. | `src/ui/online/rumble/models.ts`, `docs/ONLINE_REQUIREMENTS_MATRIX.md` |
| PIS4 | **A started match says so.** The server locks the room when the match begins; a join to it was reported as "Room is full". It is now `match-started`: "Match already started ... nobody new can join a running match", not retryable. Existing members still reconnect in `playing` (unchanged). | `src/net/client/connection.ts`, `src/ui/online/rumble/models.ts` |
| PIS5 | **Guest names are cleaned by one shared rule, on the server first.** NFKC; letters / digits / `_ . ' -` only; no default-ignorable (invisible) code points, no stacked marks; Latin may not be mixed with Cyrillic / Greek / Armenian / Cherokee (look-alike spoofing); nothing that reads as `BOT` or `BOT ...` (the filler bots' label must stay unique to bots); a duplicate in a room is renamed `Name 2` by comparing a case / accent / look-alike skeleton. Profanity is out of scope. The entry screen words the reason; the server never trusts the client (only `name` and `characterId` are read, an unknown character falls back). | `src/net/protocol.ts`, `server/src/core/Lobby.ts`, `server/test/identity.test.ts` |
| PIS6 | **The temporary identity is Colyseus' own, held in memory.** `sessionId` and the `reconnectionToken` are random 9-character nanoid secrets (54 bits), rotated on every connection (single use), never put in any message another client receives; the online client stores nothing but the display name. No accounts. The strength is acceptable for a private LAN test and a 30 s window; per-IP limits on joins and room creation are required before any public activation. | `tests/online/identity-and-wording.test.ts`, `server/test/identity.test.ts` |
| PIS7 | **Ranked play and persistent rewards stay deferred.** The server persists nothing and imports no progression, account or storage module; results carry match facts and the human / replacement-bot split only. The open policy questions stay open (`docs/CITY_RUMBLE_ONLINE.md` section 8). | `server/test/requirements.test.ts` |
| PIS8 | **A real leak found by the soak is fixed, nothing else is "optimised".** The bots' radial-footprint cache (`DangerBuilder.radialCache`) kept an entry, and the whole observation it points at, for every generator a bot ever saw: one 32-seat match grew its post-GC heap 10.08 +/- 0.11 MB/min until the room ended (the 42 -> 226 MB of the local two-browser run was mostly this, not GC lag). The cache is now dropped when the observation changes (an entry could only ever be reused for the same observation), so every cache hit and every bot decision is unchanged (identical state hash of a seeded 3600-tick match before and after; the Classic cross never reaches this code). Measurements and method: `docs/CITY_RUMBLE_ONLINE.md` section 7.7. | `src/ai/danger.ts`, `tests/ai/danger-radial-cache.test.ts`, `server/bench/soak.ts` |

## PI7+ - Classic raid rear approach and boss jammer protection (branch `claude/wahala-pi-campaign`, from production `9491d92`; docs/CLASSIC_RAID_REAR.md, docs/BOSS_JAMMER_PROTECTION.md; PI1-PI6 are the player marker's, above, so this branch continues at PI7)

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PI7 | **A guard's own about-face is seen, not felt (Easy, Normal).** When a calm raid guard's turn (the 180-degree reversal at the end of its run, a corner, a post's sweep) reveals a player who was out of its view the tick before, it does not notice him at arm's length on that tick: he is noticed by sight after `classicRaid.revealGraceTicks` (Easy 66, Normal 54, Hard 0 = unchanged), so he can see it turn and step out of view; a player already in view, one who walks into view and a guard that has been facing him are instant as before; the bot's calm swing waits the same time. Why: the founder (phone, Normal) was detected "although the guard was looking away". Measured in the real raids (`scripts/raid-dwell.ts`, 6 raids x 2 seeds, a player standing on the tile behind an unseen guard for 0.75 s): 214 of 214 (Easy) and 250 of 254 (Normal) engagements were the guard's own about-face at arm's length with the notice counter at 0, i.e. nothing to react to. After: engaged 20 % -> 3 % (Easy), 24 % -> 4 % (Normal), Hard 96 % unchanged; a watchful 0.3-0.4 s player's clean kills 14 % -> 37 % (Easy), 17 % -> 33 % (Normal); whole missions unchanged (all 216 runs complete, alerts per run 8.89 -> 8.75 Normal). Normal's 54 is the measured step (45 fails a 0.4 s reaction, 54 does not). Nothing else about perception, pace or pressure changed. | `classicRaid.revealGraceTicks`, `systems/territory.ts` (`noticeOf`), `ai/enemyBot.ts`, `scripts/raid-dwell.ts` |
| PI8 | **Relay guards: the boss's backup fighters defend the live dispatch relays (the "jammer"), more of them as the level rises.** `BossBalance.relayGuards` Easy 0, Normal 1, Hard 2 (radius 2): while the boss is shielded and the relays are the objective, the first drafted of marshal, lane blocker, escort (a fallen one is replaced) stands within 2 tiles of the live relay nearest the player (the second at the next nearest) and, as a `guard`, hunts him when he comes inside; the moment the boss is exposed they return to their roles. Why: protection of a live relay was flat across levels (about 40 % of its time, by accident of where relay 2 lies; 32 seeds, scripted player), the founder wants it to rise with the level. Measured (32 seeds, `scripts/boss-jammer.ts`): the share of the player's approaches that meet a defender 48 / 39 % -> 55 / 57 % (Normal), 55 / 42 % -> 70 / 67 % (Hard); phase 3 +36 % / +7 % (Normal), +39 % / 0 % (Hard); wins, whole-fight time and lives lost within noise; time to jam a relay unchanged (4.4-5.2 s). Easy is the production fight bit for bit (`scripts/boss-trace.ts`: 16 of 16 fights identical to `9491d92`; nothing stored where it is 0). Small on purpose; the 8-seed numbers of BB and of the first run here were too noisy to steer by. | `BOSS_BALANCE.relayGuards`, `mission/relayGuard.ts`, `mission/directives.ts`, `mission/boss.ts` |

## FU2S - online server: QUICK PLAY modes, capacity limits, security, reliability (branch `claude/wahala-fu2-server`, from production `f7cf2ae`; docs/ONLINE_LIMITS_AND_SECURITY.md)

The work items are named C1 (QUICK PLAY modes), C3 (resource exhaustion), C4 (security) and C5 (online reliability): source comments cite them as `decision FU2S-C1` ... `FU2S-C5`; the decisions are FU2S-1 and FU2S-2 (C1), FU2S-3 to FU2S-5 (C3), FU2S-6 to FU2S-8 (C4), FU2S-9 to FU2S-11 (C5). Online stays dormant and development-only; nothing below enables it in the public build, hosts anything or adds an account, ranking, reward, payment or voice. Founder requirements honoured: 32 fighters, up to 32 humans, bot takeover on a drop, a 30 s reconnection window, a 30 s public wait then clearly marked bots, Free-for-All AND Team Rumble, no new humans after the start, guest nicknames.

| # | Decision | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FU2S-1 | **QUICK PLAY has a real Free-for-All / Team Rumble choice; rooms are matched by it and nobody is moved between modes.** The client sends `mode` (`ffa` / `team`); the room type is registered with `filterBy(['mode'])`; Free-for-All is the Deathmatch ruleset, Team Rumble the Team ruleset (balanced 16 v 16 by the simulation's `assignTeams`, unchanged). A missing mode (an older client) means Free-for-All, an unknown one is refused, a room refuses a joiner who names another mode, and a Team request with no Team room opens one. | `src/net/protocol.ts` `QuickMode`, `server/src/app.ts`, `RumbleRoom.ts`, `EntryScreen.tsx`; `server/test/capacity.test.ts`, `e2e/rumble-online.spec.ts` |
| FU2S-2 | **A public room's settings are the server's; a public host changes NOTHING; friendly fire stays at the offline default (off) there.** Only the mode comes from the player; stage (Oshodi), length (300 s), bot level (standard) and friendly fire (off) are defaults whatever the client sends; a `settings` message from any member, host included, is refused with `public_room` (before or after others joined), and the room screen shows the settings read-only with the reason. Private rooms keep full host control (including friendly fire in Team). Rationale: the mode was chosen by everybody who queued for it; letting the first player flip it, or switch friendly fire on, would move the others into a game they did not pick. Public rooms are also not joinable by code (quick play is their only door), so a shared code cannot bypass the mode choice. | `Lobby.updateSettings`, `parseRoomOptions`, `models.ts roomRules` |
| FU2S-3 | **Capacity is enforced by the server, in one place, with a cost model measured on this server.** Global caps (rooms, rooms playing, connections), a CPU-cost budget (`10 % + 0.45 % per bot` of one core, budget 75 %: 3 rooms of 1 human + 31 bots, or 6 full rooms), an event-loop-health gate, and a strict FIFO start queue: a room that cannot start waits in its countdown, is told ("The servers are busy ... as soon as a slot is free") and is closed with `server_busy` after 120 s. Every number is an env variable with a documented default and a test; a bad value falls back to the default. Honest play is never limited by the per-address numbers alone. | `server/src/core/guard.ts`, `limits.ts`, `RumbleRoom.lobbyTick` |
| FU2S-4 | **Per-player limits key on a temporary guest session id, not on the address.** The page makes 128 random bits in memory (never stored: a reload is a new guest) and sends them as `gid`; per-guest buckets limit room creation, joins and failed code guesses, and cap the rooms a guest owns. The address only carries generous ceilings (carrier-grade NAT puts many players behind one address) and is the bucket of any request that sends no `gid`. Honest limit: a script can invent guest ids, so the real protection against it is the global caps and the per-address ceilings, enforced where the room is built; no WebSocket message can create a room or start a match. The client address is the TCP peer, or `X-Forwarded-For` only from `RUMBLE_TRUSTED_PROXIES`. | `guard.ts`, `clientIp.ts`, `connection.ts guestSessionId` |
| FU2S-5 | **Refusals have one wire format and plain words.** `server_busy` (503), `rate_limited` (429), `room_unavailable` (404) travel as `code;retry=<seconds>;<text>`; the client turns them into "The servers are busy ... try again in about 25 seconds", "Slow down a little ...", "That room is not open ...". A join by code answers the SAME way for a wrong code, a locked / started room, a full room and a public room (no code oracle), failed joins and failed reconnects are throttled, and the retry hint is jittered. | `protocol.ts encodeBusy / parseBusy`, `app.ts guardedInvoke`, `models.ts errorMessage` |
| FU2S-6 | **Production refuses what is test-only or unauthenticated.** `/metrics` does not exist in production without `RUMBLE_METRICS_TOKEN` (404) and compares the token in constant time; `RUMBLE_TEST_FAST` under `NODE_ENV=production` stops the server at start (and `RUMBLE_LIMITS=off` is only read in that mode); the HTTP `reconnect` door is development-only. Rooms are logged by a hash of their code; room codes, session ids and tokens appear in no log line. | `app.ts`, `options.ts`, `RumbleRoom.ts`; probes in section 7 of the doc |
| FU2S-7 | **Reserved and impersonating nicknames are refused by the shared rule.** `admin`, `moderator`, `system`, `staff`, `official`, `support`, `server`, `wahalateam` ... as a word anywhere, `mod`, `host`, `owner`, `wahala` ... as the whole name, compared on the look-alike-folded skeleton with leet digits and trailing digits folded. Honest names that merely contain such a word pass; no profanity filter (the nickname is the only player-written text). A server-assigned public name (the Ops audit's SRV-4 option) was NOT chosen: the founder approved guest nicknames. | `protocol.ts looksReserved` |
| FU2S-8 | **The 54-bit Colyseus token is documented, not raised.** `generateId()` is bound inside Colyseus; 128 bits would need a fork. Instead: the production reconnect door is the WebSocket, which needs the room id, the session id AND the token (108 bits), the HTTP door is development-only, tokens are single-use, rotated and live 30 s, guesses are rate-limited. Reload handling is unchanged (memory only, PIS6): a reload cannot reclaim or extend a seat; the server's single 30 s window per drop decides. | docs section 6, `room.expiry.realtime.test.ts` |
| FU2S-9 | **A second drop before the join acknowledgement no longer loses the seat (a real defect).** A reconnected socket that drops again before its page ran the join acknowledgement was "never joined" to Colyseus, `allowReconnection` refused it at once and the room logged `dropped` and `left for good` in the same millisecond: a permanent bot seat and a refused (4003) page although the window had just started. The room now clears that flag before holding the seat (`dropsBeforeAck` in `/metrics`). Found by the two-browser e2e under CPU pressure; regression test `room.reconnect-flap.test.ts` fails without the fix. | `RumbleRoom.onDrop` |
| FU2S-10 | **The e2e failures were two things, neither "load" alone, and the check is made resource-stable instead of retried.** (a) the page itself: software WebGL left a browser's main thread blocked for up to 83 s (page diagnostics), so it answered neither pings nor the join acknowledgement and the server's liveness (3 s x 4-6) correctly dropped it, both browsers at the same tick at match start; (b) the real defect FU2S9. The runner now pins the server and the browsers to different CPUs, samples the server's own event-loop delay and dropped ticks, prints a server verdict (and, in CI, fails when the SERVER stalled), the spec records each page's long tasks and socket close codes, and a `light` profile (phone-size viewport, lowest quality) cuts the browsers' CPU. No retry was added anywhere. The two-browser check is a separate, not-required CI job (`online-e2e`). | `scripts/online-e2e.mjs`, `e2e/onlineDiag.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` |
| FU2S-11 | **The 30 s reconnection window is proven with the real clock.** One test (no fake timers, production window and grace) drops two players at once: one returns at 20 s to the same fighter, the other stays away past 30 s and is refused on both doors, with a bot playing its seat for good. | `server/test/room.expiry.realtime.test.ts` |
